米尔顿·H·艾瑞克森 医学博士格雷戈里·贝特森摩谢·费登奎斯加布里埃尔·罗斯

序言Preface

It has been more than 30 years since I published Strategies of Genius Volume III and this fourth volume in the series. This gap of three decades has not been because of a lack of interest in strategies of genius but rather due to an evolution in myself and my own understanding of genius and the principles and processes that produce it. The study of genius is, for me, a lifelong pursuit—a journey that began with curiosity and has deepened into a profound respect for the complexity and potential impact of human subjective experience. As I introduce this fourth volume of the Strategies of Genius series, I find myself reflecting not only on the extraordinary individuals whose lives and work have inspired these pages, but also on the evolving nature of what it means to manifest and to model “genius” and “generative intelligence.”

距离我出版《天才的策略 第三卷》以及本系列的这第四卷，已经过去了三十多年。这三十年间的间隔，并非出于对天才策略缺乏兴趣，而是源于我自身以及我对天才、及其产生所需之原则与过程的理解所发生的演变。对我而言，研究天才是终生的追求——这是一段始于好奇心、并深化为对人类主观经验之复杂性与潜在影响的深深敬意的旅程。当我推出《天才的策略》系列的这第四卷时，我所反思的，不仅是那些其生平与著作启发了这些篇章的非凡个体，更是"彰显并建模'天才'与'生生不息的智慧'"这件事本身在不断演变的性质。

In the earlier volumes, I set out to uncover the thinking patterns, beliefs, and strategies that set certain individuals apart. I sought to demystify genius—not to diminish its wonder, but to make its lessons accessible and applicable to anyone willing to learn. In Strategies of Genius IV I continue to explore the structures and processes that underlie exceptional achievement—in particular that with respect to human transformation—but with an even greater emphasis on integration and embodiment. Genius with respect to human transformation emerges from the dialogue between different ways of knowing, and in the willingness to personally question, adapt and evolve.

在早先的几卷中，我着手揭示那些使某些个体脱颖而出的思维模式、信念与策略。我试图去神秘化天才——并非要削弱其奇妙，而是要让它的教训变得可被任何人学习与应用。《天才的策略 第四卷》延续了对卓越成就——尤其是关于人类转化方面——之底层结构与过程的探索，但比以往更强调整合与具身。关于人类转化的天才，诞生于不同认知方式之间的对话，以及亲身去质疑、适应与演化的意愿之中。

The individuals featured in this volume—like those in previous books—represent a range of fields, backgrounds, and approaches. Some are celebrated for their groundbreaking discoveries and influence on various fields of knowledge; others for their creative vision, their capacity for empathy, or their ability to inspire change. What unites them is not a single method or philosophy, but a shared commitment to exploration, learning and transformation.

本卷所收录的人物——与以往各书一样——横跨不同的领域、背景与方法。有人以其开创性发现及对诸多知识领域的影响而著称；有人则以其创造性远见、共情能力，或激发改变的力量而为人所知。将他们联结在一起的，不是某一种方法或哲学，而是对探索、学习与转化的共同承诺。

It is particularly significant to me that this volume is the first to include a woman genius, Gabrielle Roth. This is not because I have, as a man, been only influenced in my own development by other males. Women have been just as significant an influence in my life as men. This bias is a cultural filter that has, until now, brought more attention to the contributions of the males of our species than females (or non-gender affiliated individuals). My life and intellectual as well as personal development has been (thankfully) influenced by at least as many non-males as “males.” Genius is not a male attribute, it is a human attribute.

对我而言尤为意义重大的，是本卷首次纳入了一位女性天才——加布里埃尔·罗斯。这并非因为身为男性的我，在自身发展中只受过其他男性的影响。女性对我生命的影响，与男性一样深远。这种偏差是一种文化滤镜，它至今为止，让我们这个物种中男性的贡献比女性（或非性别归属的个体）得到了更多关注。我的生命、智识乃至个人的发展，所幸都受到至少与"男性"同样多的"非男性"的影响。天才不是男性的属性，它是人类的属性。

One of the central themes of this volume is the role of embodiment and lived experience. In previous volumes, I focused primarily on cognitive strategies: how geniuses think, reason, and imagine. In this book, I bring closer attention to the ways in which genius is influenced by physical action, movement and concrete interaction with the external world and with others.

本卷的核心主题之一，是具身与 lived experience（亲身经验）的作用。在之前的各卷中，我主要关注认知策略：天才如何思考、推理与想象。在本书中，我将更密切地关注天才如何受到身体动作、运动以及与外部世界和他人的具体互动所影响。

This emphasis on embodiment is not accidental. Over the years, I have come to appreciate the wisdom of the body—the subtle cues, the intuitive leaps, the capacity for learning that goes beyond words and logic. This is especially important in this (at the time of this writing) emerging influence of Artificial Intelligence, which is the complementary opposite of embodied intelligence. At this stage of development at least, AI is the result of the integration of massive amounts of data that is not stored in an organic human body and nervous system, but rather in huge digital data centers. As with many things, this is both an advantage and a limitation.

这种对具身的强调并非偶然。多年来，我愈发欣赏身体的智慧——那些细微的线索、直觉的跳跃、超越言语与逻辑的学习能力。这一点在我写作此刻人工智能（AI）日益兴起的背景下尤为重要，因为 AI 恰是具身智能的互补对立面。至少在此发展阶段，AI 是海量数据整合的产物，这些数据并非存储在有机的人体及神经系统中，而是存储于庞大的数字数据中心。如同许多事物一样，这既是优势，也是局限。

Many of the individuals featured in this volume have developed practices that integrate human mind and body, intellect and intuition. Their work reminds us that genius is not just a matter of conscious cognitive intellect, but of presence, awareness and engagement with the full spectrum of human experience.

本卷所收录的许多人物，都发展出了整合人心与身、智识与直觉的练习。他们的工作提醒我们：天才不只是有意识的认知智力，更是临在、觉察，以及对 entire spectrum of human experience（人类经验的整个光谱）的投入。

As you read this volume, I invite you to approach it not as a collection of biographies or a manual of techniques, but as a conversation—a dialogue between you and some of the minds (both cognitive and somatic) that have made an impact on our world. Notice the questions that arise, the connections that spark your curiosity, the strategies that resonate with your own experience. Use these pages as a springboard for your own exploration and your own journey of personal evolution.

当你阅读本卷时，我邀请你不要把它当作一部传记集或技术手册，而是当作一场对话——一场你与那些影响了我们世界的心智（既有认知的，也有体感的）之间的对话。留意那些浮现的问题、点燃你好奇心的联结、与你自身经验共鸣的策略。将这些篇章作为你自身探索与演化之旅的跳板。

The process of modeling genius is, at its core, an act of respect. It is a way of honoring the achievements of others while recognizing their potential within ourselves. It is also an act of humility, a reminder that there is always more to learn, more to discover, more to become. In sharing these strategies, my hope is not to prescribe a single rigid path, but to offer a toolkit—a set of lenses through which you can examine your own challenges, aspirations, and evolution.

建模天才，其核心是一种尊重之举。它既是在 honoring 他人成就的同时，认出它们在我们自己身上的潜力；也是一种谦逊的提醒——总有更多可学、可发现、可成为。分享这些策略时，我的愿望不是规定一条僵化的道路，而是提供一套工具——一组透镜，透过它们，你可以审视自己的挑战、抱负与演化。

I am deeply grateful to the many individuals who have contributed to this work—both those whose lives and work are featured here and those who have supported, challenged and inspired me along the way. The journey of exploring genius is, by necessity, a collaborative one. It is enriched by dialogue, by the sharing of ideas and by the willingness to see the world through other’s eyes.

我深深感激众多为本作品作出贡献的人——无论是其生平与著作被收录于此者，还是在沿途支持、挑战并启发我的人。探索天才的旅程，本质上必然是协作的。它因对话、因观念分享、因愿以他人之眼观世界而丰盈。

As you embark on your own exploration of these strategies, I encourage you to remain open—to new perspectives, to unexpected insights, to the possibility that genius could look different from what you expect. Remember that the strategies described here are not ends in themselves, but starting points. Adapt them, experiment with them, make them your own.

当你踏上自己对天才策略的探索时，我鼓励你保持开放——对新视角、对意外洞见、对"天才可能不同于你所预期"的可能性保持开放。请记住，此处所描述的策略本身并非终点，而是起点。去调适它们、实验它们、让它们成为你自己的。

In closing, I return to the spirit that has animated this series from its beginning: a spirit of curiosity, of wonder, of awe and of hope. The study of genius is not just about understanding the extraordinary; it is about expanding our sense of what is possible for ourselves and for our world. May this volume inspire you to pursue your own questions, to cultivate your own gifts and to contribute your own unique voice to the ongoing story of human evolution.

最后，我回到自本系列开端便推动它的那种精神：好奇心、惊奇、敬畏与希望的精神。研究天才，不只是为了理解非凡，更是为了拓展我们对自己、对这个世界之可能性的感知。愿本卷启发你去追求自己的问题、培育自己的天赋，并将你独特的声音，贡献于人类演化的未竟故事之中。

Robert Dilts July 2026 Santa Cruz, California

罗伯特·迪尔茨2026 年 7 月加利福尼亚州，圣克鲁兹

天才的模式与生生不息的智慧Patterns of Genius and Generative Intelligence

导论Introduction

The English word "genius" comes from a Latin term of the same spelling, which originally referred to a "guiding spirit" that was with a person from their birth. It, in turn, was derived from the Latin gignere, meaning to "beget, or bring forth." The implication is that genius is essentially something "generative" or creative.

英文词"genius"（天才）源自拼法相同的拉丁术语，原指一个人自出生起便相伴的"守护精灵"。它又溯源于拉丁语 gignere，意为"生养，或带来"。其意涵在于：genius 本质上就是某种"生成性"（generative）或创造性的事物。

Today the term is most commonly used to indicate "distinguished mental superiority; uncommon intellectual power; especially, superior power of invention or origination of any kind, or of forming new combinations" (Webster's Dictionary). It is also used to refer to a person "endowed with uncommon vigor of mind and superior intellectual faculties."

今天，这个词最常用于指"卓越的心智优越性；非凡的智识力量；尤指任何形式的发明或原创、或形成新组合的 Superior power"（据《韦氏词典》）。它也被用来指称"天生具备非凡心智活力与优越智识禀赋"的人。

According to NeuroLinguistic Programming (NLP), the manifestation of genius is a function of unique mental strategies and other cognitive patterns and characteristics, which can be modeled and learned. Even if a person has innate talents and creative abilities, they will not become fully manifest without effective strategies, skills and support. By the same reasoning, people who do not naturally possess certain creative thinking abilities can potentially develop them by learning more effective strategies.

根据神经语言编程（NLP），天才的彰显，是一系列独特心理策略及其他认知模式与特征的函数，而这些是可以被建模与学习的。即使一个人拥有天赋与创造能力，若无有效的策略、技能与支持，它们也不会完全彰显。同理，那些天生并不具备某些创造性思维能力的人，也有可能通过学习更有效的策略来发展它们。

A strategy is a structured mental process used by a person to organize and orchestrate his or her actions in order to accomplish a result. A strategy is a bit like a computer program or AI algorithm. It is essentially a set of instructions that operates on the information generated by our sensory representational systems. And, similar to a program or algorithm, you can apply the same strategy to a variety of different types of contents. For example, a particular creativity strategy, learning strategy or decision-making strategy, with some adaptation, could be applied to subjects as diverse as music, physics, health or business.

所谓策略（strategy），是一个人用以组织与编排其行动以达成结果的、结构化的心理过程。策略有点像计算机程序或 AI 算法。它本质上是一组指令，运作于我们的感官表象系统所生成的信息之上。并且，与程序或算法类似，你可以将同一策略应用于多种不同类型的内容。例如，某个特定的创造力策略、学习策略或决策策略，稍加调适，便可用于音乐、物理、健康或商业等截然不同的主题。

While strategies have a definite structure, they are not always conscious (and frequently are unconscious). Key elements of their structure may be revealed through specific types of linguistic and behavioral cues. Strategies can also at times be "reverse engineered" by examining characteristics of the results they produce.

策略虽有确定的结构，却未必是有意识的（而且常常是无意识的）。其结构的关键要素，可通过特定类型的语言与行为线索显现出来。策略有时也能通过审视其产生结果的特征被"逆向工程"出来。

Thus, the purpose of exploring strategies of genius is not to try to identify the one "real" map or model of how a particular individual thought. Rather, it is to identify sequences of inner processes that add more possibilities to our own strategies so that we can be more creative and effective in a practical and concrete way. The goal of this book series has been to analyze important historical figures in order to identify key aspects of the way in which they viewed and interacted with the world that can enrich our own experience and performance.

因此，探索天才策略的目的，并非试图辨认某个人"真实"思考方式的唯一"地图"或模型。相反，是要辨识出内在过程的一连串序列，为我们的策略增添更多可能性，使我们在实践与具体层面更具创造力与效能。本系列丛书的目标，一直是分析重要的历史人物，以辨识他们看待并与世界互动方式中的关键面向，从而丰富我们自身的经验与表现。

My particular interest – in relation to my own mission – has been how to apply the strategies of genius that I have identified to human issues. In other words, to explore how can we apply these strategies so that we can

我个人的兴趣——关涉我自己的使命——在于如何将已知的天才策略应用于人类议题。换言之，去探索我们如何应用这些策略，使我们得以

策略Strategies

become more intelligent about our own human processes. In that regard, all of the geniuses to be studied in this volume happened to have applied their creativity to such human issues.

……对我们自身的人类过程变得更聪明。就此而言，本卷所研究的所有天才，恰好都将他们的创造力应用于此类人类议题：

  • Milton H. Erickson, M.D.: psychiatry and hypnotherapy
  • Gregory Bateson: anthropology and social systems
  • Moshe Feldenkrais: body mechanics and mind-body healing
  • Gabrielle Roth: personal transformation through movement
  • 米尔顿·H·艾瑞克森 医学博士： 精神医学与催眠治疗
  • 格雷戈里·贝特森： 人类学与社会系统
  • 摩谢·费登奎斯： 身体力学与身心疗愈
  • 加布里埃尔·罗斯： 通过动作实现个人转化

策略的层次Levels of Strategies

I pointed out in Strategies of Genius Volume I that strategies can operate at different levels:

我在《天才的策略 第一卷》中指出，策略可在不同层次上运作：

  • A micro-strategy focuses on how exactly a particular person is thinking within a specific moment in order to accomplish a particular task. If somebody is engaging in a process of remembering a particular piece of information, lets say a telephone number, what do they do with that information in order to store it and recover it from within their brain or bio-computer? On this micro-level you might want to know exactly what size that person is visualizing the telephone number in his or her mind. Is there a particular color in which that person pictures the number? Does the person verbally repeat the number internally? Does the person have a feeling somewhere in his or her body? This would be a micro-strategy. It would be like assembly language or machine code in a computer.
  • 微观策略（micro-strategy） 聚焦于某个特定的人，在某个特定时刻，为完成某项特定任务，究竟如何思考。如果某人正在进行记忆某条信息的 process——比如说一个电话号码——他对那信息做了什么，以便将其存储于脑中或"生物计算机"内并随后取回？在这个微观层次上，你可能想知道此人脑海中可视化那个电话号码的确切大小。此人是否以某种特定颜色来描绘那个数字？此人是否在内心 verbally 重复它？此人身体某处是否有一股感觉？这便是微观策略。它就像是计算机中的汇编语言或机器码。
  • 宏观策略（macro-strategy） 更像是建模"成功"或"领导力"。一套完整关于成功或领导力的策略，不会是微观策略，而是一个整合了许多微观策略的更高层次程序。它可能发生在更长的时间跨度中。有时，一个过程更一般性的步骤，对于达成特定结果才是重要的，而你在微观层次上如何从 A 到 B 到 C，并不重要，或者可能需要显著变化。重要的是你从 A 抵达了 C，无论微观步骤如何。你个人如何抵达，由你决定。因此，宏观策略关乎思维过程更一般性的运作与步骤。
  • A macro-strategy would be more like modeling "success" or "leadership." An overall strategy for success or leadership is not going to be a micro-strategy but rather a higher-level program that will incorporate many micro strategies. It might be something that takes place over a much longer period of time. Sometimes it is the more general steps of a process that are important for reaching a particular result, and how specifically you get from A to B to C on a micro-level is not important or may require significant variation. What is important is that you get from A to C regardless of the micro steps. The way you personally get there is up to you. So, a macro-strategy would have to do with the more general operations and steps of a thinking process.

此外，还有一种可称为"元策略（meta-strategy）"者，它将若干宏观策略整合为更一般的过程，如"改变""转化""成长""对齐"等。

There is also what could be termed a "meta-strategy" which would integrate a number of macro-strategies into a more general process such as "change," "transformation," "growth," "alignment," etc.

在本系列之前的各卷中，我检视了多位知名"天才"的微观策略，包括阿尔伯特·爱因斯坦、沃尔夫冈·阿玛多伊斯·莫扎特、列奥纳多·达·芬奇、华特·迪士尼、尼古拉·特斯拉、亚里士多德、西格蒙德·弗洛伊德，以及柯南·道尔爵士笔下的夏洛克·福尔摩斯。我还归纳出这些创造性天才所共享的一系列宏观策略，汇为我所谓的"天才的模式"（patterns of genius）。

In the previous volumes of this series I examined the micro strategies of a number of well-known "geniuses," including Albert Einstein, Wolfgang Amadeus Mozart, Leonardo da Vinci, Walt Disney, Nikola Tesla, Aristotle, Sigmund Freud and Sir Arthur Conan Doyle's Sherlock Holmes. I also identified a series of macro strategies shared by these creative geniuses that I summarized in what I have called "patterns of genius."

天才的关键模式Key Patterns of Genius

Some key patterns of genius identified by this study so far include:

本研究迄今辨识出的若干天才关键模式包括：

  1. Emphasizing the use of imagination.
  1. 强调运用想象力。

Geniuses have the ability to envision something new and different. Genius means to be "generative" and to create something that has not existed before. Generativity focuses on developing higher-level processes that can function in an evolutionary way towards new possibilities. As Albert Einstein pointed out, "imagination is more important than knowledge." Knowledge can only tell us what we already know. Imagination opens doors to new realities.

天才拥有设想崭新而不同之事物的能力。天才意味着"生成性"，意味着创造出此前不存在之物。生成性聚焦于发展出更高层次的过程，使其能以演化的方式运作，朝向新的可能性。正如阿尔伯特·爱因斯坦所指出的："想象力比知识更重要。"知识只能告诉我们已然知晓之事。想象力则为新现实打开大门。

Webster's Dictionary defines imagination as the "act or process of forming a conscious idea or mental image of something never before wholly perceived in reality by the imaginer (as through a synthesis of remembered elements of previous sensory experiences or ideas as modified by unconscious mechanisms)." Imagination, then, relates to mental representations or "images" which have been internally generated or constructed. It involves forming a mental representation of something that is not necessarily related to one's past experience, nor in the ongoing environment. However, as Webster's points out, imagination does involve "the power to recombine the materials furnished by experience or memory."

《韦氏词典》将 imagination（想象力）定义为"形成某种此前从未在现实中整体被感知之事物的、有意识观念或心智图像的行为或过程（如通过对既往感官经验或观念之记忆元素的综合，并经潜意识机制修正）"。因此，想象力关乎内在生成或建构的心智表征或"图像"。它涉及形成某事物的心智表征，而该事物未必与一个人的过去经验相关，也未必在当下环境之中。然而，正如《韦氏》所指出的，想象力确实涉及"重组经验或记忆所提供材料的力量"。

Imagination may be used to create dreams, visualize outcomes, and anticipate the longer-term effects of those dreams and outcomes on our lives. According to philosopher Bertrand Russell, "It is only through imagination that men become aware of what the world might be." By using our imagination and creating mental images, we stimulate and alert our neurology to a particular direction, triggering self-organizing processes which begin to automatically and unconsciously work towards achieving the outcomes we have imaged. When we imagine a goal or dream in our mind's eye, it allows us to recognize and mobilize the resources necessary to turn imagination into reality—what Walt Disney called "imagineering."

想象力可用于创造梦想、可视化结果，并预想那些梦想与结果对我们生命的长期影响。据哲学家伯特兰·罗素所言："唯有通过想象力，人类才得以察觉世界可能成为的样子。"通过运用想象力并创造心智图像，我们刺激并警醒自己的神经系统朝向特定方向，触发自组织过程——它开始自动且无意识地运作，朝着我们想象出的结果迈进。当我们在心眼前想象一个目标或梦想时，它使我们得以辨识并调动必要的资源，将想象变为现实——这正是华特·迪士尼所称的"想象工程"（imagineering）。

  1. Making numerous links between the senses.
  1. 在感官之间建立大量联结。

When engaging their imagination, geniuses tend to use all of their senses and create "synesthesias" (connections) between the senses. Mozart is probably the best example of this—he felt, saw, and even tasted his music. Einstein claimed to derive feelings from his internal imaginary constructs. He had the ability to put himself in his internal pictures, to bring what he imagined into his nervous system, and make it real.

当调动想象力时，天才倾向于运用其全部感官，并在感官之间创造"联觉"（synesthesia，即联结）。莫扎特或许是最佳例证——他感受、看见、甚至品尝他的音乐。爱因斯坦声称能从他内在想象的构造中获取感受。他有能力置身于自己的内在画面之中，将他想象之物带入神经系统，并使之成真。

〔图 p071〕

The more ways you have to understand something, the more you understand it. Can you represent it visually, verbally, somatically...?

因此，天才的一个关键模式，是调动多元智能（multiple intelligences）。多元智能的基本原则是：你理解某事物的方式越多，你对它的理解就越深。 如果你只有一种

Thus, a key pattern of genius is to engage multiple intelligences. The basic principle of multiple intelligences is that the more ways you have to understand something, the more you understand it. If you only have a

言语的方式来表达或理解某事物，你便受到极大限制。然而，若你还拥有一种体感的方式与一种视觉的方式，你便能更具创造性地表达它，更完整地理解它。

verbal way to express or understanding something, you are very limited. If, however, in addition you have a somatic way and a visual way, you can express it more creatively and understand it more completely.

不同的感官，及与之相关的智能，充当了我们感知与经验的滤镜。举例而言，视觉图像在呈现某一现象或经验之关键要素间的相互关系时，比词语更有效。言语表征更多来自"左脑"，聚焦于对象与序列；而视觉表征由"右脑"生成，强调模式与关系。在图像中引入色彩，也能带出关于感受的、全然不同层次的细微差别。

Different senses, and the intelligences associated with them, act as filters on our perception and experience. Visual images, for instance, are more effective than words for bringing out the interrelationships between the key elements of a particular phenomenon or experience. Verbal representations come more from the "left-brain" and focus more on objects and sequencing while visual representations are generated by the "right-brain" and emphasize patterns and relationships. Incorporating color into the images can also bring out a whole different level of nuance with respect to feelings.

另一项基本的智能领域，是身体中所承载的——即所谓"体感智能"（somatic intelligence）。它常通过身体语言来表达。许多我们说出口或可视化的东西，也可以用身体作为一种"体感模型"来展示。即便是"惯性""创造性""自由""贡献""开放"等看似抽象的概念，也能通过姿态与身体动作来表达，而这往往能带来比大量言语更深的领会与洞见。

One of the other essential areas of intelligence is that contained in the body; what is called "somatic intelligence." This is frequently expressed through body language. Many of the things we say or visualize, we can also show with our body as a type of "somatic model." Even seemingly abstract concepts like "inertia," "creativity," "freedom," "contribution," "openness," etc., can be expressed through gestures and body movements, which can lead to deeper understanding greater insight than a lot of verbalization.

仅通过一套有限的滤镜去感知某个情境或经验，可能会局限我们的理解，以及我们所感知到的可用可能性。能够在不同表征形式之间移动，对创造力与创新极为重要。

Perceiving a situation or experience through only one limited set of filters can potentially limit our understanding and the possibilities we perceive available. Being able to move between different forms of representation is extremely important for creativity and innovation.

  1. 进入特殊状态以接通创造性的潜意识过程。
  1. Accessing special states for access to creative unconscious processes.

多数天才都直接承认潜意识过程在其工作中的重要性。许多创造性人才声称，他们一些最好的点子，是在清晨淋浴、并未有意识地聚焦任何特定事物时冒出来的。创造性人才的另一种常见说法是："我把能找到的信息一股脑塞进脑子，直到彻底精疲力竭、再也装不下任何东西。然后我去睡觉。醒来时，答案就来了！"莫扎特将他创造性的过程描述为一场"愉悦、鲜活的梦"。列奥纳多甚至更进一步，提出了一些通过凝视墙壁等来刺激潜意识联结的过程。

Most geniuses directly acknowledge the importance of unconscious processes in their work. Many creative people, for instance, claim they have gotten some of their best ideas when they are in the shower in the morning and not consciously focused on anything in particular. Another common claim by creative people goes something to the effect of, "I stuff my mind full of the information that I can find until I am completely exhausted, and I can't fit anything anymore in there. Then I go to sleep. When I wake up, I have the answer!" Mozart described his creative processes as a "pleasing, lively dream." Leonardo even went so far as to suggest some processes for stimulating unconscious associations by staring at walls, etc.

接通我们的"创造性潜意识"，涉及让"频道打开"，并连接到超越我们有意识认知心智的智能。这种状态可用缩写 COACH 来刻画。

Gaining access to our "creative unconscious" involves getting our "channel open" and connecting to an intelligence beyond our conscious cognitive mind. This type of state can be characterized by the acronym COACH.

C居中（Centered）

〔图 p070〕

C Centered

A觉察地关注（Attending with Awareness）

O Open

C联结（Connected）

A Attending with Awareness

H涵容、尊重、好客（Holding, Honoring, Hospitable）

C Connected

这标示着一种内在状态：你于自身——尤其是你的腹部中心（身体真正的物理中心）——居中；对新的信息、观念与可能性开放；对周遭与内在所发生之事，以觉察与正念关注；与多元智能及内在资源（头、心、腹）相连，也置身于你所从属的更大资源场域之中联结；并以创造性、好奇心与尊重（好客）的心态涵容当下发生的一切。

H Holding, Honoring, Hospitable

相反的情况，发生于我们的"频道关闭"、从而与创造性潜意识的洞见与智慧断开连接之时。这会将我们 plunge 进无益的生存策略，如攻击、逃避、僵化或放弃（战、逃、冻、垮）。这种"退化"的内在状态，可用字母 CRASH 来概括：

This indicates an internal state where you are centered in yourself, especially your belly center (the actual physical center of your body); open to new information, ideas and possibilities; attentive to what is going on around you and within you with awareness and mindfulness; connected to multiple intelligences and inner resources, head, heart and gut, as well as in the bigger field of resources of which you are a part; and holding whatever is happening from a mindset of creativity, curiosity and respect (hospitality).

C收缩（Contraction）

The opposite occurs when our "channel is closed" and we are disconnected from the insight and wisdom of our creative unconscious. This can plunge us into unhelpful survival strategies such as attack, escape, rigidity or giving up (fight, flight, freeze, fold). This "degenerative" internal state can be summarized by the letters CRASH:

R反应（Reactivity）

〔图 p069〕

C Contraction

S分离（Separation）

R Reactivity

H敌意、伤害与憎恨（Hostility, Hurting and Hating）

A Analysis Paralysis

当我们处于 CRASH 状态时，我们的滤镜关闭了。我们陷入"神经肌肉锁结"（neuromuscular lock）。似乎一切都失灵了，人也容易感到挫败、防御、无助与受困。当人们处于 CRASH 状态时，他们想什么无关紧要，说什么无关紧要，做什么也无关紧要。

S Separation

要培育天才，重要的是拥有一些练习，能帮助创造并强化 COACH 状态，使我们得以与最好的自己及创造性潜意识保持连接。当生活平稳推进时，保持有资源的状态很容易；但要在动荡时期依然保持创造与创新，我们必须已将 COACH 所定义的那些品质发展到"进入肌肉"的程度。

H Hostility, Hurting and Hating

  1. 调动多重视角，容纳不同的观点。

When we are in a CRASH state, our filters are shut down. We are in a state of "neuromuscular lock." Nothing seems to work, and it is easy to feel frustrated, defensive, helpless and trapped. When people are in a CRASH state, what they think makes no difference, what they say makes no difference, and what they do makes no difference.

天才最常见的特征之一，是能够容纳关于某一主题或过程的、比常人更多的视角，并找到无人涉足的视角。

To cultivate genius, it is important to have practices that help to create and strengthen the COACH state so that we can stay connected to the best of ourselves and our creative unconscious. It is easy to stay resourceful when life moves smoothly, but in order to remain creative and innovative during turbulent times, we must have developed the qualities defined by COACH until they are "in the muscle."

天才的特质，还在于能够同时持有不同、甚至相异的观念与意见。

  1. Engaging multiple perspectives and entertaining different points of view.

除了能够采取不同观点，天才还拥有认同不同知觉位置（perceptual positions）的能力。例如，华特·迪士尼若不能亲自演绎他笔下动画角色的行为，便无法描述它们。他还拥有抛开自身知觉位置、并假定其观众世界观的能力。

One of the most common characteristics of genius is to be able to entertain more perspectives of a particular subject or process than is typical, and to find the perspective(s) that no one else has taken.

天才的关键能力之一，是认同（进入第二位置）他们所工作的对象，即便那是无生命的物体。自称与计算机相关的天才声称，能像计算机那样观看世界。米开朗基罗能进入一块石头的第二位置。他说："我不是在雕刻雕像。雕像已在大理石中，等着我把她释放。我只是不停凿刻，直到她自由。"

characteristic of genius is the ability to simultaneously hold different, and even disparate, ideas and opinions simultaneously.

天才常采取的另一种知觉位置，被称为"元位置"（meta position）。meta 一词源自希腊语，意为"在……之上""在……之间"或"超越"。在英语中，它也常用来表示"关于"。"元模型"（meta model）即关于其他模型的模型。"元认知"（metacognition）是对自身认知过程的觉知，即关于认知的认知。"元信息"（meta messages）则是关于其他信息的信息，它们提供框架或语境标记，影响那些信息的意义。

In addition to being able to take different points of view, geniuses have the ability to identify with different perceptual positions. Walt Disney, for example, could not describe the behavior of his animated characters without simultaneously acting them out himself. He also had the ability to leave his own perceptual position behind and assume the world view of his audiences.

采取元位置，是一项能够深思熟虑地反思特定情境与互动的关键技能。就一次互动而言，元位置涉及从那次互动中抽离（disassociate），并如同见证者而非参与者般观察它。采取元位置的能力，也使你得以与互动本身拉开距离，而不致 stuck 于某一视角或对其患近视。

One of the key abilities of geniuses is to identify (take second position) with whatever they are working with, even if they are inanimate objects. People who are geniuses with computers claim to be able to view the world as a computer would. Michelangelo could go to second position with a piece of stone. He said, "I don't make the statue. The statue is already in the marble waiting for me to release it. I just keep chiseling until it is free."

元位置涉及：将你与另一人之间的互动，当作你是一名旁观者般加以反思。

Another perceptual position that is frequently taken by geniuses is known as "meta position." The term meta is a Greek word meaning "over," "between" or "above." In English, it is also often used to mean "about." A "meta model," for example, is a model about other models. "Metacognition" is the awareness of one's own cognitive processes, i.e., cognition about cognition. "Meta messages" are messages about other messages, which provide frames or context markers that influence the meaning of those messages.

  1. 在不同区块大小与思维层次之间来回切换。

Taking a meta position is a key skill for being able to reflect thoughtfully upon particular situations and interactions. With respect to an interaction, meta position involves disassociating from that interaction and observing it as though you were a witness to it and not a participant. The ability to take meta position also allows you to get some distance from the interaction itself and not become stuck in or myopic with respect to one perspective.

天才能够在更广阔的愿景，与彰显大局所需的具体行动之间轻松移动。他们能处理细小的片段，却不被所有细节缠住。他们也能看见大画面，而不丢失小片段。天才似乎独有地能平衡二者。天才还能够在抽象模型与原则，以及那些抽象的具体表达之间移动。他们能从所工作的具体例子中找出抽象原则，并在具体例子中体现代抽象关系。这形成一种循环，使他们能通过具体世界的反馈来精炼理论，同时透过更抽象原则的反馈来精炼他们的物理作品。

〔图 p068〕

Meta position involves reflecting on an interaction between yourself and another as if you were an observer.

天才的一项重要原则是：任何只顾有益于我们所从属的全息层级中某一部分、却以牺牲另一部分为代价的行动，本质上都会出问题。当我们或蓄意或无意地排除、甚至试图摆脱或摧毁全息层级的关键部分时，便会产生可预测且不可避免的问题。

  1. Moving back and forth between different chunk sizes and levels of thinking.
  1. 对所从属的、超越个体身份的事物，保有连接与贡献的具身感受。

Geniuses are able to move easily between the broader vision and specific actions required to manifest the bigger picture. They can work with the little pieces and yet not become caught up in all of the details. They are also able to see the big picture without losing sight of the little pieces. Geniuses seem to be uniquely able to balance both. Geniuses are also able to move between abstract models and principles and specific concrete expressions of those abstractions. They are able to find the abstract principles in the concrete examples they are working with, and to embody abstract relationships in specific examples. This forms a kind of loop that allows them to refine their theories through feedback from the concrete world, and at the same time refine their physical works through feedback from more abstract principles.

在论及解剖学的著作引言中，列奥纳多豪迈地宣称："我要创造奇迹。"谈及对动画的热情，华特·迪士尼坚称："这不是卡通媒介，我们在此要征服世界……凡人类心智所能构想的，动画都能解释。"史蒂夫·乔布斯宣告："我想在宇宙上砸出凹痕。"论及研究物理的缘由，爱因斯坦说："我想知道上帝的思绪，其余皆为细节。"所有天才的一个共同特征，是他们将自己的工作视为源自某物、并服务于大于自身之物。

This capacity can be related to the notions of "holons" and "holarchies," initially proposed by Arthur Koestler. The term holon refers to entities that are in themselves whole while simultaneously being part of larger wholes and made up of smaller wholes. As human beings, for example, we are whole and independent individuals in and of ourselves. At the same time, we are also part of successively bigger systems that include but also transcend us. We are also made up of smaller wholes. Specifically, we are made up of whole atoms (which themselves are made of subatomic particles), that make up whole molecules, that combine to create whole cells, which join together to make whole organs and a whole interconnected nervous system and ultimately a whole body. We, in turn, are part of progressively larger wholes: a family, a professional community, a culture, the human race, the whole system of living creatures on this planet and ultimately the whole universe. This creates a type of holarchy made up of the different levels of wholes. Such holarchies also apply to our psychological make up as much as our physical reality.

我们的身份感与目的感，可被视为由两个互补的面向构成：我们所谓的"小我"（ego）与"灵性/灵魂"（soul）。我们的小我，关乎这样一个事实——我们自身是一个独特而分离的整体。因此，小我取向于个人安全与保障、认可、控制、成就与自我利益。在身份层次上，小我关乎社会角色，以及我们觉得为了达成那些目标、自己应当或需要成为的人。在目的层次上，小我取向于生存、认可与个人的雄心。

An important principle of genius is that any action that focuses on benefiting one part of the holarchy to which we belong at the expense of another will be inherently problematic. When we either knowingly or unwittingly, exclude or even attempt to get rid of or destroy crucial parts of a holarchy it creates predictable and unavoidable problems.

我们的灵魂，关乎这样一个互补的事实——我们同时属于一个更大的全息层级。我们的灵魂，可被视为我们带着进入世界的、独特的生命力量、本质或能量，它向外表达给我们所从属的全息子。因此，灵魂更聚焦于诸如服务、贡献、连接、关怀与合作之类的动机。在身份层次上，灵魂关乎我们的使命，以及我们带入世界的独特禀赋。在目的层次上，灵魂取向于我们希望透过我们去创造、却超越我们的、对世界之愿景。

  1. Having a felt sense of connection and contribution to something beyond individual identity.

支持小我与灵魂之间的平衡，是有效且生态（ecological）的创造力与创新的另一项关键原则。某些尝试性的解方可能强调小我而牺牲灵魂，制造孤立与自恋。另一些则贬低甚至妖魔化小我，导致自我关怀的缺失与耗竭。重要的是认识到：小我的膨胀与萎缩，都不可持续、也都不具生产性。在滋养性创造天才的隐含目标中，维持小我与灵魂之间健康而动态的平衡，始终在列。

In the introduction to his work on anatomy, Leonardo boldly stated, "I wish to work miracles." Of his passion for animation, Walt Disney maintained, "This is not the cartoon medium, we have worlds to conquer here... Whatever the mind of man can conceive, animation can explain." Steve Jobs declared, "I want to put a dent in the universe." Of his reasons for studying physics, Einstein maintained, "I want to know God's thoughts, everything else is details." One common characteristic of all geniuses is that they perceive their work as coming from something and serving something larger than themselves.

我们属于不断更大的整体：家庭、专业社群、这个星球上所有生物的整体系统，最终是整全宇宙。

Our identity and sense of purpose can be viewed as being composed of two complementary aspects: what we could call our "ego" and our "soul." Our ego relates to the fact that we are a unique and separate whole in and of ourselves. Thus, our ego is oriented towards things like personal safety and security, approval, control, achievement and self-benefit. At the identity level, ego relates to our social roles and who we feel we should be or need to be in order to achieve those things. At the level of purpose our ego is oriented toward survival, recognition and our personal ambitions.

  1. 强烈面向未来，并相信可能性。

Our soul relates to the complementary fact that we are simultaneously part of a larger holarchy. Our soul can be considered to be the unique life force, essence or energy that we come into the world with that is expressed outwardly to the holon we are part of. Thus, our soul is more focused on motivations such as service, contribution, connection, care and cooperation. At the identity level, soul relates to our mission and the unique gifts that we bring into the world. At the level of purpose, of our soul is oriented toward our vision of what we want to create in the world through us but that is beyond us.

天才与生成性变化，总是面向未来。天才的一个关键特征，是对其未来愿景的信念。正如华特·迪士尼所言："只要你能梦想，你就能实现。"天才也不断在其创造性工作中搜寻新的机会与可能性，无论其领域是艺术、科学、音乐、娱乐、技术，等等。

Supporting the balance between ego and soul is another crucial principle for effective and ecological creativity and innovation. Some attempted solutions can emphasize ego at the sacrifice of the soul, creating isolation and narcissism. Others demean or even vilify the ego, leading to lack of self-care and burnout. It is important to realize that neither ego inflation nor deflation is sustainable or productive. Maintaining a healthy and dynamic balance between ego and soul is an implicit goal of creative geniuses.

有效创造力与创新的一项基本原则是："能量流向注意力所在之处。"亦即，能量跟随注意力。如果我将注意力放在写下（或阅读）这些文字上，那主要就是我能量所 devoted 之处。如果我将注意力转向下一章想写什么，我的能量就会跟随那个焦点。如果我将注意力带到检视已写内容中的拼写或语法错误上，那便会成为我能量的焦点。

〔图 p066〕

We are part of progressively larger wholes: a family, a professional community, the whole system of living creatures on this planet and ultimately the whole universe.

我们通过所谓的结果框架（outcome frame），将注意力导向积极的未来。结果框架的基本强调，在于建立并维持对某个目标或所欲状态的焦点与注意。

  1. Maintaining a strong orientation toward the future and a belief in what is possible.

确立结果框架，意味着即便在处理障碍与挑战时，也将某个目标或所欲状态保持在前景。

Genius and generative change are always oriented toward the future. And a key characteristic of geniuses is the belief in their visions of the future. As Walt Disney maintained, "If you can dream it, you can do it." Geniuses are also constantly searching for new opportunities and possibilities in their creative work, whether their field is art, science, music, entertainment, technology, etc.

A fundamental principle of effective creativity and innovation is that "energy flows where attention goes." That is, energy follows attention. If I put my attention on writing (or reading) these words, that is primarily what my energy will be devoted to. If I shift my attention to what I want to write in the next chapter, my energy will follow that focus. If I bring my attention to looking for spelling or grammar mistakes in what I have already written, that will be the focus of my energy.

Attention can be very tightly focused or loosely focused. I can put my attention on the details of specific words or on big picture dreams and ideas. I can direct my attention to the past or the future, to myself or to others, to concrete objects or to open space. These will all influence the quality and level of energy that I engage.

We direct our attention toward a positive future through what is known as the outcome frame. The basic emphasis of the outcome frame is on establishing and maintaining focus and attention on a goal or desired state.

Establishing an outcome frame involves keeping a particular goal or desired state in the foreground, even when addressing obstacles and challenges.

问题框架（Problem Frame）Problem Frame

〔图 p065〕

  • What is to be avoided?
  • Why is it a problem?
  • What caused it?
  • Whose fault is it?

结果框架（Outcome Frame）Outcome Frame

  • What do you want?
  • How can you get it?
  • What resources are available?
  • 你想要什么？
  • 你如何得到它？
  • 有哪些资源可用？

Innovation and creative problem solving clearly require a strong focus on an outcome frame.

创新与创造性问题解决，显然需要一个对结果框架的强烈聚焦。

An outcome frame stands in contrast with a problem frame. A problem frame places the emphasis on "what is wrong" or what is "not wanted," as opposed to what is desired or "wanted." A problem frame thus directs attention and energy to what is wrong or not working. Alternatively, an outcome frame leads to a focus on desired outcomes and effects, and the resources required to attain them. Thus, an Outcome Frame involves staying solution focused and oriented toward positive possibilities in the future. A problem frame fixates on what is not working, why it won't work and whose fault it is.

结果框架，与问题框架形成对照。问题框架将强调放在"什么出了错"或"不想要的"，而非所欲或"想要的"。因此，问题框架将注意力与能量导向出错或失灵之处。相反，结果框架导向对所欲结果及效果、以及达成它们所需资源的聚焦。因此，结果框架涉及保持解决导向，并取向于未来的积极可能性。问题框架则固着于什么不管用、为何行不通、以及这是谁的错。

  1. Staying determined and operating in a "feedback" frame as opposed to "failure" frame.
  1. 保持坚定，以"反馈"框架而非"失败"框架运作。

Geniuses frequently have ideas that challenge and transform old ways of thinking. According to the philosopher Arthur Schopenhauer, all great new ideas go through three stages. The first is ridicule; the second is violent opposition; then, finally, they are accepted as having always been "self-evident." Geniuses have the ability to stick with what they believe, even when they are getting no external acknowledgment or support. They also have a unique ability to perceive lack of success not as failure but as feedback for where to look next. As one creative inventor put it, "I think of a failure as just a solution to a problem other than the one I am working on at the moment."

天才常有挑战并转化旧有思维方式的观念。据哲学家亚瑟·叔本华所言，所有伟大的新观念都要经历三个阶段。第一是嘲笑；第二是激烈反对；最终，它们被接受为"不证自明"。天才拥有坚持其所信的能力，即便得不到任何外在认可或支持。他们也独有这样一种能力：不将成功之缺位视为失败，而是视为"下一步该往哪里看"的反馈。正如一位创造性发明家所言："我把一次失败，仅仅看作对我当下所钻研问题之外的另一个问题的解方。"

Staying determined and effectively dealing with failure involves the development of a certain amount of emotional intelligence. Emotional intelligence involves the ability to interact successfully with emotions by recognizing them, understanding their function and responding resourcefully to them. Being able to intelligently respond to the difficult feelings brought up by failure or the potential of failure is an important skill shared by creative geniuses.

保持坚定并有效应对失败，涉及发展出一定程度的情绪智能（emotional intelligence）。情绪智能涉及通过辨认情绪、理解其功能并具资源地回应之，来与情绪成功互动的能力。能够明智地回应由失败或失败的可能性所引发的困难感受，是创造性天才共享的一项重要技能。

The primary areas involved in applying emotional intelligence are: (1) dealing with uncomfortable emotional states and (2) stimulating or eliciting resourceful emotional states. Emotional reactions are typically considered to be the "juice" which brings energy into a particular situation or interaction. Emotional responses themselves, however, can be perceived as either "positive" or "negative." Positive emotions are associated with motivation and enthusiasm. Negative emotions are associated with problems and limitations.

应用情绪智能的主要领域是：（1）处理不适的情绪状态；（2）刺激或引出有资源的情绪状态。情绪反应通常被视为为特定情境或互动注入能量的"汁液"。然而，情绪反应本身，可被视为"正向"或"负向"。正向情绪与动机和热情相关；负向情绪与问题和局限相关。

Usually, though, emotions themselves are not the problem. It is the behavior produced by the emotion, and the effects of that behavior on others, which determines whether a particular emotion is a problem or a resource. Emotions are a type of energy whose purpose to stimulate us into action in some way. Any emotion held in a CRASH state is probably going to produce problematic reactions. The same emotion held in a COACH state will fulfill its function in a more appropriate way.

不过，通常情绪本身并不是问题。是情绪所产生的行为，以及该行为对他人的影响，决定了一个特定情绪究竟是问题还是资源。情绪是一种能量，其目的在于以某种方式刺激我们行动。任何被置于 CRASH 状态中的情绪，都极可能产生成问题的反应。同样的情绪若处于 COACH 状态，便会以更恰当的方式履行其功能。

Dealing with "negative" emotions in an emotionally intelligent way involves (a) staying in a COACH state while experiencing the emotion, (b) seeking and understanding the function that the emotion is intended to serve, and (c) bringing inner resources that help to add more behavioral alternatives, given the function of the emotion and the specific context related to the emotional response.

以情绪智能的方式处理"负向"情绪，涉及（a）在体验该情绪时保持在 COACH 状态；（b）探寻并理解该情绪所欲服务的功能；以及（c）鉴于该情绪的功能及与情绪反应相关的具体情境，调动能增添更多行为替代选项的内在资源。

The function of "fear," for instance, is usually "protection." The function of "anger" may be motivation to "act," or "set boundaries." Once the function has been understood, appropriate choices of behavior can be explored and connected with the function of the emotion. Rather than behaviorally responding with violence when angry, for example, a person can develop other choices which satisfy the intention of the anger: i.e., talking about his or her feelings, taking a walk, focusing on a project, etc.

以"恐惧"为例，其功能通常是"保护"。 "愤怒"的功能，可能是"行动"或"设立边界"的动机。一旦理解其功能，便可探索并将适当的行为选择，与情绪的功能相连。例如，与其在愤怒时以暴力行为回应，一个人可以发展出其他满足愤怒意图的选择：谈论他或她的感受、散个步、聚焦某个项目，等等。

The basic competences needed for emotional intelligence are:

情绪智能所需的基本能力为：

  1. Recognizing the presence of a particular emotional state.
  1. 辨认特定情绪状态的存在。
  2. 认可（Acknowledging） 该状态的存在，不加评判。
  3. 涵容（Holding） 该情绪状态于"平等心"（equanimity）与好奇（COACH 状态）的环境之中。
  4. 理解 该情绪状态及其功能。
  5. 为 该情绪状态注入资源（Resourcing），将其与其他互补的情绪与状态相连。
  6. 转化（Transforming） 或精炼该情绪状态的表达，使其更契合其功能。
  7. 整合（Integrating） 该情绪状态，作为更大系统的一个贡献性部分。
  1. Acknowledging the presence of that state without judgment.

这些情绪智能关键能力的精髓，由正念教师塔拉·布拉赫（Tara Brach）的 RAIN 模型所概括。

  1. Holding the emotional state in an environment of "equanimity" and curiosity (COACH state).

辨认（Recognize） - 将觉察带向情绪状态

  1. Understanding the emotional state and its function.

允许（Allow） - 认可该状态的存在，不加评判

  1. Resourcing the emotional state by connecting it to other complementary emotions and states.

探究（Investigate） - 理解该情绪状态的功能（正向意图）

  1. Transforming or refining the expression of the emotional state to be more harmonious with respect to its function.

滋养（Nurture） - 连接到转化该状态表达的内在资源

  1. Integrating the emotional state as a contributing part of a larger system.

懂得如何处理"负向情绪"，是运作于"反馈框架"中不可或缺的一项技能。

The essence of these key competences of emotional intelligence are summarized by mindfulness teacher Tara Brach's RAIN Model.

〔图 p063〕居中女性剪影，4 张不同表情的小头像围绕（左上 1、右上 2、左下 3、右下 4）

Recognize - bringing awareness to the emotional state

Allow - acknowledging the presence of that state without judgment

Investigate - understanding the function (positive intention) of the emotional state

Nurture - connecting to inner resources that transform the expression of the state

Knowing how to deal with "negative emotions" is an indispensable skill for operating in a "feedback frame".

9. 运用隐喻与类比。9. Using metaphors and analogies.

Geniuses are constantly using metaphors and lateral or non-linear thinking strategies. Metaphor or analogy seems to be at the core of many acts of genius. Mozart used the analogy of putting together morsels to create a meal to compose his music. Einstein used metaphorical constructs and "thought experiments," such as a blind beetle walking on a beach ball, three dimensional shadows or riding on a light beam, to formulate and explain his

天才持续运用隐喻，以及横向或非线性的思维策略。隐喻或类比，似乎处于诸多天才之举的核心。莫扎特用"将小块拼凑成一餐"的类比来作曲。爱因斯坦运用隐喻性构造与"思想实验"——如一只盲甲虫在皮球上行走、三维的阴影、或骑乘在一束光上——来表述并解释他的

theories. It would seem that the use of metaphor allows the genius to focus on the deeper principles and not get caught up in content or the constraints of reality.

理论。隐喻似乎让天才得以聚焦于更深的原则，而不被内容或现实的约束所缠住。

The term "metaphor" comes from the Greek word metapherein, meaning "to carry over, or transfer" (meta = "beyond, between, over" + pherein = "to bring, or to bear"). In Greek, a "metaphor" is something that moves other things between places. A moving van or baggage cart, for example, would literally be a "metaphor" in Greece. When applied to deeper levels of experience, what becomes "transferred" or "carried over" by a "metaphor" are relationships, placement of attention, values, beliefs, presuppositions, etc. As author Julian Jaynes pointed out:

"隐喻"（metaphor）一词源自希腊语 metapherein，意为"搬运，或转移"（meta = "超越、之间、之上" + pherein = "带来，或承载"）。在希腊语中，"metaphor"是使事物在地点之间移动的东西。例如，搬家货车或行李车，在希腊便字面上就是一种"metaphor"。当应用于更深层的经验时，被隐喻"转移"或"搬运"的，是关系、注意力的放置、价值、信念、预设，等等。正如作家朱利安·杰恩斯（Julian Jaynes）所指出的：

It is by metaphor that language grows. The lexicon of language... is a finite set of terms that by metaphor is able to stretch out over an infinite set of circumstances, even to creating new circumstances thereby. A theory is... a metaphor between a model and data. And understanding in science is the feeling of similarity between complicated data and a familiar model... understanding a thing is arriving at a familiarizing metaphor for it... (The Origin of Consciousness in the Breakdown of the Bicameral Mind, 1975)
语言正是借由隐喻而生长。语言的词汇……是一组有限的术语，借由隐喻得以伸展至无限多样的情境，甚至从而创造新的情境。理论……是模型与数据之间的一个隐喻。而科学中的理解，是在复杂数据与熟悉模型之间的相似之感……理解一件事，就是为它抵达一个令人熟悉化的隐喻……（《双脑合一意识的崩解：意识的起源》，1975）

Jaynes' claim that "understanding" comes from the connection between new data and a familiar model through the process of metaphor illustrates the importance metaphors play in building our models of the world. According to linguist George Lakoff (Metaphors We Live By, 1980), we often think of metaphors as being simply "illustrations" of reality, but in many ways our perceptions of reality are influenced by the deeper metaphors we "live by." That is, we organize our perception of reality according to metaphors rather than the other way around. Metaphors offer simple but very highly encoded representations of fundamental relationships. They are often the most effective way of representing deeper level issues relating to values and identity. For example, thinking of an organization as a "machine," a "bee-hive" or a "football team" can dramatically change the perception of the organization.

杰恩斯声称"理解"来自新数据与熟悉模型经由隐喻过程所发生的连接，这说明了隐喻在构建我们世界观（models of the world）中所扮演的重要角色。据语言学家乔治·莱考夫（George Lakoff，《我们赖以生存的隐喻》，1980），我们常将隐喻仅仅视为现实的"图解"，但在许多方面，我们对现实的感知，是受我们所"赖以生存"的更深隐喻所影响的。亦即，我们是依照隐喻来组织我们对现实的感知，而非相反。隐喻提供简单却高度编码的基本关系表征。它们常常是表征关乎价值与身份的、更深层议题的最有效方式。例如，将一个组织想成一台"机器"、一个"蜂巢"或一支"橄榄球队"，会戏剧性地改变对该组织的感知。

Shifting the metaphors we use to understand a situation or phenomenon can often triggers creativity and leads to new perspectives. Metaphorical representation is a very common and powerful way of engaging new associations with respect to the understanding of an idea, situation or concept. It is also a useful tool to transfer learning between different contexts. It stimulates a type of thinking that leads to the level of abstraction necessary to transfer or apply particular learnings between contexts.

转换我们用以理解某一情境或现象的隐喻，常能触发创造力并带来新视角。隐喻性表征，是一种非常常见且有力的方式，用以就某一观念、情境或概念的理解，调动新的联结。它也是一种将学习在不同语境间转移的有用工具。它刺激一种思维，导向在语境间转移或应用特定学习所需的抽象层次。

"Something tells me that we don't see our organization in the same way..."
"有某种东西告诉我，我们看待自己组织的方式并不相同……"

world. Nikola Tesla, too, emphasized the importance of maintaining focus on the "great underlying principles." Einstein's quest for the unified field theory represented a search for the deepest structures in the universe. Freud continually sought multiple levels of successively deeper structures behind the surface symptoms and behaviors of his analytical subjects.

……的世界。尼古拉·特斯拉也强调，保持对"伟大底层原则"的聚焦十分重要。爱因斯坦对统一场论的追寻，代表着对宇宙中最深层结构的探索。弗洛伊德则不断在其分析对象表层症状与行为之下，追索层层递进的更深结构。

As an illustration of the relationship between "deep structure" and "surface structures," most of us learned to write using our right or left hand. Yet, once our hand has learned this skill, it can be immediately transferred to other parts of the body. For instance, we can fairly easily write our name in sand with our left big toe or make letters with our elbow or by holding a pencil in our mouth, even though the physical structure of these parts of our bodies is completely different from our hand. The deep structure related to the form of the letters is not tied to any particular part of the body. It can be generalized to many surface structures.

作为"深层结构"与"表层结构"之间关系的一个例证：我们大多数人都是用右手或左手学会书写的。然而，一旦我们的手学会了这项技能，它便可以立即转移到身体的其他部位。举例而言，我们相当容易用左脚大脚趾在沙地上写出自己的名字，或用肘部、或用嘴含笔写出字母，尽管这些身体部位的物理结构与手完全不同。与字母形状相关的深层结构，并不绑定于身体的任何特定部位。它可以被一般化到许多表层结构之上。

These types of phenomena are what anthropologist and systems theorist Gregory Bateson (who I will be covering in depth later in this book) referred to as "the patterns which connect." As he described it, "the pattern which connects is a metapattern. It is a pattern of patterns..." He maintained that the "way to begin to think about the pattern which connects is to think of it as primarily a dance of interacting parts and only secondarily pegged down by various sorts of physical limits." This requires a unique type of thinking process.

这类现象，正是人类学家与系统理论家格雷戈里·贝特森（本书稍后我会深入介绍他）所称的"联结的模式"（the patterns which connect）。正如他所描述的："联结的模式是一种元模式（metapattern）。它是模式的模式……"他主张："开始思考联结之模式的方法，是将其想成首要是一支互动部分的舞蹈，其次才被各类物理限制所钉住。"这要求一种独特的思维过程。

Aristotle asserted that "it will be intuition that apprehends the primary premises" - meaning that deep structures cannot be determined through conscious cognitive analysis alone. He claimed that deep structure patterns

亚里士多德断言："将把握首要前提的，必是直觉"——意即深层结构无法单靠有意识的认知分析来确定。他声称，深层结构的模式

〔图 p061〕

Metaphors are the most effective way of representing deeper level issues relating to values and identity.

10. 以系统性思维运作，并聚焦于"表层结构"之下的"深层结构"。10. Thinking systemically and focusing on the "deep structure" beyond the "surface structure."

One of the most essential patterns of genius is the ability to think "systemically" rather than "mechanically." The inner strategies of geniuses typically allow them to track whole systems of interacting elements. Perhaps the most definitive characteristic of genius is the commitment to get to the "deep structure" beyond the "surface structure." Aristotle and Leonardo both claimed that they were out to find the "first principles" of the natural

天才最本质的模式之一，是"系统性"而非"机械性"思考的能力。天才的内在策略，通常使他们能够追踪互动要素构成的整个系统。或许天才最具决定性的特征，是致力于抵达"表层结构"之下的"深层结构"。亚里士多德与列奥纳多都宣称，他们意在为自然

or "universals" were perceived in terms of a set of content-free qualities that he called "common sensibles," which were the discriminations that were shared by all the senses.

或"共相"（universals）寻找一组无内容（content-free）的特质，他称之为"共同可感物"（common sensibles），即所有感官所共享的判别。

Common sensibles are [patterns like] movement, rest, number, figure, magnitude, unity; these are not peculiar to any one sense but are common to all.
共同可感物是[诸如]运动、静止、数、形、量、统一体这类模式；它们不专属于任何单一感官，而是为所有感官所共有。

It is significant that Aristotle's "common sensibles" are not a function of any particular sensory modality. They are what Gregory Bateson called a "meta-pattern." For example, "intensity" is something you can register in any sense. You can have intensity of color, sound, taste, smell or touch. It is the same with number"; you can see three things, hear three things, feel three things, etc. Location and movement are also perceptible via all the senses. You can see, hear, feel or smell that something coming from a particular location or moving in a particular direction. These qualities are not a function of only one sense. They are something that can be shared by all the senses and facilitate the transfer of information between the senses. According to Aristotle, common sensibles allowed us to do our higher-level mental processing.

值得注意的是，亚里士多德的"共同可感物"并非任何特定感官模态的功能。它们是格雷戈里·贝特森所称的"元模式"。例如，"强度"是你能以任何感官登记之物。你可以拥有颜色的强度、声音的强度、味道的强度、气味的强度或触觉的强度。数与"强度"同理：你能看见三个东西、听见三个东西、感觉三个东西，等等。位置与运动也能经由所有感官被感知。你能看见、听见、感觉到或闻到某物来自特定位置或朝特定方向移动。这些特质并非仅属于一种感官的功能。它们是所有感官所能共享、并促进感官间信息传递之物。据亚里士多德，共同可感物使我们得以进行更高层次的心理加工。

Intuition is a primarily unconscious process that functions through a type of "resonance." I have a video that I like to show about resonance in which different types of horns are blown near an open piano, such that you can see and hear the piano strings. Blowing a horn produces a sound that causes different piano strings to vibrate. Even after the sound created by the horn stops, you can hear the resonance in the piano strings. There is no direct physical contact between the horn and the piano, other than the fundamental vibrations carried in the air. This is good analogy to what I mean by "resonance" with respect to identifying first principles and deep structures.

直觉（Intuition）主要是一种潜意识过程，经由一种"共鸣"（resonance）运作。我有一段很喜欢展示的、关于共鸣的视频：不同的号角在近旁一架打开的钢琴旁吹响，使你能看见并听见钢琴的琴弦。吹响号角产生的声音，使不同的钢琴琴弦振动起来。即便号角产生的声音停止后，你仍能听见钢琴琴弦中的共鸣。号角与钢琴之间，除了空气中传递的基本振动之外，并无直接的物理接触。这很好地类比了我所谓"就首要原则与深层结构进行辨识"的"共鸣"。

Neuroscientists have observed the same type of phenomena when studying the nervous system (Resonance Behaviors and Mirror Neurons, Rizzolatti et al, 1999). The same "mirror neurons" in the brain of a monkey will "fire" (become activated) when the monkey watches another monkey (or even a human) "grasp" something - regardless of the type of object or whether it is with left or right hand, mouth, feet, etc. The same neurons will also fire when the monkey sees a "graspable" object. Grasping then is a behavioral deeper structure, which unites a particular class of behaviors as well as a particular class of objects, that is produced by a type of resonance in the nervous system

神经科学家在研究神经系统时（Resonance Behaviors and Mirror Neurons，Rizzolatti 等，1999），观察到了同类现象。当一只猴子观看另一只猴子（甚至一个人）"抓握"某物时——无论对象类型为何、用左手还是右手、嘴、脚等等——猴子脑中相同的"镜像神经元"会"放电"（被激活）。当猴子看见一个"可抓握"的物体时，相同的神经元也会放电。因此，抓握是一种行为上的深层结构，它统合了某类行为以及某类物体，由神经系统中的一种共鸣所产生。

While geniuses like Aristotle, Leonardo, Einstein and Tesla sought to find those deep structures and first principles governing the natural world, the geniuses who I will profile in this book extended that search into the internal universe of subjective experience and beyond. For instance, Gregory Bateson asked:

尽管像亚里士多德、列奥纳多、爱因斯坦与特斯拉这样的天才，寻求的是支配自然世界的那些深层结构与首要原则，但本书所刻画的天才们，将这一探索延伸到了主观经验的内部宇宙及更远之处。例如，格雷戈里·贝特森问道：

What pattern connects the crab to the lobster and the orchid to the primrose and all the four of them to me? And me to you? And all the six of us to the amoeba in one direction and to the back-ward schizophrenic in another? What is the pattern which connects all the living creatures?
是什么模式将螃蟹联结到龙虾，将兰花联结到报春花，又将这四种全都与我相联？并将我与你自己相联？又将所有我们六者联向一个方向的变形虫，以及另一个方向的倒退型精神分裂者？联结所有生物的模式是什么？

As we shall see - like Bateson - Milton Erickson, Moshe Feldenkrais and Gabrielle Roth all sought the deeper structures behind human behavior and transformation.

正如我们将看到的——与贝特森一样——米尔顿·艾瑞克森、摩谢·费登奎斯与加布里埃尔·罗斯，全都追寻着人类行为与转化背后的深层结构。

In fact, in the coming chapters we will see how all of the macro-strategies and patterns of genius are employed by these individuals to creatively address a variety of individual and social issues.

事实上，在接下来的章节中，我们将看到这些个人如何运用所有宏观策略与天才模式，去创造性地应对各类个人与社会议题。

When combined together these patterns of genius form the basis of what I call "generative intelligence."

当这些天才模式结合在一起时，便构成了我所谓"生生不息的智慧"（generative intelligence）的基础。

生生不息的智慧：创造的能力Generative Intelligence: The Capacity to Create

Generative intelligence (or GQ) is the capacity to produce new patterns, new meanings, and new possibilities in response to changing contexts and emerging realities. It is not merely intelligence in the sense of analysis, memory, or problem-solving. It is the ability to participate creatively and consciously in the ongoing evolution of ourselves, our relationships, and the systems of which we are a part.

生生不息的智慧（Generative Intelligence，或 GQ）是一种在回应变化的情境与浮现的现实时，产生新模式、新意义与新可能性的能力。它不只是分析、记忆或解题意义上的智能。它是以一种创造性且有意识的方式，参与我们自身、我们的关系，以及我们所从属之系统的持续演化的能力。

From the perspective of my Strategies of Genius work, Generative Intelligence emerges from the interaction of these ten fundamental "patterns of genius." Each pattern contributes a different dimension of the generative process, and together they form an ecology of creativity, innovation, transformation, and human development.

从我"天才的策略"研究的角度看，生生不息的智慧源自这十项基本"天才模式"的互动。每一模式贡献了生成过程的一个不同维度，它们共同构成了一个支持转化、适应与涌现的创造力、创新与人类发展的生态学。

Individually, each of these patterns contributes to creativity and innovation. Together, they create a synergistic ecology that supports transformation, adaptation, and emergence.

个别而言，这些模式中的每一个都贡献于创造力与创新。合在一起，它们创造了一种协同的生态，支持转化、适应与涌现。

When integrated, the 10 Patterns of Genius enable people to:

当被整合时，这 10 项天才模式使人们得以：

  • imagine new futures,
  • embody multiple intelligences,
  • access creative states,
  • hold diverse perspectives,
  • think systemically,
  • remain connected to purpose,
  • learn through feedback,
  • and generate new meaning in complex environments.
  • 想象新的未来，
  • 体现代多元智能，
  • 接通创造性状态，
  • 涵容多元视角，
  • 以系统性思维运作，
  • 与目的保持连接，
  • 透过反馈学习，
  • 并在复杂环境中生成新的意义。

In this sense, Generative Intelligence is not simply a cognitive skill. It is an integrated way of being—one that unites imagination, embodiment, relationship, systems awareness, and purpose into a living process of creation and evolution.

在此意义上，生生不息的智慧不只是一种认知技能。它是一种整合的存在方式——将想象力、具身、关系、系统觉察与目的，统一为创造与演化的一个活的过程。

The geniuses you will be reading about in this book provide many examples of how and why this form of intelligence is so important and impactful.

你将在本书中读到的天才们，提供了许多关于这种智能为何如此重要且影响深远的例证。

天才的模式与生生不息的智慧Patterns of Genius and Generative Intelligence

第一章：米尔顿·H·艾瑞克森 医学博士——行动中的天才模式Chapter 1: Milton H. Erickson, M.D. — Patterns of Genius in Action

〔图 p056〕Milton H. Erickson 肖像插图

章节概览Chapter Overview

In this chapter, I invite you into the magic of Milton H. Erickson's work—not simply as a collection of stories and techniques, but as an expression of a profoundly generative way of perceiving and engaging human experience. Erickson's genius did not come from rigid formulas. It emerged from his ability to meet each person as unique, engage unconscious processes, and transform even severe symptoms into pathways for growth and change.

在本章中，我邀你进入米尔顿·H·艾瑞克森工作的魔力——不只是作为故事与技巧的合集，而是作为一种深刻生成性的、感知并投入人类经验的方式的表达。艾瑞克森的天才，并非来自僵化的公式。它源自他那种能力：将每个人视为独特，调动潜意识过程，并将即便严重的症状也转化为成长与改变的路径。

What follows is both a map and an invitation: a map of key patterns modeled from Erickson's work and an invitation to begin developing these patterns within yourself.

接下来的内容既是一幅地图，也是一份邀请：一幅从艾瑞克森工作中建模出的关键模式之地图，以及一份在你自身之内开始发展这些模式的邀请。

先跟后带：影响的基础Pacing and Leading: The Foundation of Influence

At the core of Erickson's approach is pacing and leading. He first entered another person's model of the world—matching language, physiology and experience—before creatively leading them toward new possibilities. This was not manipulation, but deep respect. When people feel truly met, they naturally become more available for change.

艾瑞克森方法的核心，是先跟后带（pacing and leading）。他先进入另一个人的世界观——匹配其语言、生理与经验——然后创造性地带领对方走向新的可能性。这不是操控，而是深深的尊重。当人们感到真正被迎见，他们自然变得更愿意改变。

利用原则：一切皆可成为资源Utilization: Everything Can Become a Resource

One of Erickson's most important principles was utilization: whatever a person brought—their beliefs, resistance, behaviors or symptoms—could become part of the solution. Rather than fighting symptoms, Erickson asked: How can this become a resource? This reflects a deeply generative orientation: every behavior serves some positive purpose at some level.

艾瑞克森最重要的原则之一，是利用（utilization）：一个人所带来的任何东西——其信念、阻抗、行为或症状——都能成为解方的一部分。艾瑞克森不问与症状对抗，而是问：这如何能成为一种资源？ 这反映出一种深刻的生成性取向：每一种行为在某一层面上都为某种正向目的服务。

米尔顿模型：从精确走向可能The Milton Model: From Precision to Possibility

Where the Meta Model seeks specificity, Erickson often used ambiguity, metaphor and presupposition to engage the unconscious mind. This became known as the Milton Model. Rather than engaging conscious analysis, Erickson created experiences that allowed change to emerge organically.

当元模型（Meta Model）寻求 specificity（特定性）时，艾瑞克森常运用模糊（ambiguity）、隐喻与预设（presupposition）&#x6765;调动潜意识心智。这被称为米尔顿模型（Milton Model）。艾瑞克森不诉诸有意识的解析，而是创造让改变有机涌现的体验。

正向意图与换框的魔力Positive Intention and the Magic of Reframing

A key pattern throughout this chapter is Erickson's focus on the positive intention beneath problematic behavior. By respecting the deeper purpose of a symptom rather than judging it, he was able to preserve identity, create new behavioral choices and transform symptoms into solutions.

贯穿本章的一个关键模式，是艾瑞克森对问题行为之下正向意图的聚焦。通过尊重症状更深的目的、而非评判它，他得以保存身份、创造新的行为选择，并将症状转化为解方。

将症状转化为解方Transforming Symptoms into Solutions

Time and again, you will notice a recurring strategy - a type of psychological Aikido:

你会一再注意到一个反复出现的策略——一种心理上的合气道（Aikido）：

  • Erickson joins the symptom,
  • reframes or amplifies it,
  • 艾瑞克森加入症状，
  • 对其换框或放大它，
  • 并将其重定向向更有用的表达。
  • and redirects it toward a more useful expression.

如此，他将内在冲突转化为整合。

In doing so, he transforms inner conflict into integration.

初心："不知"的力量Beginner's Mind: The Power of "Not Knowing"

Underlying Erickson's work was a remarkable capacity for curiosity, acceptance and non-reactivity.

艾瑞克森工作的底层，是一种非凡的好奇、接纳与非反应（non-reactivity）的能力。

One of the most important lessons I learned from him personally was his repeated statement, "I don't know" when asked for suggestions about how to treat particular clients. This reflected not ignorance, but deep respect for the uniqueness of each person and moment. Erickson remained open to what was emerging rather than imposing preconceived solutions.

我从他那里亲自学到的最重要教训之一，是当被问及如何治疗特定个案时，他反复说"我不知道"。这反映的不是无知，而是对每个人与每个当下之独特性的深深尊重。艾瑞克森对正在浮现之物保持开放，而非强加预设的解方。

更深的邀请The Deeper Invitation

Erickson taught primarily through experience rather than explanation. His interventions engaged the whole person—mind, body and unconscious process. As you explore these patterns, I encourage you to look beyond specific techniques and begin to sense the deeper processes from which they arise. Erickson's genius was not just in what he did, but in:

艾瑞克森主要通过体验而非解释来教学。他的介入调动了整个人——心智、身体与潜意识过程。当你探索这些模式时，我鼓励你看穿具体技巧，开始去感知它们所由生的更深层过程。艾瑞克森的天才，不只在于他做了什么，更在于：

  • his curiosity,
  • his trust in unconscious processes,
  • his respect for individuality,
  • and his ability to see possibility where others saw pathology.
  • 他的好奇心，
  • 他对潜意识过程的信任，
  • 他对个体性的尊重，
  • 以及他在他人看见病理之处看见可能性的能力。

These qualities form the basis of what I call generative intelligence.

这些品质，构成了我所谓生生不息的智慧的基础。

Ultimately, this chapter is not simply about learning about Erickson's strategies and methods. It is about developing a more flexible, ecological and generative relationship to human experience itself. That shift—from trying to fix problems to engaging what is present in a way that allows something new to emerge—is the essence of Erickson's enduring legacy.

最终，本章不只是在学习艾瑞克森的策略与方法。它关乎发展出一种对人类经验本身更具弹性、更具生态性、更具生成性的关系。这一转化——从试图"修理问题"，转向以允许某种新事物涌现的方式投入当下之所是——正是艾瑞克森持久遗产的精髓。

〔图 p054〕

Milton Erickson's interventions were great examples of "generative intelligence," combining curiosity, trust in the unconscious processes, respect for individuality and his ability to see possibility where others saw pathology.

米尔顿·艾瑞克森那卓越而创新的心理治疗、催眠与沟通策略，堪称传奇。他的作品与治疗创新，数十年来持续是国际大会与会议的议题。在有生之年，艾瑞克森（1901–1980）被公认为世界顶尖的医学催眠从业者。他是美国临床催眠学会（American Society for Clinical Hypnosis）的创会会长，也是该学会专业期刊的创办人与主编。艾瑞克森的临床记录令人惊叹——无论是否使用催眠，他成功治疗过的医学与精神科议题种类之多（如你将在本章若干案例中所见）。艾瑞克森备受赞誉的创造力、观察力与独特方法，构成了一整套治疗与催眠程序风格的基础。事实上，正是艾瑞克森的工作，构成了神经语言编程（NLP）许多原始原则与技巧最底层的基石。

Milton Erickson's brilliant and innovative strategies for psychotherapy, hypnosis and communication are legendary. His work and therapeutic innovations have continued to be the subject of international congresses and conferences for several decades. During his lifetime, Erickson (1901-1980) was acknowledged to be the world's leading practitioner of medical hypnosis. He was the founding president of the American Society for Clinical Hypnosis as well as the founder and editor of that society's professional journal. Erickson's clinical record is astounding in the number of different kinds of medical and psychiatric issues he was able to treat successfully (as you will see by some of the examples presented in this chapter) – both with and without the use of hypnosis. Erickson's highly acclaimed creativity, powers of observation and unique approach form the foundation of a whole style of therapeutic and hypnotic procedures. It is Erickson's work, in fact, that is most at the foundation of many of the original principles and techniques of Neuro-Linguistic Programming.

艾瑞克森与 NLP 的联结The Connection of Milton Erickson to NLP

In the Fall of 1975, NLP co-founders Richard Bandler and John Grinder had just finished the manuscript of their first book The Structure of Magic. Grinder, who was a professor of linguistics at the University of California at Santa Cruz, showed the manuscript to anthropologist and systems theory pioneer Gregory Bateson, who was also teaching at UCSC at the time. The manuscript outlined a dozen key language patterns called the Meta Model that Grinder and Bandler had modeled from highly successful psychotherapists, such as Fritz Perls (Gestalt therapy) and Virginia Satir (Family therapy). The idea of the book, and thus its name, was that what seemed to be "magical" results achieved by these experts could be described and transferred to others through the process of identifying their deeper structure. The fundamental premise was that the power of language came more from its form than its contents.

1975 年秋，NLP 共同创始人理查德·班德勒（Richard Bandler）与约翰·格林德（John Grinder）刚刚完成他们第一本书《魔法的结构》（The Structure of Magic）的手稿。格林德当时是加州大学圣克鲁兹分校的语言学教授，他将手稿拿给同在 UCSC 任教的人类学家与系统理论先驱格雷戈里·贝特森看。手稿勾勒出十几个被称为元模型的关键语言模式，那是格林德与班德勒从极为成功的心理治疗师——如弗里茨·皮尔斯（Fritz Perls，完形治疗）与维吉尼亚·萨提尔（Virginia Satir，家庭治疗）——身上建模而来的。这本书的构想，以及其名称的由来，在于：这些专家看似"魔法般"的成果，可以通过辨识其深层结构的过程来被描述并转移给他人。其基本前提是：语言的力量更多来自其形式，而非其内容。

According to Bandler and Grinder, effective therapists listened for certain patterns and categories of language and asked predictable questions rather than getting caught up in the details and drama of the content of their clients' words. These questions helped people to address certain generalizations, deletions and distortions that arose in their verbal descriptions of life events by prompting them to be more precise and specific about what they were saying. This, in turn, supported them to achieve a fuller representation of their experiences, widen their mental model of the world and perceive more choices, especially in situations of pain or distress. Thus, Meta Model patterns and questions were designed to guide people to become more conscious of their use of language and how it impacted their internal experience in either a positive or negative way. As Virginia Satir wrote in her introduction to this seminal work, "What Richard Bandler and John Grinder have done is to watch the process of change over time and to distill from it the patterns of the how process."

据班德勒与格林德所言，有效的治疗师会倾听某些语言模式与类别，并提出可预测的提问，而非陷入案主话语内容的细节与戏剧性之中。这些问题帮助人们处理其生命事件口语描述中出现的某些泛化、删减与扭曲，促使他们对自己所言变得更精确、更具体。这转而支持他们达成对经验的更完整表征、拓宽其关于世界的心智模型，并感知到更多选择——尤其是在痛苦或困扰的情境中。因此，元模型的模式与提问，旨在引导人们更觉察自己语言的使用，及其如何以正面或负面的方式影响内在经验。正如维吉尼亚·萨提尔在这部开创性著作的引言中所写："理查德·班德勒与约翰·格林德所做的，是观察随时间推移的改变过程，并从中提炼出'如何改变'这一过程的模式。"

Bateson was impressed with the work and made arrangements for Bandler and Grinder to meet Milton Erickson, a long-time colleague and friend

贝特森对这项工作印象深刻，便安排班德勒与格林德去见米尔顿·艾瑞克森——他长期以来的一位同事与朋友

of Bateson's, to see if they could create a similar model of the complex communication patterns used by Erickson in his hypnotic and therapeutic work. This led to the development of some of the most important foundations of NLP.

——看看他们能否为艾瑞克森在催眠与治疗工作中所用的复杂沟通模式，建立一个类似的模型。这促成了 NLP 若干最重要基石的发展。

Like Bateson, Bandler and Grinder quickly became captivated by Erickson; struck by his prowess with language and his ability to so profoundly and positively influence people. Their experience with Erickson led them to completely rethink their view on the Meta Model. They realized that, instead of asking questions that made his clients challenge their assumptions by becoming more consciously aware of their use of language and their internal experience, Erickson used the Meta Model patterns in reverse. Rather than being more and more specific, Erickson was more likely to be "artfully vague." He actually purposely used language to create generalizations, deletions and distortions as part of his hypnotic and therapeutic work. The way he did it (as we shall see in this chapter), however, also served to enrich his clients' mental models of the world and help them perceive more possibilities and choices.

与贝特森一样，班德勒与格林德迅速被艾瑞克森迷住；为他驾驭语言的高超本领，以及深刻而正面地影响他人的能力所震撼。他们与艾瑞克森的经验，使他们彻底重新思考对元模型的看法。他们意识到，艾瑞克森不是提出那些让案主透过更有意识地觉察其语言与内在经验、从而挑战自身预设的问题，而是反其道而行之——倒用元模型模式。艾瑞克森不是越来越具体，反而更可能"巧妙地模糊"。他实际上刻意运用语言来制造泛化、删减与扭曲，作为其催眠与治疗工作的一部分。然而，他这样做的方式（如我们将在本章所见），同样丰富了案主关于世界的心智模型，并帮助他们感知到更多可能性与选择。

Bandler and Grinder called this approach, along with Erickson's use of metaphor, presupposition and embedded suggestion, "the Milton Model" and published the results in their next book Patterns of the Hypnotic Techniques of Milton H. Erickson, M.D. (1976). Like Satir and Bateson, Erickson found the work impressive. As he wrote in his preface, "[I]t is a much better explanation of how I work than I, myself, can give."

班德勒与格林德将这一方法，连同艾瑞克森对隐喻、预设与嵌入式暗示（embedded suggestion）的运用，称为"米尔顿模型"，并将结果发表在他们下一本书《米尔顿·H·艾瑞克森医学博士的催眠技巧模式》（Patterns of the Hypnotic Techniques of Milton H. Erickson, M.D.，1976）中。与萨提尔和贝特森一样，艾瑞克森认为这项工作令人印象深刻。正如他在序言中所写："[这]比我所能给出的，更好地解释了我如何工作。"

unless soul clap its hands and sing, and louder sing for every tatter in its mortal dress. — William Butler Yeats
除非灵魂拍手而歌，且为 mortal 衣衫上的每一处褴褛唱得更响。—— 威廉·巴特勒·叶芝（William Butler Yeats）

我与米尔顿·艾瑞克森的个人经历My Personal Experience with Milton Erickson

My connection with Milton Erickson also began in the Fall of 1975. I was in my Junior year at the University of California at Santa Cruz and had recently changed my major from Physics to Politics. As part of my study of politics, I decided that it would be important to know something about language and linguistics. My older brother Michael was a graduate student in Linguistics at Harvard University at the time and also encouraged me to look into that area of study as well.

我与米尔顿·艾瑞克森的联结，也始于 1975 年秋。那时我正读加州大学圣克鲁兹分校大三，刚把专业从物理转到了政治。作为政治学习的一部分，我认定懂一点语言与语言学很重要。我哥哥迈克尔当时是哈佛大学语言学研究生，也鼓励我去探究那个研究领域。

As a result, I walked into a beginning linguistics class with over 200 students taught by a professor named John Grinder. During our first full class period Grinder taught the Meta Model language patterns. It was the first immediately practical thing I had ever learned in school. Even though I had no training as a therapist and had not even taken a class in psychology, I found to my astonishment that I was able to ask questions as if I were an experienced psychotherapist. I discovered that by my asking a few well-chosen questions, family members, classmates, friends and even casual acquaintances experienced valuable insights and breakthroughs. It became clear that recognizing a few fundamental language patterns and knowing the right questions to ask gave one incredible power to stimulate change and growth.

于是，我走进了一门由一位名叫约翰·格林德教授授课、有 200 多名学生的初级语言学课堂。在我们第一个完整的上课时段，格林德讲授了元模型语言模式。那是我在学校里学到的第一件立即可用的东西。尽管我毫无治疗师训练，连心理学课都没上过，我却惊讶地发现，自己竟能像一位经验丰富的心理治疗师那样提问。我发现，透过提出几个精心选择的问题，家人、同学、朋友乃至泛泛之交，都体验到了宝贵的洞见与突破。事情变得很清楚：认出几个基本的语言模式、并知道该问什么正确的问题，便赋予了人刺激改变与成长的惊人力量。

I immediately began to apply the Meta Model language patterns to the Socratic Dialogues that I was studying in a political philosophy class and

我立刻开始将元模型语言模式应用到一门政治哲学课上研读的《苏格拉底对话录》中，

wrote a paper on it (published later in my book Applications of NLP, 1983). I showed how the Socratic method of dialectic systematically challenged assumptions by questioning language patterns known in the Meta Model as modal operators of necessity—words like "should," "must," "need to"—and universal quantifiers—words such as "always," "every time," "never," "only," etc.

并就此写了一篇论文（后来发表于我的《NLP 的应用》[Applications of NLP]，1983）。我展示了苏格拉底的辩证方法，如何系统地透过质疑元模型中称为必要性模态算子（modal operators of necessity）——如"应该""必须""需要"——以及全称量词（universal quantifiers）——如"总是""每次""从不""只有"等——来挑战预设。

At that time, Grinder and Bandler had basically only used the Meta Model for therapeutic purposes, so to apply it to politics was new and innovative. Grinder found this interesting, as he had co-taught some courses on politics and had even written a book on Marxist economics.

那时，格林德与班德勒基本只将元模型用于 therapeutic 目的，因此将其应用于政治是新颖而创新的。格林德觉得这很有趣，因为他曾合开过一些政治课程，甚至写过一本关于马克思主义经济学的书。

By the Winter of 1975, Bandler and Grinder were deeply into their study of Erickson, traveling down to Phoenix, Arizona on a regular basis in order to observe and model Erickson. After one of the first such trips, I remember John Grinder coming into class and excitedly saying, "Everything you know is wrong" (a humorous reference to an album by a popular comedy group of the time called The Firesign Theater). By this, he meant that he and Bandler had realized that what they had been teaching about the Meta Model was only partially true and that Erickson's unusual and innovative use of language took the potential for using language to facilitate change to another level. It was after this that John started sharing stories and examples of the powerful and astonishing things that Milton Erickson had been able to accomplish.

到 1975 年冬，班德勒与格林德已深度投入对艾瑞克森的研究，定期南下亚利桑那州凤凰城，以观察并建模艾瑞克森。在最早几次这样的出行之后，我记得约翰·格林德走进课堂，兴奋地说："你所学的一切都是错的"（幽默地指涉当时一个流行喜剧团体 The Firesign Theater 的专辑）。他的意思是，他与班德勒意识到，他们此前所教的关于元模型的东西只是部分正确，而艾瑞克森那不寻常且创新的语言运用，将运用语言促进改变的潜力提升到了另一层次。此后，约翰开始分享米尔顿·艾瑞克森所能达成的那些强大而惊人的事迹与例子。

元团体（Meta Group）的"空间学院"The Meta Group's "Academy of Space"

It was also about this time that John Grinder started inviting me up to the exploratory "Meta" group meetings on Alba Road in the hills of Ben Lomond, California. As group leaders, Bandler and Grinder perfectly captured the revolutionary spirit of the times. Their method of research was highly "heuristic," meaning to lead with experience. Their philosophy was act first—start with experience—and then "digitalize" (i.e., describe in words what had been learned).

大约也是在这个时候，约翰·格林德开始邀我上加州贝隆蒙（Ben Lomond）山区阿尔巴路（Alba Road）上探索性的"元"（Meta）小组聚会。作为小组带领者，班德勒与格林德完美捕捉了那个时代的革命精神。他们的研究方法高度"启发式"（heuristic），意即以体验为先。他们的哲学是：先行动——从体验开始——然后"数字化"（即，用语言描述所学到的）。

One of their ways of modeling Erickson, for example, was to reproduce with us everything that Erickson had done with his clients. Once they could do it themselves and then teach us to do it, they were certain that they had the pattern explicitly identified. Their mode of teaching was also very immediate and experiential. They would do something, point to one of us, give us a few instructions and we would go and immediately repeat it with someone else. We would then come back and reflect with them on what had happened; what had worked and what hadn't. The next step was to see if we could achieve the same result without needing to induce a trance state. This is how many of the original NLP techniques were developed.

举例而言，他们建模艾瑞克森的一种方式，是在我们身上重现艾瑞克森对其案主所做的一切。一旦他们自己能做到、进而教会我们做到，他们便确信已明确辨识出那个模式。他们的教学方式也极为即时与体验化。他们会做点什么，指向我们中的某个人，给几句指示，我们便立刻去找另一个人重复。然后我们回来，与他们一起反思发生了什么、什么有效、什么无效。下一步，是看我们能否在不诱发 trance（催眠恍惚）状态的情况下，达成同样的结果。许多原始的 NLP 技巧就是这样发展出来的。

We tried out practically every hypnotic phenomenon, including positive and negative hallucinations, catalepsy, deep trance identification, age regression, post-hypnotic suggestions and projection into the future. It was not an uncommon experience, for example, to see somebody fully age regressed to two years old, crawling around and acting like a child as a means to enhance his or her ability to learn a language. We experimented with everything from ESP to creating, and then reversing, phobias. We sometimes humorously referred to it as the "Academy of Space," and we were the "space cadets."

我们几乎试遍了每一种催眠现象，包括正性与负性幻觉、僵直（catalepsy）、深度 trance 认同、年龄回溯、后催眠暗示，以及投射向未来。例如，看见有人完全年龄回溯到两岁、在地上爬并像个孩子般举止，以此增强其学习语言的能力，并非罕见经验。我们从 ESP（超感官知觉）到创造、再逆转恐惧症，一切皆实验。我们有时幽默地称其为"空间学院"（Academy of Space），而我们则是"太空学员"（space cadets）。

拜访艾瑞克森博士Visiting Dr. Erickson

In June of 1976, when I was 21 years old, I made my first trip to Phoenix, Arizona to see Milton Erickson. At that time, Erickson typically only accepted to meet with individuals who had at least a PhD in psychology. Even though I had no degree or professional experience at all, Erickson kindly agreed to let me visit at the request of John Grinder. I had drawn a pencil portrait of Erickson from a photo in one of his books and given it to Grinder as a present. John took it as an "unconscious request" on my part to see Erickson and immediately called and set up an appointment for me to go visit the so-called "wizard of the desert." Grinder sent along the portrait I had made as a gift to Erickson. Grinder and Bandler had also just finished the second volume of Patterns of the Hypnotic Techniques of Milton H. Erickson, M.D. and so Grinder also gave me a copy of the manuscript to bring to Dr. Erickson.

1976 年 6 月，我 21 岁，第一次前往亚利桑那州凤凰城见米尔顿·艾瑞克森。那时，艾瑞克森通常只肯会见至少拥有心理学博士学位的人。尽管我毫无学位或专业经验，艾瑞克森出于约翰·格林德的请求，仍 kindly 同意让我拜访。我曾根据他某本书中的一张照片，为他画了一幅铅笔肖像，作为礼物送给格林德。约翰将其视为我"无意识地想要见艾瑞克森的请求"，立刻打电话为我约了这位所谓"沙漠巫师"的门诊。格林德把我画的肖像作为礼物一并送给艾瑞克森。当时格林德与班德勒也刚完成《米尔顿·H·艾瑞克森医学博士的催眠技巧模式》第二卷，于是格林德又给了我一份手稿副本，让我带给艾瑞克森博士。

Erickson also quickly exhibited his famous sense of humor. When I was first introduced to him, I handed him the portrait that I had drawn of him and brought to him from Grinder. He looked at it, handed it to Mrs. Erickson and said with a twinkle in his eye, "Betty, why don't you file this in the circular filing cabinet" (meaning the garbage can). Then he glanced at me with a sly grin to see how I would respond. I laughed and said it was his and he was free to do whatever he wanted with it. I saw later that it was displayed prominently along with other gifts that Dr. Erickson had received.

艾瑞克森也迅速展现了他著名的幽默感。当我被介绍给他时，我递上那幅为他画的、从格林德处带来给他的肖像。他看了看，递给艾瑞克森太太，眼中闪着光说："贝蒂，为什么不把它归档进圆形文件柜呢"（意指字纸篓）。然后他斜眼狡黠一笑，看我作何反应。我笑了，说那是他的，他尽可以随意处置。后来我看见它与艾瑞克森收到的其他礼物一起，被醒目地展示着。

I was a college student with little money and Dr. and Mrs. Erickson generously offered to let me stay in their guesthouse (which was in the same building on their property as Erickson's office). I roomed there with another young college student—Jeff Zeig, who later created the Erickson Foundation, which has grown to become one of the most globally recognized and influential organizations in the field of psychotherapy. The friendship we began there has continued to this day.

我当时是个没什么钱的大学生，艾瑞克森夫妇慷慨地让我住进他们的客房（与其办公室同在一栋楼里）。我与另一位年轻大学生杰夫·蔡格（Jeff Zeig）同住，他后来创立了艾瑞克森基金会（Erickson Foundation），如今已成为心理治疗领域全球最知名、最具影响力的组织之一。我们在那里结下的友谊延续至今。

When I handed him the manuscript of Bandler and Grinder's new book on his work, I could see how he had to very consciously take it in his hand and place it on his desk, given the effects that his second bout with polio had on the simplest acts of coordination. At the end of that first day, he turned to me with the same wry smile he had shown earlier and said, "And now do you know that I am nothing like what Grinder and Bandler said I was?" I laughed again and said that I had already known that and this was why I had wanted to come down and see what he was really like for myself.

当我递给他班德勒与格林德关于其工作的新书手稿时，我能看见他必须非常有意识地用手接过去、再放到桌上——考虑到小儿麻痹症第二次发作对他最简单协调动作的影响。在第一天结束时，他带着早先那种讽刺的微笑转向我说："那么，你现在知道我根本不像格林德与班德勒所说的那个我了吧？"我又笑了，说我已经知道这一点，而这正是我想亲自下来看看他究竟什么样的原因。

I had, of course, heard all about Erickson's genius from Grinder and Bandler, who confidently claimed that Erickson was "the most evolved communicator on the planet."
