In the NLP world, this is the highest praise that anyone could receive. I had read everything by Erickson and about him that I could get my hands on. Naturally, I was in absolute awe of him. I was immediately struck, however, by the deep humanity, humility and generosity that he and Mrs. Erickson showed to me and the others around them.

当然，我从格林德与班德勒那里听尽了关于艾瑞克森天才的种种，他们自信地宣称艾瑞克森是"这个星球上演化程度最高的沟通者"。在 NLP 世界里，这是任何人所能得到的最高赞誉。我读过手边所有艾瑞克森写的以及写他的东西。自然地，我对他绝对敬畏。然而，我立刻被他与艾瑞克森太太对我及周遭他人所展现的那种深厚的人性、谦逊与慷慨所打动。

初心与"不知"的力量Beginner's Mind and the Power of "Not Knowing"

Naturally, I was full of questions for him. I would ask things along the lines of "If you use this particular approach with a person who has that particular type of issue, will it produce a certain result?" Erickson would invariably reply, "I don't know." I would ask, "Is it better to use this process or that

自然地，我对他满腹疑问。我会问类似这样的事："如果你对某个有特定类型议题的人用这特定方法，它会产生某个结果吗？"艾瑞克森总是回答："我不知道。"我会问："用这个过程好，还是那个

"soul" would come and "clap its hands and sing, and louder sing for every tatter in his mortal dress" and he would come alive, and some type of very real and unexpected magic would become possible. When he got you in his gaze you would feel like you were the only one in the room and the most important person in the world to him.

……好？"他都回答"我不知道。"我渐渐明白，这些回答并非出于无知，而是源于一种 pose（姿态）——一种"不知"的 pose。通过持续地说"我不知道"，艾瑞克森避免了预设、判断或 prematurely（过早地）跳向结论。他 model（建模）了一种"初心"（beginner's mind）——那是一种对正在发生之事保持开放、好奇与接纳的状态。这恰恰是 COACH 状态的一个面向。

As with all great teachers, what I learned from Erickson came mostly from his way of being. The acceptance, affection, generosity and sponsorship Erickson showed me is something that I deeply appreciated and have attempted to share with my own clients and students as a part of the legacy I received from him.

艾瑞克森也常引用叶芝的诗句，并显然 deep（深切地）认同它。对他而言，这首诗捕捉了某种本质：当一个人停止"知道"、停止试图掌控，而只是临在于当下之所是时，那"灵魂"便会前来，"拍手而歌，且为 mortal 衣衫上的每一处褴褛唱得更响"，他便会活过来，某种非常真实而意外的魔法便成为可能。当他的目光锁定你时，你会觉得自己是房间里唯一的人，也是对他而言世界上最重要的人。

与所有伟大的老师一样，我从艾瑞克森身上学到的，主要来自他的存在方式。艾瑞克森展现给我的接纳、慈爱、慷慨与提携（sponsorship），是我深切感激、并试图作为从他那里承接的遗产之的一部分，分享给我自己的案主与学生。

最后的祝福A Final Blessing

The last time I saw Dr. Erickson in person was in 1979, the year before his death. I was engaged to be married to my first wife Anita, and we wanted get Dr. Erickson's "blessing." I wrote him and explained the situation and he graciously allowed us to visit (even though neither of us had a PhD). As was typical of his notoriety at that time, there were a number of other visitors in his office as well, perhaps a half a dozen of us altogether.

我最后一次亲眼见到艾瑞克森博士，是在他去世前一年、1979 年。我当时已订婚，未婚妻是阿妮塔（Anita），我们想要得到艾瑞克森博士的"祝福"。我写信说明了情况，他 graciously 允许我们拜访（即便我们两人都无博士学位）。正如他当时名气所惯有的，他办公室里还有若干其他访客，大概我们总共半打人。

Dr. Erickson did his usual masterful interaction with his audience. He would pass around a particular object, given to him by one of his patients, and comment afterwards on who was right-handed or left-handed (or potentially ambidextrous) by the way they had handled the object. He also passed around his "topsy-turvy" book – a book that had illustrations that depicted a particular scene when looking at them from a normal perspective. But, if you turned the book upside down, they showed a completely different interpretation of events. Again, he would comment about what he observed by the way a person reacted and what they noticed about the images in the book. By doing this, he made sure to have a special connection with each person in the room in some unique way.

艾瑞克森如常地、大师般地与他的听众互动。他会传阅一件由某位病人赠予他的特定物件，事后根据人们处置该物件的方式，评论谁惯用右手或左手（或可能两手通用）。他也传阅他那本"颠三倒四"的书——一本从正常视角看描绘某个场景、但若倒过来则呈现完全另一种事件诠释的图册。同样，他会就一个人反应的方式、以及其从书中图像所注意到的事物加以评论。透过这样做，他确保与房间里的每个人，都以某种独特方式建立了特别联结。

After the group session, Erickson invited me and my fiancée into his house to see his collection of ironwood sculptures amongst other precious items (which included the drawing I had given him years earlier). While we were there, he pointed to a replica of Rodin's "The Kiss" that someone had given them. He commented that it was the "quintessential representation of femininity" and winked at us. We were both very touched and took it as his way of giving us his blessing for our marriage.

小组 session 之后，艾瑞克森邀我与未婚妻进屋，看他与 ironwood（铁木）雕塑等珍贵物品（其中包括我多年前给他的那幅画）的收藏。在那里，他指向有人赠予他们的一尊罗丹《吻》（"The Kiss"）的复制品。他评论说那是"女性特质的经典表征"，并向我们眨了眨眼。我们两人都深受触动，将其视为他对我们婚姻的祝福。

As we were leaving, Anita made one of the most astute observations of Erickson that I have heard. She pointed out that, having had lots of exposure to Erickson's work and the books that had been written about him, Erickson was not particularly subtle or elegant with regard to the language patterns that he used. (Notably, Erickson was tone deaf and so his "embedded commands" were quite obvious for anyone who had any training in that area.) She commented that others, like Richard Bandler and Stephen Gilligan, were much more adept and subtle. She pointed out, however, that in spite of the, to her, "blatant" suggestions, she would not have considered not responding, because the degree of connection and rapport was so strong, she would have not wanted to do anything to jeopardize it. To me, this deep quality of rapport, more than clever language patterns, was at the core of Erickson's genius.

离开时，阿妮塔说出了我所听过对艾瑞克森最敏锐的观察之一。她指出，尽管大量接触过艾瑞克森的工作与写他的书，艾瑞克森本人所用的语言模式其实并不特别微妙或优雅。（值得注意的是，艾瑞克森音盲，因此他的"嵌入式指令"对任何受过该领域训练的人来说都相当明显。）她评论说，其他人如理查德·班德勒与斯蒂芬·吉利根（Stephen Gilligan），要熟练与微妙得多。然而她指出，尽管对她而言那些暗示"露骨"，她却从未考虑过不回应，因为联结与亲和（rapport）的程度如此之强，她根本不想做任何可能危及它的事。对我而言，这种深层的亲和品质，而非巧妙的语言模式，才是艾瑞克森天才的核心。

1980 年 3 月 25 日March 25, 1980

Milton Erickson died on the 25th of March 1980 at the age of 78. I have a very vivid memory of that time period. I had just turned 25 years old a few days earlier and was giving an NLP seminar in Toronto Canada. At 2am in the morning on March 26th I awoke with a start and was sure that someone was trying to open the chained door of my hotel room. I would have sworn that I heard the door hitting against the chain several times. The experience was so vivid that I woke up Anita to check if she had heard the attempt to open the door, which she had not. I even got up and went to the door, opened it and looked out into the silent, empty hallway. No one was there, but the sense was so strong that I noted the time.

米尔顿·艾瑞克森于 1980 年 3 月 25 日去世，享年 78 岁。我对那段时间有非常鲜明的记忆。几天前我刚满 25 岁，正在加拿大多伦多带一个 NLP 研讨班。3 月 26 日凌晨 2 点，我猛然惊醒，确信有人正试图打开我酒店房门上那道链锁。我发誓听见门好几次撞在链子上。体验如此鲜活，以至于我唤醒阿妮塔，问她是否听见了开门的尝试——她没有。我甚至起身走到门边，打开它，望向寂静空荡的走廊。那里空无一人，但那感觉如此强烈，以至于我记下了时间。

The next day I received the news that Dr. Erickson had passed away at exactly that time (11pm in Phoenix Arizona). I have sometimes said jokingly that he was stopping by to say goodbye on his way out. Of course, it is much more likely to have been coincidence, or some type of synchronicity created by the disturbance in a larger collective unconscious that his passing had produced.

第二天，我收到消息：艾瑞克森博士恰好在那个时刻（凤凰城当地时间晚上 11 点）过世。我有时开玩笑说，他是顺路来道别的。当然，这更可能是巧合，或某种由他离世在更大的集体潜意识中所搅动出的 synchronicity（共时性）。

Deep connection and rapport were at the core of Milton Erickson's work... the technique came later.

深厚的联结与亲和，是米尔顿·艾瑞克森工作的核心……技巧是后来才有的。

行动中的艾瑞克森：先跟后带与利用Milton Erickson in Action: Pacing, Leading and Utilization

The most fundamental and important strategy employed by Erickson to weave his magic was the process of pacing and leading. According to NeuroLinguistic Programming, pacing involves observing and feeding back key verbal and non-verbal cues from another person, in order to strengthen resonance and rapport. It involves having the flexibility to pick up and incorporate other people's vocabulary and behavior into one's own vocabulary and actions.

艾瑞克森编织其魔法所运用的最根本、最重要的策略，是先跟后带的过程。根据神经语言编程，跟（pacing）&#x6D89;及观察并反馈另一个人的关键言语与非言语线索，以强化共鸣与亲和。它需要具备灵活性，将别人的词汇与行为拾取并融入自身的词汇与行动之中。

For instance, one way to develop rapport is by listening to the kinds of language patterns a person uses and then doing a type of "active listening" by matching some of their words. So, if somebody says, "I feel that we need to go more deeply into this," you might say, "Yes, I understand that you have a feeling that we need to explore this." In addition to matching key words, you can match voice tone and tempo, and physical posture.

举例而言，发展亲和的一种方式，是倾听一个人所用的语言模式类型，然后通过匹配其部分词语来做一种"主动倾听"。所以，如果某人说"我感觉（feel）我们需要更深入地探究这一点"，你或许会说："是的，我理解你有一个感觉（feeling），我们需要探索这一点。"除了匹配关键词，你还可以匹配声调与节奏，以及身体姿态。

带（Leading）Leading

Leading involves the attempt to get another person to change, add to or enrich his or her thoughts or behaviors. We lead by suggesting other words, ideas or actions. We can also lead by shifting our own actions.

带涉及试图让另一个人改变、增添或丰富他或她的思想或行为。我们通过建议其他词语、观念或行动来带领。我们也可以通过转换自己的行动来带领。

The basic idea of pacing and leading is to incrementally introduce somebody to changes in their behavior or world view by first matching and acknowledging, and then widening their model of the world. For instance, when people are being introduced to something new, it is best to start with something familiar and then move to what is novel.

先跟后带的基本理念，是透过先匹配并认可、再拓宽其世界观，渐进式地将某人引入其行为或世界观的改变。举例而言，当人被引介到某个新事物时，最好从熟悉之物开始，再转向新奇之物。

In many ways, pacing and leading is a type of psychological Aikido. Aikido is a form of martial art developed in Japan. The word literally means the "way of adapting the spirit," from the Japanese ai "together, unify," ki "spirit" or "energy" and do "way." The implication of this name is that Aikido is a process of joining with some form of energy and then redirecting it.

在许多方面，先跟后带是一种心理上的合气道（Aikido）。合气道是日本发展出的一种武术。这个词字面意为"调和精神之道"，源自日语 ai（共同、统一）、ki（精神或能量）与 do（道）。这个名称的意涵是：合气道是一个与某种能量接合、再将其重定向的过程。

Erickson was a master at this type of "Aikido" – meeting his clients at their own model of the world, pacing their way of thinking and then elegantly leading them to a more useful way of organizing their experience. As an example, in his Collected Papers (Vol. VI, p. 124) Erickson describes how he treated a woman with three children who:

艾瑞克森是这类"合气道"的大师——在他的案主自身世界观之处迎见他们，跟其思维方式，再优雅地带他们走向一种更有用的、组织其经验的方式。例如，在他的《论文集》（Collected Papers，卷 VI，p.124）中，艾瑞克森描述了他如何治疗一位有三个孩子的妇女，她：

During the course of listening to a sermon one Sunday... had become horribly distressed to find herself unaccountably impelled to utter a variety of obscenities, particularly vulgarisms concerning body functions and sexual activity, all being ascribed to Jesus. She fought the overpowering vocal impulses desperately along with compelling desires to grimace, to gesticulate, and to posture.

在某个周日的布道过程中……发现自己莫名其妙地被迫说出各种污言秽语，尤其是关于身体功能与性活动的粗话，且全都被归到耶稣头上，因而陷入极度痛苦。她拼命对抗那压倒性的发声冲动，连同那想要扮鬼脸、做手势与摆姿势的强烈欲望。

In the terms of today's psychology, the symptoms described above are put under the label of Tourette's Syndrome – an uncontrollable mental state that seems like another identity altogether "takes over" one's behavior in a way that comes into conflict with one's normal identity and patterns of behavior. This particular woman could do nothing to stop the symptoms. They did not diminish even after she returned home and locked herself in her room, and continued into the next day completely out of control. Finally, Erickson was called in to help by the woman's desperate husband who thought Erickson might be able to take away the symptoms using hypnosis.

用今天心理学的术语，上述症状被归入"妥瑞氏症"（Tourette's Syndrome）——一种失控的心智状态，仿佛另一个全然不同的身份"接管"了一个人的行为，与其正常身份及行为模式相冲突。这位妇女对她自己的症状无能为力。即便回到家把自己锁在房里，症状也未减轻，并持续到第二天完全失控。最后，妇女的绝望丈夫打电话请艾瑞克森帮忙，以为艾瑞克森或许能用催眠把症状去掉。

Erickson's treatment is a classic example of his strategy for pacing and leading his clients into more healthy and resourceful mental state. He reports:

艾瑞克森的治疗，是他那种将案主先跟后带、引向更健康、更有资源之心智状态的策略的经典范例。他报告道：

All appropriate and fitting hypnotic suggestions were offered in the proper sequence and progression that the author's experience had disclosed to be most reasonably effective. However, those suggestions were embellished, interlarded, couched, and elaborated with obscenities, vulgarities, and profanities that far exceeded the worse she had uttered.
所有恰当且合宜的催眠暗示，都以作者经验所揭示最合理有效的顺序与进程给出。然而，那些暗示被修饰、穿插、包装并详述以污言秽语、粗话与亵渎之语，其程度远超她曾说出口最糟的。

She was utterly appalled, horrified, and what was most important, completely silenced with a rigid fixation of her attention upon the author and the hypnotic suggestions being offered to her in such a peculiar emphatic fashion. (p. 125)

她彻底惊愕、恐惧，而最重要的是，因作者以如此奇特而强调的方式给出催眠暗示、将其注意力 rigid（僵硬地）固定于作者身上，而完全沉默了。（p.125）

Erickson's interspersal of vulgarities with standard hypnotic suggestions served to both surprise the woman and at the same time establish rapport with both aspects of her identity via the dual communication of "appropriate" suggestion and vulgarity. In a way, by mirroring and then exaggerating the problem behavior, Erickson effectively assumed the process, so the woman no longer needed to continue it herself.

艾瑞克森将粗话与标准催眠暗示相互穿插，既让那妇女惊讶，同时又透过"恰当"暗示与粗话的双重沟通，与她身份的两个面向都建立了亲和。在某种意义上，通过 mirror（映照）继而夸大问题行为，艾瑞克森有效地"接管"了那个过程，于是那妇女不再需要自己继续它。

Once this state of rapport was established, Erickson proceeded to lead by suggesting to the woman that (1) she was to continue her symptomatic behavior in a "satisfying" but "better" manner, and (2) she was to purposefully practice both her own utterances and some of those that Erickson had voiced. He went on to suggest to her that:

一旦这种亲和状态建立，艾瑞克森便着手带，向那妇女建议：（1）她要以一种"令人满意"却"更好"的方式，继续其症状行为；（2）她要有意练习她自己的发声，以及艾瑞克森曾发出的某些发声。他进而向她建议：

[S]ince her manifestations would occur in either the presence or absence of others, her symptomatology could be entirely adequate if she alone knew of it. Thus her utterances need not be so loud, since she could hear even the softest whispers as well as the loudest shouts. Additionally, the posturing could also be minimal, since she could be aware of it and any associated thoughts, however minimal the postural movements were . . . Also, she was to develop new gesticulations more awkward and less meaningful to the observer than were the "grinds and bumps" which constituted a part of her motor manifestations. Coughing, gasping, choking, yawning, if necessary learning how to belch voluntarily, crossing her legs violently, or whatever else she felt could be bearable were suggested, and she was made to demonstrate the suggested acts . . . [Further] that weekly, biweekly, or even less frequently she would go into the garage, close it, turn on the car radio full volume, and "let loose with everything." (pp. 128-129)
*[由]于她的表现无论在有他人或无人时都会发生，只要她自己知道，她的症状表现便完全足够。因此她的发声无需那么响亮，因为她连最轻的耳语与最响的呼喊都能听见。此外，姿势动作也可减到最少，因为她能觉察它以及任何相关的想法，无论姿势动作多微小……同时，她要发展出新的、比构成其动作表现一部分的"碾磨与碰撞"更笨拙、更无意义的手势。咳嗽、喘息、窒息、打哈欠、**若有必要学习自愿打嗝、剧烈交叉双腿，或任何她觉得可忍受的，都被建议，且她被要求示范所建议的动作……[进一步]每周、每两周、甚至更不频繁地，她要走进车库，关上门，把汽车收音机开到最大音量，然后"彻底释放一切。"（pp. 128-129）*

There are a number of important elements to Erickson's process of leading.

艾瑞克森的带过程，有若干重要要素。

  1. His suggestion to continue her symptoms in a "satisfying" but "better" manner, presupposes that symptoms may serve some positive purpose. The great Swiss psychologist Carl Jung pointed out that "the unconscious is always compensating for the biases of the conscious." Perhaps the woman was very self-critical and repressed natural emotional responses which she judged as "negative" because of her belief system and identity as a mother and "proper" Christian. Such a situation could lead to unnecessary stress and self-hatred that could make life miserable and unbearable. Thus, the outbursts could be these natural emotional responses erupting in a distorted (CRASH) form. As Erickson would say, the symptom is often an attempt at a solution. By encouraging, taking on and even exaggerating elements of the symptoms himself, Erickson "reframed" their meaning and opened up other ways of perceiving them, indicating that their purpose or intention could be met in a better and more satisfying manner. By separating the behavior from the purpose or intention behind it, Erickson opened the possibility that there were other choices of behavior and that, in her basic identity, the woman was not a "bad" person for having
  1. 他建议那妇女以"令人满意"却"更好"的方式继续其症状，预设了症状可能服务于某种正向目的。伟大的瑞士心理学家卡尔·荣格（Carl Jung）指出："潜意识总是在补偿意识之偏。"也许这位妇女极度自我批判，并压抑了那些她基于作为母亲与"正派"基督徒的身份与信念体系而判为"负面"的自然情绪反应。这种情况可能导致不必要的压力与自我憎恨，使生活痛苦而难忍。因此，这些爆发可能是那些自然情绪反应以扭曲（CRASH）形式喷涌而出。正如艾瑞克森所说，症状常常是一种对解方的尝试。通过鼓励、承接、甚至夸大症状的元素，艾瑞克森"换框"了它们的意义，并开启了以其他方式来感知它们的可能，表明其目的或意图能以更好、更令人满意的方式被满足。通过将行为与行为背后的目的或意图分离，艾瑞克森开启了存在其他行为选择的可能，并且在她的基本身份上，这位妇女并非因为有症状就是个"坏"人。如此，她的身份得以确认，而行为则被打开以朝向改变。
  2. 通过建议那妇女实际练习这些冒犯性行为，艾瑞克森在建立一种信念：症状是可以被掌控的，因而可被变化与改变。如果一个人试图停止或对抗这些行为，那人体内的身份裂痕反而可能恶化并更具破坏力。通过将"练习症状"的建议，与"寻找一个同样'令人满意'却'更好'的表达"的建议结合，艾瑞克森建立了一个生态的、正向强化的反馈回路，其中行为的正向意图或次级获益（secondary gains）得以保留，而具体行为表达则被变化为更恰当的。
  3. 艾瑞克森关于练习的建议，包含了三个不同的微观策略，妇女可借此找到更恰当的表达形式：

the symptoms. This way, her identity remains validated, and the behavior is opened up to change.

a. 逐步增强或减弱行为的强度（例如许多人会压低声音咒骂）；

  1. By suggesting that the woman actually practice the offensive behaviors, Erickson is building the belief that it is possible to be in control of the symptoms so that they may be varied and changed. If one tries to stop or fight against the behaviors, the schism within the person's identity can actually grow worse and more disruptive. By combining the suggestion that the woman practice the symptoms with the suggestion to find an equally "satisfying" but "better" expression of them, Erickson is establishing an ecological and positively reinforcing feedback loop in which the positive intentions or secondary gains of the behavior may be preserved while the specific behavioral expression is varied to something more appropriate.

b. 逐步变化行为表达的类型，转向更常见或可接受的；以及

  1. Erickson's suggestions for practice involve three distinct micro-strategies through which the woman might find a more appropriate form of expression:

c. 寻找更恰当的情境（如车库），以充分表达行为。

a. gradually increasing or reducing the intensity of the behaviors (many people swear under their breath for instance);

艾瑞克森报告，那妇女能够充分练习并内化新的行为模式，以至于在大部分时间里，她能度过一天而不出现症状——定期走进车库去"把它从系统里排掉"，先是每周一次，再每月一次，直到最终彻底无症状。七年后，艾瑞克森因另一件事见到这位妇女。她仍无症状，并"带着 amusement（有趣地）回忆起先前的状况"，还向艾瑞克森演示，自己即便自愿说出那些粗话、做出那些动作，也毫无痛苦或失控。

b. gradually varying the types of expression of the behaviors to something more common or acceptable; and

c. finding a more appropriate context (such as the garage) in which to fully express the behaviors.

Erickson reports that the woman was able to practice and incorporate the new patterns of behavior well enough that, for the most part, she could get through the day without a manifestation of the symptoms – periodically going into the garage to "get it out of her system," first at weekly intervals and then monthly intervals, until finally she was symptom free. Seven years later Erickson saw the woman on a different matter. Still symptom free, she "reminisced with amusement about her previous condition" and demonstrated to Erickson that she could even voluntarily speak the vulgarities and make the movements without any distress or loss of control.

运用天才的模式Applying Patterns of Genius

Pacing and leading can be considered a type of meta-strategy in that it can incorporate a number of other macro and micro strategies. While pacing and leading the woman described in this case, for instance, Erickson applied a number of macro-strategies or Patterns of Genius that I summarized in the Introduction to this book:

先跟后带可被视为一种元策略，因为它能整合若干其他的宏观与微观策略。例如，在同步并引导本案所述妇女时，艾瑞克森运用了我于本书导论中归纳的若干宏观策略或天才模式：

  • Accessing special states for access to creative unconscious processes. Erickson's unusual use of hypnotic suggestions interrupted the woman's spiraling CRASH state and shifted her into a state where she was "completely silenced with a rigid fixation of her attention upon the author and the hypnotic suggestions being offered to her in such a peculiar emphatic fashion." This shift in her inner state opened up the possibility of inserting something new into the behavior pattern.
  • 进入特殊状态以接通创造性潜意识过程。 艾瑞克森对催眠暗示的不寻常运用，打断了妇女螺旋下坠的 CRASH 状态，将她shift（转移）进一种"因作者以如此奇特而强调的方式给出催眠暗示、将其注意力 rigid 固定于作者身上，而完全沉默"的状态。她内在状态的这一转变，开启了在行为模式中植入某种新东西的可能。
  • 调动多重视角。 通过将"污言秽语、粗话与亵渎之语"穿插进他与妇女的专业互动，艾瑞克森融合了两个不同视角。艾瑞克森建议妇女可以一种同样"令人满意"却"更好"的方式继续其症状行为，
  • Engaging multiple perspectives. By interspersing "obscenities, vulgarities, and profanities" into his professional interaction with the woman, Erickson blended two different perspectives. Erickson's suggestion that the woman could continue her symptomatic

这针对问题行为创造了一种元位置。

behavior in an equally "satisfying" but "better" manner creates a type of meta position to the problematic behavior.

  • 在不同区块大小与思维层次之间来回切换。 艾瑞克森引导妇女将问题行为 chunk（归类）为更小的成分，并开始将其变化为"咳嗽、喘息、窒息、打哈欠、学习自愿打嗝、剧烈交叉双腿"等。如此，妇女本质上是将那些成分 honoring（尊重）为其内在"全息层级"的一部分，并将断裂的"全息子"部分重新整合。
  • 强烈面向未来，并相信可能性。 在介入中，艾瑞克森通过将注意力与感知从当下的"问题框架"转向未来的"结果框架"——建议存在同样令人满意却"更好"、更恰当的表达——清晰地转移了妇女。
  • 保持坚定，以"反馈"框架而非"失败"框架运作。 艾瑞克森的方法也展现了大量的情绪智能，以及对 RAIN 过程的清晰落实。Recognize（辨认）行为模式——透过映照它；Allow（允许）它——建议妇女继续它，甚至定期"彻底释放一切"；Investigate（探究）它——建议"她能觉察它以及任何相关的想法，无论姿势动作多微小"；以及 Nurture（滋养）它——让她寻找更好、更恰当、满足其目的的表达。可以说，RAIN 的 R 与 A（辨认与允许）是一种跟，而 I 与 N（探究与滋养）是一种带。
  • 以系统性思维运作，并聚焦于"表层结构"之下的"深层结构"。 通过提醒妇女（a）"既然她的表现无论在有他人或无人时都会发生，只要她自己知道，她的症状表现便完全足够"，以及（b）她能觉察"任何相关的想法，无论姿势动作多微小"，艾瑞克森承认了其症状的系统性本质，并将注意力导向表层表现之下的深层结构。
  • Moving back and forth between different chunk sizes and levels of thinking. Erickson guided the woman to chunk the problematic behavior into smaller components and begin to vary them into "coughing, gasping, choking, yawning, learning how to belch voluntarily, crossing her legs violently," etc. By doing so, the woman was essentially honoring them as part of her inner "holarchy" and reintegrating disconnected parts of her "holon."

所有这些"宏观策略"都对介入的成功结果有所贡献。

  • Maintaining strong orientation toward the future and a belief in what is possible. In his intervention, Erickson clearly shifted the woman's attention and perception regarding her behavior from a Problem Frame in the present to an Outcome Frame in the future by suggesting that there were equally satisfying but "better" and more appropriate expressions.
  • Staying determined and operating from a "feedback" frame as opposed to "failure" frame. Erickson's approach also demonstrated a great deal of emotional intelligence and a clear implementation of the RAIN process. Recognizing the behavior pattern by mirroring it back; Allowing it by suggesting that the woman continue it and even periodically "let loose with everything"; Investigating it by suggesting "she could be aware of it and any associated thoughts, however minimal the postural movements were"; and Nurturing it by having her seek better and more appropriate expressions that satisfied its purpose. It could be said, in fact, that the R and A of RAIN (recognize and allow) are a type of pacing, and the I and N (investigate and nurture) are a form of leading.
  • Thinking systemically and focusing on the "deep structure" beyond the "surface structure". By reminding the woman that (a) "since her manifestations would occur in either the presence or absence of others, her symptomatology could be entirely adequate if she alone knew of it" and (b) she could be aware of "any associated thoughts, however minimal the postural movements were," Erickson acknowledges the systemic nature of her symptoms and directs attention the deeper structure beneath the surface manifestations.

All of these "macro-strategies" contributed to the successful outcome of the intervention.

"利用"原则The "Utilization" Principle

Erickson's acceptance of the woman's symptoms and encouragement to practice them is also an example of what is known as the "utilization" principle, which is considered one of Erickson's greatest contributions to hypnosis and psychotherapy. With respect to behavior change, utilization means the creative acceptance and use of any pattern—habits, beliefs, perceptions, attitudes, symptoms, or resistances—as a means for achieving a goal (in this case, a therapeutic outcome). In other words, it means utilizing whatever response your client offers you for some positive purpose.

艾瑞克森对妇女症状的接纳与鼓励其练习，也是所谓"利用"原则的一个范例，该原则被视为艾瑞克森对催眠与心理治疗最大的贡献之一。就行为改变而言，利用意味着对任何模式——习惯、信念、感知、态度、症状或阻抗——的创造性接纳与运用，作为达成某个目标（在此即治疗结果）的手段。换言之，它意味着将案主提供给你的任何反应，运用于某个正向目的。

The utilization principle could be considered another type of meta-strategy, as Erickson typically integrated a number of patterns of genius into his process of utilization as well. Notice how many of the same principles

利用原则也可被视为另一种元策略，因为艾瑞克森通常也将若干天才模式整合进他的利用过程之中。请注意前文案例中所辨识的许多相同原则，

identified in the previous case example apply in the following account. Presented in the book Phoenix (Gordon & Meyers-Anderson, 1982), Erickson describes how he was able to get through to a difficult mental patient in a psychiatric hospital.

如何也适用于下述叙述。在《凤凰城》（Phoenix，Gordon & Meyers-Anderson, 1982）一书中，艾瑞克森描述了他如何得以接通一家精神病院里一位难缠的精神病患。

Now a patient had been at Worcester for nine years. The patient had been brought in by the police with no identification marks on his clothing, we could get no information from him at all... We'd be social and say, "good morning." He replied with word salad—"bucket of lard, didn't pay up, sand on the beach," things like that... just irrelevant words all mixed up that didn't make any sense at all. And when I came there, I was intrigued with the patient, intrigued by him. I endeavored many times to elicit his name, and all I ever got was a continuous outpouring of word salad. So I sent my secretary out and told her, "Take down his word salad and transcribe it for me." I went through that word salad and then I prepared a word salad similar to his but not precisely the same. And one morning at eight o'clock I said, "good morning," and he replied with a big paragraph of word salad to which I responded with a big paragraph of word salad. We responded and we "word saladed" each other for a couple of hours. Finally, he said, "Why don't you talk sense Dr. Erickson?" I said, "I'd be glad to, what is your name?" He told me. "Where are you from?" and I started taking a good history and all of the sudden he began his word salad again. So, I responded in word salad. By the end of the day, I had a complete and medical examination—a very good account of him. And thereafter if I wanted to talk to him and he replied with word salad so did I. Well,
一位病人已在伍斯特（Worcester）待了九年。病人是由警察送来，衣物上无任何身份标记，我们完全无法从他那里获得任何信息……我们友好地说"早安"。他回以"词语沙拉"（word salad）——"一桶猪油、没付清、沙滩上的沙"之类……尽是些毫无意义、全然混杂的不相干字词。当我到那里时，我被这位病人迷住了，对他深感好奇。 我多次努力引出他的名字，得到的却始终是一股脑倾泻的词语沙拉。于是我派秘书出去，吩咐她："把他那词语沙拉记下来，替我转写。"我细读那词语沙拉，然后准备了一份类似却不完全相同的词语沙拉。一天早上八点我说"早安"，他回了一大段词语沙拉，我也回了一大段词语沙拉。我们彼此回应，彼此"词语沙拉"了几个小时。最后他说："艾瑞克森医生，你为什么不说说人话？"我说："我很乐意，你叫什么名字？"他告诉了我。"你从哪来？"我便开始好好采集他的病史，突然间他又开始了他的词语沙拉。于是，我也以词语沙拉回应。到那天结束时，我已对他完成了完整的内科与医学检查——一份关于他的极佳记录。此后，若我想同他说话而他回以词语沙拉，我也照样。嗯，

he soon dropped his word salad. And within a year he was able to be released from the hospital and get a job. Now I certainly didn't do any therapy. I just met him at his own level.

他很快便丢掉了他的词语沙拉。一年内，他得以出院并找到工作。我当然没做任何治疗。我只是以他自己的层次迎见了他。

Obviously, people unfamiliar with Erickson's tactics and approach might initially think that he was as crazy as the patient if they happened upon Erickson talking to the man in "word salad." In fact, Erickson reports that in his handling of the case he was "at first rather censoriously criticized by others." And yet, this process of "pacing" and "utilization" was a powerful way of establishing rapport with the patient. Rather than fight against the "sick person," this approach allows the individual to evolve his or her symptoms to something more appropriate, while still preserving whatever positive purpose those symptoms may be serving. It allows the part of the person's identity (holon) that has become separated or alienated to become integrated in with the rest of the individual as a functional behavior or put

显然，不熟悉艾瑞克森策略与方法的人，若偶然撞见艾瑞克森以"词语沙拉"与那人交谈，起初可能会以为他同病人一样疯。事实上，艾瑞克森报告说，在他处理这个案子时，曾"起初遭到他人相当严厉的批评"。然而，这种"同步"与"利用"的过程，是建立与病人亲和的强有力方式。它不对抗"病人"，而是让个体将其症状演化向更恰当之物，同时仍保留那些症状可能服务的任何正向目的。它使那从人的身份（全息子）中分离或异化出去的部分，得以作为功能性行为，或与人的其余部分整合进来，

〔图 p041〕

Erickson's use of "pacing" and "utilization" allowed the patient to evolve his symptoms to something more appropriate while preserving whatever positive purpose they were serving. An illustration of being truly "intrigued" with the other.

例如，艾瑞克森指出，在上述病人出院后，他定期向艾瑞克森汇报，且"总是以一点词语沙拉来开启或结束他的汇报"（期待艾瑞克森也同样回应），并狡黠地补上一句："生活中来点 nonsense（胡闹）不错，是吧，医生？" 若艾瑞克森曾试图以权力与权威、或以负向强化行为技巧，将症状从那人的行为中根除，他或许会夺走那人身份中一个非常重要且必要的部分。

For instance, Erickson notes that after the above patient's release, he reported back to Erickson on a regular basis, and that he "invariably initiated his report or terminated it with a bit of word-salad," (expecting the same from Erickson) and wryly adding, "Nothing like a little nonsense in life, is there, Doctor?" Had Erickson tried to eradicate the symptom from the man's behavior through power and authority or negatively reinforcing behavioral techniques, he may have taken away a very important and necessary part of the man's identity.

艾瑞克森那句"我被这位病人迷住了，对他深感好奇"，或许提供了他成功最重要的钥匙之一——他 genuinely（真心地）对病人的身份与福祉感到好奇与感兴趣。艾瑞克森不将他们仅仅视为专业挑战、一个待治的症状、一个"坏"人甚至"病"人，而是真心想要理解并帮助他们。

Erickson's statement, "I was intrigued with the patient, intrigued by him" probably provides one of the most important keys to his successes – that he was genuinely intrigued by and interested in the identity and wellbeing of his patients. Rather than consider them merely a professional challenge, a symptom to be treated, a "bad" person or even a "sick" person, Erickson truly wanted to understand them and help them.

将症状转化为解方Transforming Symptoms into Solutions

Another case that illustrates Erickson's genius involves that of a twenty-one-year-old woman who came to Erickson out of desperation (Uncommon Therapy, pp. 71–73). She told him, "I think I'm too inferior to live. I've got no friends, I stay by myself, and I'm too homely to get married. I thought I'd see a psychiatrist before I committed suicide. I'm going to try you for three months' time, and then if things aren't straightened out that's the end." She explained that she was an unwanted child, and her parents had died in a car accident soon after she graduated from high school. She lived alone, got a job as a secretary in a construction firm and had no social life. She had never dated, considering herself too unattractive, primarily due to her "physical defect"—what she considered to be a large gap between her front teeth, which she covered with her hand as she talked. (The gap was only about one eighth of an inch wide and not particularly noticeable.)

另一个展现艾瑞克森天才的案例，涉及一位二十一岁的年轻女子，她绝望地来找艾瑞克森（《非比寻常的治疗》[Uncommon Therapy]，pp. 71–73）。她告诉他："我觉得自己太差劲，不配活着。我没有朋友，独来独往，又太丑嫁不出去。我想在自杀前先见个精神科医生。我打算拿你试三个月，如果事情没理顺，那就到此为止。"她解释说，自己是个不受欢迎的孩子，父母在她高中毕业后不久便死于车祸。她独居，在一家建筑公司做秘书，没有社交生活。她从未约会过，认为自己太没吸引力，主要因为她的"身体缺陷"——她视为门牙间一道大缝，说话时用手遮着。（那缝其实只有约八分之一英寸宽，并不特别显眼。）

Erickson observed that, while the young woman was actually pretty, her hair was unkempt and uneven, and she wore unflattering clothes of unmatched colors that had rips in them. He also learned from her that there was a young man at her office who showed up at the drinking fountain

艾瑞克森观察到，尽管这年轻女子其实挺漂亮，她的头发却蓬乱不齐，穿着颜色不搭、还有破洞、不衬她的衣服。他还从她那里得知，办公室里有个年轻男子常出现在饮水机旁，

attractive, she ignored him and never spoke to him, considering herself too homely for him to be interested in her.

显得很有吸引力，她却无视他、从不与他说话，认为自己太丑，他不可能会对她感兴趣。

One of Erickson's first questions to the woman was whether she had any savings in the bank. A bit surprised, the young woman responded that she did but wondered why she was asking. Erickson said that, since she was "going downhill anyway," she "might as well have one last fling" before she died. This last fling would include taking the money she had in the bank and spending it on herself to get new clothes and a new hairstyle. Erickson also gave her the task of learning to squirt water through the gap between her front teeth until she could "achieve a distance of six feet with accuracy." While she considered this task ridiculous, Erickson reminded her that she had committed to trying him for three months and that, if things weren't straightened out, it would be the end anyway. So, she had nothing to lose.

艾瑞克森对那女子的第一个问题之一是：她在银行里有没有积蓄。年轻女子有些惊讶，回答说有，但奇怪他为何问这个。艾瑞克森说，既然她"反正是在走下坡路"，她"不妨在死前痛快地挥霍（fling）最后一次"。这最后一次挥霍，包括拿银行里的钱花在自己身上，买新衣服、做个新发型。艾瑞克森还给她一项任务：学会透过门牙间的缝喷水，直到能"准确喷到六英尺远"。尽管她觉得这任务荒谬，艾瑞克森提醒她，她已承诺试他三个月，如果事情没理顺，反正就结束了。所以，她没什么可损失的。

Once the young woman had new clothes, nice hair and had become skillful at squirting water through the gap in her teeth, Erickson gave her the assignment to play a "practical joke" on the young man at the water fountain. When he appeared, she was to "take a mouth full of water and squirt it at him" through the gap in her teeth. She was then to take one step towards him, turn around, and "run like hell" down the corridor.

一旦年轻女子有了新衣、漂亮头发，并熟练于从牙缝喷水，艾瑞克森便给她一项差事：在饮水机旁那年轻男子身上搞个"恶作剧"。当他出现时，她要"含一口水，透过牙缝朝他喷去"。然后她要朝他迈一步，转过身，"拼命跑"下走廊。

Horrified, the young woman initially rejected this idea as impossible. Erickson again reminded her that, since she was going downhill and was going to end it anyway, there was no reason to object. It would be a part of her "last fling." After considering Erickson's reasoning, she decided to do it.

年轻女子吓坏了，起初拒绝这主意，认为不可能。艾瑞克森再次提醒她，既然她在走下坡路、反正要结束这一切，没有理由反对。这会是她"最后一次挥霍"的一部分。考虑过艾瑞克森的理由后，她决定照做。

The next Monday she went to work dressed in her new outfit, with her hair done and waited at the water fountain. When the young man approached, she squirted him through her teeth and took off. To her surprise, he laughed, chased after her and, when he caught her, kissed her. The next day, as the young lady approached the water fountain, the young man sprang out from behind a telephone booth and squirted her with a water pistol. She responded by squirting him back through her teeth. This interaction resulted in another kiss. The following day they went out to dinner together and soon they were dating.

下周一，她穿上新装、做好头发，在饮水机旁等候。年轻男子靠近时，她透过牙缝喷他，然后跑掉。令她惊讶的是，他大笑着追上去，追上时吻了她。第二天，当年轻女子走近饮水机，年轻男子从电话亭后蹦出来，用水枪喷她。她回以透过牙缝喷他。这互动又换来一个吻。次日，他们一起出去吃了晚餐，很快开始约会。

When she saw Erickson again, she reported what had happened and said she was revising her opinion of herself. She wanted him to do a critical review of her since she had been doing quite a bit of thinking on the subject. He told her that when he first met her, she had appeared as a person who didn't care for herself or take good care of herself, much in contrast with the person she saw in front of him now. He pointed out that she had previously thought she had a dental defect instead of an asset and now had evidence from a masculine point of view that she was attractive. Furthermore, by cooperating in her therapy she had through her own efforts changed her life.

当她再次见到艾瑞克森，她汇报了发生的事，说自己在修正对自己的看法。她想让他做个批判性的回顾，因为她已就这个主题想了很多。他告诉她，初见时她显得是个不照顾自己、不好好对待自己的人，与他眼前这个人形成鲜明对比。他指出，她先前认为自己有牙齿缺陷而非资产，如今却有了来自男性视角的证据：她是有吸引力的。此外，透过配合治疗，她已靠自己的努力改变了人生。

Erickson reports that, several months later the young woman sent him an announcement of her engagement to the young man from the office and six months after that an announcement of their marriage. Fifteen months later, Erickson received a photograph of the couple's home, an announcement of the birth of their first child, and news of her new husband's promotion.

艾瑞克森报告，几个月后，年轻女子给他寄来了她与办公室那年轻男子订婚的喜讯；六个月后，又寄来了他们结婚的消息。十五个月后，艾瑞克森收到一张这对夫妇家的照片、他们第一个孩子出生的喜讯，以及她新婚丈夫升职的消息。

案例反思Reflections on the Case

This case provides another example of Erickson's masterful application of pacing, leading and utilization to transform symptoms into solutions. He

这个案例提供了另一个艾瑞克森大师级运用先跟后带与利用、将症状转化为解方的范例。他

begins his intervention by telling the young woman that, since she was already "going downhill" and contemplating suicide, she "might as well have one last fling." This accomplishes the psychological Aikido of channeling the woman's "away from" energy towards something more positive.

在介入之初告诉年轻女子，既然她已在"走下坡路"并考虑自杀，她"不妨痛快地挥霍最后一次"。这完成了一种心理上的合气道，将女子"远离"的能量，导引向更正面之物。

Erickson then continues to leverage the woman's threat to commit suicide as a motivation to take the actions necessary to change her life. If she objects to a task or suggestion that Erickson has made in order to promote a positive shift in her behavior, he reminds her that she is desperate to change and seriously contemplating suicide, so there is nothing to lose. The implication is that continuing her current limitations is not an option and, if she does end up killing herself, whatever she is concerned about regarding the behavior change won't matter to her anyway.

艾瑞克森接着 leverage（ leverage，善用）女子自杀的威胁，作为采取必要行动改变其生活的动机。若她反对艾瑞克森为促成其行为的正向转变而提出的任务或建议，他便提醒她：她已绝望地想要改变、并认真考虑自杀，因此没什么可损失的。其意涵是：延续她当前的局限并非选项，而即便她最终自杀，她所担心的关于行为改变的事，对她而言也都不再重要。

Erickson also brilliantly utilizes the "physical defect" of the space between the young woman's front teeth in order to make it perhaps her most precious asset (at that juncture in her life) by having her learn how to turn it into a "squirt gun." This unique use of the power of imagination is perhaps Erickson's most unparalleled gift and the source of his genius.

艾瑞克森也 brilliantly（精妙地）利用了年轻女子门牙间缝隙这一"身体缺陷"，透过让她学会将它变成一把"水枪"，使其成为或许她此生此刻最珍贵的资产。这种对想象力力量独一无二的运用，或许正是艾瑞克森最无与伦比的礼物，也是他天才的源泉。

The sequence of events with the young man at the water fountain clearly leads the young woman towards a meta position with respect to her current beliefs about herself. Erickson's "critical review" of her serves to secure and reinforce that meta position so that she can update her perceptions and beliefs about herself, her capacity for influencing her life and her future.

那年轻男子在饮水机旁的 sequence（一连串事件），清晰地引导年轻女子朝向关于她当前自我信念的一种元位置。艾瑞克森对她的"批判性回顾"，旨在巩固并强化那元位置，使她得以更新对自己、对自己影响生命的能力以及对自己未来的感知与信念。

There is a profound lesson in Erickson's genius for all of us. We all either deflate or inflate our perceptions of ourselves in particular areas of our lives.

在艾瑞克森的天才之中，对我们所有人有一则深远的教训。我们在生活某些领域里，要么贬低、要么夸大对自己的感知。

Erickson understood that deeply and did not judge it. But he recognized that either bias would create an unhealthy and unsustainable trajectory in our lives. He saw symptoms as the attempt of our unconscious mind to try to balance or compensate for that unfortunate bias.

艾瑞克森深切理解这一点，且不加以评判。但他认识到，任一种偏差都会在我们生命中制造出不健康、不可持续的轨迹。他将症状视为我们的潜意识心智试图平衡或补偿那不幸偏差的尝试。

While this and the previous examples involve people with fairly serious problems, it is obvious that the Erickson's meta-strategies, macro and micro strategies have much to offer in other more every-day contexts. Coaches, consultants, managers, teachers, salespeople, parents have much to gain by adopting Erickson's meta-strategies of pacing and leading and utilization in conjunction with the patterns of genius.

尽管这个与前一个案例涉及相当严重问题的人，但显然，艾瑞克森的元策略、宏观与微观策略，在其他更日常的情境中亦大有用处。教练、顾问、经理、教师、销售人员、父母，透过采用艾瑞克森的先跟后带及利用的元策略，并辅以天才模式，都将获益良多。

培养从元位置涵容"症状"的能力Developing the Ability to Hold "Symptoms" from a Meta Position

Key to Erickson's ability to apply the meta-strategies of pacing, leading and utilization, in order to help people transform their symptoms into a solution, was his capacity to welcome and "hold" or "create a space for" the expression of those symptoms. Doing so involves developing an inner state of "aware non-reactivity," or what we have called "meta position." Before going on to some of the other strategies of genius employed by Milton Erickson, I would like to present some exercises that can help you to learn to create such a space for yourself and others.

艾瑞克森得以运用先跟后带与利用的元策略，来帮助人们将症状转化为解方，其关键，在于他那种欢迎并"涵容"或"为"那些症状的表达"创造空间"的能力。这样做，涉及发展出一种"觉察的非反应性"（aware non-reactivity）的内在状态，或我们所谓"元位置"。在继续介绍米尔顿·艾瑞克森所运用的其他天才策略之前，我想先呈现一些练习，帮助你学会为自己与他人创造这样的空间。

Typically, we learn to hold ourselves and each other depending on how we ourselves have been held. Psychologist Donald Winnicott developed the notion of holding environments. This refers to the way a child is held physically, emotionally and psychologically by its primary caretakers. The child, in response and reaction to this holding environment, then learns to

通常，我们学习涵容自己与他人，取决于我们自身曾被如何涵容。心理学家唐纳德·温尼科特（Donald Winnicott）发展出涵容环境（holding environments）的观念。这指的是孩子被其主要照顾者以身体、情绪与心理方式所涵容的方式。作为对这涵容环境的回应与反应，孩子进而学会

hold itself in ways that unconsciously reproduce the deeply familiar holding environment of infancy and early childhood. Research indicates that the way a baby is held in the womb also influences the way it learns to relate with itself and the world later in life.

以无意识地复现婴儿期与幼年期那极熟悉的涵容环境的方式，来涵容自己。

As the three case examples I have presented illustrate, an important part of Erickson's genius was helping people learn to hold themselves and their feelings and symptoms in a way that was more resourceful than the way they had previously learned to hold them.

如我所呈现的三个案例所示，艾瑞克森天才的重要部分，在于帮助人们以比他们先前所学更富资源的方式，来涵容自己、自己的感受与症状。

The metaphorical image of a parent (or significant other) holding a crying baby can help us to explore this process of holding. Imagine that the parent represents a type of meta position, which is not caught in reaction but is an aware presence, and the baby represents whatever feeling, sensation, thought that we are experiencing. In meta position, we are able to "be with" a symptom or reaction without "becoming" it.

一位父母（或重要他人）抱着一个哭泣婴儿的比喻性意象，能帮助我们探索这"涵容"过程。想象父母代表一种元位置，它不陷入反应，而是一种觉察的临在；而婴儿代表我们所经验的任何感受、感觉或思想。在元位置上，我们得以"与"一个症状或反应同在，而不"成为"它。

In NLP II: The Next Generation (2010) my co-authors and I suggest that the following qualities are helpful for creating a positive "holding environment."

在《NLP II：下一代》（NLP II: The Next Generation，2010）中，我的合著者与我建议，以下品质有助于创造正向的"涵容环境"：

  • non-reactivity
  • unconditional acceptance of the experience exactly as it is
  • no agenda to change anything about the experience
  • patience, taking time
  • unwavering attention to the experience
  • trust that all is well as it is, that the experience has a positive intention and purpose
  • a sense of being held in a field greater than oneself
  • kindness toward the experience
  • a non-intrusive curiosity about the experience
  • 非反应性
  • 对经验如其所示的无条件接纳
  • 无意改变经验任何部分的议程
  • 耐心，花时间
  • 对经验坚定不移的注意力
  • 信任一切本就安好，经验有其正向意图与目的
  • 一种被大于自身之场域所涵容的感觉
  • 对经验的善意
  • 对经验一种非侵入的好奇

It is also helpful to identify unuseful behaviors or attitudes towards the experience such as:

认出对经验无用（unuseful）的行为或态度也有帮助，例如：

  • wanting it to go away
  • wanting it to be different, to change
  • analyzing or explaining it
  • identifying with it (getting lost in it)
  • 想要它走开
  • 想要它不同、改变
  • 分析或解释它
  • 认同于它（迷失于其中）

A positive holding environment emerges naturally from the practice of a COACH state and the RAIN process.

正向的涵容环境，自然地从 COACH 状态与 RAIN 过程的练习中涌现。

In order to transform their expression, symptoms (like a baby in distress) need to be held from a state of presence, curiosity and non-reactivity. Through this type of holding, the expression of the symptoms can move from a state of contraction and separation to a state of relaxation and reconnection to the larger "holon." The purpose is not to get rid of the symptoms, but rather to relate to them in a more resourceful and creative way. When we do this, we recuperate the energy that was previously spent in avoiding, fighting

为了转化其表达，症状（如一个痛苦中的婴儿）需要被从一种临在、好奇与非反应的状态中涵容。透过这类涵容，症状的表达能从收缩与分离的状态，移动到放松与重新连接更大"全息子"的状态。目的不是摆脱症状，而是以更富资源、更具创造性的方式与之建立关系。当我们这样做时，我们 recuperate（回收）了先前耗费在回避、对抗

or suppressing the symptom and are able to redirect that energy to creatively explore a better, more satisfying and more appropriate expression of the intention behind the symptom.

或压抑症状上的能量，并得以将那能量重定向，去创造性地探索症状背后意图的一种更好、更令人满意、更恰当的表达。

The larger frame or psychological field in which we hold symptoms, or any experience, generally determines the impact or influence they have upon us. Family therapy innovator Virginia Satir, for instance, used to frequently ask her clients two questions when they were struggling with some life challenge. The first question was: "How do you feel?" A client might respond to this question by answering that he or she felt angry, sad, afraid, guilty, or some other type of difficult feeling. Then Virginia would ask a second question: "How do you feel about feeling that way?"

我们涵容症状或任何经验的更大框架或心理场域，通常决定了它们对我们的影响或作用。家庭治疗创新者维吉尼亚·萨提尔，例如，常在她案主挣扎于某个人生挑战时问两个问题。第一个问题是："你感觉如何？"案主可能回答感到愤怒、悲伤、害怕、内疚，或其他某种困难感受。然后维吉尼亚会问第二个问题："你对有那种感觉感觉如何？"

The answer to this second question is quite significant and determines a lot about the impact and meaning the answer to the first question will have. It defines our relationship with the first feeling. For instance, it makes quite a difference if someone feels calm or curious about feeling angry rather than feeling guilty, helpless or frustrated about feeling angry. It is these second feelings that determine the ease and quality with which we are able to stay centered and resourcefully hold the first set of feelings. If we hold one CRASH state within another CRASH state we end up in a negatively reinforcing spiral. Thus, an important question is, "What is the quality of your relationship with any given content of your experience?" As my colleague Richard Moss likes to ask, "Is your fear safe inside of you?"

第二个问题的答案相当重要，并决定了第一个问题答案的影响与意义之大半。它定义了我们与第一种感受的关系。例如，一个人对感到愤怒感到平静或好奇，与对感到愤怒感到内疚、无助或挫败，差别相当大。正是这些"第二层的感受"，决定了我们得以多轻松、多高质量地保持居中，并富有资源地涵容第一组的感受。若我们将一个 CRASH 状态涵容于另一个 CRASH 状态之中，我们便陷入一个负向强化的螺旋。因此，一个重要的问题是："你与自身经验的任何给定内容，关系是怎样的？"正如我的同事理查德·莫斯（Richard Moss）喜欢问的："你的恐惧在你之内是安全的吗？"

The impact of this dynamic was clear in Erickson's example of the woman fighting against her inner urge to "utter a variety of obscenities, particularly vulgarisms concerning body functions and sexual activity, all being ascribed

这一动力的冲击，在艾瑞克森那个与内在冲动搏斗的妇女案例中清晰可见——她"被迫说出各种污言秽语，尤其是关于身体功能与性活动的粗话，全都被归到

〔图 p037〕

to Jesus" as well as the "compelling desires to grimace, to gesticulate, and to posture." Her reactions to those impulses, and to herself for having them, were horror, shock, distress and shame. Erickson's intervention allowed her to gain a meta position to both the compulsive feelings and her distress about them, creating the possibility for something new to emerge.

通往元位置的"咒语"（Mantras）"Mantras" for Moving to Meta Position

In the generative change work that I developed with my colleague Dr. Stephen Gilligan (also a dedicated student of Erickson), we use several statements, or "mantras" as we call them, which represent Erickson's attitude

在我与同事斯蒂芬·吉利根博士（Stephen Gilligan，也是艾瑞克森的忠实学生）所发展的生成性改变工作中，我们使用几个陈述，或我们所谓的"咒语"，它们代表了艾瑞克森对症状、障碍与阻抗的态度

toward symptoms, obstacles and resistances. They are a powerful way to create a positive and emotionally intelligent inner holding environment for experiences or responses that usually precipitate some form of CRASH. The four fundamental statements are:

（pose）。它们是一种强有力的方法，为那些通常会引发某种 CRASH 的经验或反应，创造正向且情绪智能的内在涵容环境。这四个基本陈述是：

  1. That's interesting.
  2. I'm sure it makes sense.
  3. Something needs to be held, heard or healed.
  4. Welcome....
  1. 那真有意思。（That's interesting.）
  2. 我相信它自有道理。（I'm sure it makes sense.）
  3. 有什么需要被涵容、被听见或被疗愈。（Something needs to be held, heard or healed.）
  4. 欢迎……（Welcome....）

The statement "That's interesting" activates the R of the RAIN process. It reminds us to Recognize the response and that there is something to be learned or better understood regarding the situation; that it too is an area where we can potentially evolve and grow.

"那真有意思"这个陈述，启动了 RAIN 过程的 R。它提醒我们辨认（Recognize）那反应，以及关于该情境有某事值得学习或更好理解；那也是一个我们潜在能演化与成长之处。

The affirmation "I'm sure it makes sense" helps us to Allow whatever the experience is. It brings the awareness that whatever is happening is part of a larger holarchy and deserves to be recognized, treated respectfully and can benefit from receiving our resources.

"我相信它自有道理"这肯定，帮助我们允许（Allow）无论那经验是什么。它带来一种觉知：无论发生什么，都是更大全息层级的一部分，值得被认出、被尊重对待，并可以从接收我们的资源中获益。

The assertion that "Something needs to be held, heard or healed" stimulates us to Investigate the symptom or experience. It acknowledges the fact that there is most likely some positive intention behind the response, even if we do not understand what it is yet.

"有什么需要被涵容、被听见或被疗愈"这断言，刺激我们去探究（Investigate）那症状或经验。它承认一个事实：那反应背后极可能有着某种正向意图，即便我们还不懂那是什么。

The declaration "Welcome" opens the possibility to Nurture the symptom or experience. It means that we are intentionally giving it a place and bring connections to inner resources, instead of trying to "get rid" of it.

"欢迎"这宣告，开启了滋养（Nurture）那症状或经验的可能。它意味着我们有意识地给它一个位置，并连接到内在资源，而非试图"摆脱"它。

Gilligan and I have found practicing these statements with ourselves and others to be quite powerful. They often bring an almost immediate relaxing and a marked lessening of inner turmoil. This allows people to begin to connect to their resources and to the larger holon of themselves.

吉利根与我发现，对自己与他人练习这些陈述相当有力。它们常带来近乎即刻的放松，以及内在 turmoil（纷扰）的显著减轻。这让人们开始连接其资源，以及自己更大的全息子。

练习：创造正向的涵容环境Practicing Creating a Positive Holding Environment

Inspired by Dr. Erickson (and Virginia Satir), I have developed the following exercise to help myself and others discover and apply the resources needed to keep from CRASHing when faced with strong and difficult or "negative" emotions or reactions. It involves going to meta position as well as entering a COACH state so that the causes of those reactions can be identified and transformed. As you will see, it requires using somatic intelligence (through body movement and gestures) as much as or more than intellectual reasoning. It is a process that you can do yourself or guide others through.

受艾瑞克森博士（与维吉尼亚·萨提尔）启发，我发展了以下练习，帮助自己与他人，在面临强烈而困难或"负面"的情绪或反应时，发现并运用所需的资源，以避免 CRASH。它涉及进入元位置与进入 COACH 状态，以便辨识并转化那些反应的原因。你将看到，它需要运用体感智能（透过身体动作与姿态），其程度不亚于甚至可能超过智识推理。这是一个你可以自己做、或引导他人做的 process。

  1. Identify a situation in which you experience a reaction or response that you are not able to hold resourcefully, and which consequently creates an obstacle or drags you into some form of CRASH state. Put yourself into that experience using memory or imagination. Bring your attention to your inner state and explore the question, "How do I feel?" Whatever the answer, repeat to yourself the four statements "That's interesting," "I'm sure it makes sense," "Something needs to be heard, held or healed," "Welcome." Bring awareness to the feeling and allow your body to naturally express it in a gesture and movement (e.g., clenching fists).
  1. 辨识一个你经验到某种你无法富有资源地涵容的反应或回应、并因此制造障碍或将你拖入某种 CRASH 状态的情境。用记忆或想象，将自己置入那经验。将注意力带向你的内在状态，并探索这个问题："我感觉如何？"无论答案是什么，对自己重复四个陈述："那真有意思""我相信它自有道理""有什么需要被听见、涵容或疗愈""欢迎。"将觉察带向那感受，并允许你的身体以姿态与动作自然表达它（例如握紧拳头）。
  2. 现在，从你正经验这问题性回应或感受的位置后退一步。从这新的 physical 位置，反思那个正在经验感受与回应的你。你对那回应和/或感受感觉如何？你对自己在感觉/经验它们时感觉如何？你与那些回应/感受、以及与经验着它们的你，关系是怎样的？极可能你会经验到某种其他形式无用或缺乏资源（CRASH）的回应，如挫败、无助、自我评判或绝望等。再次，针对这第二层的感受/回应，重复四个陈述："那真有意思""我相信它自有道理""有什么需要被听见、涵容或疗愈""欢迎。"找出表达这第二层感受或回应的姿态或动作（例如双手推开）。
  3. 现在退到第三个位置（元位置），在那里你能观察前两个位置、它们相关的回应以及它们的关系。将自己置入一种有资源的状态，在其中你居中、开放、清醒，并与自己及一个更大的支持系统（COACH 状态）相连。什么内在资源（如信任、接纳、好奇、力量等）能帮助你"保持接地、并让一切都在 perspective（视角）之中"？什么资源能让你从自己更大的觉察自我（全息子）来涵容这两组感受，而非 stuck（困住）于其中或与它们冲突？
  4. 将你所辨识的资源充分带入身体。（若需要，你可以透过为资源寻找参考经验，并尽可能完整地重温它们来促进。）找出一个表达这资源、并使其在你身体中 present（在场）的姿态与动作（例如双臂在你身前打开）。
  5. 带着第 4 步的资源与姿态，退回到第二个位置。不要试图改变任何东西。只是将与该第二位置相关的感受与回应，连同那资源一起涵容。做出你所选资源的姿态与动作。当你这样做时，留意你对第一组回应与感受、以及那正经验它们的"你"的感知与态度发生了什么转变。你对第一组感受与回应的感觉有何转变？
  6. 现在回到你放置那初始感受与回应情境的第一个位置，带上你所辨识的资源。再次，不要试图改变任何东西。只是将那些感受与回应，连同新资源一起涵容。做出你所选资源的姿态与动作。你现在对那些初始的回应与感受感觉如何？你涵容那些感受与回应的能力发生了什么变化？什么变得可能了？
  1. Now take a step back from the location in which you were experiencing this problematic response or feeling. From this new physical location, reflect upon the you who is experiencing the feelings and response. How do you feel about that response and/or feelings? How do you feel about yourself for feeling/experiencing them? What is your relationship with those responses/feelings and with yourself when you are feeling/experiencing them? It is more than likely that you will experience some other form of unhelpful or unresourceful (CRASH) response such as frustration, helplessness, self-judgment or despair, etc. Again, with respect to this second feeling/response, repeat the four statements "That's interesting," "I'm sure it makes sense," "Something needs to be heard, held or healed," "Welcome." Find the gesture or movement that expresses this second feeling or response (e.g., hands pushing away).

上图中，那对灵性双翼，代表着与超越个体身份之资源场域的联结。

  1. Now step back to a third location (meta position) where you are able to observe the first two positions, their associated responses and their relationship. Put yourself into a resourceful state in which you are centered, open, awake and connected to yourself and a larger system of support (the COACH state). What inner resource (e.g., trust, acceptance, curiosity, strength, etc.) could help you to "keep grounded and to keep it all in perspective?" What resource(s) would allow you to hold both sets of feelings from your larger aware self (holon) rather than become stuck in them or in conflict with them?
  1. Bring the resource(s) you have identified fully into your body. (If you need to, you can facilitate this by finding reference experiences for the resources and reliving them as fully as you can.) Find a gesture and movement that expresses this resource and brings it present in your body (e.g., arms open in front of you).
  1. Step back into the second location bringing the resources and gesture from step 4. Don't attempt to change anything. Just hold the feelings and rponses associated with the second location along with the resource. Make the gesture and movement associated with the resource you have chosen. As you do, notice what shifts in your perception and attitude toward the first set of responses and feelings and the "you" who is experiencing them in that situation. What shifts in your feeling about the first set of feelings and responses?
  1. Now return to the first location in which you placed the situation where you experienced the initial set of responses and feelings, bringing the resource you have identified with you. Again, don't attempt to change anything. Just hold the feelings and responses along with the new resource. Make the gesture and movement associated with the resource you have chosen. How do you feel now about those initial responses and feelings? What changes in your ability to hold those feelings and responses? What becomes possible?

〔图 p035〕Diagram showing a human figure with ethereal wings and a flowchart with three boxes: "1. How do you feel? (FIRST FEELING)" → "2. How do you feel about feeling that way? (SECOND FEELING)" → "3. What inner resource do you need to hold both feelings? Meta Position and COACH STATE"

In the image above, the ethereal wings represent the connection to a field of resources beyond our individual identity.

转化"CRASH"状态Transforming "CRASH" States

Once we are able to hold the problematic response and feelings from a meta position (the R and the A of RAIN – Recognize and Allow) it becomes possible to Investigate them (understand their function and positive intention) and Nurture them (connect them to inner resources that transform their expression) and integrate them. To transform and integrate a response, it is necessary to understand its positive intention and connect it to other complementary resources.

一旦我们得以从元位置涵容那问题性回应与感受（RAIN 的 R 与 A —— 辨认与允许），便有可能去探究它们（理解其功能与正向意图）并滋养它们（将它们连接到能转化其表达的内在资源），进而整合它们。要转化并整合一个回应，必须先理解其正向意图，并将其连接到其他互补的资源。

You can explore this in a practical way by going through the following steps.

你可以通过以下步骤，以务实的方式探索这一点。

  1. Associate into the problematic response and feelings that you were working with in the previous exercise that was creating a "CRASH" state. Repeat the gesture or movement associated with or represents that response and feeling state (e.g., clenching fists). Notice in what way that movement leads you away from your COACH state. (It most likely increases some form of CRASH or "neuromuscular lock.")
  1. 关联到（associate into）你在前一练习中所处理的、正在制造"CRASH"状态的问题性回应与感受。重复与那回应及感受状态相关、或代表它的姿态或动作（例如握紧拳头）。留意那动作以何种方式将你带离 COACH 状态。（它极可能增强某种形式的 CRASH 或"神经肌肉锁结"。）
  2. 现在回到你前一练习第三步所辨识的资源状态，做出与那资源相关的姿态或动作（例如双臂在你身前打开）。
  3. 保持在 COACH 状态中，缓慢移回与问题性回应及感受相关的动作。非常缓慢地重复那动作（例如握紧拳头）数次，保持居中并高度觉察。当你这样做时，思考那动作及相伴情绪状态的正向意图。它试图为你做或完成什么？
  4. 将那动作及感受的正向意图保持在你的觉察中，缓慢回到与资源状态相关的姿态（例如双臂在你身前打开）。觉察这资源如何能支撑那问题性回应及/或感受的正向意图。
  5. 保持在 COACH 状态中，练习在两个姿态之间缓慢、优雅且正念地来回移动。留意在两个姿态与动作之间存在许多组合与变化的表达。谨记艾瑞克森的微观策略：逐步增强或减弱某一特定行为的强度、逐步变化行为的表达类型，以及寻找更恰当的情境。探索将两种姿态与动作融合为单一姿态与动作的方式。那整合后的动作，带来了何种新的洞见或可能？
  1. Now go back to the resource state you identified in the third step of the previous exercise and make the gesture or movement associated with that resource (e.g., arms open in front of you).

正如健美操与身体锻炼能改善你身体的健康与体能，研读与心智练习能改善你的认知功能，练习这类练习也能强化你的情绪智能，以及 characterizes（构成）米尔顿·艾瑞克森天才策略的那种无评判觉察、元位置与创造性好奇。

  1. Staying in the COACH state, move slowly back to the movement associated with the problematic response and feeling. Repeat the movement (e.g., clenching fists) several times very slowly, staying centered and with heightened awareness. As you do, consider the positive intention of the movement and associated emotional state. What is it trying to do or accomplish for you?

在我们继续探索艾瑞克森对语言及"语言的魔力"（Sleight of Mouth）的创新运用时，谨记这一点十分重要。

  1. Keeping the positive intention of the movement and the feeling in your awareness, slowly return to the gesture associated with the resource state (e.g., arms open in front of you). Become aware of how this resource can support the positive intention of the problematic response and/or feeling.

艾瑞克森对语言的魔力的直觉掌握

  1. Remaining in the COACH state, practice moving back and forth between the two gestures, slowly, gracefully and mindfully. Notice how there are many combinations and variations of expression between the two gestures and movements. Keep in mind Erickson's micro strategies of gradually increasing and reducing the intensity of a particular behavior, gradually varying the types of expression of a behavior and finding a more appropriate context. Explore ways of blending the two gestures and movements into a single gesture and movement. What new insight or option becomes possible as a result of the integrated movement?

米尔顿·艾瑞克森效力与"魔法"的主要部分，是他运用语言影响人们思想与行为的创造性能力。事实上，艾瑞克森对语言的直觉运用，正是我发展出十四种所谓"语言的魔力"模式的主要灵感来源——这些模式见于《语言的魔力：对话式信念改变之魔法》（Sleight of Mouth: The Magic of Conversational Belief Change，1999）与《语言的魔力 第二卷：词语如何改变世界》（Sleight of Mouth Volume II: How Words Can Change Worlds，2023）两书。

Just like calisthenics and physical exercise can improve the health and fitness of your body and studying and mental practice can improve your cognitive functioning, practicing exercises like this can strengthen your emotional intelligence and your capacity for the non-judgmental awareness, meta position and creative curiosity that characterize Milton Erickson's strategies of genius.

语言的魔力模式本质上可概括为"言语换框"（verbal reframes），它们影响信念、信念所由形成的心理地图，以及这些信念所产生的行为。信念在激励并形塑我们的思想与行动上，扮演强大而重要的角色。改变信念，常能触发行为的深刻转变。

It will be important to keep this in mind as we move on to explore Erickson's innovative use of language and "Sleight of Mouth."

事实上，"语言的魔力"一词源自 sleight of hand（手技），一种常以纸牌戏法形式呈现的魔法。这类魔法的特征，是"方才还在，转眼消失"的体验。例如，一个人或许将黑桃 A 放在牌堆顶，但当魔术师拾起那张牌时，它已"变"成红心皇后或小丑。语言的魔力的言语模式，能透过创造感知的根本转变，产生类似的"魔法"特质，进而促成行为的显著改变。

Milton Erickson's Intuitive Mastery of "Sleight of Mouth"

米尔顿·艾瑞克森发展出对语言的魔力模式所代表之微观策略的直觉掌握。在接下来的小节中，我将呈现各种语言的魔力模式、生成它们的相应微观策略，以及艾瑞克森如何运用它们产生往往突然而戏剧性改变的例子。我先前曾在《语言的魔力 第二卷》各章中呈现这些例子，以illustrate（说明）特定语言模式。我将它们全收集于此一处，是为了说明艾瑞克森在结合天才模式的情况下，运用先跟后带与利用的天才。

A major part of Milton Erickson's effectiveness and "magic" was his creative ability to use language to influence people's thoughts and behaviors. In fact, Erickson's intuitive use of language was one of the main inspirations for my development of the fourteen so-called "Sleight of Mouth" patterns described in the books Sleight of Mouth: The Magic of Conversational Belief Change (1999) and Sleight of Mouth Volume II: How Words Can Change Worlds (2023).

因此，即便这些例子可能与语言的魔力书中相同，我在此连续呈现它们的目的，是为对艾瑞克森的天才带来新的洞见。

Sleight of Mouth patterns can be fundamentally characterized as "verbal reframes" which influence beliefs, the mental maps from which beliefs have been formed and the behaviors they produce. Beliefs play a powerful and important role in motivating and shaping our thoughts and actions. Changing beliefs can often trigger profound shifts in behavior.

In fact, the term "Sleight of Mouth" is drawn from sleight of hand, a type of magic frequently done in the form of card tricks. This kind of magic is characterized by the experience, "now you see it, now you don't." A person may place an ace of spades at the top of the deck, for example, but, when the magician picks up the card, it has "transformed" into a queen of hearts or a Joker. The verbal patterns of Sleight of Mouth can produce a similar sort of "magical" quality by creating fundamental shifts in perception that can lead to significant changes in behavior.

Milton Erickson developed an intuitive mastery of the micro-strategies represented by the Sleight of Mouth patterns. In the following section, I will present the various Sleight of Mouth patterns, the accompanying micro strategy that generates them and examples of how Erickson used them to produce often sudden and dramatic change. I have previously presented these examples in the various chapters of Sleight of Mouth Volume II as a way to illustrate particular language patterns. I have collected them all together here in one place in order to illustrate Erickson's genius with pacing, leading and utilization in conjunction with patterns of genius. So, even though the examples may be the same as in the Sleight of Mouth book, my purpose in

presenting them in succession here is to bring new insights into Erickson's genius.

意图（Intention）Intention

Intention is the first Sleight of Mouth pattern. It involves directing attention to the purpose or intent behind a belief or behavior rather than focus on the content. Intent is the cornerstone of all of the other Sleight of Mouth patterns, as it addresses the underlying motivation behind the belief or behavior and its contents – in particular, the "positive intention."

意图是第一个语言的魔力模式。它涉及将注意力导向一个信念或行为背后的目的或意图，而非聚焦于内容。意图是所有其他语言的魔力模式的基石，因为它 addressing（处理）的是信念或行为及其内容背后的底层动机——尤其是"正向意图"。

The NLP principle of positive intention maintains that, at some level, our feelings, thoughts and behavior have been developed to serve some positive intent or purpose – even if the feelings, thoughts and behaviors themselves are problematic or harmful. For instance, the positive intention behind violent or aggressive behavior is often "protection." The positive intention or purpose behind fear is usually "safety." The positive purpose behind anger can be to "maintain boundaries." Hatred may have the positive purpose of "motivating" a person to take action. The belief, "if I try X, I will fail," could be motivated and sustained by the intention to prevent disappointment.

NLP 的正向意图原则主张：在某一层面上，我们的感受、思想与行为，是为服务某种正向意图或目的而发展出来的——即便那些感受、思想与行为本身是有问题的或有害的。例如，暴力或攻击行为的正向意图常是"保护"。"恐惧"的正向意图或目的通常是"安全"。"愤怒"的正向目的，可能是"维持边界"。憎恨可能具有"激励"一个人采取行动的正向目的。"如果我去试 X，我会失败"这个信念，可能由"避免失望"的意图所驱动与维持。

Intentions are invariably related to values (e.g., safety, honesty, control, growth, connection, achievement, etc.) and provide the underlying motivation for particular feelings, thoughts and actions. Intentions at one level are sometimes derivatives of other deeper intentions. The intention to "attack someone," for example, may come from the deeper intention to "protect oneself." Similarly, the intention to "punish someone," may come from the deeper intention to "teach them something."

意图总是与价值相关（例如安全、诚实、控制、成长、联结、成就等），并为特定的感受、思想与行动提供底层动机。某一层面的意图，有时是其他更深层意图的衍生物。"攻击某人"的意图，可能源自"保护自己"的更深意图。类似地，"惩罚某人"的意图，可能源自"教导他某事"的更深意图。

Any attempt to strengthen, question or transform a belief, and the associated responses, needs to take into account the positive intention that the belief and corresponding behavior was developed to serve. If a new possibility or other perspective does not resonate with the intention that spawned the belief and which is holding it in place, it is likely to be rejected or viewed with suspicion. This is why Intent is the first Sleight of Mouth pattern. Finding a resonant positive intention is going to make the rest of the conversation or intervention much easier and more impactful.

任何强化、质疑或转化一个信念及相关回应的尝试，都须将那信念及相应行为所发展出来以服务其用的正向意图纳入考量。若一个新的可能或另一视角，与催生该信念并使其保持到位（in place）的意图不相共鸣，它很可能被拒绝或投以怀疑。这正是为何意图是第一个语言的魔力模式。找到一个能共鸣的正向意图，将使对话或介入的其余部分容易得多、也更有影响力。

The micro strategy for effectively applying the Sleight of Mouth pattern of Intent is expressed by the questions: What is the positive purpose or intention of this belief or behavior it leads to? What does this belief or behavior do positively for the person who has formed it?

有效运用语言的魔力意图模式的微观策略，由以下问题表达：这个信念或行为所导致的正向目的或意图是什么？这个信念或行为，对形成它的那个人，正面地做了什么？

A focus on the positive intention behind thoughts and behaviors was a major foundation for Milton Erickson's work as a psychiatrist and healer. His claim that "the symptom is the solution" meant that symptoms are frequently an attempt to bring some type of balance or wholeness into a person's life but were coming out in a distorted (CRASH) expression.

聚焦于思想与行为背后的正向意图，是米尔顿·艾瑞克森作为精神科医师与治疗者工作的一个主要基础。他那"症状即解方"的主张，意味着症状常常是试图为一个人生命带入某种平衡或整体性，却以扭曲（CRASH）的表达冒现出来。

A good example of Erickson's emphasis on positive intention is his approach to the woman with "Tourette's Syndrome" described earlier. Erickson brought the woman's attention to the intent behind her own words and behaviors by suggesting that she was to continue her symptomatic behavior in a "satisfying" but "better" manner.

艾瑞克森强调正向意图的一个好例子，是他对前述那位"妥瑞氏症"妇女的处理。艾瑞克森透过建议她以"令人满意"却"更好"的方式继续其症状行为，将那妇女的注意力导向了她自己言语与行为背后的意图。

[Since her manifestations would occur in either the presence or absence of others, her symptomatology could be entirely adequate if

*[既然她的表现无论在有他人或无人时都会发生，只要她自己知道，她的症状表现便完全足够。

she alone knew of it. Thus, her utterances need not be so loud, since she could hear even the softest whispers as well as the loudest shouts. Additionally, the posturing could also be minimal, since she could be aware of it and any associated thoughts, however minimal the postural movements were.*

因此她的发声无需那么响亮，因为她连最轻的耳语与最响的呼喊都能听见。此外，姿势动作也可减到最少，因为她能觉察它以及任何相关的想法，无论姿势动作多微小。]*

Clearly, Erickson was directing the woman's focus to the intent behind her words and behaviors rather than to the content of her utterances and actions. For those words and behaviors to become "satisfying" but "better," they would need to fulfill the intention behind them.

显然，艾瑞克森是将那妇女的注意力，导向其言语与行为背后的意图，而非其发声与行动的内容。要让那些言语与行为变得"令人满意"却"更好"，它们须满足其背后的意图。

It is important to note that, before Erickson could direct attention to the intention of the behaviors, he had to first interrupt the woman's ongoing "CRASH-Away From" cycle, which was spiraling out of control. Given Erickson's role as a hypnotherapist, and his propensity to engage people's "creative unconscious" rather than their conscious cognitive processes, he did not get any explicit statements of the woman's belief or intentions. However, the fact that the symptoms emerged in church and that she was "horribly distressed" about them, makes it easy to assume that she probably thought that they were "bad" or "sinful," that there was no positive intention behind them, and that she might be "crazy" or "possessed!"

值得注意的是，在艾瑞克森能将注意力导向那些行为的意图之前，他必须先打断那妇女正在失控螺旋的"CRASH—远离"循环。鉴于艾瑞克森作为催眠治疗师的角色，以及他倾向于调动人们的"创造性潜意识"而非其有意识的认知过程，他并未得到那妇女信念或意图的任何明确陈述。然而，症状在教堂中浮现、且她对此"极度痛苦"的事实，使我们容易假设：她或许认为自己那些言行是"坏"或"有罪"的，它们背后没有任何正向意图，而她自己可能"疯了"或"被附身了"！

By mirroring (and exaggerating) her words and behavior back to her from a COACH state, not only did Erickson shock the woman out of her CRASH state but simultaneously created a powerful dilemma. A respected psychiatrist was able to say and do the same things she was doing and was clearly not bad, out of control, crazy or possessed. This shifted the attention from the words and behaviors themselves to the intention behind them. What is their source and what is their intended purpose? By having the woman continue her symptomatic behavior in a "satisfying" but "better" manner, Erickson also shifted from an "away from" problem frame to a "toward" outcome frame.

通过从 COACH 状态中映照（并夸大）她的言语与行为回给她，艾瑞克森不仅将那妇女从她的 CRASH 状态中震醒，同时创造了一个强大的两难。一位受敬重的 psychiatrists（精神科医师），能够说出并做出与她相同的事，且显然不是坏的、失控的、疯的或被附身的。这将注意力从言语与行为本身，转移到了它们背后的意图。它们的来源是什么，它们 intended（旨在）的目的又是什么？通过让那妇女以"令人满意"却"更好"的方式继续其症状行为，艾瑞克森也将框架从"远离"的问题框架，转移到了"朝向"的结果框架。

In such cases, it was not Erickson's style to inquire what the woman discovered about the intentions or "associated thoughts" that accompanied the words and behaviors. Given that the woman was clearly religious, it is likely that she had judgments about certain thoughts and feelings that she had tried to repress, and which consequently erupted out in a CRASH induced form. As a result, she had never had any coaching on how to identify the purpose of those thoughts and feelings and how to express them in an appropriate way that fulfilled their ultimate intention. This is what Erickson was able to accomplish through the skillful use of the Sleight of Mouth pattern (and micro strategy) of Intent. In this way, the symptoms were indeed transformed into a solution for her.

在此类案例中，探究那妇女发现了关于那些意图或伴随言语行为的"相关想法"什么，并非艾瑞克森的风格。鉴于那妇女显然笃信宗教，她极可能对某些她试图压抑的思想与感受有过评判，因而它们以 CRASH 诱发的形式喷涌而出。结果，她从未得到过关于如何辨识那些思想与感受之目的、以及如何以恰当方式表达它们以满足最终意图的 any（任何）教练。这正是艾瑞克森得以通过巧妙运用意图的语言的魔力模式（及微观策略）所达成的。如此，症状确实被转化成了对她的解方。

重新定义（Redefine）Redefine

The Sleight of Mouth pattern of Redefine involves substituting new words for those used in the belief statement that mean something similar but have different implications. Redefining is fundamentally a form of renaming or relabeling. In its simplest form, Redefine involves restating the belief using different vocabulary that reduces any potential CRASH associated with the belief and gives more positive choices regarding the belief and its contents.

重新定义的语言的魔力模式，涉及以意义相近但意涵不同的新词，替换信念陈述中所用的词。重新定义本质上是一种重命名或重新贴标签。在其最简单的形式中，重新定义涉及以不同的词汇重述信念，以减少与该信念相关的任何潜在 CRASH，并为该信念及其内容提供更正面的选择。

Redefining is a function of a micro strategy focusing on finding another way to name or talk about a potentially limiting belief that reduces any CRASH associated with it and opens up other possibilities. This micro strategy primarily focuses on the question: What is another word (or phrase)

重新定义是一种微观策略的功能，该策略聚焦于：寻找另一种命名或谈论某个可能具限制性之信念的方式，以减少与其相关的任何 CRASH，并开启其他可能性。这微观策略主要聚焦于这个问题：对于信念陈述中所用的某个词（或短语），有什么另一个词（或短语）

for one of the words (or phrases) used in the belief statement that means something similar but has more positive implications?

意义相近、却具有更正面的意涵？

Milton Erickson creatively used this micro strategy to apply the pattern of Redefine with a young woman who had developed an "awful inferiority complex" about her looks. She had been in an automobile accident in her early teens that had left her with a small scar on the right side of her mouth. She felt the scar was "disgusting" and had developed a habit of hiding the scar from anyone's sight, continually covering the right side of her face with her hand. Although right-handed, she had learned to eat with her left hand so she could keep her face hidden with her right hand. She could not drive a car because that would leave her face uncovered. She would only go out on dates at night and then only walk on the right side of the boy she was dating so that they would not see the scar. (Collected Papers IV, pp. 465-469)

米尔顿·艾瑞克森创造性地运用这微观策略，对一位对自己外貌发展出"可怕的劣等感"的年轻女子运用了重新定义模式。她少年时遭遇车祸，右嘴角留下一道小疤痕。她觉得那疤痕"恶心"，并养成了用手遮住疤痕、不让任何人看见的习惯。尽管惯用右手，她却学会了用左手吃饭，以便能用右手遮住脸。她无法开车，因为那会让脸暴露。她只在夜晚约会，且只走在约会对象右侧，这样他们就看不到疤痕。（《论文集 IV》，pp. 465-469）

Erickson discovered that the young woman liked to draw and gave her the assignment to investigate and learn everything she could about "beauty marks" and "beauty patches" that some women used. She was then to make a number of sketches showing various beauty marks and their locations on women's faces. She also had her make self-portrait of her own face. When they compared all of the drawings at their next meeting, she was surprised and shocked to discover that her scar looked exactly like some beauty marks. This completely shifted her perception of her scar as something that should be considered "disgusting" to something that could actually enhance the beauty of her face. As a result of this redefinition, Erickson reports that she "rapidly freed herself of the habit of covering her mouth and lost her feeling of inferiority."

艾瑞克森发现这年轻女子喜欢画画，便给她一项任务：去调查并学习一些女性所用的"美人痣"（beauty marks）与"美容贴片"（beauty patches）的一切。她接着要画若干草图，展示各种美人痣及其在女性脸上的位置。他还让她画一张自己的自画像。当他们下次会面比较所有图画时，她惊讶而震惊地发现，她的疤痕看起来与某些美人痣一模一样。这彻底转变了她的感知：从认为疤痕"应当被视为恶心"，转变为它实际上能enhance（增添）她脸庞之美。由于这重新定义，艾瑞克森报告她"迅速摆脱了遮住嘴的习惯，并失去了对自己的劣等感"。

Erickson's use of multiple intelligences, by engaging the young woman's interest in drawing, helped to increase the resonance of redefining the "scar" as a possible "beauty mark."

艾瑞克森对多元智能的运用——通过调动那女子对画画的兴趣——帮助提升了将"疤痕"重新定义为可能的"美人痣"的共鸣。

后果（Consequence）Consequence

The Sleight of Mouth pattern of Consequence involves directing attention to an effect (positive or negative) of the belief statement, or the generalization defined by the belief. The micro strategy behind the pattern of Consequence involves exploring, what is an effect of the belief, or the relationship defined by the belief that can be leveraged for something productive? Is there any positive benefit or by-product of this belief?

后果的语言的魔力模式，涉及将注意力导向信念陈述的某一效果（正向或负向），或由该信念所定义的泛化。后果模式背后的微观策略，探索：该信念的某一效果，或由信念所定义的关系，有什么可被 leverage（善用）于生产性之事？这信念有任何正向的益处或副产品吗？

Milton Erickson would frequently use this pattern as form of pacing and leading with his clients to shift their focus to a more productive direction – as a type of verbal Aikido. A classic example is presented in the book Uncommon Therapy (p. 28). Erickson was on the staff at a psychiatric hospital where there was a young patient who called himself Jesus. He paraded about as the Messiah, wore a sheet draped around him, and attempted to impose Christianity on people. One day Erickson approached him on the hospital grounds and said, "I understand you have had experience as a carpenter." Since Jesus was supposedly a carpenter's son and would have worked in his youth as a carpenter, the patient could only reply that he had. Erickson went on to ask him if he was interested in serving Mankind. Of course, that would be a consequence of being Jesus as well. The young man had to agree. Erickson then mentioned that the Psychology Laboratory needed some bookcases built and used the two consequences as leverage to involve the young man in a project to build them. This gave the young man something productive to do that brought more grounded relationships as

米尔顿·艾瑞克森常将此模式作为与案主同步并引导的一种形式，将其焦点转向更具生产性的方向——一种言语上的合气道。一个经典例子见于《非比寻常的治疗》（p.28）。艾瑞克森曾任职于一所精神病院，院内有一位自称耶稣的年轻病患。他像弥赛亚般游行，身披床单，并试图将基督教强加于人。一天，艾瑞克森在院区接近他，说："我了解你有过做木匠的经验。"既然耶稣据称是木匠之子、年轻时必做过木匠，那病患只能回答他做过。艾瑞克森接着问他是否有兴趣服务人类。当然，那也是身为耶稣的一个后果。那年轻人不得不赞同。艾瑞克森进而提到，心理实验室需要搭建一些书架，并善用这两个后果，让那年轻人参与一个搭建书架的项目。这给了那年轻人一些生产性的事可做，并带来了更接地气的人际关系，

well appreciation from others. According to Erickson, the patient eventually became a productive handyman for the Psychology Laboratory.

以及对他人的 appreciation（欣赏）。据艾瑞克森，那病患最终成了心理实验室一名有产出的勤杂工。

〔图 p029〕

Erickson's use of the pattern of "consequence" is illustrated by this story. If you are "Jesus," then you know carpentry, and you want to serve Mankind. We need help your help to build bookcases! This is your opportunity!

另一个引人入胜的例子见于《凤凰城》（pp. 44-46），艾瑞克森描述了他与一位相信自己将被拉进外太空的年轻男子的工作。艾瑞克森叙述道：

Another intriguing example is presented in the book Phoenix (pp. 44-46) in which Erickson describes his work with a young man who believed he was going to be pulled into outer space. Erickson recounts:

我接到一位年轻人的电话，他告诉我："我在一艘船上当水手，我非常害怕自己会进入轨道（orbit）。"我告诉他，我认为继续在那艘船上工作是不明智的。于是，他找了份在矿场的工作。而他发现在即便身处地下一英里深处，他仍执念于进入轨道的恐惧。

I received a telephone call from a young man who told me, "I'm working on a ship as a seaman and I'm awfully afraid I'm going to go into orbit." I told him I thought it would be inadvisable to continue working on board that ship. So, he got a job working in a mine. And he found that even if he were a mile deep into the earth he was still obsessed with the fear of going into orbit.

那年轻人看了其他几位精神科医师，他们建议住院与电击治疗来处理他的妄想。那男子决定来凤凰城与艾瑞克森工作，艾瑞克森试了几种未成功的介入，例如让那年轻人在街上走时"数自己的脚步并记住街名"作为分心。据艾瑞克森：

The young man saw some other psychiatrists who recommended hospitalization and electro-shock therapy to treat his delusion. The man decided to come to Phoenix to work with Erickson, who tried several unsuccessful interventions, such as having the young man "count his steps as he walked along the street and to memorize the street names" as a distraction. According to Erickson:

那"我要进入轨道了，我要进入轨道了"执念着他……干扰着他。他无法入睡，因为他害怕自己会进入轨道。而我最终意识到，我为他做不了什么，只能安坐下来向他解释："显然，进入轨道是你的命运。现在宇航员进入轨道……他们会再回到地球上。既然你终将进入轨道，何不把它了结（GET IT OVER WITH）？"于是，我让他服用盐丸、带一壶水，并让他每天约十四小时沿着这周围山顶行走，他必须在晚上 10:30 进来报告自己尚未进入轨道。但他睡得很好，正如你会在山顶带着水壶每天走约十四小时那样。最终，他开始对进入轨道有了一点点怀疑。

That, "I'm going to go into orbit, I'm going to go into orbit," obsessed him... interfered with him. He couldn't get very much sleep because he was afraid he was going to go into orbit. And I finally realized I couldn't do anything for him except settle down with him and explain to him, "Now apparently it is your destiny to go into orbit. Now astronauts go into orbit... they come back to earth again. And as long as you are going to go into orbit why not get it OVER with?" So, I had him take salt pills and a canteen of water and I had him walk about fourteen hours a day along the tops of mountains around here, and he had to come in at 10:30 at night to report that he had not yet gone into orbit. But he slept well, as you would walking around on mountain tops with a canteen of water and walking for about fourteen hours a day. And finally, he began to get just a little bit dubious about going into orbit.

艾瑞克森报告，最终，那年轻人的姐姐从加州打电话来，问她弟弟能否去她那里帮她做点零工。艾瑞克森告诉那年轻人他可以去，因为他姐姐住在山附近，若他觉得自己要进入轨道，随时能带着水壶徒步上山顶。

Erickson reports that, eventually, the young man's sister from California called and asked if her brother could come to her place to do some odd jobs for her. Erickson told the young man he could go because his sister lived near mountains, and he would always be able to hike up to the mountains with his canteen if he felt as if he were going to go into orbit.

通过运用"后果"模式，艾瑞克森从问题框架转移到了结果框架，邀请他的病患在山中行走"朝向"他的命运。

〔图 p028〕

By using the pattern of "Consequence" Erickson shifted from a problem frame to an outcome frame, inviting his patient to walk in the mountains "towards" his destiny.

案例反思

Several months later, the young man came back to visit Erickson and told him he now realized that the fear of going into orbit was a crazy delusional idea and thanked Erickson for saving him from the state mental hospital and electro-shock treatment. According to Erickson, the man went on to get a job, later married a woman with a child, and was happy being a husband and a father.

除了对后果的精妙运用，这个案例也illustrate（说明）了若干天才模式。那年轻人"我非常害怕自己会进入轨道"的陈述，是一种"远离—CRASH 循环"的清晰指标。"远离"制造更多 CRASH，而 CRASH 又制造更多"远离"。

Reflections on the Example

艾瑞克森最初在环境与行为层次（在矿场工作、让那人数脚步并记街名）的介入尝试，看似合理，但它们全仍假定一个问题框架，并将注意力导向回避或摆脱那信念及其所产生的 CRASH 状态。这所产生的阻抗，可能产生强化那循环的悖论性效果。

In addition to the brilliant utilization of Consequence, this case illustrates a number of patterns of genius. The young man's statement, "I'm awfully afraid I'm going to go into orbit," is a clear indicator of a type of "away from – CRASH cycle." The "away from" creates more CRASH and the CRASH creates more "away from."

艾瑞克森显然保持坚定，并将这些不令人满意的结果视为"反馈"。在他对语言的魔力后果模式的成功运用中，他从环境与行为层次"向上归类"（chunked up）到了目的与身份层次。艾瑞克森告诉那年轻人，显然他的"命运就是进入轨道"。艾瑞克森接着唤起"宇航员"的身份——他们进入轨道并"再回到地球上"——其意涵是那人也能像宇航员一样回到地球上。艾瑞克森进而强调后果："既然你终将进入轨道，何不把它了结？！" 那将注意力从回避问题，转移到了走向结果。而顺理成章地，沿着山顶行走
