Erickson's first attempted interventions at the levels of environment and behavior (working in a mine, having the man count his steps and memorize street names) seem reasonable, but they all still assume a problem frame and direct attention toward avoiding or getting rid of the belief and the CRASH state it is producing. The resistance this produces can have the paradoxical effect of reinforcing the cycle.

应当让进入轨道的转变发生得更快，因为那年轻人会更靠近天空。这与整天在地下一英里深的矿场工作以试图回避那后果，正好相反。

Erickson clearly stayed determined and took these unsatisfactory results as "feedback." In his successful application of Sleight of Mouth pattern of Consequence, he "chunked up" from the levels of environment and behavior to the levels of purpose and identity. Erickson tells the young man that apparently it is his "destiny to go into orbit." Erickson then invokes the identity of "astronauts" who go into orbit and "come back to earth again" – an implication being that the man could be able to come back to earth again, like the astronauts. Erickson then emphasizes the consequence "as long as you are going to go into orbit why not GET IT OVER WITH?" That shifts attention from avoiding a problem to going toward an outcome. And it follows that walking

每天沿山顶行走十四小时且睡得好，也无疑支持那年轻人转变视角、从 CRASH 状态移动到 COACH 状态、有大量时间与他的创造性潜意识相处，并从元位置反思他的经验。这允许那人自己开始产生自己的怀疑，并很可能对其信念背后的更深层意图有所洞见。

along the tops of mountains should make transition to orbit happen even more quickly since the young man would be closer to the sky. It is the exact opposite of working all day in a mine a mile deep in the earth to try to avoid the consequence.

Walking fourteen hours a day along the tops of mountains and sleeping well also undoubtedly supported the young man to shift his perspective, move from a CRASH state to COACH state, have lots of time with his creative unconscious and to reflect on his experience from meta position. This allows the man himself to begin to have his own doubts and probably get some insight into the deeper intention behind the belief.

另一结果（Another Outcome）Another Outcome

An outcome is the result of some action or condition. Some outcomes are desirable and intended. Others are unwanted or unexpected. When taking action, outcomes (desired or undesired) are generally the key focus of our attention and energy. They are the center point around which we organize our thoughts and behavior. Thus, shifting an outcome can bring about a major change in the way we are thinking and acting. This is the main purpose of the Sleight of Mouth pattern of Another Outcome. Another Outcome essentially involves switching attention to a related but different result than that addressed or implied by a particular belief statement.

结果是某一行动或状态的结果。有些结果是可欲且 intended（有意的）。其他则是 unwanted（不想要的）或意外的。采取行动时，结果（可欲或不可欲）通常是我们注意力与能量的关键焦点。它们是我们据以组织思想与行为的中心点。因此，转移一个结果，能为我们思考与行动的方式带来重大改变。这正是语言的魔力另一结果模式的主要目的。另一结果本质上涉及将注意力切换到某个相关的、却不同于某特定信念陈述所论及或暗示的结果。

Thus, the micro strategy associated Another Outcome is to create a switch in focus from one outcome to a different outcome that shifts attention and energy to a more useful or productive direction. It explores: What other related outcome could be more useful and productive than the one stated or implied by the belief? If the belief focuses on avoiding or going away from a problem, what desired state could it be redirected toward?

因此，与另一结果相关的微观策略，是创造一种从某一结果到另一结果的焦点切换，将注意力与能量转向更有用或更具生产性的方向。它探索：有什么其他相关的、比该信念所陈述或暗示的更有用、更具生产性的结果？若该信念聚焦于回避或远离某个问题，能被重定向向什么所欲的状态？

Since most limiting beliefs are likely stated in an away from form (problem frame), a more useful outcome can be found by refocusing on what to go towards instead (outcome frame). A classic example is that of a man who came to Milton Erickson because of phantom limb pain. He had fallen off a roof and injured his arm so badly that it had to be amputated. He suffered pain in the vanished limb for months and found no relief in various kinds of treatment. Finally, he traveled to Phoenix to visit Erickson. Though skeptical because of the past failures to treat his symptoms, the man wanted to see if Erickson could "use hypnosis to take the pain away." Instead, Erickson said to him, "I think anyone who has suffered as much phantom limb pain as you have deserves at least as much phantom limb pleasure," and made that the initial focus of their hypnotic work. Through this approach, the man was ultimately able to make good progress with respect to the relief of his symptoms.

由于大多数限制性的信念很可能以远离的形式陈述（问题框架），一个更有用的结果，可以通过重新聚焦于要走向（towards）什么、而非远离什么（结果框架）来发现。一个经典例子是：一位因幻肢痛（phantom limb pain）来找米尔顿·艾瑞克森的男子。他从屋顶摔下，手臂重伤到必须截肢。那消失的肢体让他痛了数月，各种治疗都无缓解。最终，他远赴凤凰城见艾瑞克森。尽管因过去治疗失败而怀疑，那人仍想看看艾瑞克森能否"用催眠把疼痛拿走"。艾瑞克森却对他说："我认为，任何承受过如你这般多幻肢痛的人，都至少该配得同等多的幻肢愉悦"，并将此设为他们催眠工作的初始焦点。通过这方式，那人最终在缓解症状上取得了良好进展。

Erickson's response to the man brilliantly shifts the outcome from an away from problem frame (getting rid of the phantom limb pain) to a toward outcome frame (experiencing at least as much pleasure as the pain the man has endured). It creates a major shift in how attention and energy is focused. Like the young man who believed he was going to "go into orbit" in the previous example, trying to suppress or get rid of a symptom can paradoxically bring even more attention and energy to it and can become a "CRASH-Away From" loop. Proposing to create "phantom limb pleasure" introduces the possibility that if you can experience pain, you can then equally experience pleasure. Rather than numbing out, it also brings out the potential for something more positive to come from the loss of the arm

艾瑞克森对那男子的回应，精妙地将结果从远离的问题框架（摆脱幻肢痛）转移到了朝向的结果框架（体验至少与他承受过的疼痛同等的愉悦）。它为注意力与能量的聚焦方式带来了重大转变。如同前例中相信自己会"进入轨道"的年轻人，试图抑制或摆脱一个症状，可能悖论性地带来更多注意力与能量投注其上，并成为一个"CRASH—远离"循环。提议创造"幻肢愉悦"，引入了这样一种可能：若你能体验疼痛，你同样能体验愉悦。它并非使人麻木，而是从失去手臂中带出更正面事物的潜力，

by making it a possible source of pleasure as opposed to only trauma. It is another way of turning the symptom into a solution.

将其变为可能的愉悦之源，而非仅是创伤。这是将症状转化为解方的另一种方式。

向下归类（Chunking Down）Chunking Down

A "chunk" is a part or a piece of something. The term "chunking down" refers to breaking down some object or experience smaller pieces or, conversely, integrating it into something bigger. As I mentioned in the Introduction, moving back and forth between different chunk sizes and levels of thinking is one of the shared patterns of genius. Chunking Down involves dissecting or dividing something into its component pieces. For example, a "car" may be chunked down into "tires," "engine," "brake system," "transmission," etc. Each of those "chunks" may in turn be chunked down into their component parts, and so on. Similarly, sentences can be chunked down into clauses, which can be chunked down into phrases, which can be chunked down into words, which can be chunked down into syllables, which can be chunked down into letters.

一个"块"（chunk）是某物的一个部分或片段。"向下归类"（chunking down）一词，指将某物分解成更小的片段，或反之将其整合进更大的事物。正如我在导论中所言，在不同区块大小与思维层次之间来回移动，是天才的共享模式之一。向下归类涉及将某物剖析或分割为其组成片段。例如，"汽车"可被向下归类为"轮胎""引擎""刹车系统""变速箱"等。那些"块"各自又能被进一步向下归类为其组成部件，依此类推。类似地，句子可被向下归类为从句，从句再向下归类为短语，短语再向下归类为词，词再向下归类为音节，音节再向下归类为字母。

Processes can also be chunked down into sub-processes. That is essentially what I am calling a "strategy." Creativity, for instance, could be chunked into phases such as dreamer, realist and critic. Each of those phases could be chunked down further. Dreamer, for example, could be chunked down into getting into a relaxed and open posture, engaging visual imagination, asking questions about future possibilities, etc.

过程也能被向下归类为子过程。那本质上正是我所谓"策略"所指。例如，创造力可被归类为梦想家、现实主义者与批判者等阶段。那些阶段各自又能被进一步向下归类。例如，梦想家可被向下归类为进入放松而开放的姿态、调动视觉想象、询问关于未来可能性的问题，等等。

Chunking down can change the meaning or impact of something in the sense that what is true for the whole is not necessarily true for its parts. I cannot fit a whole watermelon in my mouth, for example, but I could easily fit small chunks of it. Similarly, a problem that appears overwhelming may be chunked down into a series of smaller more manageable problems.

向下归类能改变某物的意义或影响，因为对整体为真的，对其部分未必为真。例如，我无法将整个西瓜放进嘴里，却能轻松放进它的一小块。类似地，一个看似压倒性的问题，可被向下归类为一系列更小、更易管理的难题。

The micro strategy employed by the Sleight of Mouth pattern of Chunk Down involves breaking the elements of the belief into small enough pieces that it changes the relationship defined by the belief. It is a function of asking the questions, "What smaller elements or chunks are implied by the belief but have a richer or more positive relationship than the ones stated in the belief? What might be true or not true for the part that is different from the larger whole."

语言的魔力向下归类模式所运用的微观策略，涉及将信念的要素分解到足够小的片段，以改变该信念所定义的关系。它是提出以下问题所发挥的功能："该信念暗示了哪些更小的要素或块，却具有比信念中所陈述者更丰富或更正向的关系？对该部分为真、而对更大的整体为假的，可能是什么？"

Milton Erickson demonstrated a good example of this application of chunking down when working with a man who was convinced that he was "unhypnotizable." He said to Erickson, "You will never put me into a trance. Many others have tried, and no one succeeded." Erickson's response was, "You are absolutely right. I cannot put YOU into trance... just your arm." Erickson then reached out and lifted the arm of the slightly confused man and held it up while looking meaningfully into the man's eyes. When he released the arm, it remained suspended without the man intentionally trying to keep it raised. Erickson then suggested to the arm that it "continue floating upward" which, to the man's surprise, it did. This was the beginning of a productive series of sessions where the man did eventually learn to experience other types of hypnotic phenomena that were very helpful for some of the personal challenges he was facing.

米尔顿·艾瑞克森与一位坚信自己"无法被催眠"的男子工作时，示范了这向下归类应用的绝佳例子。他对艾瑞克森说："你永远无法让我进入 trance（催眠恍惚）。许多人试过，没人成功。"艾瑞克森回应道："你说得完全对。我无法把你放进 trance……只能是你的手臂。"艾瑞克森接着伸出手，抬起那略显困惑男子的手臂，握着它举高，同时意味深长地注视他的眼睛。当他放开手臂，它悬在半空，无需那男子有意保持举起。艾瑞克森进而向那手臂建议"继续向上漂浮"，令那男子惊讶的是，它真的漂上去了。这是一系列富有成效的会谈的开端，那男子最终确实学会了体验其他类型的催眠现象，这对他所面临的一些个人挑战颇有帮助。

By chunking down from the identity level ("YOU") to a part of the body (the arm) and a behavior (lifting/floating) Erickson was able to bypass resistance coming from a belief about what "hypnosis" was supposed to be and like. Erickson no doubt also realized that at least some part of the person

通过从身份层次（"你"）向下归类到身体的一部分（手臂）与一个行为（抬起/漂浮），艾瑞克森得以绕过来自关于"催眠"应当如何的某种信念的阻抗。艾瑞克森无疑也意识到，既然那男子起初就来到了这里，那人的至少某一部分

was actually interested in having the experience since the man had come there to begin with. Erickson's Chunk Down gave him the opportunity to create resonance and rapport with that part without threatening the parts affected by the limiting belief. His use of pacing and leading then allowed him to encourage the part of the man who wanted to experience hypnotic phenomena to safely express itself.

实际上对拥有那经验感兴趣。艾瑞克森的向下归类，给了他与那部分建立共鸣与亲和的机会，而不威胁到受那限制性信念影响的部分。他对先跟后带的运用，进而让他得以鼓励那想要体验催眠现象的男子部分，安全地表达自己。

向上归类（Chunking Up）Chunking Up

Chunking Up involves moving to a larger, broader or more abstract level of generalization or classification. For example, cars, trains, boats or airplanes could all be chunked up to "forms of transportation," which could be chunked up even further to "types of movement." Processes, of course, can also be chunked up. Jogging, swimming, rowing or biking could be chunked up to "types of exercise," which can be chunked up to "healthy activities," etc.

向上归类涉及移动到更大、更广或更抽象的泛化或分类层次。例如，汽车、火车、船只或飞机，都能被向上归类为"交通形式"，进而再向上归类为"运动类型"。过程当然也能向上归类。慢跑、游泳、划船或骑车，能被向上归类为"运动类型"，再向上归类为"健康活动"，等等。

The Sleight of Mouth pattern of Chunking Up involves generalizing an element of a belief statement to a larger classification or grouping that shifts the impact or implications of the belief. Effectively using the Sleight of Mouth pattern of Chunk Up entails generalizing an element of a statement or judgment to a larger classification or group in order to create a new or enriched perception of the statement or judgment being expressed. One assumption of this pattern is that what is true for the part is not necessarily true for the larger whole, or for the other parts of that same whole. If a particular spelling is inaccurate, for instance, it does not mean that the idea expressed by that spelling is also inaccurate or that all of the other words are also spelled wrong. If a tire is flat on a car, it does not mean the steering wheel also has a problem or that the whole car is also damaged.

语言的魔力向上归类模式，涉及将信念陈述的一个要素泛化到一个更大的分类或群组，以转变该信念的影响或意涵。有效运用语言的魔力向上归类模式，须将陈述或判断的一个要素泛化到更大的分类或群组，以对所述陈述或判断创造一种新的或 enriched（丰富了的）感知。这模式的一个预设是：对部分为真的，对更大的整体、或对同一整体中的其他部分，未必为真。 例如，某个拼写不准确，并不意味着那拼写所表达的观念也不准确，或所有其他词也都拼错。若汽车一只轮胎瘪了，并不意味着方向盘也有问题，或整辆车也受损。

Thus, the micro strategy that drives the Sleight of Mouth pattern of Chunking Up is the exploration of the questions, "What larger classification of the elements making up the belief can lead to a richer or more productive results? What might be true or not true for the larger classification that is different from the parts which make it up?"

因此，驱动语言的魔力向上归类模式的微观策略，是探索这些问题："构成该信念的要素中，有什么更大的分类能带来更丰富或更具生产性的结果？对那更大的分类为真、而与其组成部分不同的，可能是什么？"

As usual, Milton Erickson provides a good example of how this micro strategy can help bring about a profound and positive transformation. In Uncommon Therapy (pp. 115-119) he describes the case of an obese 21-year-old old woman who came to his office and said, "My father is dead, my mother is dead, my sister is dead, and that is all that's left for me." The woman's parents had been severe alcoholics and were quite cruel to her and her sister. Her sister had a birth defect and died of kidney disease. After the death of all her family members she had taken a job scrubbing floors, living alone in an old shack. She felt hideous and ugly, claiming, "I am four feet ten inches. I weigh between two hundred and fifty and two hundred and sixty pounds. I am just a plain, fat slob. Nobody would ever look at me except with disgust."

一如往常，米尔顿·艾瑞克森提供了一个好例子，说明这微观策略如何帮助带来深刻而正向的转化。在《非比寻常的治疗》（pp. 115-119）中，他描述了这样一例：一位二十一岁的肥胖女子来到他的办公室，说："我父亲死了，我母亲死了，我姐姐死了，而那就是留给我的全部。"那女子的父母都是严重的酒鬼，对她与姐姐相当残酷。她姐姐有先天缺陷，死于肾病。在所有家人去世后，她找了份擦洗地板的工作，独自住在一间老旧棚屋里。她觉得自己丑陋，声称："我身高四英尺十英寸。体重在二百五十到二百六十磅之间。我只是个 plain（平常的）、fat（胖的）废物（slob）。除了厌恶，没人会看我一眼。"

As part of her treatment, Erickson instructed her to go to the library and look through anthropology books to find out about the "hideous kinds of women men will marry," and to read about the way people intentionally disfigure themselves with scars, tattoos, piercings, etc. He then told her to spend the next week sitting in the busiest section of the city looking at the "peculiar shapes and faces of the things men will marry" as well as those that women marry. According to Erickson, after that assignment, "She stated with simple wonderment that she had actually seen women almost as homely as she was who wore wedding rings. She had seen men and women

作为治疗的一部分，艾瑞克森指示她去图书馆，翻阅人类学书籍，去了解"男人会娶的、骇人的各类女子"，并阅读人们如何刻意以疤痕、刺青、穿孔等 self-disfigure（自我毁容）自己。他接着告诉她，下一周要坐在城市最繁忙的地段，去看"男人会娶的奇特形状与面孔"，以及女人会娶的那些。据艾瑞克森，在那项作业之后，"她带着单纯的惊奇说，她确实看见了几乎与她一样其貌不扬、却戴着婚戒的女子。她看见过

who seemed to be man and wife, both of whom were hideously fat and clumsy." He told her that she was "beginning to learn something."

看似夫妻的男女，两人都骇人地肥胖且笨拙。"他告诉她，她"开始学到点什么了"。

Her next assignment was to go to the library and read all the books she could on the history of cosmetology and to "discover what constituted desirable beauty to the human eye." Erickson then told her to return to the library and "look through books dealing with human customs, dress, and appearance – to find something depicted that was at least five hundred years old and still looked pretty."

她的下一项作业，是去图书馆读她能找到的关于化妆史的所有书，并"发现对人眼而言什么构成了可欲的美"。艾瑞克森接着告诉她回到图书馆，"翻阅涉及人类习俗、衣着与外貌的书籍——找一样 depicted（描绘的）至少有五百年历史、且看起来仍漂亮的东西。"

After she had completed these assignments, Erickson told her that for two weeks she was to go into one women's clothing store after another and ask the clerks for advice about what she ought to wear and to "become curious about what she might look like if she weighed 150 lbs." At the next session, the woman asked Erickson if she could be permitted to "see what she could do about herself"

在她完成这些作业后，艾瑞克森告诉她，接下来两周，她要走进一家又一家女装店，向店员征求她该穿什么的 advice（建议），并"对她若重达 150 磅会是什么样子变得好奇"。在下次会谈时，那女子问艾瑞克森，是否允许她"看看自己能对自身做些什么"。

Within one year, she weighed 150 lbs. In time she enrolled in and graduated from the university, got a job as a fashion artist, got her teeth fixed and got engaged. According to Erickson, fifteen years later she was still married with three children.

一年内，她体重降至 150 磅。不久她进入大学、毕业，找了份时装艺术家的工作，整了牙，并订了婚。据艾瑞克森，十五年后她仍已婚，有三个孩子。

Reflections on the Example

案例反思

The woman's statement, "I am just a plain, fat slob. Nobody would ever look at me except with disgust," is clearly negative identity judgment couched within a problem frame. Erickson's assignments were all Chunking Up beyond her individual identity in some ways – to types of "women" and "men," "shapes and faces," criteria for "desirable beauty" and "human customs, dress and appearance." The purpose Erickson's instruction to "find something depicted that was at least five hundred years old and still looked pretty," for instance, was clearly to bring attention to deeper, archetypal organizing principles that functioned within these larger classifications. This and the other assignments created a shift from a problem frame to an outcome frame and helped neutralize the woman's negative identity judgments.

那女子"我只是个平常的、胖的废物。除了厌恶，没人会看我一眼"的陈述，显然是一个包裹在问题框架中的负向身份判断。艾瑞克森的各项作业，全都是向上归类、超越她个体身份的某些方式——到"女子"与"男子"的类型、"形状与面孔"、"可欲之美"的标准以及"人类习俗、衣着与外貌"。例如，艾瑞克森指示"找一样至少有五百年历史、且看起来仍漂亮的东西"，其目的显然是将注意力带到这些更大分类中运作的、更深的原型性（archetypal）组织原则。这项作业及其他作业，创造了从问题框架到结果框架的转变，并帮助中和了那女子的负向身份判断。

In addition to Chunking Up, Erickson was engaging the patterns of genius of taking multiple perspectives and entertaining different points of view as well as thinking systemically and focusing on the "deep structure" beyond the "surface structure." The result was that the woman began to view herself within the context of the great diversity and possibilities of a system much bigger than the confines of her dysfunctional and destructive family. When we learn more about what is possible within the whole, we discover more about what is possible for the individual parts. This can be one of the positive effects of chunking up. When we recognize that we are part of a bigger holon, more possibilities become available to us.

除了向上归类，艾瑞克森还调动了天才模式中的"采取多重视角与容纳不同观点"，以及"以系统性思维运作并聚焦于'表层结构'之下的'深层结构'"。其结果，是那女子开始在她那远比 dysfunctional（功能失调）且 destructive（破坏性）的家庭所限更为庞大的、一个系统的巨大多样性与可能性之中，来看待自己。当我们学到关于整体中可能之事的更多，我们便发现了关于个体部分可能之事的更多。这可以是向上归类的一项正向效果。当我们认识到自己是更大全息子的一部分时，更多可能性便为我们所用。

类比（Analogy）Analogy

Analogies can be defined as, "the transference of the relation between one set of objects to another set for the purpose of brief explanation" (Such as, "the coordination of the people in the team was as smooth as a well-orchestrated symphony"). Analogies are intimately related to metaphors, which are defined as, "a figure of speech in which a word or phrase literally denoting one kind of object or idea is used in place of another to suggest a likeness or similarity between them (as in 'drowning in money')." Analogies and metaphors involve thinking about one situation or phenomena in terms of something else, as is often done in stories, fairy tales and parables.

类比可被定义为："为简要说明而将一组对象间的关系，转移到另一组对象之上"（例如"团队成员间的协调，顺畅如一场精心编排的交响乐"）。类比与隐喻密切相关；隐喻被定义为："一种修辞，其中一个字面意指某类对象或观念的语词或短语，被用以替代另一对象，以暗示两者间的相似或类似（如'淹没在金钱里'）"。类比与隐喻，涉及以他物之 terms（角度）来思考某一情境或现象，正如故事、童话与寓言中常做的那样。

Rather than Chunking Up or Chunking Down, finding Analogies can be considered a type of Chunking Laterally. Chunking Laterally involves identifying phenomena, at a similar level, that share common features; for instance, “driving a car” could be likened to “riding a horse,” “peddling a bicycle” or “sailing a boat.” According to anthropologist, communication specialist and systems theory pioneer Gregory Bateson, at some level, “Everything is a metaphor for everything else.” Metaphorical thinking is one of our most important forms of intelligence and is responsible for much or our creativity. We generate these metaphors and analogies by chunking laterally to find similarities between one system and another.

与其向上或向下归类，寻找类比可被视为一种横向归类（Chunking Laterally）。横向归类涉及辨识在相似层次、共享共同特征的现象；例如"开车"可类比于"骑马""蹬自行车"或"驾船"。据人类学家、沟通专家与系统理论先驱格雷戈里·贝特森，在某一层面上，"万物互为隐喻"。隐喻性思维是我们最重要的智能形式之一，并为我们的创造力负大部分责任。我们通过横向归类，在某一系统与他系统间寻找相似，来生成这些隐喻与类比。

The effective application of the Sleight of Mouth pattern of Analogy engages chunking laterally to find a metaphor or analogy for some aspect of a belief statement that opens a new perspective or shifts the implications of the belief to something more productive. The micro strategy associated with the patterns of Analogy explores what is some other relationship which is analogous to that defined by the belief statement (a metaphor for some aspect of the belief), but which has more positive or productive implications or can stimulate new possibilities?

语言的魔力类比模式的有效运用，调动横向归类，为信念陈述的某一方面找到一个隐喻或类比，开启新的视角，或将信念的意涵转向更具生产性的方向。与类比模式相关的微观策略，探索：有什么其他关系，类比于该信念陈述所定义的（信念某方面的隐喻），却具有更正向或更具生产性的意涵，或能刺激新的可能性？

〔图 p023〕

Milton Erickson was constantly applying this type of thinking process with his patients. As an example, if Erickson was dealing with a married couple who had a conflict over sexual relations, he might approach the problem metaphorically. He would choose some aspect of their lives that was analogous to sexual relations and suggest a way to change that as a means of influencing their sexual behavior. He might, for example, talk to them about having dinner together and ask them about their preferences. He might learn that the wife liked to “linger over hors d'oeuvres” before dinner, while the husband was a “meat and potatoes” man and preferred to dive right into the main course. Or the wife might prefer a quiet and leisurely dinner, while the husband wanted the finish the meal quickly and move on to other things. Erickson would then end the conversation with the instruction that the couple discuss and co-arrange a series of dinners that were satisfactory

成功后，更令人满意的晚餐，会自发地引领那对夫妻走向更相互满意的性关系。

to both of them. When successful, the more satisfying dinners would spontaneously lead the couple to more mutually satisfying sexual relations.

较不明显的类比与隐喻，在配以恰当技巧时也同样有用。一个引人入胜的例子由米尔顿·艾瑞克森描述（《非比寻常的治疗》，pp. 301-306）：一位做花匠的男子，脸侧长了一个肿块，结果竟是恶性肿瘤。在被宣告只剩一个月可活后，那男子陷入苦恼，并产生剧痛。麻醉剂几乎无缓解，且他对作为治疗所给予的过量药物产生了毒性反应。因脸部受损，那男子还做了气管切开，只能透过书写沟通。一位亲属请艾瑞克森为他做催眠止痛。那男子非常怀疑，连提及"催眠"一词都不喜欢。得知他是花匠，艾瑞克森用一个番茄植株的类比，开始与那男子谈论观看番茄植株生长的乐趣：

Analogies and metaphors that are less obvious can also be quite useful, when applied with the appropriate skill. A fascinating example is described by Milton Erickson (Uncommon Therapy, pp. 301-306) in which a man who was a florist developed a growth on the side of his face that turned out to be a malignancy. After being told he had a month to live, the man became distressed and developed severe pain. Narcotics were giving him little relief, and he had developed toxic reactions to the excessive medication given as treatment. The man had also had a tracheotomy because of the damage to his face and could only communicate through writing. A relative asked Erickson to do hypnosis with him for the pain. The man was very skeptical and disliked even the mention of the word “hypnosis.” Knowing that he was a florist, Erickson, used an analogy to a tomato plant and began talking to the man about the joys of watching tomato plants grow:

说到番茄植株，它长得好慢。你看不见它长，听不见它长，但它确实在长——茎上最先那些小叶般的东西，茎上细密的茸毛。那些茸毛叶子上也有，就像根上的纤毛；若你能想象一株植物有感觉，它们必让番茄植株感觉很舒服、很安逸，然后，你看不见它在长，感觉不到它在长，但另一片叶子出现在那小番茄茎上，然后又是一片。或许番茄植株在生长时确实感到舒服而安宁。它每天生长、生长、生长，看着一株植物生长却看不见、感觉不到它的生长，只知道一切都在变好，那小番茄植株又添了一片叶、再一片、又一条枝，它正舒适地向各个方向生长。

Speaking of the tomato plant, it grows so slowly. You cannot see it grow, you cannot hear it grow, but grow it does — the first little leaflike things on the stalk, the fine little hairs on the stem. Those hairs are on the leaves too, like the cilia on the roots; they must make the tomato plant feel very good, very comfortable if you can think of a plant as feeling, and then, you can't see it growing, you can't feel it growing, but another leaf appears on that little tomato stalk and then another. Maybe the tomato plant does feel comfortable and peaceful as it grows. Each day it grows and grows and grows, it's so comfortable to watch a plant grow and not see its growth, not feel it, but just know that all is getting better for that little tomato plant that is adding yet another leaf and still another and a branch, and it is growing comfortably in all directions.

艾瑞克森以这种方式说了一阵。会谈后，那花匠的身心状况显著改善。那男子不仅对催眠的价值不再怀疑，还热情地请求艾瑞克森再来。他比预测多活了四倍的时间。

Erickson went on in this fashion for a while. Following the session, the florist's mental and physical condition improved markedly. Not only was the man no longer skeptical about the value of hypnosis, but he also enthusiastically requested Erickson to return. He lived four times longer than predicted.

类比、隐喻与横向归类，也是天才的关键模式之一，并自身构成了一整个智能领域。它们帮助我们找到其他类型智能所无法找到的模式，并生成可能性。在此例中，艾瑞克森从未明确对那男子说"你就像番茄植株"。这类比做得更 subtle（微妙），且如同所有语言的魔力模式的有效运用，涉及从问题框架（远离疾病与疼痛）到结果框架（朝向生长中的平和与舒适）的转变。当然，其效力也取决于它所产生的与倾听者的共鸣程度，而这又在很大程度上依赖倾听者与说话者间的亲和程度。若艾瑞克森对一位珠宝匠或木匠用同一类比，极可能不会有同样的 positive（正向）效果。

Analogies, metaphors and chunking laterally are also one of the key Patterns of Genius and constitute a whole area of intelligence in and of themselves. They help us to find patterns and generate possibilities that other types of intelligence are unable to do. In this example, Erickson never explicitly said to the man, “You are like the tomato plant.” The analogy is made more subtly and, as with the effective use of all Sleight of Mouth patterns, involves a shift from a problem frame (away from illness and pain) to an outcome frame (toward growing peace and comfort). Of course, its effectiveness is also dependent on the degree of resonance it creates with the listener, which depends a great deal on the listener's level of rapport with the speaker. Had Erickson used the same analogy with a jeweler or a carpenter, it would most likely not have had the same positive effect.

反例（Counter Example）Counter Example

A Counter Example is an example, experience or piece of information that challenges the universality of a particular belief or generalization about the world. Counter examples are essentially exceptions to a rule, i.e.: an example or experience that does not fit the generalization defined by a belief statement.

反例，是挑战某一关于世界的特定信念或泛化之普遍性的例子、经验或信息。反例本质上是规则的例外，即：不符合某信念陈述所定义之泛化的例子或经验。

Finding counter examples involves checking the comprehensiveness of the equivalence or cause-effect asserted or implied by the belief statement to see if there are any exceptions to that rule. The micro strategy involved in applying the Sleight of Mouth pattern of Counter Example explores what is an example or experience that is an exception to the premise or rule defined by the belief/generalization that can bring more nuance and flexibility?

寻找反例，涉及检验该信念陈述所断言或暗示的等价或因果的全面性，看是否有任何该规则的例外。运用语言的魔力反例模式的微观策略，探索：有什么例子或经验，是该信念/泛化所定义的前提或规则的例外，能带来更多细微差别与灵活性？

Milton Erickson presents a good example of this (Uncommon Therapy, pp. 197-198) in his intervention with a fourteen-year-old girl who had developed the belief that her feet were much too large. For three months the girl had been becoming more and more obsessed with her feet and progressively more withdrawn, not wanting to go to school or to church or to be seen on the street. The girl would not allow the subject of her feet to be discussed, and she would not go to see a doctor. No amount of reassurance by her mother had any influence, and the girl was becoming increasingly reclusive. Erickson reports:

米尔顿·艾瑞克森呈现了一个好例子（《非比寻常的治疗》，pp. 197-198）：他介入一位十四岁女孩，她发展出自己脚太大的信念。三个月来，那女孩对她双脚的执念越来越强，并愈发退缩，不愿上学、不愿去教堂、不愿在街上被人看见。那女孩不允许讨论她脚的话题，也不愿去看医生。母亲再多的 reassurance（安慰）都无影响，女孩愈发与世隔绝。艾瑞克森报告：

I arranged with the mother to visit the home on the following day under false pretenses. The girl would be told that I was coming to examine the mother to see if she had the flu... When I arrived at the home, the mother was in bed. I did a careful physical examination of her, listening to her chest, examining her throat, and so on. The girl was present. I sent her for a towel, and I asked that she stand beside me in case I needed something... As I finished my examination of the mother, I maneuvered the girl into a position directly behind me. I was sitting on the bed talking to the mother, and I got up slowly and carefully and then stepped back awkwardly. I put my heel down squarely on the girl's toes. The girl, of course, squawked with pain.

我与母亲安排次日以虚假借口家访。女孩会被告知我是来给母亲做检查、看她是否染上流感……我到家时，母亲躺在床上。我对她做了仔细的体格检查，听她的胸、查她的喉咙，等等。女孩在场。我差她去拿毛巾，并请她站我身旁，以防我需要什么……当我结束对母亲的检查，我将女孩 maneuver（挪）到正对我身后的位置。我坐在床上与母亲说话，然后缓慢而小心地起身，接着笨拙地后退一步。我将脚跟正正踩在女孩的脚趾上。女孩自然痛得大叫。

I turned on her and in a tone of absolute fury said, “If you would grow those things large enough for a man to see, I wouldn't be in this sort of situation.” The girl looked at me, puzzled, while I wrote out a prescription and called the drugstore. That day the girl asked her mother if she could go out to a show, which she hadn't done in months. She went to school and church, and that was the end of a pattern of three months' reclusiveness.

我转向她，以绝对愤怒的语气说："要是你把这些东西长到让一个男人能看见，我就不会陷进这种境地了。"女孩 puzzled（困惑地）看着我，而我开了张处方、打电话给药房。那天女孩问母亲，她能否出去看场电影——她已数月没做过了。她去上学、去教堂，而那长达三个月的与世隔绝模式，就此结束。

Erickson's admonishment that, “If you would grow those things large enough for a man to see, I wouldn't be in this sort of situation,” created a direct counter example to the girl's belief that her feet were “too big.” That, combined with the fact that he had stepped on her toes because her feet weren't “large enough for a man to see,” was enough to effectively challenge her limiting belief. As Erickson points out, there was no further trouble about the feet.

艾瑞克森那句"要是你把这些东西长到让一个男人能看见，我就不会陷进这种境地了"，对女孩"脚太大"的信念创造了一个直接的反例。那一点，加上他因她的脚"不够大到让男人看见"而踩到她脚趾的事实，足以有效地挑战她的限制性信念。正如艾瑞克森指出，此后脚部再无 trouble（麻烦）。

It is worth noting that (in terms of the various patterns of genius), in addition to adding another perspective (his as a “man”), Erickson was also intentionally making a link between his words and the physical sensations (the pain) in the girl's foot, which makes his counter example all the more convincing.

值得注意的是（就各类天才模式而言），除了增添另一视角（他作为"男人"的视角），艾瑞克森还刻意将他的言语与女孩脚上的身体感觉（疼痛）相联结，这使他的反例更有说服力。

准则层级（Hierarchy of Criteria）Hierarchy of Criteria

Hierarchy of Criteria relate to the degree of importance or meaning that people attach to various actions and experiences. A person's hierarchy of criteria is essentially the order of priorities that person applies in order to act in the world. An example of a Hierarchy of Criteria would be a person who values “health” more than “financial success.” Such a person would tend

准则层级，关乎人们赋予各类行动与经验的重要性或意义程度。一个人的准则层级，本质上是那人为在世上行动所运用的优先顺序。准则层级的一个例子，是某人将"健康"看得比"财务成功"更重。这样的人倾向于

to put his or her health “first.” This person would probably structure his or her life more around activities that promote personal vitality and physical sustainability rather than professional opportunities. A person whose Hierarchy of Criteria placed “financial success” over “health” would have a different lifestyle. He or she might sacrifice health and physical well-being in order to “get ahead” monetarily.

将他的健康"放第一"。他或她可能会将生活更多围绕促进个人活力与身体可持续性的活动来组织，而非职业机会。一个将"财务成功"置于"健康"之上的准则层级的人，会有不同的生活方式。他或她可能为"出人头地"而在金钱上牺牲健康与身体安康。

The Sleight of Mouth pattern of Hierarchy of Criteria involves reevaluating (or reinforcing) a belief statement according to an effect or criterion that is potentially more important and impactful than the one addressed by the belief statement. The effective use of the Sleight of Mouth pattern of Hierarchy of Criteria is about shifting attention to more profound and impactful criteria. The micro strategy driving it focuses on what is a potentially more important or impactful criterion that has not yet been considered which could bring a significant change in perceptions or priorities?

语言的魔力准则层级模式，涉及根据一个可能比该信念陈述所论及者更重要、更有影响力的效果或准则，来重新评估（或强化）该信念陈述。语言的魔力准则层级模式的有效运用，是关于将注意力转向更深刻、更有影响力的准则。驱动它的微观策略，聚焦于：有什么尚未被考量、可能带来感知或优先级之重大改变的、潜在更重要或更有影响力的准则？

Not surprisingly, the work of Milton Erickson provides an excellent example of how to apply this micro strategy, combined with multiple intelligences, to create a significant life shift for one of his patients. In Teaching Seminar (pp. 188-189) Erickson recounts the case of a 52-year-old woman who had cancer and was in great pain. Her doctor told Erickson, “I can give her a double shot of morphine and Demerol and Percodan, all at the same time, plus nine grains of sodium amytal. It doesn't even make her drowsy, she suffers so much pain.” The doctor sent her to Erickson by ambulance to see if he could do anything to help. When she entered the room she said to Erickson (who was 70 at the time), “Sonny, do you really think that your hypnotic words will so alter my body when powerful chemicals have no effect on it?” According to Erickson:

毫不意外，米尔顿·艾瑞克森的工作提供了一个绝佳例子，说明如何结合多元智能运用这微观策略，为其一位病患创造重大的生命转变。在《教学研讨》（Teaching Seminar，pp. 188-189）中，艾瑞克森叙述了一位五十二岁、患癌且剧痛的女子的案例。她的医生告诉艾瑞克森："我能给她双倍剂量的吗啡、地美罗（Demerol）与镇痛新（Percodan），同时再加九格（grains）的阿米妥钠（sodium amytal）。那甚至让她不困，她痛得这么厉害。"医生用救护车将她送来，看艾瑞克森能否帮上忙。当她进屋时，对艾瑞克森（当时七十岁）说："小子，你真以为你那些催眠的词语，能在强效化学品都对它无效时，如此改变我的身体吗？"据艾瑞克森：

I said, “Madam, as I look at your eyes, the pupils are dilating and contracting steadily, and your facial muscles are quivering. So, I know you are suffering constant pain — constant, stabbing, pulsating pain. I can see it with my eyes. Now tell me, Madam, if you saw a lean, hungry tiger in the next room, slowly walking into the room and eyeing you hungrily and licking its chops, how much pain would you feel?” She said, “I wouldn't feel any under those circumstances. Well, my goodness. I don't feel any pain now. May I take that tiger back to the hospital with me?” I said, “Certainly, but I will have to tell your doctor.” She said, “But don't tell the nurses. I want to have some fun with the nurses. Every time they ask me if I am having pain, I am going to tell them, look under the bed. If the tiger is still there, I haven't got a bit of pain.”

我说："女士，当我看着你的眼睛，瞳孔稳定地放大又收缩，你的面部肌肉在颤抖。所以，我知道你正承受持续的疼痛——持续、刺痛、搏动性的疼痛。我用眼睛能看见。那么告诉我，女士，若你看见隔壁房间有一只 lean（精瘦的）、饥饿的老虎，缓缓走进房间、饥渴地盯着你、舔着它的嘴，你会感觉到多少疼痛？"她说："那种情况下我一丝疼痛也不会有。哎呀，我现在一丝疼痛也没有了。我能把那只老虎带回医院吗？"我说："当然，但我得告诉你医生。"她说："但别告诉护士。我想拿护士寻点开心。每次她们问我是否疼痛，我就告诉她们，往床下看。如果老虎还在那，我就一点痛也没有。"

In this example, the inner state elicited by the criterion of “survival” interrupted and overrode the experience of pain (and the CRASH state it triggered) more than the “powerful chemicals” that had been ineffective. Erickson did not have to verbally name the deeper criterion of “survival.” His question, “if you saw a lean, hungry tiger in the next room, slowly walking into the room and eyeing you hungrily and licking its chops, how much pain would you feel?” implicitly establishes a hierarchy of survival over pain. Clearly, the visual image Erickson conjured through his words was as important as the “words” themselves in creating the shift in criteria.

在此例中，由"生存"准则所唤起的内在状态，打断并盖过了疼痛（及其所触发的 CRASH 状态）的体验，其程度超过了那些无效的"强效化学品"。艾瑞克森无需言语点出"生存"这一更深层准则。他的问题"若你看见隔壁房间有一只精瘦、饥饿的老虎……你会感觉到多少疼痛？"隐含地确立了生存高于疼痛的层级。显然，艾瑞克森用言语召唤出的视觉意象，与"词语"本身同样重要，才创造了准则上的转变。

Erickson also “paced” the woman before “leading” by acknowledging that he could “see” how much pain she was experiencing. This serves the purpose of helping to get the necessary level of rapport for his words to have a deep enough effect.

艾瑞克森也先"跟"那女子——承认他能"看见"她正经验多少疼痛——再"带"。这服务於帮助获得其词语产生足够深层影响所需的亲和程度。

应用于自身（Apply to Self）Apply to Self

Applying a belief statement to itself involves evaluating the belief according to the generalization or criteria defined by that statement. For example, if a person expresses a belief such as, “You cannot trust words,” the belief could be applied to itself by asking, “Since you cannot trust words, how can you trust the words you just said?” As another example, if a person says, “It is wrong to make generalizations,” one could respond, “Then, are you sure that you are not wrong to make that generalization?”

将一个信念陈述应用于其自身，涉及根据该陈述所定义的泛化或准则，来评估该信念。例如，若一个人表达"你无法信任词语"这样的信念，可将其应用于自身而问："既然你无法信任词语，你如何能信任你刚说的那些词？"另一个例子：若一个人说"做泛化是错的"，可回应："那么，你确定你做那个泛化本身没有错吗？"

A key purpose of applying a belief or generalization to itself is to discover whether the belief (or the belief holder) is a congruent example of the generalization and criteria expressed by the belief statement or not. Frequently, applying a belief to itself prompts us to reconsider the validity or universality of the belief. As with all Sleight of Mouth patterns, the purpose of this is to prompt a new or different perspective or to introduce more flexibility.

将信念或泛化应用于自身的一个关键目的，是发现该信念（或信念持有者）是否是其陈述所表达之泛化与准则的一个 congruent（一致的）例子。频繁地，将信念应用于自身，促使我们重新考量该信念的有效性或普遍性。如同所有语言的魔力模式，此目的在於引发新的或不同的视角，或引入更多灵活性。

The micro strategy supporting the pattern of Apply to Self involves exploring questions such as: What happens when you view the belief statement or its consequences through the filter of belief or its criteria? Can the belief statement itself be challenged or confirmed according to the generalization or criteria defined by the belief? Is the statement (or ongoing behavior/intent of the speaker) an example of the belief or its criteria?

支撑"应用于自身"模式的微观策略，涉及探索此类问题：当你透过该信念或其准则的滤镜来看这信念陈述或其后果时，发生了什么？该信念陈述本身，能否根据该信念所定义的泛化或准则被挑战或确认？这陈述（或说话者持续的行为/意图）是该信念或其准则的一个例子吗？

Apply to Self can be used to leverage a belief or belief statement to change itself. That is, the generalization can be leveraged to actually bring about change in the generalization. Milton Erickson describes a, typically, creative example of this in his work with an eight-year-old boy who had become

应用于自身，可被用来 leverage（善用）一个信念或信念陈述以改变其自身。亦即，那泛化可被 leverage 来实际促成该泛化的改变。米尔顿·艾瑞克森在与一位八岁男孩的工作（男孩在其单亲母亲开始与其他男人约会后出现

a troublemaker after his mother, a single parent, started dating other men (Uncommon Therapy, pp. 218-221). He began destroying things and defying his mother and his teachers. His mother's usual disciplinary methods were ineffective and instead tended to escalate the boy's rebellious behavior. At a loss about what to do, the mother brought the boy to see Erickson. The boy boastfully declared that Erickson could not do anything to stop him, saying “I can do whatever I please” and that he would “stomp” anyone who “got in his way.” Erickson responded that it was unnecessary for him to do anything to change the boy's behavior because the boy would change his behavior “all by himself.”

行为问题，《非比寻常的治疗》，pp. 218-221）中，描述了一个典型的创造性例子。男孩开始毁坏东西、违抗母亲与老师。母亲惯用的管束方法无效，反而倾向于 escalation（升级）男孩的反叛行为。不知所措，母亲带男孩来见艾瑞克森。男孩 boastfully（夸耀地）宣称艾瑞克森无法做任何事阻止他，说"我爱干什么就干什么"，并会"跺"（stomp）任何"挡他路"的人。艾瑞克森回应说，他无需做任何事来改变男孩的行为，因为男孩会"全靠自己"改变。

Erickson sent the mother away for a while and challenged the boy to stomp as hard as he could, adding that he “probably could not stomp the floor hard enough to make it worthwhile.” The boy promptly stomped his foot on the floor with all his might. Erickson responded that his effort “was really remarkably good for a little eight-year-old boy and that he could probably repeat it a number of times, but not very many.” The boy angrily shouted that he could “stomp that hard fifty, a hundred, a thousand times” if he wished. Erickson replied that he was only eight years old, and no matter how angry he was he couldn't stomp a thousand times. At this, the boy furiously declared his intention of “stomping a hole in the floor even if it took a hundred million stomps.” Skeptically, Erickson said to him that “such a small boy would almost certainly not be able to stomp even 50 times without getting tired and wanting to sit down.” The boy started repeatedly stomping his foot as hard as he could. Erickson told the boy he was impressed with his stomping ability but again doubted that the boy could keep it up.

艾瑞克森让母亲离开一会儿，并 challenge（激将）男孩尽力跺脚，补充说"你大概没法跺得地板够响、值得那么做"。男孩立刻用尽全力跺脚于地。艾瑞克森回应说，那努力"对一个八岁小男孩而言确实相当了不起，而且他大概能重复几次，但不会很多"。男孩愤怒地吼道，只要他愿意，他能"跺那么响五十次、一百次、一千次"。艾瑞克森回说他才八岁，无论多愤怒，都不可能跺一千次。闻此，男孩 furiously（狂怒地）宣称他打算"就算跺一亿次也要在地板跺个洞"。艾瑞克森怀疑地说，这么小的男孩"几乎肯定连五十次都跺不到就会累、想坐下"。男孩开始反复全力跺脚。艾瑞克森告诉男孩，他对男孩跺脚的能力印象深刻，但再次怀疑男孩能撑下去。

After intensifying his efforts, the boy “reached a count of thirty before he realized that he had greatly overestimated his stomping ability.” Erickson offered the boy the option of “just patting the floor a thousand times with his foot, since he really couldn't stand still and rest without wiggling around and wanting to sit down.” The boy rejected the floor-patting and instead “declared his intention of standing still,” assuming a stiff, upright position with his hands at his sides.

在 intensify（加力）他的努力后，男孩"数到三十便意识到，他大大高估了自己的跺脚能力"。艾瑞克森给男孩一个选项："既然你真的无法站着不动休息、不扭动、不想坐下，那就用脚轻拍地板一千次吧。"男孩拒绝了轻拍地板，反而"宣称他打算站着不动"，摆出僵硬、直立的姿势，双手垂于身侧。

Erickson again expressed doubt that the boy could remain standing “without fidgeting or wanting to sit down.” He showed the boy the desk clock and commented about “the slowness of the minute hand and the even greater slowness of the hour hand despite the seeming rapidity of the ticking of the clock.” Erickson then went about his business, making notes on other cases he was working with. Over the next hour and a half, the boy started to lean on the chair when he thought Erickson wasn't looking and to sit down when Erickson went out of the room but always being sure to stand up when Erickson came back into the room.

艾瑞克森再次表示怀疑男孩能"不扭动或想坐下"地保持站立。他给男孩看桌上的钟，评论"分针的缓慢，以及时针更甚的缓慢，尽管钟的滴答看似飞快"。艾瑞克森接着忙自己的事，记录他正处理的其他案例。在接下来一个半小时里，男孩在以为艾瑞克森没看时倚着椅子，在艾瑞克森走出房间时坐下，但总确保在艾瑞克森回到房间时站起来。

By the time his mother returned the boy's attitude had completely changed. On the way home, the boy told his mother that Erickson was “a nice doctor,” and there was no further trouble at school or in the neighborhood. When the mother decided to get married sometime later, the boy wouldn't fully accept the stepfather until he found out that Erickson approved of the man.

到母亲回来时，男孩的态度已彻底改变。在回家路上，男孩告诉母亲，艾瑞克森是"一位好医生"，学校与邻里都不再有麻烦。当母亲日后决定结婚，男孩在发现艾瑞克森认可那男人前，始终不完全接受继父。

案例反思Reflections on the Example

In this example, Erickson applied the boy's statement that he could “do whatever I please” and that he would “stomp” anyone who “got in his

在此例中，艾瑞克森将男孩"我爱干什么就干什么"、以及他会"跺"任何"挡他路"

way” to the boy's own desire to rebel. By encouraging the boy to “do what he wanted” (rebelling and stomping) Erickson was able to get the boy to reevaluate whether he really wanted or could “do whatever he pleased.” By challenging the boy to stomp the floor as hard as he could, the boy had to face the fact that the person who was most in his way was himself, as a result of his own overestimation of his own abilities — especially when he was in a CRASH state. Ultimately, the boy realized “all by himself” that maybe he didn't really want to just “do what whatever he pleased” and that it was important to have others that he could trust that knew more than he did to help him set limits.

的人的陈述，应用到了男孩自身的反叛欲望。通过鼓励男孩"做他想做的"（反叛与跺脚），艾瑞克森让男孩重新评估：他是否真的想要、或能否"爱干什么就干什么"。通过激将男孩尽力跺地板，男孩不得不面对这个事实：最挡他路的，是他自己——源于他对自身能力的过高估计，尤其当他在 CRASH 状态时。最终，男孩"全靠自己"意识到，或许他并非真想只是"爱干什么就干什么"，而拥有他可信赖、且懂得比他多的人来帮助他设定界限，是很重要的。

In an undefined world where intellectual and emotional fluctuations create an enveloping state of uncertainty that varies from one mood and one moment to the next, there can be no certainty or security. [The boy] sought to learn what was really strong, secure, and safe, and he learned it in the effective way one learns not to kick a stone with a bare foot or to slap a cactus with the bare hands.

在一个 undefined（未定义的）世界里，智识与情绪的波动制造出一种笼罩性的不确定状态，随每一情绪、每一刻而变，那里不可能有确定或安全。[男孩]试图学习什么才是真正强大、安全、可靠的，而他以一种有效的方式学到了——正如一个人学到不该用赤脚踢石头、或赤手扇仙人掌。

Once again, Erickson creates a link between words and direct experience by connecting the boy's statements to his own behavior and its consequences (like kicking a stone with a bare foot or slapping a cactus with bare hands), as well as using the visual feedback of the clock. The benefit of this form of learning is that it encourages people to apply their judgments and generalizations to their own beliefs and to themselves so that they are able to assess the consequences of those beliefs on their own.

又一次，艾瑞克森将言语与直接经验相联结，把男孩的陈述连接到他自己的行为及其后果（如赤脚踢石头或赤手扇仙人掌），并运用钟的视觉反馈。这种学习形式的好处在于，它鼓励人们将他们的判断与泛化，应用于自身的信念与自身，使他们能自行评估那些信念的后果。

Erickson's comment about the boy's need to learn “what was really strong, secure, and safe” in “an undefined world where intellectual and emotional fluctuations create an enveloping state of uncertainty that varies from one mood and one moment to the next” is a demonstration of Erickson's ability engage multiple perspectives — in this case, second position with the boy. Erickson was clearly focusing on the intention behind the boy's symptomatic behavior and not simply trying to stop or get rid of it. This type of emotional intelligence helps to stay curious to discover what is possible and transform the symptom into a solution.

艾瑞克森关于男孩需要在一个"智识与情绪的波动制造出笼罩性不确定状态、随每一情绪与每一刻而变"的"未定义世界"中，学习"什么才是真正强大、安全、可靠"的评论，是艾瑞克森运用多重视角能力的一个示范——此例中，是與男孩的 second position（第二位置）。艾瑞克森显然聚焦于男孩症状行为背后的意图，而非仅仅试图停止或消除它。这类情绪智能，有助于保持好奇以发现可能之事，并将症状转化为解方。

改变框架大小（Changing Frame Size）Changing Frame Size

A frame is something that surrounds or encloses something else. It establishes the borders and constraints surrounding a phenomenon or interaction. Framing something creates a context in which that phenomenon or interaction occurs and is evaluated. Frames greatly influence the way that specific experiences and events are interpreted and responded to because they create reference points that direct attention to particular aspects of a phenomenon or experience.

框架是环绕或围住他物的东西。它确立了环绕某一现象或互动的边界与约束。框定（framing）某物，创造了一个该现象或互动发生其中、并受其评估的语境。框架极大地影响特定经验与事件被诠释与回应的方式，因为它们创造了将注意力导向现象或经验的特定面向的参考点。

A painful memory, for example, may loom as an all-consuming event when perceived within the short-term frame of the five minutes surrounding the event. That same painful experience may seem almost trivial when framed against the background of one's lifetime. An event that seems unbearably frustrating when we consider it with respect to our own desires and expectations, for instance, may suddenly seem unimportant when framed with respect to tragedies that can befall an entire community.

例如，一段痛苦的记忆，若被感知于事件前后五分钟这一短期框架内，可能 loom（浮现）为吞噬一切的事件。同一痛苦经验，若框定在人的一生这一背景下，可能显得几乎微不足道。一个当我们考量自身欲望与期待时显得难以忍受的挫折事件，若框定在可能降临整个社群的悲剧之背景下，可能突然显得不重要。

The Sleight of Mouth pattern of Change Frame Size involves shifting the

语言的魔力改变框架大小模式，涉及通过将其置于更长（或更短）的时间框架、更多人（或从个体视角）、或更大或更小的视角之语境中，来转移

impact or implication of a belief statement by placing it in the context of a longer (or shorter) time frame, a larger number of people (or from an individual point of view) or a bigger or smaller perspective. The micro strategy involves considering what is a longer (or shorter) time frame, a larger number or smaller number of people, or a bigger or smaller perspective that would change the implications of the belief to be something more flexible or positive?

一个信念陈述的影响或意涵。这微观策略涉及考量：有什么更长（或更短）的时间框架、更多或更少的人数、或更大或更小的视角，能将信念的意涵改变为更灵活或更正面的？

As usual, there was a fascinating and instructive example from the work of Milton Erickson demonstrating how the Sleight of Mouth pattern of Change Frame Size can be used in a creative way; both narrowing and widening the frame related to a particular situation. In this instance it involved one of his children. The following is excerpted from Erickson's account in Uncommon Therapy.

一如往常，米尔顿·艾瑞克森的工作中有一个迷人而具启发性的例子，示范了改变框架大小模式如何被创造性地运用——既 narrowing（收窄）也 widening（拓宽）与特定情境相关的框架。此例中涉及他的一个孩子。以下摘自艾瑞克森在《非比寻常的治疗》中的叙述。

Three-year-old Robert fell down the back stairs, split his lip, and knocked an upper tooth back into the maxilla. He was bleeding profusely and screaming loudly with pain and fright His mother and I went to his aid.

三岁的罗伯特从后楼梯摔下，裂了嘴唇，将一颗上牙撞进上颌。他血流如注，因疼痛与惊吓而尖声大哭。他母亲与我前去救助。

No effort was made to pick him up. Instead, as he paused for breath for fresh screaming, I told him quickly, simply, sympathetically and emphatically, “That hurts awful, Robert. That hurts terrible.”

我们没有试图抱起他。相反，趁他暂停吸气以准备新一轮大哭，我快速、简单、同情且强调地说："那很痛，罗伯特。那痛得可怕。"

Then I told Robert, “And it will keep right on hurting.”

然后我告诉他："而且它会一直痛下去。"

The next step for him and for me was to declare, as he took another breath, “And you really wish it would stop hurting.” The suggestion was made, “Maybe it will stop hurting in a little while, in just a minute or two.”

对他说出的下一步，是他另吸一口气时，我宣告："而你真的希望它停止痛。"那建议是："也许过一小会儿、就一两分钟，它就会停止痛。"

The next comment was, “That's an awful lot of blood on the pavement. Is it good, red, strong blood? Look carefully, Mother, and see. I think it is, but I want you to be sure.”

下一句是："人行道上好多血。是好的、红的、强壮的血吗？仔细看，母亲，看清楚。我认为是，但我要你确定。"

His mother picked him up and carried him to the bathroom, where water was poured over his face to see if the blood “mixed properly with water” and gave it a “proper pink color.” Then the redness was carefully checked and reconfirmed, following whirls the “pinkness” was reconfirmed by washing him adequately, to Robert's intense satisfaction, since his blood was good, red, and strong and made water rightly pink. Then came the question of whether or not his mouth was “bleeding right” and “swelling right.” Close inspection, to Robert's complete satisfaction and relief, again disclosed that all developments were good and right and indicative of his essential and pleasing soundness in every way.

他母亲抱起他，带到浴室，用水冲他的脸，看血是否"与水恰当混合"并给它"恰当的粉红色"。接着仔细核对并重新确认那红色，随即在充分清洗后重新确认那"粉红"，令罗伯特极度满意——因为他的血是好的、红的、强壮的，并让水恰当地变粉。接着是问题：他的嘴是否"流得对血"与"肿得对"。仔细检视，令罗伯特完全满意与 relief（释然），再次显示一切发展都是好的、对的，并在每一方面都指示着他本质而可喜的健全。

Next came the question of suturing his lip. [I stated] regretfully that, while he would have to have stitches taken in his lip, it was most doubtful if he could have as many stitches as he could count. In fact, it looked as if he could not even have ten stitches, and he could count to twenty. Regret was expressed that he could not have seventeen stitches, like his sister, Betty Alice, or twelve, like his brother, Allan; but comfort was offered in the statement that he would have more stitches than his siblings Bert, Lance, or Carol. Thus the entire situation became transformed into one in which he could share with his older siblings a common experience with a comforting sense of equality and even superiority. In this way he was enabled to face the question of surgery without fear or anxiety, but with hope of high accomplishment and the desire to do well the task assigned him, namely, to “be sure to count the stitches.”

接下来是缝合他嘴唇的问题。[我]遗憾地说，虽然他嘴唇必须缝针，但他能否有他数得出的那么多针，是极可怀疑的。事实上，看起来他连十针都缝不了，而他能数到二十。我表示遗憾，他不能有像他姐姐贝蒂·爱丽丝那样的十七针，或像他哥哥艾伦的十二针；但我安慰说，他会比他的兄弟姐妹伯特、兰斯或卡罗尔缝更多针。于是整个情境被转化为一个他能与其兄姐共享、带着安慰性的平等感甚至优越感的 common experience（共同经验）。如此，他得以不带恐惧或焦虑、而是怀着高度成就的希望，以及做好所指派任务——即"务必数清针数"——的渴望，来面对手术问题。

There are three different stages in this account where Erickson establishes different types and sizes of frames: the first addresses Robert's direct physical pain, the second deals with Robert's fear of his bleeding and injury; and the third prepares him for the suturing of his lip.

这段叙述中有三个不同阶段，艾瑞克森确立了不同类型与大小的框架：第一个处理罗伯特的直接身体疼痛，第二个处理罗伯特对流血与受伤的恐惧，第三个为他准备嘴唇缝合。

With respect to the pain, Erickson makes several statements that pace and lead Robert's experience to something more resourceful: (1) That hurts awful, Robert. That hurts terrible. (2) And it will keep right on hurting. (3) And you really wish it would stop hurting. (4) Maybe it will stop hurting in a little while, in just a minute or two. In addition, from shifting from a problem frame (that hurts terrible) to an outcome frame (you really wish it would stop hurting), Erickson's words help narrow his son's attention from a type of “eternity of the present,” where it seems like the pain will never end, to just “a minute or two.”

关于疼痛，艾瑞克森做了若干陈述来同步并引导罗伯特的经验朝向更有资源之物：（1）那很痛，罗伯特。那痛得可怕。（2）而且它会一直痛下去。（3）而你真的希望它停止痛。（4）也许过一小会儿、就一两分钟，它就会停止痛。此外，从"那痛得可怕"的问题框架，转移到"你真的希望它停止痛"的结果框架，艾瑞克森的言语帮助将儿子的注意力，从一种"当下的永恒"——仿佛疼痛永无止境——收窄到仅仅"一两分钟"。

The next intervention widened his son's perceptual frame from a fearful focus on “bleeding” to the larger frame of health and healing. Erickson's statements were: That's an awful lot of blood on the pavement. Is it good, red, strong blood? Look carefully, Mother, and see. I think it is, but I want you to be sure. Erickson's choice of words of “good, red, strong blood” are all attributes that indicate something positive. The fact that the blood “mixed properly with water” and gave it a “proper pink color” also place the situation squarely in a short-term positive outcome frame that Robert's mouth was “bleeding right” and “swelling right.” (It is also notable that Erickson's emphasis on the link to the visual attributes of “color” and “swelling” dissociates and directs attention away from the physical and emotional feelings of pain and fear, and towards particular qualities of visual imagery.)

艾瑞克森最后的陈述，将框架大小拓宽到包含罗伯特那些幸存于可能更糟伤害、且显然仍活着并茁壮的兄弟姐妹的"共同经验"。正如艾瑞克森指出，整个情境被转化为一个他能与其兄姐共享、带着安慰性的平等感甚至优越感的共同经验。

Erickson's last statements widen the frame size to include the “common experience” of Robert's siblings who have survived potentially worse injuries and are clearly still alive and thriving. As Erickson points out, the entire situation became transformed into one in which he could share with his older siblings a common experience with a comforting sense of equality and even superiority.

又一次，艾瑞克森在先跟后带上的技巧，以及他调动多种感官、多重视角、并与一个小男孩进入第二位置、进而引导他到 COACH 状态与元位置的能力，是将他的反应从"疼痛与惊吓"转化为"满意与释然"的关键。

Once again, Erickson's skill with pacing and leading, and his ability to engage multiple senses, multiple perspectives and to enter second position with a small boy and then guide him to COACH state and meta position were key to transforming his response from “pain and fright” to “satisfaction and relief.”

世界观（Model of the World）Model of the World

A model of the world represents an entire frame of mind. It constitutes much more than just another point of view; it is a complete world view. Shifting to another model of the world essentially involves shifting to completely different role or identity. An engineer, an artist, a salesperson, doctor, an athlete, a shaman, a billionaire, a refugee and a physicist would likely have different models of the world with different priorities, different perceptions and different interpretations of the same events or situations. Switching from one model of the world to another will most likely bring about a significant shift in the way a person would perceive and respond to a particular situation.

世界观代表一整套心智框架。它远不止是另一种观点；它是一个 complete（完整的）世界观。切换到另一种世界观，本质上涉及切换到完全不同的角色或身份。一位工程师、艺术家、销售人员、医生、运动员、萨满、亿万富翁、难民与物理学家，很可能拥有不同的世界观，带着不同的优先级、对同一事件或情境的不同感知与不同诠释。从一种世界观切换到另一种，极可能带来一个人感知与回应特定情境方式的重大转变。

The Sleight of Mouth pattern of Model of the World is about evaluating a belief statement or generalization from the framework of a different model of the world that would have a totally different perspective. Effectively

语言的魔力世界观模式，是关于从一个会具备全然不同视角的、不同世界观的框架，来评估一个信念陈述或泛化。有效

considering other models of the world is, of course, a micro strategy more than simply a verbal formula. It involves exploring what is a different model of the world (i.e., role or identity) that would provide a very different perspective on the belief that could open up other more productive or resourceful possibilities or choices?

地考量其他世界观，当然是一种微观策略，而非仅是言语公式。它涉及探索：有什么不同的世界观（即角色或身份），能对那信念提供一个非常不同的视角，开启其他更具生产性或有资源的物力或选择？

A good example of this is the case of a medical technician (Phoenix, pp. 67-69) working at the same hospital as Milton Erickson at one point in his career, who had a particularly large rear end (buttocks). Erickson noticed this but was also aware that the woman was very religious and on visiting days would stand outside of the hospital and offer to babysit the visitors' children.

一个好例子，是曾与米尔顿·艾瑞克森在某段职业生涯中同院工作的一位医疗技术员（《凤凰城》，pp. 67-69），她有特别大的臀部（buttocks）。艾瑞克森注意到了，但也知道那女子非常虔诚，在探视日会站在医院外，主动提出帮访客照顾孩子。

One day the woman developed a severe case of hiccups that the medical doctors could not cure. Eventually, they all recommended psychiatric consultation, which meant seeing Erickson. She refused to see him at first, but after her boss threatened to discontinue her free medical care, she gave in. When Erickson entered the consulting room, he told her to stay quiet until she heard what he had to say. He told her, “I know you like children. And you think because you've got such a great big fanny, no man will ever look at you. You would know better if you had read the Song of Solomon. The pelvis is mentioned as the cradle of children. The man who will want to marry you, the man who will fall in love with you, will look at that great big, fat fanny of yours and see only a cradle for children. He will be a man who wants to father a lot of children. And he will see a beautiful cradle for children.”

一天，那女子严重打嗝，医师们治不好。最终，他们都建议精神科会诊，即看艾瑞克森。她起初拒绝见他，但在老板威胁停止她的免费医疗后，她让步了。当艾瑞克森进入咨询室，他让她保持安静，直到听他说完。他告诉她："我知道你喜欢孩子。而你认为，因为你有个这么大的屁股，没男人会看你一眼。如果你读过《所罗门之歌》（Song of Solomon），你就懂了。骨盆被称为儿童的摇篮。会想娶你、会爱上你的那个男人，会看着你那巨大、肥胖的屁股，只看见一个儿童的摇篮。他会是个想要一大窝孩子的男人。而他会看见一个美丽的儿童摇篮。"

The woman's hiccups went away later that day and a few months later the woman came to Erickson's office and showed him her engagement ring. A few more months after that, she brought in her fiancé who told Erickson about the plans they had for building a home that was going to have a lot of bedrooms and “a great, big nursery” for a lot of children.

那女子的打嗝当天稍后消失，几个月后她来到艾瑞克森办公室，给他看她的订婚戒指。又过几个月，她带来未婚夫，他告诉艾瑞克森他们建家的计划——要有很多卧室，以及给很多孩子用的"一个大大的育婴室"。

In this example, Erickson was able to show the woman that what she saw as a serious flaw in her body (her large rear end) had a very different meaning from another Model of the World (a cradle for children for a man who wants a large family). This shifted her perspective of herself and transformed her flaw into an asset (the symptom into a solution). Again, it is important to note that Erickson began by pacing the woman's limiting belief before leading to his reference to a different Model of the World. This helped to establish rapport, which is key for his words to have the necessary level of resonance, especially given the woman's resistance to come to see Erickson. His reference to a passage from the Bible also lent a type of credibility to his reframe, since the woman likely came from a religious background.

在此例中，艾瑞克森得以向那女子展示：她视为身体严重缺陷之物（她的大臀部），从另一种世界观（对一个想要大家庭的男人而言，是儿童的摇篮）看来，具有非常不同的意义。这转变了她对自身的视角，并将她的缺陷转化为资产（将症状转化为解方）。再次值得注意的是，艾瑞克森在引导到他对另一种世界观的参照之前，先同步了那女子的限制性信念。这有助于建立亲和，而那对于他的言语产生必要程度的共鸣至关重要，尤其鉴于那女子对来见艾瑞克森的阻抗。他引用《圣经》的一段，也为他的换框增添了一种可信度，因为那女子很可能出自宗教背景。

现实策略（Reality Strategy）Reality Strategy

A Reality Strategy involves the sequence of mental tests and internal criteria an individual applies in order to evaluate whether or not a particular experience or event is “real” or “really happened.” It is essentially the strategy by which we distinguish “fantasy” from “reality.” This often has to do with the sensory qualities of the internal representation we are examining.

现实策略涉及一个人用以评估某一特定经验或事件是否"真实"或"真的发生了"的心智测试与内在准则的序列。它本质上就是我们区分"幻想"与"现实"的策略。这常关乎我们所检视的内在表征的感官特质。

From the NLP perspective, we can never know exactly what reality is, because our brain doesn't really know the difference between imagined experience and remembered experience — i.e., the map will never be the territory. The same brain cells are used to represent memory, imagination and ongoing experience. There are no specific parts of the brain that have been designated for “fantasy” versus “reality.”

从 NLP 视角看，我们永远无法确切知道现实是什么，因为我们的大脑并不真正知道想象经验与记忆经验之间的差别——亦即，地图永非疆域。相同的脑细胞被用于表征记忆、想象与进行中的经验。大脑中没有特定部位被指定给"幻想"而非"现实"。

The Sleight of Mouth pattern of Reality Strategy involves identifying, examining and evaluating the particular cognitive perceptions (the person's “map”) upon which a belief has been built. It is essentially scrutinizing different aspects of a belief statement and exploring the question, “How, specifically, do you know that?” It engages a micro strategy that involves examining: What cognitive perceptions of the world are necessary to have built this belief? How would one need to perceive the world in order for this belief to be true? What are the specific perceptions (and qualities of perception) and assumptions upon which this belief has been built, and which continue to hold it in place? How might those be adjusted to add more possibilities?

语言的魔力现实策略模式，涉及辨识、检验并评估一个信念所建于其上的特定认知感知（那人的"地图"）。它本质上是在 scrutinizing（审视）信念陈述的不同面向，并探索这个问题："具体而言，你如何知道？"它调动一种微观策略，涉及审视：构建此信念，需要什么对世界的认知感知？要让此信念为真，一个人须如何感知世界？此信念建于其上的、并持续使其到位的具体感知（及感知的特质）与假设是什么？那些如何被调整以增添更多可能性？

When working with Reality Strategies with Sleight of Mouth, we are not necessarily trying to bring a particular belief into question. Reality strategies can also be leveraged to create new beliefs or substitute one belief for another.

与现实策略运用语言的魔力时，我们未必试图将某一特定信念置于质疑之下。现实策略也能被 leverage 来创造新信念，或以一信念替换另一信念。

A fascinating example of how reality strategies can be creatively used to shift a belief is the case of a woman who came to see Milton Erickson because she had an airplane phobia. Her boss had told her that she had to fly to Dallas (from Phoenix) for a business trip or else she would lose her job.

一个关于现实策略如何被创造性运用以转变信念的迷人例子，是一位因飞机恐惧症来找米尔顿·艾瑞克森的女子。她的老板告诉她，她必须飞往达拉斯（从凤凰城）出差，否则就会失去工作。

Ten years previously she had been on an airplane that crashed. Nobody on the plane was hurt but over the next five years she began to feel more and more fearful whenever she flew. She was fine when she entered the plane and when the plane was taxiing, but the moment the plane took off she was gripped with fear. Once the plane touched down, she was able to relax again.

十年前，她曾在一架坠毁的飞机上。机上无人受伤，但在接下来五年里，她每当飞行便感到越来越恐惧。她上机时与飞机滑行时都还好，但飞机一起飞，她就被恐惧攫住。一旦飞机落地，她便能再次放松。

Erickson used a version of Reality Strategy to help the woman get over her phobia. According to him (Teaching Seminar, pp. 64-70):

艾瑞克森运用一种现实策略的版本，帮助那女子克服她的恐惧症。据他（《教学研讨》，pp. 64-70）：

I said, "Go into a trance and hallucinate being 35,000 feet up in the air, traveling 650 miles per hour ground speed." She was shuddering frightfully, bent over, her forehead touching her knees. I said, "And now, I want you to have the plane descend and by the time it reaches the ground all your fears and phobias, anxiety and devils of torture will slide off your body and into the seat beside you." And so, she hallucinated landing the plane, awakened from the trance and suddenly leapt out of the chair with a scream and came rushing to the other side of room saying, "They are there. They are there!"

我说："进入 trance，幻觉自己身在离地 35,000 英尺的高空、以地面速度每小时 650 英里行进。"她可怕地颤抖着，弯着腰，额头抵着膝盖。我说："现在，我要让飞机下降，等它抵达地面时，你所有的恐惧与恐惧症、焦虑与折磨的魔鬼，都会滑离你的身体、滑进你身旁的座位。"于是，她幻觉飞机降落，从 trance 中醒来，突然尖叫着跳离椅子，冲到房间另一侧说："它们在那。它们在那！"

Erickson had clearly fully engaged the woman's power of imagination to create a compelling experience in which she experienced all of her "fears and phobias, anxiety and devils of torture" sliding off her body and "into the seat" beside her. The imaginary experience was fully represented in all of her sensory representational systems to the degree that it passed her "reality strategy" criteria.

艾瑞克森显然充分调动了那女子的想象力，创造了一个 compelling（令人信服的）经验，在其中她体验到自己所有的"恐惧与恐惧症、焦虑与折磨的魔鬼"滑离身体、"滑进座位"到她身旁。那想象的经验在她的所有感官表象系统中被充分表征，到其程度通过了她的"现实策略"准则。

Erickson proceeded to call his wife in and ask her to sit in the chair. According to him, the woman said, "Please Mrs. Erickson, don't sit in that chair." Mrs. Erickson continued walking toward the chair and the patient rushed forward and physically prevented her from sitting in the chair.

艾瑞克森接着打电话叫妻子进来，请她坐在那椅子上。据他，那女子说："拜托艾瑞克森太太，别坐那椅子。"艾瑞克森太太继续朝椅子走去，而那病患冲上前、身体挡住她，不让她坐下。

At this point, Erickson told her, "Your therapy is complete. Have a good time flying to Dallas and flying back to Phoenix and call me from the airport and tell me how much you enjoyed the plane trips." The following Saturday Erickson got an excited phone call from the woman, who had just landed at the airport, exclaiming, "It was magnificent. It was utterly wonderful, the most beautiful experience of my life!"

此时，艾瑞克森告诉她："你的治疗完成了。好好享受飞往达拉斯、再飞回凤凰城的旅程，并在机场打电话给我，告诉我你有多享受那趟飞行。"下个周六，艾瑞克森接到那女子兴奋的电话，她刚在机场落地，喊道："那太壮丽了。 utterly（彻底地）美妙，我生命中最美的经验！"

Erickson frequently used trance as a way to help his clients gain access to creative unconscious processes. He would then work with reality strategies and the overlap between what happened in "trance" – which frequently included imagination and "hallucination" – and the person's "waking reality." In this example, Erickson paced the woman's fear by first having her imagine that she was "35,000 feet up in the air, traveling 650 miles per hour ground speed." He then led her by having her imagine that the plane was descending while the reasons for her airplane phobia (her "fears," "anxiety," etc.) left her body into the seat next to her.

艾瑞克森频繁运用 trance 作为帮助案主接通创造性潜意识过程的方式。他接着与现实策略、以及"trance"中发生的（常包含想象与"幻觉"）与那人"清醒现实"之间的重叠一起工作。在此例中，艾瑞克森先让那女子想象自己"身在离地 35,000 英尺高空、以地面速度每小时 650 英里行进"，从而同步了她的恐惧。他接着引导她：想象飞机正在下降，而她飞机恐惧症的原因（她的"恐惧""焦虑"等）离开她的身体、进入她身旁的座位。

This is not something that would be able to happen in normal "waking reality" but could be done in the context of a hypnotic trance hallucination. When the woman awoke, leapt out of her chair and rushed to the other side of room saying, "They are there. They are there!" it was an indication that the imaginary incident that took place in a trance state had transferred to her waking state.

这不是在正常的"清醒现实"中能发生之事，却能在催眠 trance 幻觉的语境中做到。当那女子醒来、跳离椅子、冲到房间另一侧说"它们在那。它们在那！"时，那指示着：在 trance 状态中发生的想象事件，已转移到她的清醒状态。

Erickson took advantage of this overlap between the "trance hallucination" and "waking reality" to make a dramatic shift to an outcome frame by saying, "Your therapy is complete. Have a good time flying to Dallas and flying back to Phoenix and call me from the airport and tell me how much you enjoyed the plane trips." This validated that what had happened in the imaginary scenario was somehow now "real" and by leaving her "fears and phobias" in the chair, the woman would be able to travel comfortably without them.

艾瑞克森善用这"trance 幻觉"与"清醒现实"之间的重叠，以戏剧性地转变到结果框架，说："你的治疗完成了。好好享受飞往达拉斯、再飞回凤凰城的旅程，并在机场打电话给我，告诉我你有多享受那趟飞行。"这确认了：在想象情境中发生的事，如今在某种意义上是"真实"的；而通过将她的"恐惧与恐惧症"留在椅子上，那女子将能毫无它们地舒适旅行。

It is important to note that it would not have worked for Erickson to simply have the woman imagine that she had taken a comfortable, eventless flight and try to make it seem real. Doing so would not have paced her experience

值得注意的是，若艾瑞克森仅仅让那女子想象自己经历了一段舒适、无事的飞行、并试图使其显得真实，是不会奏效的。那样做无法同步她的经验、

and addressed the perceptions (and qualities of perception) upon which the belief at the root of her phobia had been built.

也无法处理那建于其恐惧症根源信念之上的感知（及感知的特质）。

It is also significant that Erickson's instruction "And now, I want you to have the plane descend..." puts the woman in charge of the experience and into a type of meta position to what she is experiencing. The fact that the woman experienced the causes of her phobia as moving outside of her body makes it possible for something else to take their place. This is why Erickson's instructions to have a good time and to call to say how much she had enjoyed the trip were so fully and easily received by the woman without resistance.

同样重要的是，艾瑞克森的指示"现在，我要让飞机下降……"将那女子置于经验的主导地位，并进入一种对她所经验之物的元位置。那女子将她恐惧症的原因体验为移出自己身体之外的事实，使别的东西取代其位置成为可能。这正是为何艾瑞克森"好好享受、并打电话说你多享受这趟旅行"的指示，能被那女子毫无阻抗地、充分而轻易地接收。

元框架（Meta Frame）Meta Frame

Constructing a Meta Frame involves reflecting on a belief statement from a larger frame of reference that brings a different perspective or understanding to the belief, what its impact is or how it was formed. As I have mentioned previously, the term meta is a Greek word meaning "over," "between" or "above." A Meta Frame is, in essence, a belief about a belief that changes or enriches the perception of that belief. Meta Framing frequently diffuses the impact of a limiting belief by shifting a person's perspective of the belief to an observer of his or her own mental processes. Taking a meta perspective with respect to a belief helps a person to recognize that it is indeed a "belief" and not necessarily the only interpretation of reality.

构建元框架，涉及从一个更大的参照框架反思一个信念陈述，为那信念、其影响为何、或它如何形成，带来不同的视角或理解。正如我先前提及，meta 一词源自希腊语，意为"在……之上""在……之间"或"超越"。元框架本质上是一个关于信念的信念，它改变或丰富了该信念的感知。元框架常通过将一个人对信念的视角，转移到其自身心智过程的观察者，来 diffuse（化解）一个限制性信念的影响。就一个信念采取元视角，帮助一个人认识到：它确实是一个"信念"，而非对现实唯一可能的诠释。

When applying the Sleight of Mouth pattern of Meta Frame to limiting beliefs, the focus of the micro strategy is on the question, "What is a belief about this belief that could change or enrich the perception or implication of the belief?"

将语言的魔力元框架模式运用于限制性信念时，微观策略的焦点在这个问题上："有什么关于此信念的信念，能改变或丰富该信念的感知或意涵？"

Milton Erickson utilized a type of Meta Frame with a 19-year-old dental assistant who fainted or became extremely nauseated and distressed every time she saw blood (Collected Papers II, pp. 189-190). The dentist who employed her referred her to Erickson. She insisted that he treat her in one session and not psychiatrically, but only with hypnosis. Erickson induced a trance and then asked her if she would mind if he smoked while she was in trance. She assented. He then told her that, during the time it took him to smoke the cigarette, she would "review all the traumatic incidents in her life and anything connected with blood or fear or fainting."

米尔顿·艾瑞克森对一位十九岁、每次见血就昏厥或极度恶心与苦恼的牙医助理（《论文集 II》，pp. 189-190），运用了某种元框架。雇用她的牙医将她转介给艾瑞克森。她坚持要艾瑞克森一次会谈内、且非精神科地、只用催眠治疗她。艾瑞克森导入 trance，然后问她是否介意他在她 trance 时抽烟。她同意。他接着告诉她，在他抽完那根烟的时段里，她将"检视生命中所有创伤性事件，以及任何与血、恐惧或昏厥相关的东西"。

He finished the cigarette, and she awakened. They then just chatted about various things unrelated to the problem for the remainder of the time. As Erickson was about to conclude the session, the young woman protested that he "should have really done something for her problem if he was to charge her a fee." Erickson picked up on her statement and agreed with her, repeatedly emphasizing the idea that he "should have really done something" for her problem. The young woman left perplexed but was surprised to find that the next day and thereafter she no longer fainted at the sight of blood. Her employer called Erickson and reported the dramatic change he witnessed the next day. He said that she had observed and handled bloody teeth and dental procedures all day without the slightest sign of discomfort. In fact, she did not seem to even notice the change.

他抽完了那根烟，她醒了过来。在余下时间里，他们只是聊些与问题无关的各种话题。当艾瑞克森正要结束会谈时，那年轻女子抗议说，既然他要收她的费，就"本该真的为她的问题做点什么"。艾瑞克森抓住她这句话，表示同意，并反复强调这个念头：他"本该真的为她的问题做点什么"。那女子困惑地离开了，却惊讶地发现，第二天以及此后，她再见血都不再昏厥。她的雇主打电话给艾瑞克森，报告了自己次日目睹的巨大变化。他说，她一整天都在观察并处理带血的牙齿和牙科操作，却没有丝毫不适的迹象。事实上，她似乎连这变化都没注意到。

Erickson turned the woman's constraint that "he treat her in one session and not psychiatrically, but only with hypnosis" into an advantage. By having the woman briefly "review all the traumatic incidents in her life and anything connected with blood or fear or fainting" in a trance state, as opposed to a CRASH state, Erickson was having her unconsciously

艾瑞克森将那女子"他须在一次会谈内、非精神科地、只用催眠治疗她"的限制，转化成了优势。借由让那女子在 trance 状态（而非 CRASH 状态）中短暂"检视生命中所有创伤性事件，以及任何与血、恐惧或昏厥相关的东西"，艾瑞克森是在让她于无意识中

shift her inner relationship to those incidents. Then, instead of doing anything "psychiatrically," he chatted about things unrelated to the problem. Erickson's repeated emphasis of the woman's seeming complaint that he "should have really done something" for her problem to charge her a fee, turned the statement into a subtle and powerful type of Meta Frame. The frame, in this case, was the belief that Erickson should have, indeed, "done something" for her problem if he was charging a fee and that something really should have happened.

转变自己对那些事件的内在关系。接着，他没有做任何"精神科"的事，只是聊些与问题无关的话题。艾瑞克森反复强调那女子看似的抱怨——既然收费，他就"本该真的为她的问题做点什么"——将这句话转变成了一种微妙而有力的元框架。在这个例子里，这个框架是这样一种信念：既然在收费，艾瑞克森确实"为她的问题做了什么"，而且那"什么"真的应该已经发生。

The implication of Erickson's Meta Frame was that, because he was charging a fee, he did, in fact, do something for her problem during the session and that something really did happen that the woman was not yet aware of. That is, that something had happened whether she knew it and recognized it or not. Like the placebo effect, this can engage unconscious, self-organizing changes.

艾瑞克森这一元框架的意涵是：既然他在收费，他确实在会谈中为她的问题做了什么，而且真的有"什么"发生了，只是那女子尚未意识到。也就是说，无论她是否知道、是否认出，那"什么"已经发生。如同安慰剂效应，这能唤起无意识的、自组织的变化。

In fact, the placebo effect itself is a result of a type of Meta Frame regarding a medication or procedure. It results from a belief about the credibility of some other belief (e.g., "This medicine will relieve your pain.") as well as the person asserting the belief (doctor, nurse, etc.). When we believe that some other belief is coming from a credible source, it increases the impact that belief will have on us.

事实上，安慰剂效应本身，正是关于某种药物或程序的一类元框架的结果。它源于对一个"其他信念"之可信度的信念（例如"这药会缓解你的疼痛"），也源于断言该信念的那个人（医生、护士等）。当我们相信某个其他信念来自一个可信的来源时，那个信念对我们的影响便会增强。

总结Summary

Sleight of Mouth patterns are the product of micro strategies through which we build and update our belief systems and the inner map of the world upon which they are based. Erickson's intuitive use of Sleight of Mouth by applying his meta strategies of Pacing and Leading and Utilization was a major contributor to his genius in working with clients of all types.

语言的魔力模式，是我们用以构建并更新自身信念体系、以及该体系所基于的内在"世界地图"的微观策略之产物。艾瑞克森对语言的魔力的直觉式运用，结合了他"先跟后带"与"利用"的元策略，是他之所以能天才般地与各类型案主工作的主因。

The examples I have given also illustrate how Erickson applied the Patterns of Genius (macro strategies) that he shared with the other individuals I have examined in this series. In the cases I have presented thus far, Erickson employed every one of the ten patterns of genius at some point and frequently used several of them in combination.

我所举的例子，也说明了艾瑞克森如何运用"天才的模式"（宏观策略）——亦即他与本系列中我所研究的其余人物所共享的那些模式。在我迄今呈现的案例里，艾瑞克森在某一时刻用上了全部十项天才模式，且经常将其中数项组合运用。

As I have pointed out, and the examples I have presented demonstrate, Erickson tended to take on "beginner's mind" with his clients and was always "curious to discover what was possible." He did not plan or prepare to use particular words or techniques ahead of time. However, as the example of the man who spoke "word salad" confirms, Erickson would take steps to prepare himself (i.e., having the man's words transcribed so that he could practice "a word salad similar to his but not precisely the same"). This allowed Erickson to be spontaneous but certainly not random.

正如我所指出、且我所举的例子所表明的，艾瑞克森倾向以"初心"面对案主，并且始终"好奇于去发现什么是可能的"。他不会提前计划或准备要用某些特定的词句或技术。然而，正如那位说"语词沙拉"的男子的例子所证实，艾瑞克森会采取步骤来为自己做准备（亦即，将那男子说的话转写下来，好让他练习"一套类似于对方、却不完全相同的语词沙拉"）。这使得艾瑞克森既能临场自发，又绝非随意乱来。

The following worksheet provides a way to practice some of the Sleight of Mouth micro strategies applied by Erickson (and others who have used words to create and change worlds). Taking time to practice with some common limiting beliefs that you encounter in your own line of work can actually help you to be prepared (just like a martial artist who practices particular moves over and over) to respond spontaneously and appropriately to the obstacles or symptoms you may encounter.

下方的工作单，提供了一种练习由艾瑞克森（以及其它用言语创造并改变世界的人）所运用的一些语言的魔力微观策略的方法。花时间拿你在自己工作中常遇到的某些限制性信念来练习，其实能帮你做好准备（就像武术家一遍遍地练习特定招式那样），以便临场能自发且恰当地回应你可能遭遇的障碍或症状。

语言的魔力练习问题Sleight of Mouth Practice Questions

One of the best ways to both learn and apply the Sleight of Mouth patterns used by Milton Erickson is by exploring key questions related to the various micro strategies they reflect. In a way, each of the Sleight of Mouth patterns could be considered an answer to key questions that lead to different perspectives and perceptual positions.

学习并运用米尔顿·艾瑞克森所用语言的魔力模式的最佳方式之一，是去探究与它们所反映的各种微观策略相关的关键问题。在某种意义上，每一项语言的魔力模式，都可被视为对某些能导向不同视角与知觉位置的关键问题的回答。

Start by choosing a limiting belief statement that you would like to practice with. As with the Erickson examples I have presented here, the beliefs may be either explicitly stated or presupposed by the behavior or symptom that they produce. A typical structure would be:

首先从选择一个你想练习的限制性信念陈述开始。正如我在此呈现的艾瑞克森例子，这些信念可以是明确说出的，也可以是由其所导致的行为或症状所预设的。一个典型的结构会是：

Referent (am/is/are) judgment because/therefore reason/effect.

指称项（是）评判 因为/所以 理由/效应。

  • I
  • You
  • They
  • It
  • not good
  • incapable
  • unworthy
  • impossible
  • evidence
  • cause-effect
  • 我（I）
  • 你（You）
  • 他们（They）
  • 它（It）
  • 不好（not good）
  • 无能（incapable）
  • 不配（unworthy）
  • 不可能（impossible）
  • 证据（evidence）
  • 因果（cause-effect）

The examples I have presented in this section would reflect beliefs (either spoken or unspoken) like:

我在本节所举的例子，会反映出如下这类信念（说出的或未说出的）：

I am horrible/bad because I swear/gesticulate/say inappropriate things that I cannot control. I am ugly because I have a scar on my lip. I am going into orbit therefore my future is uncertain and dangerous. I am going to suffer the rest of my life because I feel pain in a limb that no longer exists. I am just a plain, fat slob therefore nobody would ever look at me except with disgust. It is not possible to relieve my pain and other symptoms therefore my life is unbearable. I am unattractive and will be rejected because my feet are too big. You can't affect my body with words because powerful chemicals have had no effect on my pain. I am going to do whatever I please because no one can stop me. Something terrible has happened to me because my mouth hurts and I am bleeding. Men consider me undesirable and unlovable because I have a big rear end.
我是糟糕的/坏的，因为我骂人/做手势/说些我无法控制的不当之语。我是丑陋的，因为我嘴唇上有道疤。我正在"脱离轨道"，所以我的未来是不确定而危险的。我余生都将受苦，因为我感到一个已不存在的肢体在疼痛。我只是个平庸的胖子，所以除了厌恶，没人会正眼看我。我的疼痛和其他症状不可能缓解，所以我的生活无法忍受。我没有吸引力、会被拒绝，因为我的脚太大。你无法用言语影响我的身体，因为强效的化学药物都对我的疼痛毫无作用。我要为所欲为，因为没人能阻止我。可怕的事降临到了我身上，因为我的嘴在痛、我在流血。男人觉得我不可取、不值得爱，因为我的屁股太大。
I am incapable of controlling my fear of flying therefore I will lose my job. I am paying a fee therefore something should have been done to solve my problem.
我无法控制自己的飞行恐惧，所以我会丢掉工作。我付了费，所以本该有人为我的难题做点什么。

Select one of the above beliefs or write down a limiting belief you would like to practice with below.

从上述信念中任选其一，或将你想练习的一个限制性信念写在下方的横线上。

Limiting Belief: _________________________________________________

限制性信念： \_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_\_

______________________________________________________________

______________________________________________________________

See how many of the following questions you can imagine answering with respect to the belief you have chosen. There are no right or wrong answers. Some Sleight of Mouth patterns will work more easily and effectively with some belief statements than others. Practicing the various micro-strategies will prepare you to respond spontaneously later on. Imagining being in the shoes of the person who is asserting the belief and in meta position can help you to find possible answers/reframes.

看看对于你所选的信念，你能想象回答下面多少个问题。答案没有对错之分。某些语言的魔力模式，对有些信念陈述比对另一些更轻易、更有效。练习各种微观策略，会让你日后能自发地回应。想象自己站在断言该信念之人的立场、并处于元位置，有助于你找到可能的回答/换框。

  1. Intention: What is the positive purpose or intention of this belief or the behavior it leads to? What does this belief or behavior do positively for the person who formed it?
  1. 意图（Intention）： 这个信念、或它所导致的 behaviour，其正面目的或意图是什么？这个信念或行为，对形成它的人有何正面作用？

______________________________________________________________

______________________________________________________________

  1. Redefine: What is another word (or phrase) for one of the words (or phrases) used in the belief statement that means something similar but has more positive implications?
  1. 重新定义（Redefine）： 对信念陈述中所用的某个词（或短语），可否换一个意思相近、却带有更正面向的别的词（或短语）？

______________________________________________________________

______________________________________________________________
