I recall saying, "Yes, someone really should apply the notion of logical levels in a more practical everyday sense." As soon as the words left my mouth, it was as if I had given myself the command, which led to my development of the NeuroLogical Levels of Change model and applications.

贝特森模型的起源The Origins of Bateson's Model

Bateson derived the notion of different logical types of communication and learning from Bertrand Russell's mathematical theory of logical types—which states that a class of things cannot be a member of itself. According to Bateson (Steps to an Ecology of Mind, p.202):

贝特森关于沟通与学习的不同逻辑类型的概念，源自伯特兰·罗素（Bertrand Russell）的数学逻辑类型理论——该理论主张一个事物的类不能是它自身的成员。据贝特森（《走向心智生态学》，p.202）：

Our approach is based on that part of communications theory which [Bertrand] Russell has called the Theory of Logical Types. The central thesis of this theory is that there is a discontinuity between the class and its members. The class cannot be a member of itself nor can one of the members be the class, since the term used for the class is of a different level of abstraction—a different Logical Type—from terms used for members.
我们的路径基于沟通理论中被〔伯特兰〕罗素称为逻辑类型论的那一部分。该理论的核心论点是：类与其成员之间存在不连续性。类不能是自身的成员，成员之一也不能是类，因为用于类的术语，在抽象层次上——一种不同的逻辑类型——不同于用于成员的术语。

As an example, the class of even numbers cannot itself also be an even number. Similarly, the class of cats is not a particular cat. Likewise, the physical object "cat" cannot be treated the same as the entire class of cats. (The class of cats does not require milk and kitty litter, but the members of the class frequently do.) In other words, the notion of logical types distinguishes between a particular "map" and the "territory" to which the map relates; i.e., between a mental "form" and its "content."

作为例子，偶数这个类本身不能也是一个偶数。类似地，猫这个类不是某一只特定的猫。同样，物理对象「猫」不能与整个猫的类被同等对待。（猫这个类不需要牛奶和猫砂，但类的成员常常需要。）换言之，逻辑类型的概念区分了特定的「地图」与地图所关涉的「疆域」；亦即，区分了心智的「形式」与其「内容」。

Bateson's classification of logical types was also partially influenced by General Semantics founder Alfred Korzybski who emphasized the importance of differentiating between various "orders of abstraction." These include differences between what we see (our internal representations) and the external stimuli themselves, and between verbal maps and the inner representations they intend to depict. Inner representations are more abstract but more encompassing than the external reality they represent. Similarly, verbal descriptions are more abstract and potentially more encompassing than the inner representations they stand for. In fact, it was Korzybski who first said, "The map is not the Territory."

贝特森对逻辑类型的分类，也部分受到通用语义学（General Semantics）创始人阿尔弗雷德·科日布斯基（Alfred Korzybski）的影响——后者强调区分不同「抽象层级」（orders of abstraction）的重要性。这些包括我们所见（我们的内在表征）与外部刺激本身之间的差异，以及言语地图与它所意图描绘的内在表征之间的差异。内在表征比它们所表征的外部现实更抽象，却也更包容。类似地，言语描述比它们所代表的内在表征更抽象、且潜在更具包容性。事实上，「地图不是疆域」这句话，正是科日布斯基最先说出的。

According to Korzybski, differentiating orders of abstraction also included distinguishing between (a) descriptions of experiences and (b) inferences (conclusions drawn from our experiences about our descriptions of those experiences); and (c) between descriptions about descriptions, inferences based upon other inferences, affect about affect (feelings about other feelings), and between one person's abstractions and another person's abstractions, etc.

据科日布斯基，区分抽象层级也包括辨别 (a) 对经验的描述，与 (b) 推论（从我们的经验中关于那些经验的描述所得到的结论）之间的差异；以及 (c) 关于描述的描述、基于其他推论的推论、关于情感的感情（对其他感情的感受）、以及一个人与另一个人的抽象之间的区别，等等。

Bateson combined the notion of "orders of abstraction" with that of hierarchy of classification to identify several different levels of learning and change in animals and humans—each level synthesizing and integrating processes on the level below it and consequently having a greater degree of impact on the individual.

贝特森将「抽象层级」的概念与分类层级的概念相结合，辨识出动物与人类学习及改变的若干不同层次——每一层次综合并整合其下一层次的过程，从而对个人产生更大程度的影响。

贝特森对逻辑类型在游戏与幻想上的应用Bateson's Application of Logical Types to Play and Fantasy

Bateson first formally introduced the concept of "logical types" in his article A Theory of Play and Fantasy (1954). In it, Bateson argued that "play" involved distinguishing between different logical types of behavior and messages. Bateson noted that when animals and humans engage in "play" they often display the same behaviors that are also associated with aggression, sexuality, and other more "serious" aspects of life (such as when animals "play fight," or children play "doctor"). Yet, somehow, animals and humans were able to recognize, for the most part, that the play behavior was a different type or class of behavior and "not the real thing." According to Bateson, distinguishing between classes of behavior also required different types of messages. Bateson referred to these messages as "meta messages"—messages about other messages—claiming that they too were of a different "logical type" than the content of a particular communication. He believed that these "higher level" messages (which were usually communicated nonverbally) were crucial for people, and animals, to be able to communicate and interact effectively.

贝特森在其文章《游戏与幻想的理论》（A Theory of Play and Fantasy，1954）中首次正式引入「逻辑类型」的概念。文中他主张，「游戏」涉及区分不同类型的行为与信息。贝特森注意到，当动物与人类进行「游戏」时，他们常常展现出同样也与攻击、性欲，以及生命中其他更「严肃」面向（如动物「假打」、或儿童玩「医生」游戏）相关的行为。然而，动物与人类大体上能够辨认出，游戏行为是一个不同的类型或类别的行为，而「不是真格的」。据贝特森，区分行为的类别，还需要不同类型的信息。贝特森称这些信息为「元信息」（meta messages）——关于其他信息的信息——并声称它们也与特定沟通的内容处于不同的「逻辑类型」。他相信这些「更高层次」的信息（通常以非言语方式传达）对于人与动物能够有效沟通与互动至关重要。

Animals at play, for instance, may signal the message "This is play" by wagging their tails, jumping up and down, or doing some other thing to indicate that what they are about to do is not to be taken for real. Their bite is a playful bite, not a real bite. Studies of humans also reveal the use of special messages that let others know they are playing in much the same way animals do. They may actually verbally "meta-communicate" by announcing that "This is only a game," or they laugh, wink, nudge, or do something odd to show their intent.

例如，游戏中的动物，可能通过摇尾巴、上下跳，或做其他某种事，来发出「这是游戏」的信息，表明它们将要做的不是当真的。它们的啃咬是玩耍式的啃咬，而非真正的啃咬。对人类的研究也揭示出，人们使用特殊的信息让他人知道自己在游戏，方式正如动物那样。他们可能真的以言语「元沟通」宣称「这只是个游戏」，或者他们大笑、眨眼、轻推，或做某种古怪之事来显示其意图。

〔图 p107〕

The same behavior (growling) may be a member of two different classifications (play or aggression). Other behaviors serve as meta messages to show to which category a particular behavioral expression belongs.

贝特森主张，许多问题与冲突，都是对这些信息的混淆或误读所致。一个好例子是

Bateson claimed that many problems and conflicts were a result of the confusion or misinterpretation of these messages. A good example is the

来自不同文化的人们在解读彼此沟通中非言语微妙之处时所经历的困难。

difficulties that people from different cultures experience in interpreting the non-verbal subtleties of each other's communications.

贝特森对逻辑类型在精神分裂症上的应用Bateson's Application of Logical Types to Schizophrenia

In fact, Bateson next applied the concept of logical types as an explanation for some of the symptoms of serious psychological problems and mental illness. In Epidemiology of Schizophrenia (1955), Bateson maintained that the inability to correctly recognize and interpret meta messages, and to distinguish between different classes, or logical types, of behavior, was at the root of many seemingly psychotic or "crazy" behaviors. Bateson cited the example of a young mental patient who went into the pharmacy of the hospital where he was staying. The nurse behind the counter asked, "Can I help you?" The patient was unable to distinguish whether the communication was a threat, a sexual advance, an admonishment for being in the wrong place, or a genuine inquiry, etc.

事实上，贝特森接下来将逻辑类型的概念，用作对某些严重心理问题与精神疾病症状的解释。在《精神分裂症的流行病学》（Epidemiology of Schizophrenia，1955）中，贝特森主张，无法正确辨认与解读元信息、以及区分不同类别或逻辑类型的行为，是许多看似精神错乱或「疯狂」行为之根源。贝特森引了一个年轻精神病人的例子：他走进所住医院的药房。柜台后的护士问：「需要帮忙吗？」病人无法辨别这沟通是威胁、是性暗示、是因走错地方而受责难、还是真诚的询问，等等。

When one is unable to make such distinctions, Bateson contended, that individual will end up, more often than not, acting in a way that is inappropriate for the situation. He likened it to a telephone switching system that was unable to distinguish the "country code" from the "city code" and the local telephone number. As a result, the switching system would inappropriately assign numbers belonging to the country code as part of the phone number, or parts of the phone number as the city code, etc. The consequence of this would be that, again more often than not, the dialer would get the "wrong number." Even though all of the numbers (the content) are correct, the classification of the numbers (the form) is confused, creating problems. [It should be noted that this is a fundamentally different communication problem than simply having "noise" on the telephone line which obscures the numbers. The causes of logical typing confusions are quite different than the causes of noisy signals.]

贝特森主张，当一个人无法做出此类区分，他多半会以不适合该处境的方式行动。他将其比作一个无法区分「国家代码」与「城市代码」以及本地电话号码的电话交换系统。结果，交换系统会不当地把属于国家代码的号码分配为电话号码的一部分，或把电话号码的一部分当作城市代码，等等。其后果是，拨号者多半会拨到「错号」。尽管所有号码（内容）都正确，号码的分类（形式）却被混淆了，从而制造出问题。〔应当注意，这与仅仅电话线上有「噪声」遮蔽了号码的沟通问题，在根本上是不同的。逻辑类型混淆的成因，与噪声信号的成因大不相同。〕

〔图 p106〕

Bateson likened hierarchies of logical typing in human communication to different classifications of numbers in a telephone switching system.

在《迈向精神分裂症的理论》（Toward a Theory of Schizophrenia，与唐·杰克逊、杰伊·黑利、约翰·维克兰德合著，1956）中，贝特森将不同逻辑类型的概念，作为「双重束缚」的关键元素来应用——即我早先所呈现的。正如我所指出的，贝特森主张，人际双重束缚常常源于不同逻辑类型之冲突信息所创造的混淆与悖论。

In Toward a Theory of Schizophrenia (co-authored with Don Jackson, Jay Haley and John Weakland, 1956), Bateson applied the notion of different logical types as a key element of the "double bind," which I presented earlier. As I pointed out, Bateson claimed that interpersonal double binds frequently resulted from confusions and paradoxes created by conflicting messages of different logical types.

贝特森相信，厘清作为这类双重束缚根源的不同逻辑类型的信息与分类的能力，对于有效的治疗乃至一般的心理健康都至关重要。贝特森关于逻辑类型理论在沟通与心理治疗上之应用的思想，由他的同事瓦茨拉维克（Watzlawick）、巴维拉斯（Bavelas）与杰克逊在《人类沟通的语用学》（Pragmatics of Human Communication，1967）中作了进一步探索。

Bateson believed that the ability to sort out the different logical types of messages and classifications which were at the root of such double binds was essential for effective therapy, and mental health in general. Bateson's ideas about the applications of the theory of logical types to communication and psychotherapy were further explored by his colleagues Watzlawick, Bavelas and Jackson in Pragmatics of Human Communication (1967).

贝特森对逻辑类型与逻辑层次在学习过程中的应用Bateson's Applications of Logical Types and Logical Levels to the Process of Learning

Bateson's next application of the theory of logical types was to the process of learning. In The Logical Categories of Learning and Communication (1964) he extended the notion of logical typing to explain different types and phenomena of learning as well as communication. He defined two fundamental types, or levels, of learning which must be considered in all processes of change: "Learning I" (stimulus-response type conditioning) and "Learning II", or deutero learning, (learning to recognize the larger context in which the stimulus is occurring so that its meaning may be correctly interpreted). The most basic example of Learning II phenomena is set learning, or when an animal becomes "test-wise"—that is, laboratory animals will get faster and faster at learning new tasks that fall into the same class of activity. This has to do with learning classes of behavior rather than single isolated behaviors.

贝特森对逻辑类型理论的下一个应用，是学习过程。在《学习与沟通的逻辑范畴》（The Logical Categories of Learning and Communication，1964）中，他将逻辑类型化的概念扩展到解释学习以及沟通的不同类型与现象。他定义了在所有改变过程中都必须考量的两种基本类型或层次的学习：「学习 I」（刺激-反应型条件作用）与「学习 II」，或称次级学习（deutero learning，即学会辨认刺激发生的更大语境，从而得以正确解读其意义）。学习 II 现象最基本的例子是定式学习（set learning），或当一只动物变得「通晓测试」——亦即，实验动物会越来越快学会归入同一活动类别的新任务。这关乎学习行为的类别，而非单个孤立的行为。

An animal trained in avoidance conditioning, for instance, will be able to learn different types of avoidance behavior more and more rapidly. It will, however, be slower at learning some "respondent" conditioned behavior (e.g., salivating at the sound of a bell) than some animal that has been conditioned in that class of behavior earlier. That is, it will learn quickly how to identify and stay away from objects that might have an electric shock associated with them but will be slower in learning to salivate when a bell rings. On the other hand, an animal trained in Pavlovian type conditioning will rapidly learn to salivate to new sounds and colors, etc., but will be slower to learn to avoid electrified objects.

例如，一只受过回避条件作用训练的动物，会越来越快地学会不同类型的回避行为。然而，它在学习某些「应答性」条件行为（如听到铃声流口水）时，会比早先在该行为类别受过条件作用的动物更慢。也就是说，它会很快学会如何辨认并远离可能与电击相关联的物体，但在听到铃声时学会流口水却更慢。另一方面，一只受过巴甫洛夫式条件作用的动物，会快速学会对新声音与颜色等流口水，却更慢学会回避带电物体。

Bateson pointed out that this ability to learn patterns or rules of a class of conditioning procedures was a different "logical type" of learning and did not function according to the same simple stimulus-response-reinforcement sequences used to learn specific isolated behaviors. Bateson noted, for instance, that the reinforcement for "exploration" (a means of learning-to-learn) in rats is of a different nature than that for the "testing" of a particular object (the learning content of exploration). He reports (Steps to an Ecology of Mind p. 282):

贝特森指出，这种学习某类条件作用程序的模式或规则的能力，是一种不同的「逻辑类型」的学习，并不按照学习特定孤立行为所用的那种简单刺激-反应-强化序列来运作。贝特森注意到，例如，对老鼠「探索」（一种学会学习的方式）的强化，在性质上不同于对某一特定物体「测试」（探索的学习内容）的强化。他报告道（《走向心智生态学》p. 282）：

[Y]ou can reinforce a rat (positively or negatively) when he investigates a particular strange object, and he will appropriately learn to approach it or avoid it. But the very purpose of exploration is to get information about which objects should be approached or avoided. The discovery that a given object is dangerous is therefore a success in the business of getting information. The success will not discourage the rat from future exploration of other strange objects.
〔当一只老鼠探究某一特定陌生物体时，〕你可以正性或负性地强化它，它会恰当地学会靠近或避开它。但探索的目的本身，就是获取关于哪些物体该靠近或避开的信息。因此，发现某一给定物体是危险的，是获取信息这一事务中的成功。这一成功不会使老鼠打消未来探索其他陌生物体的念头。

The whole purpose of exploring strange objects is to discover whether or not they are dangerous. So, getting punished for approaching a particular strange object does not stop the rat from exploring other strange objects to discover if they are safe to approach.

探索陌生物体的全部目的，就是发现它们是否危险。所以，因靠近某一特定陌生物体而受罚，并不会阻止老鼠探索其他陌生物体以发现它们是否安全可近。

〔图 p104〕

According to Bateson, exploring an object to discover whether or not is safe is a different level of learning than avoiding objects that have been discovered to be dangerous or approaching those that have been discovered to be safe.

因此，探索一个物体以发现它危险还是安全、从而避开或靠近，在抽象秩序与学习的分类层级上，都不同于避开一个已被发现不安全的物体。探索、学习辨别任务、或变得有创造力的能力，是高于构成这些能力的具体行为的学习层次——而改变的动力学与规则，在这更高层次上是不同的。

"Exploring" an object to discover if it is dangerous or safe and to be avoided or approached, then, is a different order of abstraction and at a different hierarchy of classification of learning than avoiding an object that has been discovered to be unsafe. The ability to explore, learn a discrimination task, or be creative is a higher level of learning than the specific behaviors that make up these abilities—and the dynamics and rules of change are different on this higher level.

作为另一例子，我们概括所学的能力，其运作方式不同于初级学习。考虑学习书写的经验。大多数读者大概是用一只手，费力地掌握在纸上写出单个字母所需的特定手指、手与手臂动作，从而学会书写的。然而，一旦掌握，这一基本模式可以更快地迁移到身体的其他部位与不同的环境语境中。例如，我们每个人都大概能轻易地用大脚趾在沙里描出一个可辨识的「A」。我们甚至大概能用肘部在墙上勾出同一个字母，或用牙齿咬着铅笔在画布上造出一个可辨识的字母仿品。

As another example, our ability to generalize what we have learned functions in a different way than primary learning. Consider the experience of learning to write. Most readers probably learned to write using one their hands to laboriously master the specific finger, hand and arm movements necessary to form the individual letters on a piece of paper. Once mastered, however, this basic pattern can be transferred much more quickly to other parts of the body and different environmental contexts. For instance, each of us could probably easily make a recognizable letter "A" by tracing it in sand with our big toe. We can probably even use one of our elbows to outline the same letter on a wall or take a pencil in our teeth and create a recognizable facsimile of the letter on a piece of canvas.

令人印象深刻的是，我们用来移动脚、脚趾、肘与颈的骨骼肌肉之特定组合与关系，与手和手指的相当不同，我们却能将用身体某部分所学，迁移到许多其他部分。这显然是不同于刺激-反应条件作用的一个学习层次。

What is impressive is that the particular groups and relationships of bones and muscles we use to move our feet, toes, elbows and neck are quite different than those in our hands and fingers, yet we can transfer what we have learned using one part of the body to many others. This is clearly a different level of learning than that of stimulus-response conditioning.

贝特森的学习层次Bateson's Levels of Learning

Applying the theory of logical types and the notion of different orders of abstraction, Bateson identified a number of levels of learning—each responsible for making corrective changes and refinements in the lower level of learning upon which it operated.

应用逻辑类型理论与不同抽象层级的概念，贝特森辨识出若干学习层次——每一层次都负责对它运作其上的、较低的学习层次做出校正性的改变与精炼。

Zero learning is characterized by specificity of response [i.e., having a specific behavior in a specific environment—RD] which—right or wrong—is not subject to correction.

零学习（Zero learning）的特征是反应的特定性〔即，在特定环境中有特定行为——RD〕，无论对错，都不受校正。

Learning I is change in specificity of response by correction of errors of choice within a set of alternatives.

学习 I 是在一组替代方案中，通过校正选择错误，而产生的反应特定性之变。

Learning II is a change in the process of Learning I, e.g., a corrective change in the set of alternatives from which choice is made, or it is a change in how the sequence is punctuated.

学习 II 是学习 I 过程之变，例如，对做出选择所依据之替代方案集合的校正性改变，或是对序列如何被切分（punctuated）的改变。

Learning III is change in the process of Learning II, e.g., a corrective change in the system of sets of alternatives from which choice is made.

学习 III 是学习 II 过程之变，例如，对做出选择所依据之替代方案系统之集合的校正性改变。

(Steps to an Ecology of Mind p. 293)

（《走向心智生态学》p. 293）

〔图 p103〕

Different Levels of Learning Relate to Changes in Different Levels of Behavior

用贝特森早先的电话系统类比，学习 0 像是一个自动拨号器，无论是否成功接通正确的人，都总是拨同一个号码。学习 I 是对本地号码的校正性改变（改变特定行为的表达）。学习 II 是对区号的校正性改变（转换物体或行为的分类——例如从「安全」与「好玩」到「危险」与「保护」）。学习 III 是对国家代码的校正性改变（更大的分类系统）。

Using Bateson's earlier analogy of a telephone system, Learning 0 would be like an autodial that always dials the same number whether it successfully reaches the right person or not. Learning I would be a corrective change in the local number (altering the expression of specific behaviors). Learning II would be a corrective change in the area code (shifting the classification of objects or behaviors—from "safe" and "playful" to "dangerous" and "protective," for example). Learning III would be a corrective change in the country code (the larger system of classification).

除了这三个层次，贝特森还暗示了一种学习 IV的可能性——他认为这是任何物种的个体成员都无法达成的层次，而只能作为群体或物种整体来集体达成。学习 IV 将涉及建立完全崭新的、不属于任何当前行为类别系统的行为。学习 IV 将是一种真正革命性的学习类型，涉及创造全新的原型或行为系统。

Beyond these three levels, Bateson also hinted at the possibility of a Learning IV—a level of learning that he believed was not possible to achieve by any individual member of a species, but only collectively as a group or species as a whole. Learning IV would involve the establishment of completely new behaviors that do not fit any current system of classes of behavior. Learning IV would be a truly revolutionary type of learning that would involve the creation of whole new archetypes or systems of behavior.

当我们的祖先直立用双足、说出最初的话语，他们并非从某个既存的替代方案集合中做选择，也并非在模仿某个已存在的其他物种或生物。他们开启了对我们在星球上之角色的一场彻底革命。

When our ancestors stood up on two feet and spoke the first words, they were not selecting from some existing set of alternatives, nor were they modeling some other species or creature that already existed. They began something completely new that revolutionized our role on the planet.

  • 学习 0——无调整
  • 学习 I——恰当地调整本地号码
  • 学习 II——恰当地调整区号
  • 学习 III——恰当地调整国家代码

〔图 p103〕

  • Learning 0—No adjustment
  • Learning I—Appropriately adjust local number
  • Learning II—Appropriately adjust area code
  • Learning III—Appropriately adjust country code

贝特森学习层次的一个类比是：对电话号码的不同部分做出调整，以成功接通想要找的人。

*Learning IV—Creating a new system of telephone numbers

An analogy for Bateson's levels of learning would be making adjustments to different parts of a telephone number in order to successfully reach the desired person.

贝特森学习层次的例子Examples of Bateson's Levels of Learning

As an example of how these different levels might work, consider the situation of Pavlov and his dogs. Pavlov discovered that he could condition his dogs to salivate at the sound of a bell by repeatedly ringing the bell when he fed them. The dogs learned to associate the sound of the bell with receiving food. Pretty soon, all Pavlov needed to do was ring the bell and, even if he had no food, the dogs would begin to salivate, triggered by the sound alone.

作为这些不同层次如何运作的一个例子，考虑巴甫洛夫与他的狗的情境。巴甫洛夫发现，他可以通过在喂狗时反复摇铃，将狗 conditioned 成在铃声一响就流口水。狗学会了将铃声与获得食物相关联。很快，巴甫洛夫只需摇铃，即便没有食物，狗也会因声音 alone 而被触发流口水。

According to Bateson's levels of learning model, the dogs' initial act of salivating when given food is a case of Learning Zero. It is a preprogrammed, instinctual response that is inherited and which would be difficult, if not impossible, to extinguish.

据贝特森的学习层次模型，狗在给食时最初流口水的举动，是零学习的案例。它是一种预编程的、本能的反应，是遗传的，即便不是不可能、也极难消去。

Learning to extend the reaction of salivation from the sight and smell of the food to the sound of the bell is an example of Learning I. Through repetition and reinforcement, the dog is learning to associate the specific response of salivating (as opposed to other responses, such as yawning, licking, blinking, etc.) with the specific stimulus of a particular bell.

学会将流口水的反应从食物的气味与外观，扩展到铃声，是学习 I 的例子。通过重复与强化，狗学会了将流口水这一特定反应（相对于其他反应，如打哈欠、舔、眨眼等），与特定铃声这一特定刺激相关联。

Learning II would involve a "change in the set of alternatives from which choice is made." This would mean that, once a dog had learned to salivate at the sound of a bell, it would have to change that response to something completely different (like barking or running away) when it heard the bell (as opposed to simply increasing or decreasing the amount of salivation). Salivating is a member of a set of "eating" behaviors. Other "sets" of alternatives would be "play," "avoidance," "exploration," "aggression," etc. Making a shift at this level would obviously be more complex than Learning I.

学习 II 涉及「做出选择所依据之替代方案集合的改变」。这意味着，一旦狗学会了在铃声下流口水，它必须在听到铃声时，将那反应改变为完全不同的某物（如吠叫或逃跑）（而非简单地增减流口水的量）。流口水是「进食」行为集合的一个成员。其他的「集合」是「游戏」「回避」「探索」「攻击」等。在这一层次上做转换，显然比学习 I 更复杂。

Learning III would be an even bigger change. Bateson says it would be a "change in the system of sets of alternatives from which choice is made." A dog, for instance, is one "system" of sets of alternatives. Other animals (cats, birds, humans, wolves, etc.) would constitute different systems. To accomplish Learning III, Pavlov's dogs would have to suddenly shift from "dog-like" behaviors to "cat-like" behaviors (meowing, climbing trees, etc.) when the bell rang. This would clearly be quite challenging and, as Bateson pointed out, is practically impossible for adults of most species (although imitating other animals like dogs, cats and birds is a natural and normal pastime for human children).

学习 III 是更大的改变。贝特森说它是「做出选择所依据之替代方案系统之集合的改变」。例如，狗是一个「系统」的替代方案集合。其他动物（猫、鸟、人、狼等）会构成不同的系统。要达成学习 III，巴甫洛夫的狗将不得不在铃声一响时，突然从「狗样」行为切换到「猫样」行为（喵喵叫、爬树等）。这显然相当具有挑战性，且正如贝特森所指出的，对大多数物种的成体而言实际上不可能（尽管模仿其他动物如狗、猫、鸟，是人类儿童自然且正常的消遣）。

Learning IV would involve the development of a new species, or a significant evolution within an existing species (like growing wings, evolving a bigger brain, etc.). Such a change would make completely new and unprecedented behaviors suddenly possible.

学习 IV 涉及发展出一个新物种，或现存物种内的重大演化（如长出翅膀、演化出更大的脑等）。这样的改变将使完全崭新、前所未见的行为突然成为可能。

Thus, in Bateson's framework, a simple, mechanical reflex would be a case of "zero learning." Learning Zero processes could also include habits, addictions and other patterns that seem fixed and unchangeable. Learning Zero is a common state of affairs for many people and organizations. Many of our behaviors become unconscious and embedded habits that make it

于是，在贝特森的框架中，一个简单的机械反射会是「零学习」的案例。零学习过程也可包括习惯、成瘾，以及其他看似固定而不可改变的模式。零学习对许多人与组织而言是常见状态。我们的许多行为变成无意识且嵌入的习惯，使得我们难以

difficult to adapt and adjust effectively to changes in the world around us. This frequently leads to 'stuckness', resistance, complacency and inefficiency.

有效适应并调整我们周遭世界的变化。这常常导致「卡住」、抗拒、自满与低效。

Behavioral conditioning, psychomotor learning, process reengineering or incremental quality improvement would be operations relating to "corrective changes" with respect to particular behaviors and actions in people and organizations—Learning I. Learning I is essentially about behavioral flexibility; updating and improving the procedures and patterns of behavior that are already in place. Learning I is best facilitated by helping people to develop better "metacognition," the awareness of one's actions, internal experience and thinking processes. This is done by providing basic coaching and teaching techniques such as contrastive analysis and providing feedback.

行为条件作用、动作技能学习、流程再造或渐进式质量改进，会关乎对人与组织中特定行为与行动做出「校正性改变」的运作——学习 I。学习 I 本质上是关于行为灵活性；更新并改进已在位的行为程序与模式。学习 I 最好的促成方式，是帮助人们发展更好的「元认知」（metacognition）——对自身行动、内在经验与思维过程的觉察。这通过提供基本的教练与教学技术（如对比分析、提供反馈）来完成。

Changing higher level processes like policies, values and priorities would relate to operations that address entire sets of alternatives—Learning II. If a company, for instance, decides to shift to being more "service oriented" than "product oriented," it will require large-scale changes across whole areas of procedures and behaviors, and this will probably imply the establishment of new sets of behaviors and procedures modeled from others.

改变更高层次的过程，如政策、价值与优先项，会关乎处理整个替代方案集合的运作——学习 II。例如，如果一家公司决定从「产品导向」转向更「服务导向」，它将需要在整个程序与行为领域进行大规模改变，而这大概意味着从他人那里建模建立新的行为与程序集合。

Another example of change at the level of Learning II in an individual would be an abrupt switch from exploratory behavior to avoidance, or from aggression to exploration or play. To accomplish such an immediate and dramatic turnaround requires shifts in beliefs and values. If one believes that a certain context is "dangerous," for instance, he or she will more than likely select "avoidance" behaviors rather than those in the class of "play." On the other hand, if a person believes that a context is "safe," he or she is not likely to choose behaviors from the classes of "fight" or "flight."

学习 II 层次上改变的另一例子，是个人从探索行为到回避的突然切换，或从攻击到探索或游戏的切换。要完成这样即时而戏剧性的转向，需要对信念与价值做出转变。例如，如果一个人相信某语境「危险」，他或她多半会选择「回避」行为，而非「游戏」类别中的那些。另一方面，如果一个人相信某语境「安全」，他或她不太可能从「战斗」或「逃跑」类别中选择行为。

A good illustration of this is the rapid drop off in the number of people choosing to travel in airplanes after the September 11, 2001, hijackings. It was not a gradual change brought about by higher airfares or poorer service (which would have been an instance of Learning I). Rather, it was an immediate and intense shift brought on by the belief that it was no longer "safe" to fly. Clearly, the effects of Learning II are more immediate and far reaching than Learning I.

911 劫机事件后选择乘飞机出行的人数骤降，便是一个好说明。它不是由票价上涨或服务变差带来的渐进改变（那会是学习 I 的实例）。相反，它是由「飞行不再安全」这一信念所引发的即时而强烈的转变。显然，学习 II 的影响比学习 I 更即时、更深远。

With respect to humans, Learning II shifts are supported by the ability to take "meta position"—that is, to disassociate from oneself and consider one's actions in context and in comparison, to other "sets of alternatives."

就人类而言，学习 II 的转变得益于采取「元位置」的能力——亦即，从自身抽离，在语境中并对比地考量自身的行动，相对于其他「替代方案集合」。

"Imprinting" and personality development would relate more to the establishment of change in whole "systems" of alternative behaviors—Learning III. Shifting such "systems" essentially involves a change at the level of identity. It involves expanding our range of behavior to include possibilities outside of our current role or collection of "sets" of alternatives. The Internet and the "new economy," for instance, forced many companies to stretch into completely new management and marketing approaches, sometimes far beyond what they were used to or comfortable with.

「印记」（imprinting）与人格发展，会更多地关乎整个「系统」的替代行为之改变的建立——学习 III。转换这类「系统」，本质上涉及身份层次上的改变。它涉及拓展我们的行为范围，将我们当前角色或「替代方案集合」之外的可能性纳入。例如，互联网与「新经济」迫使许多公司延伸到全新的管理与营销路径，有时远超出它们习惯或舒适的范围。

Modeling, benchmarking and taking "second position" with others are ways to support the process of Learning III. They facilitate us to reach beyond the threshold and limits of our current sense of self and identity. As Bateson maintained, "To the degree that man achieves Learning III..., his 'self' will take on a sort of irrelevance." Bateson asserted that change at the level of Learning III was quite difficult, and that "to demand this level of performance in some men and some mammals is sometimes pathogenic."

建模（modeling）、对标（benchmarking）与对他人采取「第二位置」，是支撑学习 III 过程的方式。它们帮助我们超越当前自我与身份感的门槛与局限。正如贝特森所主张的：「人在达成学习 III 的程度上……其『自我』会带上一丝无关紧要。」贝特森断言，学习 III 层次上的改变相当困难，且「对某些人与某些哺乳动物要求这一层级的表现在某些时候是致病的」。

Acts of genius frequently have the characteristics of Learning IV—unprecedented and transformative—leading to revolutions in the way we understand and interact with the world around us. In the Silicon Valley world of technology entrepreneurs, people often distinguish between "evolutionary" and "revolutionary" technologies. Evolutionary technologies are those which make a significant improvement on what already exists, extending its functionality or characteristics in some important way or integrating it with other technologies. Revolutionary technologies are those which change or create a new industry and transform the way that people work or communicate. Things like the printing press, automobile, airplane, radio, television, personal computer, the Internet could be considered revolutionary technologies.

天才之举常常带有学习 IV 的特征——前所未有且具转化性——带来我们理解与互动周遭世界方式的革命。在硅谷科技创业者的世界里，人们常区分「进化式」与「革命式」技术。进化式技术是对已存在之物做出显著改进，以某种重要方式扩展其功能或特征，或将其与其他技术整合。革命式技术是那些改变或创造新产业、并转化人们工作或沟通方式的技术。诸如印刷机、汽车、飞机、广播、电视、个人电脑、互联网等，都可被视为革命式技术。

As Bateson suggests, the insights and awakenings that constitute Learning IV most likely come in the form of some type of inspiration or revelation that has its source beyond the individual and in the larger system or "field" surrounding us—what Bateson referred to as the "larger Mind" or "pattern which connects."

正如贝特森所暗示的，构成学习 IV 的洞见与觉醒，最可能以某种超越个体、源自我们周遭更大系统或「场域」的灵感或启示之形式到来——即贝特森所称的「更大的心智」或「连接之模式」。

贝特森学习层次小结Summary of Bateson's Levels of Learning

We can summarize Bateson's levels of learning in the following manner:

我们可以如下总结贝特森的学习层次：

  • Learning 0 is no change. It involves repetitive behaviors in which the individual, group or organization is stuck in a rut or trapped "inside the box"—e.g., habits, resistance, inertia.
  • 学习 0 是无改变。它涉及重复的行为，其中个体、群体或组织困在窠臼中或「盒子之内」——例如习惯、抗拒、惰性。
  • 学习 I 是渐进的、增量改变。它涉及通过行为灵活性与延展做出校正与适应。尽管这些修改可能有助于扩展个体、群体或组织的能力，它们仍「在盒子之内」——例如建立并精炼新的程序与能力。
  • 学习 II 是快速的、不连续改变。它涉及将反应瞬间切换到完全不同的类别或行为类别。它本质上是从一个类型的「盒子」切换到另一个——例如政策、价值或优先项的改变。
  • 学习 III 是演化式改变。它的特征是显著的改变，延展到个体、群体或组织当前身份的边界之外。我们可以说，它们不仅在「盒子」之外，还在「建筑」之外——例如角色、品牌或身份的转换。
  • 学习 IV 是革命式改变。它涉及觉醒于某种完全崭新、独特且具转化性的事物。在学习 IV 的层次上，个体、群体或组织出了盒子、出了建筑、进入一个新世界——例如完全崭新的反应、技术或能力，开启此前未知、未经测绘的可能性之门。
  • Learning I is gradual, incremental change. It involves making corrections and adaptations through behavioral flexibility and stretching. While these modifications may help to extend the capabilities of the individual group or organization, they are still "within the box"—e.g., establishing and refining new procedures and capabilities.

用一个计算机类比，存储在计算机中的数据如同学习 0。它就在那儿，不变，被一遍遍使用，无论计算机运行什么程序。对那些数据运行拼写检查程序，如同学习 I。拼写检查程序对某特定数据集做出校正性改变。

  • Learning II is rapid, discontinuous change. It involves the instantaneous shift of a response to an entirely different category or class of behavior. It is essentially the switch from one type of "box" to another—e.g., change in policies, values or priorities.

然而，如果被检查的数据不是文本，而是需要更新的数字与财务数字，运行再多次拼写检查也无法做出恰当校正。相反，用户将不得不切换到电子表格或某种会计软件。走出一个程序的「盒子」、切换到另一个，如同学习 II。

  • Learning III is evolutionary change. It is characterized by significant alterations that stretch beyond the boundaries of the current identity of the individual, group or organization. We could say that not only are they outside the "box," they are outside of the "building"—e.g., transition of role, brand or identity.

有时所用的计算机无法运行所需程序，而必须彻底换一台计算机或更改操作系统。这如同学习 III。

  • Learning IV is revolutionary change. It involves awakening to something completely new, unique and transformative. At the level of Learning IV, the individual, group or organization is out of the box, out of the building and in a new world—e.g., completely new responses, technologies or capabilities that open the door to previously unknown and uncharted possibilities.

开发一种全新装置，例如由酶与 DNA 分子而非硅微芯片构成的可编程分子计算机，会如同学习 IV。

To use a computer analogy, data stored in a computer is like Learning 0. It just sits there, unchanging, to be used over and over again, whatever programs are running on the computer. Running a spell check program on that data would be like Learning I. A spell check program makes corrective changes in a particular set of data.

If the data being checked, however, is not text but numbers and financial figures that need to be updated, no amount of running the spell checker will be able to make the proper corrections. Instead, the user would have to switch to a spreadsheet or some type of accounting software. Getting "out of the box" of one program and switching to another is like Learning II.

Sometimes the computer one is using is incapable of running the needed program and it is necessary to switch computers altogether or change operating systems. This would be like Learning III.

To develop a completely new device, such as a programmable molecular computing machine composed of enzymes and DNA molecules instead of silicon microchips, would be like Learning IV.

〔图 p099〕火箭飞船层级类比图：自下而上 Learning 0 No change（积木地基）→ Learning I Incremental change（小火箭）→ Learning II Rapid, discontinuous change（汽车）→ Learning III Evolutionary change（大火箭）→ Learning IV Revolutionary change（飞出地球的飞船）

「神经逻辑层次」的发展The Development of "Neuro-Logical Levels"

Being familiar with Bateson's work, I was already empirically aware that there was an important distinction between people's physical actions and behaviors and the deeper cognitive representations and strategies which took place in their minds. It was also obvious that processes on a behavior level were different than those on a mental level. Putting someone in jail, for instance, could stop that person from physically taking revenge on another person, but could not keep him or her from continuing to plan revenge; in fact, it can just as often encourage it. It was also clear that developing a cognitive capability involved different dynamics than simply influencing someone physically. My work with learning strategies, for instance, had convinced me that it was much more expedient to teach a person to spell by providing an effective strategy for spelling rather than to simply punish them whenever they misspelled a word.

由于熟悉贝特森的工作，我早已在经验上意识到，人的身体行动与行为，和发生在他们心智中更深层认知表征与策略之间，存在重要区分。同样明显的是，行为层次上的过程不同于心智层次上的过程。例如，把一个人关进监狱，可以阻止那个人 physically 向他人报复，却无法阻止他继续谋划报复；事实上，它同样常常助长报复。同样清楚的是，发展一种认知能力，涉及的动力学不同于单纯在身体上影响某人。例如，我在学习策略方面的工作让我确信，教一个人拼写时，提供一套有效的拼写策略，远比单纯在他拼错词时惩罚他要事半功倍。

I had also begun to work with people's beliefs and belief systems. As I did, it became evident that they were not simply another type of strategy. Instead, they often tended to operate upon particular strategies. That is, a certain belief could either function as motivation or interference to the development of a strategy.

我也已开始研究人们的信念与信念系统。随着深入，明显可见它们不仅仅是另一种类型的策略。相反，它们常常倾向于运作于特定策略之上。也就是说，某一特定信念，既可以作为发展某策略的动机，也可以成为其干扰。

As an example, I remember teaching a learning strategy I had developed to a young man who had struggled with spelling which involved visualizing the letters of the word. The strategy was quite effective, and the young man was soon spelling words frontwards and backwards that he had struggled with only moments earlier. "That's interesting," he said, "but I don't want to use it." Surprised, I asked, "Why? It is not difficult to do." "No. It is easy,"

例如，我记得我曾教一个在拼写上挣扎的年轻人一套我开发的、涉及在心智中 visualizing 词语字母的学习策略。这策略相当有效，那年轻人很快就能把他片刻前还挣扎的词正着反着拼出来。「真有意思，」他说，「但我不想用。」我惊讶地问：「为什么？这不难啊。」「是不难，」

he responded, "but I'd rather not become a stiff and insensitive person." It turns out that, as a child, the young man had a particular teacher who was very severe about spelling. The young man had made an association between spelling correctly and becoming rigid ("stiff and insensitive"). This belief the connected the capability of spelling to a certain type of identity had caused him to resist learning to spell throughout his formal education.

他回答，「但我不想变成一个僵硬、麻木不仁的人。」结果，这年轻人小时候有一位对拼写极其严厉的老师。年轻人将「拼写正确」与「变得僵硬（stiff and insensitive）」关联在了一起。这一将拼写能力与某种身份类型相连的信念，使他在整个正规教育中抗拒学习拼写。

This type of experience seemed to fit with Bateson's notion of a "hierarchy" of logical levels. It was this aspect of hierarchy that first led me to choose the particular labels I have used to describe the various levels in my Neuro-Logical Levels model. As with all key NLP distinctions, these labels did not arise as a result of some rationalization. Rather, as part of my NLP training work, I was frequently teaching seminar participants the usage of a set of verbal reframing patterns I had developed, known as "Sleight of Mouth," presented in the previous chapter. This often involved people responding to negative statements made about them by others. I began to notice that certain types of statements were typically more difficult for people to handle than others, even though the type of judgment being asserted was essentially the same. For example, compare the following statements:

这类经验似乎契合贝特森关于逻辑层次「层级」的概念。正是层级的这一面向，最初引导我选用特定标签来描述我神经逻辑层次模型中各个层次。如同所有关键的 NLP 区分，这些标签并非源于某种合理化。相反，作为我 NLP 训练工作的一部分，我常向研讨班参与者讲授一套我开发的、被称为「语言的魔力」（Sleight of Mouth）的言语换框模式——即前一章所呈现的。这常常涉及人们回应他人对自己所做的负面陈述。我开始注意到，某些类型的陈述，典型地比另一些更难让人应对，尽管所断言的判断类型本质上相同。例如，比较以下陈述：

That object in your environment is dangerous. Your actions in that particular context are dangerous. Your inability to make effective judgments is dangerous. Your beliefs and values are dangerous. You are a dangerous person.
你环境中的那个物体是危险的。你在那个特定情境中的行为是危险的。你做出有效判断的能力是危险的。你的信念与价值是危险的。你是一个危险的人。

The judgment being made in each case is about something being "dangerous." Intuitively, however, most people sense that the "space" or "territory" implied by each statement becomes progressively larger and feel an increasing sense of psychological and emotional affect with each statement.

每种情形中所做的判断，都是关于某物「危险」。然而直觉上，大多数人感觉到，每句话所暗示的「空间」或「疆域」渐次变大，并随着每句话感受到渐增的心理与情感影响。

For someone to tell you that some specific behavioral response you made was dangerous is quite different than telling you that you are a "dangerous person." I noticed that if I held a judgment constant and simply substituted a term for environment, behavior, capabilities, beliefs and values, and identity, people would feel progressively more offended or complimented, depending on the positive or negative nature of the judgment.

告诉某人他做出的某个特定行为反应危险，与告诉某人「你是一个危险的人」，是相当不同的。我注意到，如果我让一个判断保持不变，仅仅替换环境、行为、能力、信念与价值、以及身份这几个词，人们会感到渐次更被冒犯或更被赞美——取决于判断的正向或负向性质。

Try it for yourself. Imagine someone was saying each of the following statements to you:

你自己试试。想象有人对你说以下每句话：

Your surroundings are (stupid/ugly/exceptional/beautiful).

你的周遭是（愚蠢/丑陋/出众/美丽）的。

The way you behaved in that particular situation was (stupid/ugly/exceptional/beautiful).

你在那个特定情境中的行为方式是（愚蠢/丑陋/出众/美丽）的。

You really have the capability to be (stupid/ugly/exceptional/beautiful).

你真的有能力变得（愚蠢/丑陋/出众/美丽）。

What you believe and value is (stupid/ugly/exceptional/beautiful).

你所相信与看重的是（愚蠢/丑陋/出众/美丽）的。

You are (stupid/ugly/exceptional/beautiful).

你是（愚蠢/丑陋/出众/美丽）的。

〔图 p97〕

Again, notice that the evaluations asserted by each statement are the same. What changes is the aspect of the person to which the statement is referring.

环境 – 行为 – 能力 – 信念与价值 – 身份

Environment – Behavior – Capability – Beliefs and Values – Identity

这一直觉的敏感力，似乎反映了与这些陈述某种根本上「神经语言」（neurolinguistic）相关的东西。

This intuitive sensibility seemed to reflect something fundamentally “neurolinguistic” in relation to these statements.

当我意识到这些区分对应于我们用来组织生活的六个基本「W」问题时，它们落实得更加坚实：where（何地）、when（何时）、what（什么）、how（如何，倒写的 w 问题）、why（为何）与 who（谁）。

These distinctions fell into place even more solidly when it occurred to me that they corresponded to the six fundamental “W” questions that we use to organize our lives: where, when, what, how (the backward “w” question), why and who.

环境：Where? When?（何地？何时？）行为：What?（什么？）能力：How?（如何？）信念与价值：Why?（为何？）身份：Who?（谁？）

Environment: Where? When? Behavior: What? Capability: How? Beliefs and Values: Why? Identity: Who?

在我加入第六个反映贝特森系统视角的层次——场域：Who else?（还有谁？）——之后不久。这一层次还包括关于个人参与更大系统之目的感，由以下问题表达：For Whom?（为了谁？）For What?（为了什么？）

Shortly after I added a sixth level reflecting Bateson’s systemic perspective – Field: Who else? This level also included a sense of purpose with respect to one’s participation in a bigger system, expressed by the questions: For Whom? For What?

神经逻辑层次模型The Neuro-Logical Levels Model

According to my Neuro-Logical Levels model there are a number of different levels, paralleling those defined by Bateson, that influence and shape our relationships and interactions in the world:

据我的神经逻辑层次模型，存在若干不同层次，与贝特森所定义的那些平行，它们影响并塑造我们在世界中的关系与互动：

| Field/“Spiritual” | Vision & Purpose | For Whom? For What? | | A. Who I Am - Identity | Mission | Who? | | B. My Belief system - Values and Meanings | Permission & Motivation | Why? | | C. My Capabilities - Strategies and States | Maps & Plans | How? |

| 场域/「灵性」 | 愿景与目的 | For Whom? For What?（为了谁？为了什么？） || A. 我是谁 - 身份 | 使命 | Who?（谁？） || B. 我的信念系统 - 价值与意义 | 许可与动机 | Why?（为何？） || C. 我的能力 - 策略与状态 | 地图与计划 | How?（如何？） |

| D. What I Do or have Done - Specific Behaviors | Actions & Reactions | What? | | E. My Environment - External Context | Constraints & Opportunities | Where? When? |

| D. 我所做或已做之事 - 具体行为 | 行动与反应 | What?（什么？） || E. 我的环境 - 外部语境 | 约束与机会 | Where? When?（何地？何时？） |

The most fundamental level of influence on our relationships and interactions is the shared environment—i.e., when and where the operations and relationships within a system or organization take place. Environmental factors determine the context and constraints under which people operate. An organization’s environment, for instance, is made up of such things as the geographical locations of its operations, the buildings and facilities which define the “work place,” office and factory design, etc. In addition to the influence these environmental factors may have on people within the organization, one can also examine the influence and impact that people within an organization have upon their environment, and what products or creations they bring to the environment.

对我们关系与互动最基础的影响层次，是共享的环境——亦即，系统或组织内运作与关系发生的何时与何地。环境因子决定了人们运作于其下的语境与约束。例如，一个组织的环境，由诸如其运营的地理位置、定义「工作场所」的建筑与设施、办公室与工厂设计等构成。除了这些环境因子可能对组织内之人产生的影响，人们也可审视组织内之人对他们的环境所施加的影响，以及他们带给环境的产品或创造成果。

At another level, we can examine the specific behaviors and actions of a group or individual—i.e., what the person or organization does within the environment. What are the particular patterns of work, interaction or communication? On an organizational level, behaviors may be defined in terms of general procedures. On the individual level, behaviors take the form of specific work routines, working habits or job-related activities.

在另一层次，我们可以审视群体或个体的特定行为与行动——亦即，人或组织在环境中做什么。工作、互动或沟通的特定模式是什么？在组织层次上，行为可被定义为一般程序。在个体层次上，行为采取特定工作惯例、工作习惯或工作相关活动的形式。

Another level of process involves the strategies, skills and capabilities by which the organization or individual selects and directs actions within their environment—i.e., how they generate and guide their behaviors within a particular context. For an individual, capabilities include cognitive strategies and skills such as learning, memory, decision-making and creativity, which facilitate the performance of a particular behavior or task. On an organizational level, capabilities relate to the infrastructures available to support communication, innovation, planning and decision-making between members of the organization.

另一层次的过程，涉及组织或个人在其中选择并引导其环境内行动的策略、技能与能力——亦即，他们在特定语境中如何生成并引导其行为。对个体而言，能力包括认知策略与技能，如学习、记忆、决策与创造力，它们促成特定行为或任务的执行。在组织层次上，能力关乎支撑组织成员间沟通、创新、规划与决策的基础设施。

These other levels of process are shaped by values and beliefs, which provide the motivation and guidelines behind the strategies and capabilities used to accomplish behavioral outcomes in the environment—i.e., why people do things the way they do them in a particular time and place. Our values and beliefs provide the reinforcement (motivation and permission) that supports or inhibits particular capabilities and behaviors. Values and beliefs determine how events are given meaning and are at the core of judgment and culture.

这些其他层次的过程，被价值与信念所塑造——它们提供策略与能力背后的动机与指引，用以在环境中达成行为结果——亦即，人们为何在特定的时间与地点，以他们所做的方式做事。我们的价值与信念提供支撑或抑制特定能力与行为的强化（动机与许可）。价值与信念决定事件如何被赋予意义，并处于判断与文化的核心。

Values and beliefs support the individual’s or organization’s sense of identity—i.e., the who behind the why, how, what, where and when. Identity level processes involve people’s sense of role and mission with respect to their vision and the larger systems of which they are members. Identity can be viewed as being composed of two complementary aspects: the ego and the soul. The ego is oriented toward survival, recognition and ambition. The soul is oriented toward purpose, contribution and mission. Charisma, passion and presence emerge naturally when these two forces are aligned.

价值与信念支撑个体或组织的身份感——亦即，在 why、how、what、where、when 背后的 who。身份层次的过程，涉及人们关于其愿景、以及他们所隶属的更大系统之角色与使命感。身份可被视为由两个互补面向组成：自我（ego）与灵魂（soul）。自我取向于生存、认可与抱负。灵魂取向于目的、贡献与使命。当这两股力量对齐，魅力、热情与临在（presence）便自然涌现。

Typically, a mission is defined in terms of the service performed by people in a particular role with respect to others within a larger system. A particular identity or role is expressed in terms of several key values and beliefs, which determine the priorities to be followed by individuals within the role.

典型地，一项使命是根据人们在更大系统中相对于他人的特定角色所履行的服务来定义的。一个特定的身份或角色，由若干关键价值与信念来表达，它们决定了角色中个体所应遵循的优先项。

〔图 p95〕

These, in turn, are supported by a larger range of skills and capabilities that are required to manifest particular values and beliefs. Effective capabilities produce an even wider set of specific behaviors and actions, which express and adapt values with respect to many particular environmental contexts and conditions.

还有另一个最好被称为场域层次或灵性层次的层次。这一层次关乎人们对他们所隶属并参与其中的更大系统之感知——我们可称这一层次为「trans-mission（传递）」之一。这些感知关乎一个人的行动所指向的为了谁或为了什么之感，为其行动、能力、信念与角色身份提供愿景、意义与目的之感。

There is another level that can best be referred to as a field level or spiritual level. This level has to do with people’s perceptions of the larger systems to which they belong and within which they participate—we could call this level one of “trans-mission.” These perceptions relate to a person’s sense of for whom or for what their actions are directed, providing a sense of vision, meaning and purpose for their actions, capabilities, beliefs and role identity.

层级中的每一层次，都与它下一层次的现象或经验之分组相关。单一身份由一组特定的信念与价值所塑造，并反映于其中。每一信念与价值，又关联到一组特定的能力。能力关联到行为的特定分组，而行为最终关联到特定集群的环境条件。

Each level in the hierarchy is related to groupings of phenomena or experiences from the level below it. A single identity is shaped by, and reflected in, a particular group of beliefs and values. Each belief and value, in turn, is related to a particular group of capabilities. The capabilities relate to specific groupings of behaviors, and the behaviors ultimately relate to particular clusters of environmental conditions.

于是，这层次的系统可被表现为倒置的树状结构（或金字塔）。

Thus, the system of levels can be represented as an inverted tree structure (or pyramid).

当我们到达场域与「灵性」层次，我们可以翻转树状结构，使它如同树枝般向上延伸。这展示了我们所从属的、逐级更大的系统与「场域」（家庭、职业、社群、星球等）。

〔图 p95〕

When we reach the level of field and “spirit” we can flip the tree structure so that it extends upward like the branches of a tree. This illustrates the successively larger systems and “fields” of which we are a part (family, profession, community, planet, etc.).

例如，当一个人在使命与身份层次上受到挑战时，被动员的神经学层次，远比移动他或她的手所需的神经学层次要深得多。要经验环境，一个人可以被动地调整其感觉器官。要在特定环境中采取行动，一个人需要动员更多其神经系统。为了将那些行动协调成复杂序列，如跳舞或驾驶汽车，一个人必须运用甚至更多的神经系统。形成并展现关于能力、行为与环境之信念与价值，需要更深的神经承诺（包括与「心」和「腹」相关的那些）。自我感源自在所有其他层次上神经系统的总动员。那么，一般而言，越高层次的过程动员越深的神经系统承诺。

〔图 p94〕

Bateson himself (Steps to an Ecology of Mind pp. 249–250) contended that the hierarchy formed by the various levels of learning would correspond to “hierarchies of circuit structure which we may—indeed, must—expect to find in the telencephalized brain,” claiming that “we should look forward to a classification or hierarchy of neurophysiological structures which will be isomorphic with [the various levels of learning].” The concept of “Neuro-Logical Levels” proposes that different “logical levels” are a function of different types of neurological organization and mobilize successively deeper commitments of neurological “circuitry.”

「精神」，或「集体意识」。这一场域也包括其他生物与存在的「神经系统」、或信息处理网络，甚至我们的环境。正如贝特森所断言：

The level of neurology that is mobilized when a person is challenged at the level of mission and identity, for instance, is much deeper than the level of neurology that is required to move his or her hand. To experience the environment, a person can passively adjust his or her sense organs. To take action in a particular environment, a person needs to mobilize more of his or her nervous system. In order to coordinate those actions in a complex sequence, such as dancing or driving an automobile, a person has to utilize even more of the nervous system. Forming and manifesting beliefs and values about capabilities, behaviors and the environment, requires an even deeper commitment of neurology (including those related to the “heart” and “guts”). A sense of self arises from a total mobilization of the nervous system at all the other levels. In general, then, higher levels of process mobilize a deeper commitment of the nervous system.

个体心智是内在的，却不只在身体之中。它也在身体之外的路径与信息之中内在；并且存在一个更大的心智，个体心智仅是它的一个子系统。这更大的心智堪比上帝，或许也正是人们所谓「上帝」之所指，但它仍内在于总的互联社会系统与行星生态之中。

Neurologically, “spiritual” level processes have to do with a type of “relational field” between our own nervous systems and those of other people, forming a type of larger, collective nervous system. The results of this field of interaction are sometimes referred to as a group “mind,” a group

总之，神经逻辑层次由以下「层级」的神经生理回路构成：

“spirit,” or a “collective consciousness.” This field also includes the “nervous systems,” or information processing networks, of other creatures and beings, and even our environment. As Bateson claimed:

| 场域/灵性 | 个体神经系统结合形成更大的系统 || A. 身份 | 神经系统整体——深层维生功能（如免疫系统、内分泌系统、网状系统）。 || B. 信念与价值 | 边缘系统与自主控制系统（如心率、瞳孔放大等）——无意识反应。 || C. 能力 | 皮质系统——半意识行动（眼动、姿态等） || D. 行为 | 运动系统（锥体束与小脑）——意识行动。 || E. 环境 | 周围神经系统——感觉与反射反应。 |

The individual mind is immanent but not only in the body. It is immanent also in pathways and messages outside the body; and there is a larger Mind of which the individual mind is only a subsystem. This larger Mind is comparable to God and is perhaps what people mean by “God,” but it is still immanent in the total interconnected social system and planetary ecology.

In summary, Neuro-Logical Levels are made of the following “hierarchy” of neurophysiological circuitry:

| Field/Spiritual | Individual nervous systems combining to form a larger system | | A. Identity | Nervous system as a whole—deep life sustaining functions (e.g., Immune system, endocrine system and reticular system). | | B. Beliefs & Values | Limbic and autonomic control system (e.g., heart rate, pupil dilation, etc.)—Unconscious responses. | | C. Capabilities | Cortical systems—Semi-conscious actions (eye movements, posture, etc.) | | D. Behaviors | Motor system (pyramidal & cerebellum)—Conscious actions. | | E. Environment | Peripheral nervous system—Sensations and reflex reactions. |

贝特森学习层次与迪尔茨神经逻辑改变层次的平行关系The Parallel Between Bateson’s Levels of Learning and Dilts’ Neuro-Logical Levels of Change

The way in which these levels interact with each other is directly parallel to Bateson’s levels of learning:

这些层次彼此互动的方式，与贝特森的学习层次直接平行：

  • A particular behavioral reaction to a particular environmental stimulus is essentially a reflex or habit—Learning 0.
  • Corrective change in behaviors in order to reach a particular outcome involves connecting that behavior to something beyond the environmental stimuli—some internal mental map, plan or strategy. This involves the exercise of a particular capability or the development of a new one—Learning I.
  • Developments in capabilities are stimulated and shaped by beliefs and values; which function to classify and categorize aspects of our mental maps, behaviors and environment and connect them to emotions and other motivational structures—Learning II.
  • Changes in beliefs and values would involve linking to a system beyond those beliefs and values (an identity) that they have been established to serve—Learning III.
  • Getting outside that system and connecting to a larger “system of systems” (i.e., “field” or “spirit”) would be necessary to achieve a change within a particular system or identity itself—Learning IV.
  • 对特定环境刺激的特定行为反应，本质上是一种反射或习惯——学习 0。
  • 为达成特定结果而对行为做出的校正性改变，涉及将该行为与超越环境刺激之物（某个内在心智地图、计划或策略）相连。这涉及运用某项能力或发展一项新能力——学习 I。
  • 能力的发展，受到信念与价值的刺激与塑造；信念与价值发挥作用，对我们有心智地图、行为与环境的面向进行分类，并将它们与情感及其他动机结构相连——学习 II。
  • 信念与价值的改变，会涉及连向那些信念与价值所建立起来为之服务的、某个超越它们的系统（一个身份）——学习 III。
  • 走出那个系统、连向一个更大的「系统的系统」（即「场域」或「灵性」），对于在特定系统或身份本身之内达成改变是必要的——学习 IV。

〔图 p92〕

foundation for my developments with Success Factor Modeling.

除了我早先呈现的贝特森应用逻辑类型与逻辑层次概念的例子外，他并未发展出任何具体应用（因此，我在 1986 年对挪威同事说了那番话）。然而，我确已发展出一个专门应用贝特森所定义之学习层次、以更新行为模式的过程。这一过程对贝特森的天才策略提供了独特的洞见。

Besides the examples of how Bateson applied the notions of logical types and logical levels that I have presented earlier, he did not develop any specific applications (thus, my comment to my Norwegian colleague in 1986). I have, however, developed a process for updating patterns of behavior that specifically applies Bateson’s levels of learning as he defined them. This process provides a unique insight into Bateson’s strategy of genius.

通过贝特森的学习层次更新行为模式Updating Patterns of Behavior through Bateson’s Levels of Learning

The following format applies Gregory Bateson’s Logical Levels of Learning to help identify and update patterns of behavior that may have become outdated and ineffective. It involves systematically moving from Learning 0 all the way to Learning IV.

以下格式应用格里高里·贝特森的学习逻辑层次，以帮助辨识并更新那些或许已过时且无效的行为模式。它涉及系统性地从学习 0 一路移动到学习 IV。

Learning I, II and III are like the rungs of a ladder that help us to achieve the possibility of Learning IV. This process demonstrates the various types of approaches and support that help people to make the types of adjustments and changes in perspective necessary to successfully accomplish each level of learning, building upon the insights and knowledge generated by each level to support the capacity of Learning IV.

学习 I、II 与 III 如同阶梯的梯级，帮助我们达成学习 IV 的可能性。这一过程演示了各类方法与支撑，帮助人们做出那种必要的、视角上的调整与改变，以成功完成每一学习层次，并建立在每一层次所生成的洞见与知识之上，以支撑学习 IV 的能力。

The process involves the following steps:

这一过程包含以下步骤：

  1. Think of a problem situation or relationship in which you continue to fall back into an old pattern of behavior, even though it is ineffective (Learning 0). Associate into an example of the experience and internally “relive” what it is like. Demonstrate or role play the
  1. 想一个你不断退回到某个旧行为模式、即便它无效的问题情境或关系（学习 0）。融入该经验的一个例子，并在内在「重活」它的样子。演示或角色扮演你在那处境中的

behavioral response that you have in that situation and identify the structure of the pattern or habit (i.e., blaming, giving in, freezing up, shrinking, making yourself invisible, etc.). Be aware of how you do this behaviorally. Pay particular attention to your posture, movements, body tension, breathing, etc.

行为反应，并辨识该模式或习惯的结构（即指责、屈服、僵住、退缩、让自己隐形等）。觉察你是如何在行为上做到这一点的。特别留意你的姿态、动作、身体紧绷、呼吸等。

  1. Take a step back from the situation and reflect upon this pattern of behavior. Notice how you are responding both mentally and physically in that situation. Explore how you could adjust or adapt your behavior (Learning I). Role play some possibilities exploring how you could vary the current behavior you are engaging in; i.e., exaggerate it, dampen it, shift it, etc.
  1. 从情境中退后一步，反思这一行为模式。注意你在那处境中心理与身体两方面是如何反应的。探索你可以如何调整或适应你的行为（学习 I）。角色扮演一些可能性，探索你如何能让当前所从事的行为有所变化；即，夸大它、抑制它、转移它等。
  2. 从情境中再退后一步，去到一个「观察者」位置，使你正「看着自己」处于问题情境中。
  1. Take a further step back from the situation and go to an “observer” position, such that you are “watching yourself” in the problem situation.

a. 注意你至今是如何将这情境归类或分类的（即危险、严肃、紧急、威胁等）。你一直持有关于这情境的什么信念？

a. Notice how you have been categorizing or classifying this situation up until now (i.e., dangerous, serious, urgent, threatening, etc.). What is the belief you have been holding about the situation?

b. 想另一个你能够以完全不同且更具资源性的方式行动或反应的时机与情境（学习 II）——例如平静、接纳、开放、居中（centered）等。融入一个你能够展现这另一类行为的情境。

b. Think of some other time and situation in which you were able to act or respond in a completely different and more resourceful way (Learning II)—e.g., calm, accepting, open, centered, etc. Associate into a situation in which you were able to enact this other class of behavior.

c. 创造一个通往问题情境的「信念之桥」：你拥有何种信念，使你在那另一情境中能够资源性地行动？为了支撑问题情境中这新的一类行为，你需要拥有什么信念？

c. Create a “belief bridge” to the problem situation: What is the belief that you have that allows you to act resourcefully in this other situation? What belief would you need to have in order to support the new class of behavior in the problem situation?

d. 重访问题情境，并「仿佛」你拥有这信念以及与之关联的不同行为类别般去行动。会有什么不同？

d. Revisit the problem situation and act “as if” you had this belief and the different class of behavior associated with it in the problem situation. What would be different?

  1. 再次退后，使你处于自身之外，反思你自己，以及你生命中一直可用的行为范围。考虑一种完全不同的系统、拥有完全不同行为范围（身份）的可能性，而那并不是你自己的（学习 III）。
  1. Step back again so that you are outside of yourself, reflecting on yourself and the range of behaviors that you have had available to you in your life. Consider the possibility of a completely different system with a completely different range of behavior (identity) that is not your own (Learning III).

a. 找一个人、动物或存在，他/她/它在那情境中会拥有与你完全不同的策略。为那行为系统找一个角色模范，并将自己完全放入「他/她的鞋子」（第二位置）。〔若有需要，创造一个「信念之桥」以进入那模范的知觉位置（即，为了能完全进入对方，你需要什么信念？）〕

a. Find a person, animal or being that would have a completely different strategy than you in that situation. Identify a role model for that system of behavior and put yourself fully “into his or her shoes” (second position). [If you need to, create a “belief bridge” to get into the perceptual position of the model (i.e., What belief would you need in order to be able to put yourself fully into the other?)]

b. 从角色模范的视角，你作为那模范对自己的隐喻是什么？作为那模范，你的「召唤」（calling）是什么？想一个你生命中帮助你拓展「自己是谁」之感知的赞助者（sponsor），并想象将自己放回问题情境，并「仿佛」你就是这另一个人般回应，运用你所创造的召唤与隐喻。

b. From the perspective of the role model, what is your metaphor for yourself as that model? What is your “calling” as that model? Think of a sponsor in your life who helped you to expand your perception of who you are and imagine putting yourself back into the problem situation and responding “as if” you were this other person, applying the calling and the metaphor you have created.

  1. 退到学习 III 位置之外。进入一种「不知道」的状态，在其中你感到居中且对一切可能性开放，
  1. Step back beyond the Learning III location. Enter a state of “not knowing” in which you feel centered and open to all possibilities,

不做任何评判或诠释。向格里高里·贝特森所称的「连接之模式」与「更大的心智」、以及爱因斯坦所称的「上帝的思想」与「宇宙」敞开自己。想一个你生命中帮助你拓宽「何为可能」之视野的觉醒者（awakener），为这状态创造一个锚定（anchor）或符号。运用这锚定或符号来保持这状态，退经学习的其他每一层次、再回到问题情境，并自发地行动。会是一种不属于任何当前行为类别系统的、你可以做的行为吗？（学习 IV）

without making any judgments or interpretations. Open yourself up to what Gregory Bateson called the “pattern which connects” and the “larger Mind,” and what Einstein referred to as “God’s thoughts” and “universe.” Think of an awakener in your life who helped you widen your view of what was possible. Create an anchor or symbol for this state. Using the anchor or symbol to hold the state, step back through each of the other levels of learning and back into the problem situation and act spontaneously. What is a behavior you could do that would not fit any current system of classes of behavior? (Learning IV)

〔图 p90〕five summary boxes stacked vertically: Learning 4 State of Openness and Connection to a "Larger Mind" (Unconscious/Field Mind); Learning 3 Modeling a Completely New Pattern from Someone Else; Learning 2 Different Pattern Coming from a Different Situation; Learning 1 Variation within the Pattern; Learning 0 Recurring Pattern of Behavior

贝特森与认识论Bateson and Epistemology

Given his passion to discover the deeper structures of our existence, it is not surprising that Bateson had a strong interest in epistemology. Epistemology is the discipline which systematizes the structures and processes which underlie human knowledge. The term epistemology comes from the Greek words epi (meaning “above” or “upon”), histanai (meaning to “set” or “place”) and logos (meaning “word” or “knowledge”)—i.e., “that upon which we set our knowledge.” An epistemology, then, is the fundamental system of distinctions and assumptions upon which one bases and generates all other knowledge. As Bateson defined it:

鉴于他发掘我们存在之更深层结构的激情，贝特森对认识论（epistemology）有浓厚兴趣，便不足为奇。认识论是一门将人类知识底层的结构与过程系统化的学科。认识论一词源自希腊语 epi（意为「之上」或「其上」）、histanai（意为「设定」或「安放」）与 logos（意为「词」或「知识」）——亦即，「我们将其知识安放于其上的东西」。因此，认识论是一个人为基础并生成所有其他知识所依据的、基本性的区分与假设系统。正如贝特森所定义的：

Epistemology is the history of the origins of knowledge; in other words how you know what you know.
认识论是知识之起源的历史；换言之，你如何知道你所知道的。

Beginning with questions about what we can know, and how we come to know what we think we know, epistemology moves to the question, “How do we know anything at all?” Bateson explained:

从关于我们能知道什么、以及我们如何得出我们所认为的所知的问题出发，认识论转向这个问题：「我们究竟如何能知道任何事物？」贝特森解释道：

Philosophers have recognized and separated two sorts of problem. There are first the problems of how things are, what is a person and what sort of world this is. These are the problems of ontology. Second there are the problems of how we know anything, or more specifically, how we know what sort of a world it is and what sort of creatures we are that can know something (or perhaps nothing) of this matter. These are problems of epistemology.
哲学家们已经辨认并分离出两类问题。首先是关于事物如何存在、人是什么、以及这是个怎样的世界的问题。这些是本体论（ontology）的问题。其次是关于我们如何知道任何事物、或更具体地说，我们如何知道这是个怎样的世界、以及我们是何种能够知道此事（或或许一无所知）的造物的问题。这些是认识论的问题。

In the natural history of the living human being, ontology and epistemology cannot be separated. His (commonly unconscious)

在活生生之人的自然史中，本体论与认识论无法分离。&#x4ED6;（通常无意识地）

beliefs about what sort of world it is will determine how he sees it and acts within it, and his ways of perceiving and acting will determine his beliefs about its nature. The living man is thus bound within a net of epistemological and ontological premises which—regardless of ultimate truth or falsity become partially self-validating for him.

关于这是个怎样世界的信念，将决定他如何看它、并在其中行动；而他感知与行动的方式，将决定他关于其本性的信念。因此，这个活人被束缚在一张认识论与本体论前提之网中——无论终极的真伪如何，这张网对他而言部分地自我确证（self-validating）。

The fact that epistemology is considered esoteric by many Westerners is a telling indicator of how little we have examined the foundation of our own beliefs, values, and perceptions, and the behaviors which flow from them. In fact, Bateson claimed:

认识论被许多西方人视为秘传（esoteric），这一事实是一个说明性的指标，显示我们多么少地检视过自身信念、价值与感知的基础，以及从中流出的行为。事实上，贝特森主张：

The major problems in the world are the result of the difference between how nature works and the way people think.
世界上的主要问题，源于自然运作的方式与人们思考的方式之间的差异。

Bateson's statement appears to be just as true now as when he pointed it out decades ago. When we are only aware of what we think and not of how we arrived at those conclusions we are in a type of trap created by our unconscious assumptions. Bateson counseled that we should be assertive with our questions but humble with respect to our answers. Far from being esoteric, Bateson argued that our awareness of our epistemologies was essential for our future survival as a species. As he explained:

贝特森的这句话，如今与他数十年前指出时一样真实。当我们只觉察到我们所想的、却不觉察我们是如何得出那些结论时，我们便陷入了一种由无意识假设所创造的陷阱。贝特森建议，我们应对自己的问题果决，却对自己的答案谦卑。贝特森主张，认识到我们的认识论，远非秘传，而是对我们作为一个物种的未来生存至关重要。正如他所解释的：

We create the world that we perceive, not because there is no reality outside our heads, but because we select and edit the reality we see to conform to our beliefs about what sort of world we live in. The man who believes that the resources of the world are infinite, for example, or that if something is good for you then the more of it the better, will not be able to see his errors, because he will not look for evidence of them. For a man to change the basic beliefs that determine his perception – his epistemological premises – he must first become aware that reality is not necessarily as he believes it to be. Sometimes the dissonance between reality and false beliefs reaches a point when it becomes impossible to avoid the awareness that the world no longer makes sense. Only then is it possible for the mind to consider radically different ideas and perceptions.
我们创造我们所感知的世界，并非因为没有我们头脑之外的现实，而是因为我们选择并编辑所见的现实，以符合我们关于自己活在何种世界中的信念。例如，相信世界资源无限的人，或相信若某物对你有益则越多越好的的人，将无法看见他的错误，因为他不会去寻找错误的证据。一个人要改变决定其感知——其认识论前提——的基本信念，他必须首先觉察到现实未必如他所信。有时，现实与错误信念之间的失调（dissonance）会达到一个临界点，那时便无法避免地觉察到世界不再说得通。唯有到那时，心智才 possibly 考虑截然不同的观念与感知。

Of course, the concern is that by the time the person recognizes that dissonance between reality and his or her false beliefs it may be too late to act correctively.

当然，令人担忧的是，当一个人认识到现实与他或她的错误信念之间的失调时，可能已太迟而无法做出校正性的行动。

I obviously happen to agree with Bateson and hope that this exploration of his strategies and epistemology can contribute to help create a more positive future for not only we humans as a species but for nature and our planet.

我显然恰好赞同贝特森，并希望这一对他策略与认识论的探索，能有助于为不仅我们人类这一物种、也为自然与我们的星球，创造一个更正向的未来。

结论：格里高里·贝特森与天才的模式Conclusion: Gregory Bateson and the Patterns of Genius

If we step back (as Bateson liked to do) and reflect on the life and work of Gregory Bateson, what becomes most striking is not simply the range of fields he influenced—anthropology, cybernetics, communication theory, psychiatry, ecology and psychology—but the strategy and patterns of thinking that made those contributions possible.

如果我们退后一步（正如贝特森所喜欢的），反思格里高里·贝特森的生命与工作，最引人注目的，并非单纯是他所影响的领域之广——人类学、控制论、沟通理论、精神医学、生态学、心理学——而是使那些贡献成为可能的、思考的策略与模式。

Bateson's genius did not arise from a single discovery or theory. Rather, it emerged from a meta-strategy for understanding life itself. His thinking integrated many of the patterns that characterize creative genius across

贝特森的天才并非源于单一的发现或理论。相反，它从一种理解生命本身的元策略中涌现。

disciplines. At the heart of Bateson's strategy was his relentless search for what he called the pattern which connects. Instead of focusing on individual elements or isolated phenomena, Bateson continually asked deeper questions:

他的思考整合了跨学科中创造性天才所特有的许多模式。贝特森策略的核心，是他对所谓连接之模式的不懈探寻。贝特森不聚焦于个别元素或孤立现象，而是不断提出更深层的问题：

  • What relationships link these phenomena?
  • What patterns repeat across different systems?
  • What differences make a difference?
  • 什么关系联结着这些现象？
  • 什么模式在不同系统中重复？
  • 什么差异造成了差异？

Through this lens, the anatomy of a crab, the dynamics of family communication, the learning patterns of dolphins, and the ecology of human societies could all be understood as expressions of similar underlying systemic processes.

透过这一透镜，螃蟹的解剖、家庭沟通的动力学、海豚的学习模式，以及人类社会的生态学，全都可以被理解为相似底层系统过程的表现。

系统性地思考：看见深层结构Thinking Systemically: Seeing the Deep Structure

One of Bateson's most fundamental patterns of genius was his ability to think systemically and perceive deep structure rather than surface structure. Where many people see separate disciplines, Bateson saw networks of interacting relationships. He viewed mind, body, society, and environment as parts of a larger cybernetic network in which feedback loops and patterns of interaction shaped behavior and learning.

贝特森最根本的天才模式之一，是他系统性地思考、并感知深层结构而非表层结构的能力。许多人看到各自独立的学科，贝特森看到的却是互动关系的网络。他将心智、身体、社会与环境，视为一个更大控制论网络的部件，其中反馈回路与互动模式塑造了行为与学习。

From this perspective:

从这一视角看：

  • schizophrenia could be understood in terms of communication patterns within families,
  • cultural conflict could be explained through symmetrical and complementary schismogenesis,
  • and learning itself could be described as changes in patterns of difference and feedback.
  • 精神分裂症可以从家庭内部的沟通模式来理解，
  • 文化冲突可以通过对称与互补的裂化生成来解释，
  • 而学习本身可以被描述为差异与反馈之模式的改变。

Bateson's ability to hold such complex systemic perspectives reflects another key pattern of genius: engaging multiple viewpoints simultaneously.

贝特森持有如此复杂系统视角的能力，反映了另一项关键的天才模式：同时容纳多重观点。

多重观点与心智的生态学Multiple Perspectives and the Ecology of Mind

Bateson believed that wisdom came from the ability to entertain multiple descriptions of the same phenomenon. A physicist, psychologist, and anthropologist could examine the same event yet interpret it differently because each operates from a different “map of the world.” This insight became foundational for many NLP principles, including the presupposition that “the map is not the territory.”

贝特森相信，智慧源于容纳对同一现象的多种描述的能力。一位物理学家、心理学家与人类学家可以审视同一事件，却因各自运作于不同的「世界观地图」而给出不同解读。这一洞见成了许多 NLP 原则的基础，包括「地图不是疆域」这一预设。

For Bateson, the richest and wisest maps are those that integrate many perspectives, expanding the range of choices available to a system. This capacity to shift perceptual positions and explore different viewpoints is another hallmark of the pattern of genius.

对贝特森而言，最丰富、最智慧地图，是那些整合了多重观点、拓展了系统可用选择范围的地图。这种转换知觉位置并探索不同视角的能力，是天才模式的另一标志。

在思维层次之间移动Moving Between Levels of Thinking

Another remarkable feature of Bateson's thinking strategy was his ability to move fluidly between different levels of abstraction—from broad philosophical questions to precise technical formulations. He might begin with a vague intuitive hunch—a feeling that two seemingly unrelated

贝特森思考策略另一非凡特征，是他在不同抽象层次之间灵活移动的能力——从宽广的哲学问题，到精确的技术定式。他可能始于一个模糊的直觉预感——一种对两个看似无关现象共享更深层结构的感觉。从那里，他会构建一个视觉的隐喻或类比，最终将那类比转译为某一特定科学领域的精确语言。这种在归类大小之间的移动——从抽象模式到具体应用——是创造性问题解决的决定性特征。

phenomena shared a deeper structure. From there he would construct a visual metaphor or analogy and finally translate that analogy into the precise language of a particular scientific field. This movement between chunk sizes—from abstract pattern to concrete application—is a defining characteristic of creative problem solving.

隐喻与溯因思维的力量The Power of Metaphor and Abductive Thinking

Central to Bateson's creative strategy was his use of metaphor and analogy as tools for exploration and discovery. He described his thinking as largely abductive, meaning that he searched for similarities in patterns across different domains. For example, the processes governing biological evolution might illuminate social systems, while insights from anthropology might help explain psychological phenomena.

贝特森创造性策略的核心，是他将隐喻与类比作为探索与发现的工具来使用。他描述自己的思考在很大程度上是溯因的，意即他搜寻不同领域间模式的相似性。例如，支配生物演化的过程可能照亮社会系统，而来自人类学的洞见可能帮助解释心理现象。

By transferring mental operations from one field to another, Bateson was able to generate innovative solutions that specialists confined to a single discipline might never have imagined. This capacity to think metaphorically reflects another pattern of genius: a highly developed imagination capable of envisioning new relationships between ideas.

通过将心智运作从一领域转移到另一领域，贝特森得以生成专精于单一学科者可能永远无法想象的创新解法。这种隐喻性思考的能力，反映了另一项天才模式：一种高度发达的、能构想观念间新关系的想象力。

平衡想象与严谨Balancing Imagination and Rigor

Perhaps one of Bateson's most profound insights was that creativity requires a balance between loose and strict thinking. Imagination generates new possibilities; rigor tests them against reality. Bateson often summarized this balance with the observation that:

或许贝特森最深刻的洞见之一，是创造力需要在松散与严格思维之间取得平衡。想象生成新的可能性；严谨则根据现实检验它们。贝特森常以如下观察来总结这一平衡：

  • imagination alone leads to chaos,
  • rigor alone leads to paralysis.
  • 唯想象导向混乱，
  • 唯严谨导向瘫痪。

True scientific progress emerges from the dynamic interplay between the two. This balance reflects yet another pattern of genius: the ability to treat mistakes as feedback rather than failure, continually refining ideas through interaction with experience.

真正的科学进展，从两者的动态互动中涌现。这一平衡反映了又一项天才模式：将错误视为反馈而非失败、通过与实践经验的互动持续精炼观念的能力。

差异与反馈的角色The Role of Difference and Feedback

Another key principle in Bateson's strategy was his insight that information is “a difference that makes a difference.” Perception itself arises from contrast:

贝特森策略的另一关键原则，是他的这一洞见：信息即「造成差异的差异」。感知本身从对比中升起：

  • hot versus cold
  • light versus dark
  • self versus other
  • 热对冷
  • 明对暗
  • 自我对他者

In cybernetic systems, these differences create feedback loops that enable systems to self-correct and maintain stability. Understanding these feedback processes allowed Bateson to explain phenomena ranging from ecological balance to family dynamics and cultural conflict.

在控制论系统中，这些差异创造出反馈回路，使系统得以自我校正并维持稳定。理解这些反馈过程，让贝特森得以解释从生态平衡到家庭动力学再到文化冲突的现象。

超越个体自我的联结感A Sense of Connection Beyond the Individual Self

Underlying Bateson's entire worldview was a profound sense of participation in something larger than the individual self—an ecological network of mind and life. He saw human beings not as isolated individuals but as nodes

贯穿贝特森整个世界观底层的，是一种深刻的参与于大于个体自我之物的感觉——一个心智与生命的生态网络。他视人类不是孤立的个体，而是活的关系之网中的节点

within a living web of relationships. Mind, in this view, is not confined to the brain but extends through the interactions between organisms and their environment.

心智在这一观点中不被限于大脑，而是延伸通过有机体与其环境之间的互动。

This perspective gave Bateson's work both scientific depth and philosophical resonance.

这一视角赋予贝特森的工作以科学深度与哲学共鸣。

贝特森天才的遗产The Legacy of Bateson's Genius

When we examine Bateson's work through the lens of the patterns of genius, a clear strategy emerges:

当我们透过天才模式的透镜审视贝特森的工作，一条清晰的策略浮现出来：

  1. Use imagination to explore possibilities.
  2. Search for the pattern which connects seemingly unrelated phenomena.
  3. Think systemically and focus on relationships rather than isolated elements.
  4. Shift perspectives and integrate multiple descriptions.
  5. Move between abstract insight and precise formulation.
  6. Balance intuition with rigorous analysis.
  7. Treat differences as information and feedback.
  8. Maintain awareness of the larger ecological system of which we are a part.
  1. 运用想象探索可能性。
  2. 搜寻连接看似无关现象的连接之模式。
  3. 系统性地思考，聚焦关系而非孤立元素。
  4. 转换视角并整合多重描述。
  5. 在抽象洞见与精确定式之间移动。
  6. 平衡直觉与严谨分析。
  7. 将差异视为信息与反馈。
  8. 保持对我们所从属的更大生态系统的觉察。

These patterns formed the foundation not only of Bateson's own insights but also of many of the principles that later shaped Neuro-Linguistic Programming and systemic approaches to change.

这些模式不仅构成了贝特森自身洞见的基础，也构成了后来塑造神经语言程序学（NLP）与系统改变取径的许多原则的基础。

In fact, I would like to make an invitation to the reader to go back to my earlier presentation of the Presuppositions of NLP and notice how many of them were directly related to Bateson's ideas.

事实上，我想邀请读者回到我早先对 NLP 预设的呈现，并注意其中有多少直接关联于贝特森的思想。

I also invite readers to revisit Bateson's seemingly cryptic response to my manuscript presented on (page?). Hopefully, it will be possible to notice what a pure representation his comments were of the underlying strategy of his genius.

我也邀请读者重访贝特森对我手稿那封看似 cryptic 的回信（见第几页？）。希望有可能注意到，他的评论是多么纯粹地呈现了他天才策略的底层。

连接我们所有人的模式The Pattern Which Connects Us All

Ultimately, Gregory Bateson's greatest contribution may be the invitation he offers us—to see the world as an interconnected system of relationships and patterns. When we learn to look for the patterns that connect ideas, people, and living systems, we begin to think in a way that is both more creative and more ecological. In doing so, we continue the very process that defined Bateson's genius: the ongoing search for the patterns that link mind, nature, and human experience into a single evolving whole.

最终，格里高里·贝特森最大的贡献，或许是他给我们的邀请——将世界视为一个互联的关系与模式系统。当我们学会去寻找连接观念、人与生命系统的那些模式，我们便开始以一种既更具创造力、也更具生态性的方式思考。如此，我们便延续了定义贝特森天才的那一过程：对将心智、自然与人类经验联结成一个单一演化之整体的那些模式的持续探寻。

〔图 p85〕整页插图 “What is the pattern that connects us all?”：中央一位张开双臂的人物，周围环绕螃蟹、龙虾、兰花、行星、太阳、鹦鹉、水母、蜘蛛、马、蘑菇、海豚、人脸、心形、星形等生物与符号，右下角署名 Antonio Meza

第三章Chapter 3

摩西·费登奎斯（Moshe Feldenkrais）Moshe Feldenkrais

运动中的智能Intelligence in Motion

〔图 p176〕

Moshe Feldenkrais

章节概览Chapter Overview

In this chapter, we will explore the work of Moshe Feldenkrais—an extraordinary innovator who expanded our understanding of human potential by revealing the deep intelligence inherent in the body. While many approaches to change focus primarily on the mind, Feldenkrais demonstrated that thinking, feeling, perception and movement are inseparably interconnected aspects of a single system.

在本章中，我们将探索摩西·费登奎斯（Moshe Feldenkrais）的工作——一位非凡的创新者，他通过揭示深植于身体之中的智能，拓展了我们对人类潜能的理解。尽管许多改变取径主要聚焦于心智，费登奎斯却证明：思考、感受、感知与运动是单一系统不可分割地相互连接的面向。

His genius lay not simply in the techniques he developed—Functional Integration and Awareness Through Movement—but in the way he approached human functioning itself. Rather than imposing change, he elicited it by increasing awareness and expanding choice.

他的天才不在于他所发展的技术——功能整合（Functional Integration）与通过运动觉察（Awareness Through Movement）——而在于他接近人类功能本身的方式。他不是强加改变，而是通过提升觉察、拓展选择来引发改变。

身心为一体系统Mind and Body as One System

One of the foundational patterns in this chapter is the recognition that mind and body are not separate domains, but expressions of a unified cybernetic system. Feldenkrais showed that every thought has a physiological correlate and every movement reflects patterns of thinking and feeling. A change in one part of the system inevitably influences the whole.

本章基础性的模式之一，是认识到心智与身体并非分离的领域，而是统一控制论系统的表现。费登奎斯证明，每一个思想都有生理上的对应物，而每一个动作都反映着思考与感受的模式。系统中一个部分的改变，必然影响整体。

This principle later became one of the foundations for what I developed as Somatic Syntax—the study of how movement patterns both reflect and shape cognitive processes.

这一原则后来成了我所称「体感句法」（Somatic Syntax）——研究运动模式如何既反映又塑造认知过程——的基础之一。

觉察是改变的入口Awareness as the Gateway to Change

Feldenkrais emphasized that awareness precedes transformation. Rather than correcting behavior directly, he focused on helping people become aware of how they were organizing their experience. As awareness increases, unconscious patterns become visible, new degrees of freedom emerge, and change occurs naturally through the discovery of new options. Without awareness, we simply repeat habitual patterns automatically.

费登奎斯强调，觉察先于转化。他不是直接纠正行为，而是聚焦于帮助人们觉察到他们如何组织自己的经验。随着觉察提升，无意识模式变得可见，新的自由度涌现，而改变通过发现新选项而自然发生。没有觉察，我们只是在自动地重复习惯模式。

「如何」高于「什么」The "How" Over the "What"

A hallmark of Feldenkrais' strategy is his focus on process rather than content. Instead of concentrating on what a person is doing, he explored how they were doing it. By changing the organization and structure of a pattern, the outcome changes organically. This subtle distinction becomes one of the most powerful themes in the chapter.

费登奎斯策略的一个标志，是他对过程而非内容的聚焦。他不是专注于一个人在做什么，而是探索他们如何在做。通过改变一个模式的组织与结构，结果便有机地改变了。这一微妙的区分，成了本章最有力的主题之一。

探索限制以发现可能Exploring Limits to Discover Possibilities

Feldenkrais had a unique approach to working with physical limitations. He approached them not as obstacles to overcome through force, but as opportunities for learning. His strategy involved:

费登奎斯处理身体限制的方式独树一帜。他不是将它们视为需用蛮力克服的障碍，而是视为学习的机会。他的策略包含：

  1. Exploring a function until a limitation was encountered
  2. Increasing awareness of the pattern surrounding that limitation
  3. Introducing small variations that opened new possibilities
  1. 探索一项功能，直到遭遇限制
  2. 提升对环绕该限制之模式的觉察
  3. 引入打开新可能性的微小变体

Through this process, limitations become doorways to increased flexibility, awareness and choice.

通过这一过程，限制变成了通往更大灵活性、觉察与选择的门径。

体感表征与「用身体思考」Somatic Representation and "Thinking with the Body"

Another important theme is the understanding that the body itself functions as a representational system.
