Chapter 4Chapter 4

加布里埃尔·罗斯 转化之波Gabrielle Roth The Wave of Transformation

!Gabrielle Roth — pencil portrait by RBDilts '26

!加布里埃尔·罗斯——RBDilts '26 铅笔肖像

Gabrielle Roth

加布里埃尔·罗斯

…a pathway to generative intelligence.

……一条通往生生不息的智慧的路径。

章节概览Chapter Overview

In this chapter, I examine the extraordinary genius of Gabrielle Roth — a visionary teacher, dancer and mapmaker of the human spirit whose work explored the relationship between movement, awareness and transformation. Roth recognized that the body is not simply something we carry with us. It is an intelligence and teacher in its own right. Through movement, rhythm and embodied awareness, she demonstrated how transformation unfolds not only cognitively or emotionally, but somatically and systemically.

本章中，我考察加布里埃尔·罗斯（Gabrielle Roth）非凡的天才——一位富有远见的教师、舞者与人类精神的制图者，其工作探索了运动、觉察与转化之间的关系。罗斯认识到，身体不只是我们随身带着的东西。它本身即是一种智能与老师。通过运动、节奏与具身觉察，她示范了转化如何不仅于认知或情绪层面发生，更于体感与系统层面展开。

Although Roth was not directly involved in the origins of NLP, her work resonates deeply with the systemic perspectives of Gregory Bateson and many principles that later became central to Third Generation NLP and Generative Change. Like Bateson, she was fascinated by "the pattern that connects," but while Bateson explored these patterns intellectually and socially, Gabrielle explored them through movement, rhythm and embodied experience.

尽管罗斯并未直接参与到 NLP 的起源之中，她的工作却与格里高里·贝特森的系统论视角，以及后来成为第三代 NLP 与生成式改变核心的诸多原则，产生深层共鸣。如同贝特森，她着迷于"连接之模式"（the pattern that connects），但贝特森是以智识与社会的方式探索这些模式，而加布里埃尔则是通过运动、节奏与具身经验来探索它们。

As you move through this chapter, you will discover that Gabrielle's work is about far more than dance and movement in and of themselves. It is about restoring connection between body, mind, emotion, spirit and the larger systems of which we are a part. Ultimately, it is about embracing movement as a pathway to generative intelligence.

随着你通读本章，你将发现加布里埃尔的工作远不止于舞蹈与运动本身。它关乎恢复身体、心智、情感、灵性与我们所身处之更大系统之间的联结。最终，它关乎接纳运动作为一条通往生生不息的智慧的路径。

身体作为转化的门户The Body as a Gateway to Transformation

One of the central themes in Gabrielle Roth's work is that movement can access forms of intelligence unavailable to the purely cognitive mind. She deeply understood that the body stores patterns of experience and identity, and that movement can unlock unconscious resources while expanding flexibility, creativity and choice.

加布里埃尔·罗斯工作中核心主题之一，是运动能够取用纯粹认知心智所无法触及的各类智能。她深切理解，身体储存着经验与身份的模式，而运动能够解锁无意识资源，同时拓展灵活性、创造力与选择。

Gabrielle continually emphasized that healing and transformation occur when energy begins to move again. This perspective became one of the inspirations for developments in Somatic Syntax and Third Generation NLP.

加布里埃尔不断强调：当能量重新开始流动，疗愈与转化便会发生。这一视角，成了体感句法与第三代 NLP 诸多发展的灵感之一。

三种"心智"的整合The Integration of Three "Minds"

A foundational principle throughout this chapter is the interaction between the cognitive mind, the somatic mind and the "field" mind that emerges through our connection with larger systems. Roth's genius was her ability to blend and align these intelligences into a unified transformational process. This integration lies at the heart of generative intelligence.

贯穿本章的一个基础性原则，是认知心智、体感心智，以及通过我们与更大系统之联结而涌现的"场域"心智之间的互动。罗斯的天才，在于她能将这几种智能融合、对齐为一个统一的转化过程。这一整合，正是生生不息的智慧的核心。

五律波®作为改变的地图The 5Rhythms® as a Map of Change

At the core of Gabrielle's work is the Wave of the 5Rhythms®: Flowing, Staccato, Chaos, Lyrical and Stillness. These are not simply movement styles, but deep patterns of transformation that mirror change across learning, creativity, leadership, healing and personal evolution.

加布里埃尔工作的核心，是五律波®（the Wave of the 5Rhythms®）：流动（Flowing）、断奏（Staccato）、混沌（Chaos）、抒情（Lyrical）与静止（Stillness）。它们不只是运动风格，更是转化的深层模式，映照出学习、创造力、领导力、疗愈与个人演化中各层次的改变。

Each rhythm invites a different way of moving and being:

每一种节奏都邀请一种不同的运动与存在方式：

  • Flowing develops grounding, receptivity and connection.
  • Staccato brings clarity, expression and healthy boundaries.
  • Chaos allows release of control and transformation of stuck patterns.
  • Lyrical opens creativity, freedom and new possibilities.
  • Stillness supports reflection, integration and connection to a larger field.
  • 流动（Flowing）发展扎根、接纳与联结。
  • 断奏（Staccato）带来清晰、表达与健康的边界。
  • 混沌（Chaos）允许释放控制、转化卡住的模式。
  • 抒情（Lyrical）开启创造力、自由与新的可能性。
  • 静止（Stillness）支持反思、整合，以及与更大场域的联结。

By moving through these rhythms, people access deeper layers of awareness, release emotional blockages and develop greater flexibility, resilience and authenticity. Transformation happens through embodied participation rather than analysis or words.

通过这些节奏而运动，人们得以取用更深的觉察层次，释放情绪阻塞，并发展出更强的灵活性、复原力与真实性。转化通过具身的参与而发生，而非通过分析或言语。

〔图 p207〕top-right — Five dancers in motion, each labelled with one of the 5Rhythms (Flow / Staccato / Chaos / Lyrical / Stillness), ink-and-grayscale illustration by Antonio Meza

运动作为"活的语言"Movement as a "Living Language"

Gabrielle described movement as "a living language." In this chapter, we explore how gesture, posture and movement function symbolically and how the body itself operates as a representational system. Movement, in this sense, becomes more than physical action. It becomes communication with ourselves, with others and with the larger fields of which we are a part. This perspective forms a direct bridge between dance, Somatic Syntax and embodied transformation.

加布里埃尔将运动描述为"一种活的语言"。在本章中，我们探索姿态、体态与运动如何以象征的方式运作，以及身体本身如何作为一个表征系统而运作。在此意义上，运动不止是身体动作。它成为与我们自己、与他人、与我们所身处之更大场域的沟通。这一视角，构成了舞蹈、体感句法与具身转化之间的一座直接桥梁。

进入开阔空间Going Into the Open Space

One of Gabrielle's most elegant teachings was the practice of moving into the "open space" — including what she described as the "billion miles of unexplored wilderness between the head and feet of any given person." For Roth, natural leaders instinctively move toward unexplored possibilities. Innovation and generative intelligence emerge when we stop repeating familiar patterns and learn to sense openings within systems. This principle becomes a powerful metaphor for creativity, leadership and transformation itself.

加布里埃尔最优雅的教导之一，是走入"开阔空间"（open space）的练习——包括她所形容的"任何一个人头部与双脚之间、那数十亿英里的未探索荒野"。对罗斯而言，天生的领导者会本能地迈向未被探索的可能性。当我们停止重复熟悉的模式、学会去感知系统之中的开口时，创新与生生不息的智慧便涌现出来。这一原则，成了创造力、领导力与转化本身的一个有力隐喻。

更深的邀请The Deeper Invitation

Gabrielle's work is transformative because it invites us beyond habitual action and cognitive control into a deeper relationship with movement, awareness and presence. It reveals where energy has become constrained while simultaneously opening pathways toward greater freedom and wholeness.

加布里埃尔的工作之所以具有转化力，是因为它邀请我们超越习惯性的行动与认知控制，进入与运动、觉察与临在之间更深的关系。它揭示出能量在何处受到了制约，同时敞开通往更大自由与完整性的路径。

For me personally, Gabrielle Roth's work became one of the essential bridges between movement, somatic intelligence and generative transformation. Along with Gregory Bateson and Moshe Feldenkrais, she profoundly influenced my understanding of how change unfolds through living systems.

就我个人而言，加布里埃尔·罗斯的工作，成了运动、体感智能与生成式转化之间一座本质的桥梁。与格里高里·贝特森和摩西·费登奎斯一道，她深刻影响了我对于改变如何在生命系统中展开的理解。

Gabrielle reminds us that transformation is not something we force. It is something we move with. Her work teaches us how to connect, express, let go, create and reconnect with something larger than ourselves. And perhaps most importantly, she reminds us that movement is not separate from life itself. How we move … is how we live.

加布里埃尔提醒我们：转化不是我们强行制造的东西。它是我们与之共舞的东西。她的工作教导我们如何联结、表达、放下、创造，并与大于我们自身之物重新联结。或许最重要的是，她提醒我们：运动并不独立于生命本身。我们如何运动……便是我们如何生活。

加布里埃尔·罗斯Gabrielle Roth

Gabrielle Roth (1941 – 2012) was an American dancer and musician in the world music and trance dance genres, with a special interest in shamanism. She created the 5Rhythms® approach to movement in the late 1970s. There are now hundreds of 5Rhythms® teachers worldwide who use her approach in their work, and hundreds of others who have been influenced by her developments and philosophy. Roth also founded an experimental theatre company in New York, wrote three books and created over twenty albums of music with her band The Mirrors.

加布里埃尔·罗斯（Gabrielle Roth，1941–2012）&#x662F;一位美国舞者与音乐家，活跃于世界音乐与迷幻舞蹈（trance dance）领域，对萨满传统有特别兴趣。她在 1970 年代末创造了五律波®（5Rhythms®）运动体系。如今全球有数百位五律波®教师运用她的方法工作，另有数百人受到她的发展与哲学影响。罗斯还在纽约创立了一家实验剧团，写了三本书，并与她的乐队"镜子"（The Mirrors）创作了二十余张音乐专辑。

Earlier in her life, Roth taught movement in rehabilitation centers. She injured her knee in a skiing accident at 26 and was told that she needed surgery and wouldn't dance again. In the 1970s she moved to Big Sur in California, joining a group at the Esalen Institute. She found that her body healed itself through dance, despite what the doctors had said. Gestalt Therapy founder Fritz Perls asked her to teach dance at the Esalen Institute and she set out to find a structure for dance as a transformative process. Out of her work at Esalen she designed the "Wave" of the 5Rhythms®.

早年，罗斯曾在康复中心教授运动。她 26 岁时在一次滑雪事故中伤了膝盖，被告知需要手术、且再也无法跳舞。1970 年代她搬到加州大苏尔（Big Sur），加入伊莎兰学院（Esalen Institute）的一个团体。她发现，尽管医生如此断言，她的身体却通过舞蹈自我疗愈了。完形治疗（Gestalt Therapy）创始人弗里茨·皮尔斯（Fritz Perls）请她在伊莎兰学院教授舞蹈，于是她着手为舞蹈作为一种转化过程寻找一种结构。在伊莎兰的工作之中，她设计出了五律波®的"波浪"（Wave）。

〔图 p205〕top-right — cartoon of doctor/woman dialogue ("Basically, you will never dance again" / "My body seems to have a different opinion!"), ink illustration by Antonio Meza

加布里埃尔·罗斯与 NLP 的联结The Connection of Gabrielle Roth to NLP

While Roth had no direct influence on the early developments of NLP, she had important connections with two of the key figures at the foundations of NLP — Fritz Perls and Gregory Bateson. She had a particularly close relationship with Bateson in the late 1970s while he was Scholar in Residence at Esalen. In her book Maps to Ecstasy (pp 24-25) she wrote:

尽管罗斯对 NLP 的早期发展并无直接影响，她却与 NLP 奠基中的两位关键人物——弗里茨·皮尔斯与格里高里·贝特森——有着重要的联结。1970 年代末，当贝特森作为驻院学者（Scholar in Residence）在伊莎兰时，她与他关系尤为密切。在她的《狂喜地图》（Maps to Ecstasy，pp. 24-25）一书中，她写道：

He was dying of cancer, and he was doing it openly, bravely, gracefully. He participated in several of my ritual theater labs, and we even co-led a workshop we called "The Shaman and the Anthropologist," his last appearance as a teacher.
他正死于癌症，而他以开放、勇敢、优雅的姿态面对它。他参与了我的数个仪式剧场实验室，我们甚至共同带领了一场我们称为"萨满与人类学家"的工作坊——那是他作为教师的最后一幕。
Gregory was one of the most inspired and inspiring individuals I ever knew. Many powerful teachers had appreciated my work and recommended it to their students, but Gregory actually did it. He surrendered himself to it totally. Seventy-seven years old, his lungs shot, his feet so swollen he could barely walk, he never missed a beat, much less a session. My workshops are intensely physical, and yet this frail giant explored all the phases of the movements and immersed himself in the massage and ritual theater work. He was able to play with his prodigious intellect and vast knowledge and simply be in what he was doing. His mind was both full and empty. Bateson recognized that Roth was exploring through the body many of the systemic dynamics that he had identified in his work with cultures. As Roth goes on to explain: > Gregory and I — the thinker and the dancer, a most unlikely duo — met on a common ground, coming from different directions. We had both spent our lives investigating what Gregory called the patterns which connect. I call them maps, maps to choreograph our energy and lead us to ecstasy, wholeness. Gregory's work was intellectual, mine physical. I'm sure he understood mine better than I his. But his validation of my intuitive discoveries was a crucial inspiration, and it was he who first urged me to write this book.
格里高里是我所认识最有灵感、也最能激发灵感的人之一。许多有影响力的老师赏识我的工作并向他们的学生推荐，但格里高里是真正去做了。他全然投身其中。七十七岁，肺已毁败，双脚肿胀到几乎无法行走，他从未漏掉一拍，更别提一场研讨了。我的工作坊极度耗费体力，然而这位脆弱的巨匠却探索了运动的所有阶段，沉浸于按摩与仪式剧场的工作之中。他既能玩味自己广博的智识与海量的知识，又能 simply be in 他所做的事。他的心智既充实又空灵。贝特森认识到，罗斯正通过身体探索许多他曾在文化研究中辨识出的系统动力。正如罗斯进一步解释的那样：> 格里高里与我——思想者与舞者，一对最不可能的组合——从不同的方向汇聚于共同的土壤。我们毕生都在探究格里高里所谓的连接之模式。我称之为地图，用以编排我们的能量、引领我们走向狂喜与完整的地图。格里高里的工作是智识性的，我的则是身体性的。我确信他比我更懂我的工作。但他对我直觉性发现的确认，是关键的灵感；正是他最初敦促我写下这本书。

In addition to her connection and resonance with Bateson, Roth has been a major influence on a number of my development of Third Generation NLP, especially with respect to what I referred to as "Somatic Syntax" in the previous chapter. In fact, she and Moshe Feldenkrais were two of the major sources for principles and applications related to the somatic mind in Third Generation NLP. (See NLP II: The Next Generation — Chapter 2.)

除了与贝特森的联结与共鸣，罗斯对我在第三代 NLP 的诸多发展也有重大影响，尤其是上一章我所称之为"体感句法"的部分。事实上，她与摩西·费登奎斯，是第三代 NLP 中与体感心智相关的原则与应用的两大源头。（参见《NLP II：下一代》——第 2 章。）

第三代 NLP 与三心智Third Generation NLP and the Three Minds

Third generation NLP has been developing since the 1990s. Third generation NLP emphasizes whole system change and can be applied to organizational and cultural development as well as to individuals, families and teams.

第三代 NLP 自 1990 年代起不断发展。第三代 NLP 强调全系统的改变，可应用于组织与文化的发展，亦可应用于个人、家庭与团队。

All generations of NLP focus on the structure and functioning of "mind" (this is the essence of "Neuro-Linguistic Programming"). Earlier generations of NLP, however, placed attention primarily on the cognitive mind. Third generation NLP expands to include both somatic processes and larger system dynamics (i.e. "field") in the total "unit of mind." Thus, third generation NLP works with the interaction between three different intelligences or "minds":

所有世代的 NLP 都聚焦于"心智"的结构与运作（这正是"神经-语言程序学"的本质）。然而，较早世代的 NLP 主要将注意力置于认知心智之上。第三代 NLP 将体感过程与更大的系统动力（即"场域"）一并纳入整体的"心智单元"之中。因此，第三代 NLP 运作于三种不同智能或"心智"之间的互动：

  1. A cognitive mind that emerges from the brain
  2. A somatic mind centered in the body
  3. A "field" mind that comes from our connection and relationships with larger systems around us
  1. 源自大脑的认知心智
  2. 以身体为中心的体感心智
  3. 源自我们与周遭更大系统之联结与关系的"场域"心智

Third generation NLP aspires to develop and sustain an organic relationship of balance and alignment between these three minds in order to produce a deeper and more multidimensional intelligence. As we shall see, the ability to integrate these three intelligences (especially somatic intelligence) was the essence of Gabrielle Roth's genius. As my co-authors of NLP II and I wrote:

第三代 NLP 致力于发展并维系这三种心智之间一种有机的、平衡与对齐的关系，以产生一种更深、更具多维度的智能。正如我们将看到的，整合这三种智能（尤其是体感智能）的能力，正是加布里埃尔·罗斯天才的精髓。正如《NLP II》的合著者与我写道：

We want to acknowledge: Gabrielle Roth, creator of the 5Rhythms® (who shares a common mentorship with Gregory Bateson), for continuing to champion the deep importance of movement and connection with the body as key to the process of change.
我们愿致意：五律波®的创造者加布里埃尔·罗斯（她与格里高里·贝特森共享同一位导师），感谢她持续捍卫运动与身体联结作为改变过程之关键的重要地位。

我与加布里埃尔·罗斯的个人经历My Personal Experience with Gabrielle Roth

I first became aware of Gabrielle and her work through my wife Deborah (years before we fell in love and married). It was the mid-1990s and Deborah was studying with Roth to become a 5Rhythms teacher. I was immediately impressed by the elegance and depth not only of the 5Rhythms but of Gabrielle's teachings and her ability to model deeper structures that could make a profoundly transformational impact.

我最初是通过我的妻子黛博拉（Deborah）而知晓加布里埃尔及其工作的（那是在我们相爱、结婚之前数年）。那是 1990 年代中期，黛博拉正随罗斯学习，以成为一位五律波®教师。我立刻被五律波®——以及加布里埃尔的教导、和她建模更深结构的能力——所展现的优雅与深度所打动；这些更深结构能产生深远的转化性影响。

The first time I met Gabrielle in person, she walked through the door of a restaurant where we were going to have dinner with a group of other people. As she came through the door she tripped and started to tumble. I was able to catch her before she fell. With a grateful and playful smile she immediately said, "I think I'm falling for you." That was an iconic example of her wit and her capacity to create quick connections. To make a pun out of something that wasn't initially verbal — at the same time that she was actually falling — is an example of her ability to quickly see things from multiple perspectives.

我第一次与加布里埃尔面对面，是在一家餐厅门口——当时我们正要与一群人共进晚餐。她进门时绊了一下，开始踉跄要摔倒。我在她倒地之前扶住了她。她带着感激而顽皮的微笑，立刻说："我想我是爱上你了（falling for you）。"那是她机智、以及迅速建立联结之能力的标志性例证。将一件起初并非言语之事——在她实际正摔倒的同时——化为一个双关语，正体现了她从多重视角迅速看事物本质的能力。

Later that evening during dinner we had a rich and lively conversation about our common mentor Gregory Bateson. She shared with me how he had told her that she was "obsessed with the pattern that connects" — a great compliment from Gregory and absolutely true of Gabrielle. The foundation of Roth's genius was finding the pattern that connects on a deeply energetic level. In my view, she was a type of "Bateson of the body."

当晚晚餐时，我们关于我们共同的导师格里高里·贝特森，进行了一场丰富而生动的对话。她与我分享：贝特森曾告诉她，她"痴迷于连接之模式"——这是格里高里极高的赞誉，也完全契合加布里埃尔。罗斯天才的根基，在于在极深的生命能量层次上找到连接之模式。在我看来，她是某种意义上的"身体的贝特森"。

Our mutual feeling of appreciation and admiration for Gregory created a strong resonance between us. I left that first meeting with a powerful sense of having connected with a "kindred spirit."

我们彼此对格里高里的赏识与钦佩，在我们之间创造了强烈的共鸣。那次初遇之后，我带着一种与"同道灵魂"相连的强大感受离去。

Over the following years, I had a number of other lovely connections with Gabrielle and had the opportunity to experience several programs with her in New York and Paris that incorporated various of her "maps," as she called them. I am grateful that, like Gregory Bateson did at Esalen, I had the chance to experience Roth's genius first-hand. I have also had the opportunity to explore Gabrielle's approach to movement and dance with a number of other teachers trained by Roth (including my wife Deborah, of course) as well as with her son Jonathan.

此后数年，我与加布里埃尔还有过数次美好的联结，并有机会在纽约与巴黎体验她数个融入了她所谓"地图"的研习项目。我心存感激：如同格里高里·贝特森在伊莎兰那样，我得以 firsthand 体验罗斯的天才。我也曾与多位受罗斯训练的教师在运动与舞蹈上探索她的方法（当然包括我的妻子黛博拉），以及与她的儿子乔纳森（Jonathan）一同。

One such program, titled Cycles, maps out the different stages of life from birth to death. [Other maps include that of emotions (Heartbeat) and of the ego and psyche (Mirrors).] In addition to dancing various rhythms, activities in Cycles included many creative uses of individual and collective movement, such as what Gabrielle referred to as "ritual theater."

其中一个名为循环（Cycles）的项目，勾勒出从出生到死亡的生命不同阶段。〔其他地图还包括情绪地图（Heartbeat，心跳）与自我/心灵地图（Mirrors，镜子）。〕除了舞动各种节奏，循环中的活动还大量创造性地运用个人与集体的运动，例如加布里埃尔所谓的"仪式剧场"。

通过仪式剧场结合多种智能Combining Multiple Intelligences Through Ritual Theater

As one example of ritual theater, participants form small groups and take turns being the "lead actor." The lead actor might be instructed to tune

作为仪式剧场的一个例子：参与者分成小组，轮流担任"主角"。

into their sense of the "archetypal mother" and express that experience in a repetitive movement. The lead actor will then choose a group member to be in that role and take on that particular repetitive movement. Standing in front of that person (with their back to the person), the lead actor is then instructed to tune into whatever sense of their grandmother they have (even if they have no conscious memory of her) and express that as another repetitive movement. The lead actor is then to select another group member to take on that role and movement. Standing in front of that individual, the lead actor is next instructed to tune into their mother and express that as yet another repetitive movement (again irrespective of conscious memory), selecting another group member to take that role and movement pattern. Finally, the lead actor stands in front of the entire constellation they have created, tunes into the field it generates, and lets their body express whatever comes through it. Remaining group members become an audience that hold the space for all of the actors.

主角可能被引导去调频进入其"原型母亲"（archetypal mother）的感受，并以一种重复性的运动来表达那经验。主角随后会挑选一位组员来扮演该角色、并承担那特定的重复性运动。站在那人面前（背对着那人），主角接着被引导去调频进入其祖母的任何感受（即便她对祖母毫无有意识的记忆），并将其表达为另一种重复性运动。主角再挑选另一位组员来承担该角色与运动。站在那人面前，主角接下来被引导去调频进入其母亲，并将其表达为又一种重复性运动（同样不论有无有意识记忆），再挑选另一位组员承担该角色与运动模式。最后，主角站在她所创造的整个星座（constellation）之前，调频进入它所产生的场域，让身体表达任何流经它的东西。其余组员成为观众，守护着所有演员所在的空间。

A similar ritual theater can be done with the archetypal father, grandfather and father, cycling through all of the group members until each has been in every role.

类似的仪式剧场，也可以用原型父亲、祖父与父亲来做，循环经过所有组员，直到每个人都演过每个角色。

Needless to say, with the proper level of rapport and psychological safety, such activities frequently produces profound insights, understanding, connection and catharsis; not only for the lead actor but the other actors and even audience members as well. These activities are an example of Gabrielle's genius in blending and aligning cognitive, emotional, somatic and field intelligences into a single process — with movement as the primary modality. As she said:

毋庸讳言，在具备适当的融洽关系与心理安全感之下，这类活动常常为——不仅主角，其他演员，乃至观众——带来深刻的洞见、理解、联结与宣泄（catharsis）。这些活动是加布里埃尔天才的一个例证：将认知、情绪、体感与场域智能融合、对齐于单一过程之中——以运动作为首要的模态。正如她所言：

Disease is inertia. Healing is movement. If you put the body in motion, you will change. You are meant to move … It's when you stop moving through life that you get caught out of place … Moving completely through your life makes you whole … Set the body in motion and the psyche will heal itself.
疾病是惰性。疗愈是运动。若你将身体置于运动之中，你就会改变。你生来就是要移动的……正是当你停止在生命中移动时，你才脱离了正位……完整地穿越你的生命，使你成为完整……让身体动起来，心灵便会自我疗愈。

Roth's assertion that putting the body in motion would lead the psyche healing itself expresses her deep trust in the self-organizing nature of the human body and nervous system. Like Bateson, she recognized that living systems tend toward naturally self-organizing principles when properly supported.

罗斯断言"让身体动起来将引领心灵自我疗愈"，这表达了她对人体与神经系统自组织本性的深切信任。如同贝特森，她认识到：当得到恰当地支持，生命系统会趋向自然自组织的原理。

探索头部与双脚之间的疆域Exploring the Territory Between the Head and the Feet

Ritual theater, of course, is only one of the many creative and powerful types of processes that Gabrielle generated. Another example is what she called "Repertory." In this exercise, a person gets together with a partner. One of the pair begins to dance to a particular rhythm and theme, letting that theme express through their body. Their partner observes their movement, noticing which parts of their body are actively involved and which are uninvolved or immobile. The partner then gently directs attention to that part of the body through a subtle touch (say, to the lower back, shoulder blades, elbows, neck, etc.). Following the NLP principle that "energy flows where attention goes," that part of the person's body suddenly becomes active, alive and engaged with respect to the particular theme.

仪式剧场当然只是加布里埃尔所创造的众多创造性、强有力过程类型之一。另一个例子是她所谓的"剧目"（Repertory）。在这项练习中，一个人与一位搭档聚在一起。其中一方开始随某一特定节奏与主题起舞，让那主题通过其身体表达出来。其搭档观察他的运动，留意身体的哪些部位积极参与、哪些未参与或僵滞不动。搭档随后通过轻微的触碰（比如，触碰下背、肩胛、手肘、颈部等），温和地将注意力导向身体那个部位。遵循"能量流向注意之处"的 NLP 原则，那人身体的那个部位便突然活跃、鲜活，并参与到该特定主题之中。

expanded awareness, new insights, deeper connection and increased flexibility and resilience. As Roth maintained:

拓展的觉察、新的洞见、更深的联结，以及增强的灵活性与复原力。正如罗斯所坚持的：

There is a billion miles of unexplored wilderness between the head and feet of any given person. That's the place from which we depart. We want to go into that wilderness and find out what is happening. And it is different for each and every one of us.
在任何一个人头部与双脚之间，有数十亿英里的未探索荒野。那便是我们出发的地方。我们要进入那荒野，弄清正在发生什么。而这对我们每一个人，都是不同的。

Roth's statement is another iconic description of the strategies of genius: going into an unexplored territory and mapping it.

罗斯的这句话，是天才的策略另一个标志性的描述：进入一片未被探索的疆域，并将其制图。

进入"开阔空间"Going into the 'Open Space'

In fact, another one of Roth's simple but profoundly powerful movement practices is what she called the "porpoise dance." The foundation of this practice involves moving into the 'open space' around you. This space may be in front of you, behind you, to your left, to your right or any of the 360º area around you. When you are in a room full of other people all following the same instruction this becomes a very interesting and sometimes challenging exercise. It requires complete presence in the body and a somatic awareness of the space surrounding you.

事实上，罗斯简单却极其有力的一项运动练习，是她所谓的"鼠海豚之舞"（porpoise dance）。这项练习的根基，涉及走入你周遭的"开阔空间"。这空间可能在你前方、后方、左侧、右侧，或环绕你的 360º 任意方位。当你身处满屋同样遵循同一指令的其他人之中，这便成了一项非常有趣、有时颇具挑战的练习。它要求完全临在于身体，以及对环绕你的空间具备一种体感的觉察。

Gabrielle would typically start out slowly, having people look around them wherever they were in the room, identify where was the most open space around them and take a step into it. Of course, when everyone else is doing the same thing, the whole pattern of space in the environment immediately changes. Two people may even realize that they are trying to step into the same space (which may have been in front of one of them and behind the other one). So, space that was open becomes filled. Space that was filled become open. To take the next step, the open space may have shifted such that you need step backwards or in some other direction.

加布里埃尔通常起步很慢：让人们环顾所处的房间，辨认周遭哪里是最开阔的空间，并迈步走入其中。当然，当其他所有人都在做同样的事，环境中的整体空间模式便立刻改变。两个人甚至可能意识到，他们正试图踏入同一处空间（那空间对其中一人或许在前方，对另一人则在后方）。于是，原本开阔的空间被填满，原本填满的空间变得开阔。要迈出下一步，开阔空间或许已经转移，你需要向后、或朝某个别的方向迈步。

The same phenomenon happens when everyone takes the next steps. The pattern of space changes. You might find yourself stepping sideways or at a completely different angle than your previous step.

当每个人都迈出下一步时，同样的现象发生。空间模式改变。你可能发现自己朝侧向、或以与先前步伐完全不同的角度迈步。

As people began to get used to the process and move without bumping into each other, Roth would have everyone speed up a bit and begin to walk into the unfolding open spaces around them. As they mastered that, she would have them speed up and walk faster and faster. A key condition, however, was that if anyone felt that they were beginning to tense up or their breathing began to change significantly they were to slow down a bit.

当人们开始习惯这过程、且不再彼此碰撞，罗斯会让所有人稍加快速度，开始走入他们周遭不断展开的"开阔空间"。当他们掌握之后，她会让他们加速，越走越快。然而一个关键条件是：如果任何人感觉开始紧绷、或呼吸开始显著改变，他们就应当稍放慢一些。

As you speed up in this exercise you are very quickly no longer able to track what is happening and where to go next with your conscious cognitive mind. To succeed you must shift to another whole mode of somatic, systemic awareness and intelligence. When that happens, this practice creates a room full of people weaving gracefully between each other; sometimes spinning, gliding sideways, gently brushing past another moving body in a natural dance. The interaction between the people in the room begins to resemble that of a pod of porpoises in the open ocean effortlessly flowing in and around one another in organic harmony. It is truly a unique and beautiful experience.

当你在这练习中加速，你很快便无法再用有意识的认知心智去追踪发生了什么、下一步该往哪里去。要成功，你必须切换到另一种全然不同的体感、系统性觉察与智能。当那发生时，这练习创造出一个满屋的人彼此优雅交织的空间；有时旋转，有时侧向滑过，轻柔地擦过一个另一个移动的身体，如同一场自然之舞。房间中人们之间的互动，开始类似于一群在开阔海洋中、彼此毫不费力地流转穿梭于对方之内与之间的鼠海豚。那确是一种独特而美丽的经验。

walking in the opposite direction of the rest of the group to break it up.

（朝与群体其余人相反的方向行走，以打破这循环。）

〔图 p200〕

People moving into "the open space"… following this simple principle they manage to occupy the space, move, dance, and care for each other at the same time.

另一方面，如果人们无法切换到这种不同的智能形式，他们便开始遵循一种可预测的、只是在房间里无意识地绕圈行走的模式。那不需要任何真正的觉察。当每个人都朝同一方向绕圈行走，空间总在你正前方敞开。那或许容易，但显然毫无生成性。罗斯称此为"羊群之舞"（sheep dance）。（它也可被称为"僵尸之舞"。）即便人们被点明他们在做什么，若他们不做出意识的转换，也很快会跌回同一模式。常常需要一位反叛的个体突然转身、开始朝相反方向行走，才能打破它。

On the other hand, if people are unable to shift to this different form of intelligence, they begin to follow a predictable pattern of simply mindlessly walking in a circle around the room. It requires no real awareness. When everyone walks in the same direction in a circle, the space is always opening right in front of you. It may be easy, but there is clearly no generativity. Roth calls this the "sheep dance." (It could also be called the "zombie dance.") Even when people are made aware of what they are doing, they can quickly fall back into the same pattern if they do not make the shift of consciousness. It frequently takes a rebellious individual to suddenly turn around and start

运动与领导力Movement and Leadership

Going into the open space and engaging somatic systemic awareness is a fundamental quality of generative intelligence. Roth points out that is also a quality of all natural leaders. They are constantly seeking out the open space.

进入开阔空间、并调动体感系统性的觉察，是生生不息的智慧的一项基本特质。罗斯指出，这也是一切天然领导者的特质。他们持续地寻觅开阔空间。

In fact, I will sometimes use the porpoise dance process as a way to identify leaders in a group or team. As an example, I was once consulting to an e-learning company that was undergoing a major reorganization. Rapid changes in technology and customer needs required them to reinvent themselves on many levels. To succeed would clearly require a high degree of generative intelligence from a majority of the employees.

事实上，我有时会运用鼠海豚之舞这一过程，来辨识一个群体或团队中的领导者。举个例子：我曾为一家正经历重大重组的在线教育公司担任顾问。技术与客户需求的快速变化，要求他们在许多层面上自我重塑。要成功，显然需要大多数员工具备高度的生生不息的智慧。

A meeting was organized in a hotel ballroom that was attended by all of the company's employees. The CEO described the situation and the types of changes that needed to be creatively addressed by people at every level in the company. I then led the employees through a number of activities whose purpose was stimulate and align the employees to begin to creatively approach and address the types of changes that would be important given whatever their role was in the company. One of these activities was the porpoise dance, guided by Deborah.

一场会议在一家酒店的宴会厅举行，公司全体员工出席。CEO 描述了局势，以及公司各层级人员都需创造性应对的各类改变。随后我带领员工进行若干活动，目的是激发并 alignment 员工，让他们开始创造性地应对、处理那些鉴于其角色而至关重要的改变类型。其中一项活动，便是由黛博拉引导的鼠海豚之舞。

As people moved with respect to each other through the ballroom, the astute (and somewhat surprised) CEO said he was suddenly able to see clearly at a single glance – by watching how people moved with respect to each other – who was onboard with the change and who would be the natural leaders

当人们在宴会厅中彼此相对移动，那位敏锐（且略感惊讶）的 CEO 说，他单凭一眼——通过观察人们彼此之间如何移动——便能清晰地看出，谁契合这改变、谁又会成为转化的天然领导者，

of the transformation, regardless of their training, experience, current role, etc. The CEO applied this insight to reform the various teams within the company.

无论其受训、经验、当前角色等为何。CEO 将这一洞见应用于重组公司内的各个团队。

[It is interesting to note that the company did succeed in reinventing itself, grew into an industry leader in their area by finding and filling in the 'open spaces' not being addressed by other companies. They were eventually acquired several years later by a large multi-national publishing giant. As a result, the astute CEO became a multi-millionaire many times over and was able to essentially retire (and move on to other generative projects) while only in his late 30's.]

〔有趣的是，这家公司确实成功实现了自我重塑，通过寻找并填补其他公司未曾触及的"开阔空间"，成长为所在领域内的行业领导者。数年后，它被一家大型跨国出版巨头收购。结果，这位敏锐的 CEO 在三十多岁便成了身家数倍于百万的富豪，得以实质上退休（并转向其他生成式项目）。〕

加布里埃尔·罗斯鲜活的遗产Gabrielle Roth's Living Legacy

Like all of the other geniuses in this book, Roth's creative spirit has been a continuing source of inspiration in my own work. In fact, Deborah and I have incorporated Roth's teachings and works into a number of our own programs including The Power of Presence, The Hero's Journey and 5Rhythms and Coaching at the Identity Level. It has also been a major influence on the process of Generative Coaching that I developed with Dr. Stephan Gilligan as well as my other developments in the area of generative change.

如同本书中所有其他天才，罗斯的创造精神，一直是我自身工作中持续的灵感来源。事实上，黛博拉与我将罗斯的教导与著作，融入了我们数个项目中，包括当下的力量（The Power of Presence）、英雄之旅（The Hero's Journey）与身份层次的五律波与教练（5Rhythms and Coaching at the Identity Level）。它也对我和斯蒂芬·吉利根（Dr. Stephan Gilligan）博士共同发展出的生成式教练（Generative Coaching）过程，以及我在生成式改变领域的其他发展，有重大影响。

A final poignant connection with Gabrielle came in 2012 when she was dying of cancer at the same time my wife Deborah was going through her own journey with breast cancer. Like Bateson, Roth had been heavy smoker earlier in her life and had received a diagnosis of advanced lung cancer. As Roth had written years earlier about Bateson, Gabrielle moved through this last stage "openly, bravely and gracefully."

Roth passed her legacy to her son Jonathan Horan, director of 5Rhythms Global. Her work is carried on through all the teachers trained by her or her son and his staff, and through people everywhere dancing the Rhythms throughout the world. You can go to www.5rhythms.com for information about classes, teachers, music and books.

与体感心智接轨Engaging the Somatic Mind

Like Erickson, Bateson and Feldenkrais, Gabrielle Roth sought to engage an intelligence and wisdom beyond the limits of the logical, cognitive verbal mind. She viewed movement and connection with the body as key to the process of change, emphasizing the importance of somatic intelligence, which she viewed as lacking in modern society. As she explained in her book Connections:

如同艾瑞克森、贝特森与费登奎斯，加布里埃尔·罗斯寻求调动一种超越逻辑、认知、言语心智之限制的智能与智慧。她视运动与身体的联结为改变过程的关键，强调体感智能的重要性——她认为这在现代社会中是匮乏的。正如她在《联结》（Connections）一书中所解释的：

The whole point is embodiment. If we look at where we are today, particularly in Western culture, body and spirit have been separated. We walk around thinking about everything, like talking heads; big heads.
全部的要点在于具身化（embodiment）。如果我们看看今日我们置身何处，尤其是在西方文化中，身体与精神已被分离。我们走来走去，成天思考一切，像一个个会说话的脑袋；大头们。

Roth believed that the separation of body and spirit was at the root of many individual and collective problems. Like the other geniuses profiled in this book she recognized that symptoms often arise from disconnection — between mind and body, between individual and environment, between intention and action. Gabrielle understood the power of engaging the somatic mind as a way of activating a deeper systemic intelligence that could guide change in a naturally ecological manner. As she described it:

罗斯相信，身体与精神的分离，是许多个体与集体问题的根源。如同本书中刻画的其余天才，她认识到症状常常源于失联——心智与身体之间、个体与环境之间、意图与行动之间。加布里埃尔理解调动体感心智的力量，视其为激活一种更深的系统智能的方式，能以自然符合生态（ecological）的方式引导改变。正如她所描述的：

When you awaken your intuition and get in the habit of listening to and acting on it, every decision in your life will become reflective of your inner truth. The trick is not to try to work out all the answers in your head. Rather, take whatever information you need into your body, trust your gut, then dance or meditate or walk or do whatever you need to enter a relaxed state of being and you'll know what to do.
当你唤醒你的直觉、养成倾听并依其行动的习惯，你生命中的每一项决定，都将反映出你内在的真相。诀窍不在于试图在脑袋里把所有的答案都算出来。相反，把你所需的任何信息带入身体，信任你的直觉（gut），然后去跳舞、冥想、散步，或做任何你需要的事，进入一种放松的存有状态，你便会知道该怎么做。

It is a fundamental fact of our existence that our physical body and nervous system are always in the present. As I like to point out, we don't heal the past in the past. We don't create the future in the future. We heal the past by bringing it into the present through new filters. Similarly, we create the future moment by moment with every choice we make. The key is to be able to make those choices from a place of clarity and connection with what really matters to us. According to Roth:

我们存在的一项基本事实是：我们的身体与神经系统，永远处于当下。正如我所喜欢指出的，我们不在过去疗愈过去。我们不在未来创造未来。我们通过将过去带入当下、经由新的滤镜来疗愈过去。同样地，我们借着每一个所做的选择、一刻一刻地创造未来。关键在于，能够从一份清晰、并与我们真正在乎之物相联的状态，做出那些选择。据罗斯所言：

The question I ask myself and everyone else is, "Do you have the discipline to be a free spirit? Can we be free of all that binds and bends us into a shape of consciousness that has nothing to do with who we are from moment to moment, from breath to breath?"
我向自己与每个人提出的问题都是："你是否拥有成为一个自由灵魂的自律（discipline）？我们能否从一切将我们束缚、扭曲成一种与'我们一刻一刻、一息一息到底是谁'毫无关系的意识形态之中解脱出来？"

Like Moshe Feldenkrais, Roth focused on movement as the primary path to connecting with a deeper somatic wisdom. She claimed:

如同摩西·费登奎斯，罗斯聚焦于运动，视其为联结更深体感智慧的首要路径。她宣称：

Movement is my medicine, my meditation, my metaphor and my method, a living language we can rely upon to tell us the truth about who we are, who we are with, and where we are going.
运动是我的药、我的冥想、我的隐喻、我的方法，是一种我们可以倚靠的活的语言，告诉我们关于自己是谁、我们与谁同在、我们要往何处去的真相。

Roth's description of movement as a "living language" is revealing and, of course, fits perfectly with the notion of somatic syntax. All language has structure (both deep structure and surface structure) with the ultimate purpose to convey meaning. When we structure movement in order to communicate something to ourselves or others it becomes "dance."

罗斯将运动描述为"一种活的语言"，这一说法发人深省，当然也完美契合体感句法的概念。一切语言都有结构（既有深层结构，也有表层结构），其终极目的是传达意义。当我们将运动结构化、以与我们自身或他人沟通某物时，它就成了"舞蹈"。

舞蹈与深层结构Dance and Deep Structure

For Roth, dance provided the most direct access to the deepest parts of the somatic mind. As she maintained:

对罗斯而言，舞蹈提供了通往体感心智最深层部分最直接的路径。正如她所坚持的：

Dance is the fastest, most direct route to the truth — not some big truth that belongs to everybody, but the get down and personal kind, the what's-happening-in-me-right-now kind of truth.
舞蹈是通往真相最快、最直接的路径——不是那种属于每个人的大真相，而是俯身而下、切身相关的那种，是"此刻在我内在正发生什么"的那种真相。

Grolier's Encyclopedia defines dance as "an expression in rhythmic movement of an intensified sense of life, arising from an inner perception that stimulates both mind and body. By expressing that perception in the form of dance people affirm their delight in existence." Thus, dance is a multi-level process which, like all forms of language, involves surface structures that are transforms of common deep structures. This is reflected in Gabrielle Roth's claim that:

格罗利尔百科全书（Grolier's Encyclopedia）将舞蹈定义为"对一种强化了的生命的感受（sense of life）之节奏性运动中的表达，源于刺激心智与身体的内在感知。人们以舞蹈的形式表达那感知，从而肯定他们对存在的喜悦。"因此，舞蹈是一个多层次的过程，如同所有语言形式，涉及作为共同深层结构之转换的表层结构。这反映于加布里埃尔·罗斯的宣称：

We dance to fall in love with the spirit in all things, to wipe out memory or transform it into moves that nobody else can make because they didn't live it. We dance to hook up to the true genius lurking behind all the b\\s\\\\ — to seek refuge in our originality and our power to reinvent ourselves; to shed the past, forget the future and fall into the moment feet first.
我们跳舞，是为了坠入对所有事物之精神的热爱，是为了抹去记忆、或将其转化为无人能跳出的动作，因为那不是他们活出来的。我们跳舞，是为了 hook up 那潜藏于一切胡说（b\\s\\\\）背后的真正天才——在原创性与我们重塑自我的力量中寻求庇护；是为了抛下过去、忘掉未来、双脚先着地地跌入当下。

From an NLP perspective, dance is a fundamental output channel of the kinesthetic representational system that can form a gateway to the "wisdom of the body." NLP, however, has only recently been exploring the significance of dance and movement in the processes of healing, planning and change. The historical focus of NLP has been primarily on the cognitive and emotional aspects of the kinesthetic representational system. Patterns of physiology were viewed from a more static, mechanical perspective, primarily as cues or triggers for cognitive processes. NLP developments such as Spatial Sorting and Somatic Syntax have begun to build a link to dance and movement as key elements in a number of NLP processes and techniques.

从 NLP 的视角看，舞蹈是动觉表征系统的一个基本输出通道，能够成为通往"身体智慧"的门户。然而，NLP 直到最近才开始探索舞蹈与运动在疗愈、规划与改变过程中的意义。NLP 历史性的焦点，主要置于动觉表征系统的认知与情绪面向。生理的模式被从一个更静态、机械的视角看待，主要作为认知过程的线索或触发器。诸如空间排序（Spatial Sorting）与体感句法等 NLP 发展，已开始将舞蹈与运动建构成众多 NLP 过程与技术中的关键元素。

In Third Generation NLP and Generative Change work, we frequently work with what we call "somatic models." Somatic models are representations created for some aspect of a change process of change made through gestures, postures and movements of either a problem state, a desired state, or resources. For example, if a client says, "I would like to have a more harmonious relationship with my partner," we might respond, "Show that with your body. What gesture and movement represent that harmonious relationship?"

在第三代 NLP 与生成式改变的工作中，我们常常运用我们所谓的"体感模型"（somatic models）。体感模型，是为改变过程的某个面向——无论是问题状态、所欲状态，还是资源——透过姿态、体态与运动所创造的表征。例如，如果一位客户说"我希望与我的伴侣有更和谐的关系"，我们或许回应："用你的身体展示那一点。什么样的姿态与运动，代表了那段和谐的关系？"

Making such repetitive movements frequently brings much greater clarity and engagement with the change the client is seeking.

制造这类重复性的运动，常常带来对客户所寻求之改变的远更清晰的觉察与投入。

As I mentioned in the previous chapter on Feldenkrais, according to Somatic Syntax, the body itself is a representational system. This is nowhere more obvious than in dance, where movements are not simply physical actions but rather expressions of emotion, identity and relationship. In this sense dance has both behavioral and symbolic aspects and can become a powerful means to an end — that of transformation — rather than the end in and of itself. In Roth's words:

正如我在前一章论费登奎斯时提到的，依据体感句法，身体本身即是一个表征系统。这一点在舞蹈中最为明显——运动不只是身体动作，更是情感、身份与关系的表达。在此意义上，舞蹈兼具行为与象征两个面向，并能成为达到目的——即转化——的有力手段，而非其自身就是目的。用罗斯的话说：

The dance itself will become a catalyst and shake things up inside of us and to get old things moving so that we can drop and shed and let go and release.
舞蹈本身将成为一种催化剂，在我们内在搅动一切，让陈旧之物动起来，好让我们能够卸下、 shedding、放下、释放。

As Roth would wryly point out during her programs, "You didn't really think this was about dancing, did you?"

正如罗斯在她的项目中会揶揄地指出："你该不会真以为这关乎跳舞，是吧？"

节奏的转化能力The Transformational Capacity of Rhythm

One of the purest expressions of Somatic Syntax is rhythm. With respect to the body, rhythm can be defined as "a regular, repeated pattern of movement." In NLP, we view these repeating patterns of movement as somatic deep structures that can mobilize and integrate various qualities of knowing and processing within our nervous systems and somatic minds. Different rhythms can function as somatic "accessing cues" and "meta programs" that organize fundamental patterns of relationships.

体感句法最纯粹的表达之一，便是节奏。就身体而言，节奏可定义为"一种规律的、重复的运动模式"。在 NLP 中，我们将这些重复的运动模式视为体感深层结构，能够调动并整合我们神经系统与体感心智中各类认知与处理的质量。不同的节奏，能作为体感的"取用线索"（accessing cues）与"元程序"（meta programs），组织起关系的基本模式。

Similar to the way that the various types of brainwaves in our cerebral cortex (i.e., alpha, beta, delta, theta, etc.) bring about different states of consciousness in our cognitive minds, rhythms in the body bring out different states of awareness and functioning in our somatic minds.

类似于我们大脑皮层中各类脑波（即 alpha、beta、delta、theta 等）为我们的认知心智带来不同的意识状态，身体中的节奏，也为我们的体感心智唤起不同的觉察与运作状态。

Gabrielle Roth's 5Rhythms® are a powerful example of the transformational capacity of rhythm and somatic syntax. The 5Rhythms®

加布里埃尔·罗斯的五律波®，是节奏与体感句法之转化能力的一个有力例证。五律波®

are the result of Gabrielle Roth's many years of observation of how energy moves in people and in life. As she points out in Sweat Your Prayers (1997):

是加布里埃尔·罗斯长年观察能量如何在人与生命中流动的结果。正如她在《挥汗祈祷》（Sweat Your Prayers，1997）中指出的：

Energy moves in waves. Waves move in patterns. Patterns move in rhythms. A human being is all of those: energy, waves, patterns and rhythms.
能量以波的形式运动。波以模式的形式运动。模式以节奏的形式运动。一个人就是这一切：能量、波、模式与节奏。

〔图 p196〕

A human being is energy, waves, patterns and rhythms, and we can choose what rhythm we want for each situation in life.

转化之波：通过五律波®具身生生不息的智慧The Wave of Transformation: Embodying Generative Intelligence Through the 5Rhythms®

The 5Rhythms® offer not only a movement practice, but a profound map of somatic intelligence in action — a living model of how change unfolds through the body, through awareness, and through our participation in a larger field of experience.

五律波® 不仅提供一套运动练习，更是一幅处于行动中的体感智能的深刻地图——一幅关于改变如何经由身体、经由觉察、经由我们对更大经验场域之参与而展开的活的模型。

The Wave, as it is called, is both a structure and a process. It is a sequence of five rhythms — flowing, staccato, chaos, lyrical, and stillness — through which the body moves. Yet beneath this sequence lies something deeper: a set of generative principles that mirror the very dynamics of transformation across all levels of human experience.
所谓的"波浪"（The Wave），既是一种结构，也是一个过程。它是一系列五个节奏——流动、断奏、混沌、抒情、静止——身体循此而运动的序列。然而在这序列之下，潜藏着更深层的东西：一组生成性的原则，映照出人类经验各层次中转化的真正动力。

〔图 p196〕

"The Wave" as it is called, is both a structure and a process. It is a sequence of five rhythms — flowing, staccato, chaos, lyrical, and stillness — through which the body moves.

流动：生成性的根基Flowing: The Ground of Generativity

Every process of transformation begins with making connections. In the rhythm of flowing, we are invited to return to the most fundamental relationships we have: our connection to the ground, to our bodies, and to the present moment. Movement begins in the feet — rooted, receptive, and responsive. The body follows the feet and begins to move in a continuous easy motion. The movement is grounded, connected and circular. There is no forcing, no striving. Instead, there is a gentle entering into engagement with whatever is present within our bodies.

每一个转化过程，都始于建立联结。在流动的节奏中，我们被邀请回归最根本的关系：与大地、与身体、与当下时刻的联结。运动始于双脚——扎根、接纳、回应。身体跟随双脚，开始以连续而轻松的动作移动。运动是扎根的、联结的、圆形的。没有强求，没有挣扎。相反，是一种轻柔地进入与身体内一切当下之物的 engagement。

Flowing reflects one of the core patterns of genius: the ability to enter a system by attuning to it.

流动反映了天才的核心模式之一：通过调频进入一个系统来进入它的能力。

〔图 p195〕

Flowing

伟大的创新者与改变推动者——无论是在疗愈、领导力、艺术等领域——都不是从强加结构开始。他们从感知、倾听、与已然存在之物对齐开始。这正是米尔顿·艾瑞克森如此精妙地示范的精髓：在人们所在之处与他们相遇，并让改变从他们自身的经验之中涌现。

Great innovators and change agents — whether in healing, leadership, art, etc. — do not begin by imposing structure. They begin by sensing, by listening, by aligning with what is already present. This is the essence of what Milton Erickson demonstrated so masterfully: meeting people where they are and allowing change to emerge from within their own experience.

在流动之中，我们体验：

In flowing, we experience:

  • 接纳而非控制
  • 觉察而非分析
  • 临在而非表演
  • Receptivity instead of control
  • Awareness rather than analysis
  • Presence in place of performance

如果我们失去与双脚和大地的联结，我们便遭遇流动的阴影：惰性、自动性，或漫无方向地漂流。但当我们扎根，某种东西便开始积聚。如同海浪成形，能量汇聚。

If we lose our connection to the feet and the earth, we encounter the shadow of flowing: inertia, automaticity, or drifting without direction. But when we are grounded, something begins to build. Like a wave forming in the ocean, energy gathers.

断奏：形式的涌现Staccato: The Emergence of Form

As energy increases, it naturally seeks expression. From the continuity of flowing emerges the rhythm of staccato — clear, defined, intentional. If flowing is the in-breath, staccato is the out-breath. It is the moment when movement takes a specific shape. Staccato embodies another fundamental expression of somatic intelligence: the capacity to give form to energy.

随着能量增长，它自然寻求表达。从流动的连续性中，涌现出断奏的节奏——清晰、明确、有意图。如果流动是吸气，断奏便是呼气。那是运动取得具体形态的时刻。断奏体现了体感智能另一项基本的表达：赋予能量以形式的能力。

Here we move from receptivity to expression:

此处我们从接纳走向表达：

  • From sensing to acting
  • 从感知到行动
  • From possibility to commitment
  • From connection to communication
  • 从可能性到承诺
  • 从联结到沟通

This rhythm reflects the complement of yang to the yin of flowing — the interplay between openness and direction. It is through this dynamic that meaningful action becomes possible.

这一节奏反映了阳（yang）对阴（yin）之流动的互补——开放与方向之间的互动。正是通过这动力，有意义的行动才成为可能。

〔图 p194〕

Staccato

在格里高里·贝特森的工作中，我们见到类似的原则：模式通过关系而涌现。断奏是关系变得可见之处——边界在此界定、选择在此做出、意图在此具身。

In the work of Gregory Bateson, we see a similar principle: patterns emerge through relationships. Staccato is where relationship becomes visible — where boundaries are defined, choices are made, and intention becomes embodied.

当通过流动的节奏而扎根、落地，断奏带来清晰、聚焦、承诺与健康的边界。当未扎根、未落地，它便会退化为僵化、攻击性或控制。这反映了一条生生不息的智慧的关键洞见：每一项资源，当它未被体感地扎根与涵容时，都有其阴影。

When centered and grounded through the rhythm of flowing, staccato brings clarity, focus, commitment and healthy boundaries. When uncentered and ungrounded, it can devolve into rigidity, aggression, or control. This reflects a key insight of generative intelligence: every resource has a shadow when it is not somatically grounded and held.

混沌：通往转化的门户Chaos: The Gateway to Transformation

As energy continues to build, it eventually reaches a threshold where existing structures can no longer contain it. This is the rhythm of chaos. In chaos, we encounter one of the most profound patterns of genius: the ability to let go of structure in order to access a higher order.

随着能量持续积聚，它终将达到一个临界点，既有的结构再也无法容纳它。这便是混沌的节奏。在混沌之中，我们遭遇最深奥的天才模式之一：为了取用一个更高阶的次序、而放下结构的能力。

One of the key discoveries of the scientific study of chaos (chaos theory) is that a certain degree of chaos is necessary for any system to self-organize into a new order. Further, often what appears to be "chaotic" at one level can actually be quite orderly on another level. Usually, when we call something "chaotic" it is because we cannot cognitively predict its behavior, and the reason we cannot predict its behavior is because we are not able to consciously track of all of the elements in the complex system producing the behavior.

混沌的科学研究（混沌理论）的一项关键发现是：任一系统要自组织为新的次序，一定程度的混沌是必要的。进一步说，在某一层次看似"混沌"之物，在另一层次上往往相当有序。通常，当我们称某物"混沌"时，是因为我们无法在认知上预测其行为，而我们之所以无法预测其行为，是因为我们无力有意识地追踪产生该行为的复杂系统中的所有元素。

Thus, dancing chaos is not about creating disorderly movements for its own sake. It is a necessary phase in a process of transformation that allows: (a) old patterns to dissolve, (b) fixed identities to loosen and (c) new possibilities to emerge.

因此，舞动混沌，并非为了制造无序的运动而制造。它是一个转化过程中必要的一环，允许：(a) 旧有模式消融，(b) 固定的身份松脱，以及 (c) 新的可能性涌现。

In generative change, this corresponds to what is described as "going beyond the problem space" or "getting outside of the box." It is the moment

在生成式改变中，这对应于所谓&#x7684;"超越问题空间"&#x6216;"跳出盒子"。它是我们

when we release our attachment to existing solutions and open to something fundamentally new.

释放对既有解法之执着、并向某种根本崭新的事物敞开的时刻。

This is also where many change processes struggle — because chaos can feel uncomfortable, even threatening. Without a foundation in flowing and staccato, it can become overwhelming.

这也是许多改变过程挣扎之处——因为混沌可能令人不适，甚至受到威胁。若没有流动与断奏奠定的基础，它可能变得难以承受。

〔图 p193〕

Chaos

但当它被前两个节奏所创造的、由觉察与临在构成的"容器"所涵容，混沌便成为一道门户：

But when held within a "container" of awareness and presence created by the previous two rhythms, chaos becomes a gateway:

  • 从控制到臣服（surrender）
  • 从确定到开放
  • 从重复到创新
  • From control to surrender
  • From certainty to openness
  • From repetition to innovation

混沌的阴影——困惑、不堪重负、失联——在我们失去扎根时升起。但它的馈赠是更新。如同浪峰碎裂，混沌释放了先前被锁在过时模式中的能量。

The shadow of chaos — confusion, overwhelm, disconnection — arises when we lose our grounding. But its gift is renewal. Like the crest of a wave breaking, chaos frees energy that was previously locked in outdated patterns.

抒情：新生的表达Lyrical: The Expression of the New

Out of chaos emerges lyrical — the rhythm of spontaneous creativity. Here we encounter the essence of generative intelligence: the capacity to create something new that is uniquely appropriate to the moment.

自混沌之中，涌现出抒情——自发创造力的节奏。在此我们遭遇生生不息的智慧的精髓：创造出某种崭新、且独特地契合当下之物的能力。

Lyrical is not random. It is the natural expression of a system that has moved through connection, structure, and release. It is where:

抒情并非随机。它是一个穿越了联结、结构、释放的系统之自然表达。它是：

  • Creativity becomes embodied
  • Expression becomes authentic
  • Movement takes on new meaning
  • 创造力得以具身之处
  • 表达变得真实之处
  • 运动获得新意义之处

〔图 p192〕

Lyrical

这一节奏直接反映了"进入开阔空间"的原则。伟大的创新者——如艾瑞克森、贝特森、费登奎斯与罗斯——不会简单地重复既有的模式。他们持续"好奇地去发现"可能涌现的新模式是什么。

This rhythm directly reflects the principle of "going into the open space." The great innovators — like Erickson, Bateson, Feldenkrais and Roth — do not simply repeat existing patterns. They are constantly "curious to discover" what new patterns could possibly emerge.

在抒情之中，我们体验嬉戏、自由、原创与轻盈。它是浪峰上的泡沫——一个不可见过程的可视表达。然而，若没有扎根，抒情可能变得肤浅或失联。抒情节奏带来的创造力，并非某种向观念心智世界的逃逸——它是被自由表达的具身临在。

In lyrical, we experience playfulness, freedom, originality and lightness. It is the foam at the crest of the wave — the visible expression of an invisible process. Yet, without grounding, lyrical can become superficial or disconnected. The creativity that comes from the rhythm of lyrical is not some type of escape into a mental world of ideas — it is embodied presence expressed freely.

静止：整合与场域觉察Stillness: Integration and Field Awareness

The wave completes itself in stillness. Stillness is not the absence of movement — it is in a sense the space which holds the potential for all other movement. There is an old saying that "still waters run deep." This is certainly true of the rhythm of stillness.

波浪在静止之中完成自身。静止并非运动的缺席——它在某种意义上，是那容纳所有其他运动之潜能的空间。有句老话："静水流深。"这对静止的节奏而言确为真。

In stillness, we are simultaneously fully present in our bodies and deeply connected to something beyond our bodies. Stillness allows the experience of what Gregory Bateson referred to as a "larger mind" of which our individual minds are a subsystem. It is a very deep "pattern which connects" us to a larger system. In my own work, stillness corresponds to a "spiritual" level in which we have access to a "field" intelligence beyond our own individual minds.

在静止之中，我们同时全然临在于身体、并深深联结于身体之外之物。静止容许体验到格里高里·贝特森所谓的"更大的心智"（larger mind）——我们个体的心智只是它的一个子系统。那是一种非常深的"连接之模式"，将我们与更大的系统相连。在我自身的工作中，静止对应于一个"灵性"层次，在其中我们能取用一个超越个体心智的"场域"智能。

Centered stillness brings deep presence, integration and alignment with purpose. Its shadow — lethargy, dissociation, disembodiment — arises when we disconnect from our own somatic center while attempting to connect to the larger field. Grounded stillness is the embodied awareness that we are truly a part of a larger holarchy. And it is from stillness that the wave begins again.

扎根的静止，带来深的临在、整合，以及与目标的 alignment。它的阴影—— lethargy、解离、离身（disembodiment）——在我们试图联结更大场域、却与自身的体感中心失联时升起。扎根的静止，是那具身的觉察：我们真正是更大全息层级（holarchy）的一部分。而正是从静止之中，波浪再次开始。

〔图 p191〕

Stillness

活出波浪Living the Wave

While practicing the 5Rhythms® Wave, Roth invited people to develop their awareness of the space around them and to learn to move attention into different parts of the body. She reminded them to continually "move into the empty spaces" and encouraged them to explore different ways of moving through space and of moving different body parts, thus progressively expanding their repertoire of ways of moving.

在练习五律波®的波浪时，罗斯邀请人们发展对周遭空间的觉察，并学习将注意力移入身体的不同部位。她提醒他们持续"移入空的空间"，鼓励他们探索穿越空间、以及运动不同身体部位的不同方式，从而逐步拓展他们运动方式的 repertoire。

Moving with awareness through the structure of the wave allows us to bring movement to unconscious patterns and to develop new patterns that progressively bring more freedom, flexibility and choice.

带着觉察、穿过波浪的结构而运动，让我们得以将运动带给无意识的模式，并发展出新的模式，逐步带来更多的自由、灵活与选择。

As a movement practice, the 5Rhythms® teaches us how following process contained in a clear structure can bring new awareness, insights, transformation and more choice – how discipline allows us to be a free spirit. A somatic practice like the 5Rhythms® helps us to regain our flexibility of expression and gain access to all dimensions of ourselves.

作为一项运动练习，五律波®教导我们：遵循一个清晰结构中所含的过程，能带来新的觉察、洞见、转化与更多选择——纪律（discipline）如何让我们成为一个自由灵魂。像五律波®这样的体感练习，帮助我们重获表达的灵活性，并取用我们自身的全部维度。

One of the paradoxes revealed by the 5Rhythms® is that structure enables freedom. The Wave provides a clear framework, yet within that framework there is infinite possibility. This reflects a deeper principle of generative intelligence: Freedom does not come from the absence of structure, but from exploration of what is possible within it. When we engage fully with such a structure:

五律波®所揭示的悖论之一是：结构赋能自由。波浪提供了一个清晰的框架，然而在这框架之内，有无限的可能性。这反映了一条生生不息的智慧的更深层原则：自由并非来自结构的缺席，而是来自对其内可能之事的探索。当我们全然投入这样的结构：

  • Discipline becomes a pathway to creativity
  • Awareness becomes a gateway to transformation
  • Structure becomes a container for emergence

In Gabrielle's words:

  • 纪律成为创造力的路径
  • 觉察成为转化的门户
  • 结构成为涌现的容器
In flowing you discover yourself. In staccato you define yourself. Chaos helps you dissolve yourself, so you don't end up fixed and rigid in the self you've discovered and defined. Lyrical inspires you to devote yourself to digging deep into the unique expression of your energy. And stillness allows you to disappear into the big energy that holds us all so you can start the whole process over again.

用加布里埃尔的话说：

在流动中你发现自己。在断奏中你定义自己。混沌帮助你消融自己，好让你不会在已发现、已定义的自己身上变得固定而僵化。抒情启发你奉献自己，深挖你能量的独特表达。而静止让你消失于那怀抱我们所有人的大能量之中，好让你能重新开始整个过程。

While the wave of the 5Rhythms® provides a powerful map of transformation, the learning happens first and primarily living it in the body. Through the wave, the intelligence of the somatic mind will nurture the cognitive mind. But it is ultimately a learning process that begins in (and never leaves) the body.

The following exercise provides a way to experience the transformational potential of each of the 5Rhythms®:

尽管五律波®的波浪提供了一幅有力的转化地图，但学习首先且首要地发生于身体的活出之中。通过波浪，体感心智的智能将滋养认知心智。但它终究是一个始于身体（且从不离开身体）的学习过程。

以下练习，提供了一种体验五律波®各自转化潜力的方式：

驾驭改变之波Riding the Wave of Change

  1. Create a location that represents your current state. Put yourself into your present experience of that problem or situation. See what you see, hear what you hear and feel what you feel regarding that situation. Allow your body to create a somatic expression — a gesture and/or repetitive movement — of how you experience your present state.
  1. 创造一个代表你当前状态的位置。将自己投入你对那问题或情境的当下经验之中。看见你所见、听见你所听、感受你所感，关于那情境。允许你的身体创造出一种体感表达——一个姿态和/或重复性运动——来表达你如何体验你当前的状态。
  2. 向前数步，进入一个代表你所欲状态的位置。将自己投入你对"那会是什么样"的经验之中。看见你所见、听见你所听、感受你所感，关于那经验。再次，允许你的身体创造出一种体感表达——一个姿态和/或重复性运动——来表达你如何体验你所欲的状态。
  3. 在你当前状态与所欲状态的位置之间，腾出一片空间，让你能将五个节奏逐一带入：流动、断奏、混沌、抒情与静止。
  4. 经过以下过程，逐一取用五个节奏：
  1. Move forward several steps into a location the represents your desired state. Put yourself into your experience of how it would be. See what you see, hear what you hear, feel what you feel regarding that experience. Again, allow your body to create a somatic expression — a gesture and/or repetitive movement — of how you experience your desired state.

a. 走到你当前状态的位置，做出与该经验相连的那个姿态和/或运动。向前迈步，将那运动带入流动的节奏。让你的身体跟随双脚，开始以连续而轻松、扎根、联结、圆形的运动移动。练习联结与接纳，吸气，并在运动中"与"当前状态经验"同在"。以流入与你所欲状态相连的体感表达作结。

  1. In between the locations for your present and desired states, make a space into which you can bring each of the five rhythms: flowing, staccato, chaos, lyrical and stillness.

b. 回到你当前状态的位置，再次做出与该经验相连的那个姿态和/或运动。向前迈步，将那运动带入断奏的节奏。扎根你的双脚，让它们找到一种重复的节拍，以你步伐的力量将其标出，深深感到与你自身及环境的联结。感知你的心跳，当你呼气时，用你的身体做出强而清晰、分明的运动与姿态。练习将聚焦、专注、承诺与清晰带入当前

  1. Go through the following process, accessing each of the five rhythms:

a. Go to the location of your present state and make the gesture and/or movement associated with that experience. Step forward and bring that movement into the rhythm of flowing. Let your body follow your feet and begin to move in continuous easy movement that is grounded, connected and circular. Practice connection and receptivity, breathing in and "being with" the present state experience in movement. End by flowing into the somatic expression associated with your desired state.

状态经验。以有力而自信地移入与你所欲状态相连的体感表达作结。

b. Return to the location of your present state and again make the gesture and/or movement associated with that experience. Step forward and bring that movement into the rhythm of staccato. Ground your feet and let them find a repetitive beat, marking it out with strength in your steps, feeling deeply connected to yourself and to your environment. Sense your heartbeat and as you breathe out, make strong, clear and distinct movements and gestures with your body. Practice bringing focus, concentration, commitment and clarity into the present

c. 再次从你当前状态的位置、以及与之相连的体感表达开始，向前迈步，将那表达带入混沌的节奏。在双脚保持稳固扎根的同时，让你的步伐左右交替。松开你的脖颈与头部，让你的身体如同由颤动的橡胶制成般运动。允许自己释放任何紧张或僵化，为旧有模式带入灵活性。释放任何与当前状态经验相连、被卡住的能量。以将混沌的松动与释放，带入与你所欲状态相连的体感表达作结。

d. 从你当前状态的位置、以及与之相连的体感表达开始，向前迈步，将那表达带入抒情那轻盈而自由的节奏。让自己探索自发的创造力，表达当下对你而言真实、独特而鲜活的一切。允许自己原创、不可预测、全然鲜活。用任何似乎恰当的身体与能量形式，来为当前状态经验带来轻盈，与之嬉戏。将那自由之感，骑乘进入与你所欲状态相连的体感表达之中。

state experience. End by moving strongly and confidently into the somatic expression associated with your desired state.

e. 再一次，回到你当前状态的位置与与之相连的体感表达。向前迈步，将那表达带入最后的静止节奏。当你做出与当前状态相连的运动与姿态，缓慢而正念地移动，并不时停顿。感受能量以一种形式的全幅临在——那形式让你既能联结自身、又能超越自身、联结更大的场域。体验自己作为一个移动中心之静止点，与周遭空间相连。让自己平稳地抵达与你所欲状态相连的体感表达。

c. Start again the location of your present state, making the gesture and/or movement associated with that experience. Step forward and bring that movement into the rhythm of chaos. Keeping a firm rooting in your feet, alternate your steps from side to side. Let go of your neck and head and let your body move as if it were made of jiggling rubber. Allow yourself to release any tension or rigidity and bring flexibility into old patterns. Free up any stuck energy associated with the present state experience. Finish by bringing the looseness and release of chaos into the somatic expression associated with your desired state.

  1. 反思你从每个节奏所学到的。

d. Beginning in the location of your present state and the somatic expression associated with that experience, step forward and bring that expression into the light and free rhythm of lyrical. Let yourself explore spontaneous creativity and express whatever is true, unique and alive for you in the moment. Allow yourself to be original, unpredictable, and fully alive. Play with whatever physical and energetic forms seem appropriate to bring lightness to the present state experience. Ride that sense of freedom into the somatic expression associated with your desired state.

e. Once more, go back to the location of your present state and the somatic expression associated with that experience. Step forward and bring that expression into the final rhythm of stillness. As you make the movements and gestures associated with the present state, move slowly and mindfully, pausing from time to time. Feel the full presence of energy in a form that allows you to connect both to yourself and beyond yourself to a larger field. Experience yourself as the stillpoint of a moving center, connected with the space surrounding you. Let yourself smoothly arrive at the somatic expression associated with your desired state.

  1. Reflect on what you have learned from each rhythm.

作为生生不息的智慧模型的波浪The Wave as a Model of Generative Intelligence

Perhaps the most important insight of Gabrielle Roth's 5Rhythms is that the Wave is not confined to the dance floor. As Roth emphasized, how we move is how we live. The patterns we enact in movement reflect the patterns we enact in life: where we resist; where we control; where we let go; where we connect; where we create.

或许加布里埃尔·罗斯五律波®最重要的洞见，在于波浪并不局限于舞池。正如罗斯所强调的，我们如何运动便是我们如何生活。我们在运动中践行出的模式，反映了我们在生活中践行出的模式：我们在何处抗拒、在何处控制、在何处放下、在何处联结、在何处创造。

By bringing awareness to these patterns, we gain the ability to transform them. This is the essence of generative practice:

通过对这些模式带入觉察，我们获得了转化它们的能力。这便是生成式实践的精髓：

  • Making the unconscious conscious
  • Expanding our repertoire of responses
  • Increasing flexibility and choice
  • 将无意识变为有意识
  • 拓展我们的回应 repertoire
  • 增进灵活性与选择

Movement, like a wave, brings renewal. It opens space in the system allowing new possibilities to emerge. One of the things that makes the 5Rhythms® so powerful is that they are not just a movement practice — they

运动，如同波浪，带来更新。它在系统中敞开空间，让新的可能性涌现。使五律波®如此有力的因素之一，在于它们不只是一套运动练习——它们

are a living model of generative intelligence. Each rhythm reflects a phase in the process of transformation:

是一幅生生不息的智慧的活模型。每一个节奏，都反映着转化过程的一个阶段：

  1. Flowing – Entering the system (connection)
  2. Staccato – Giving form (expression)
  3. Chaos – Letting go (transformation)
  4. Lyrical – Creating anew (innovation)
  5. Stillness – Integrating (alignment with the larger field/ ecosystem)
  1. 流动 – 进入系统（联结）
  2. 断奏 – 赋予形式（表达）
  3. 混沌 – 放下（转化）
  4. 抒情 – 重新创造（创新）
  5. 静止 – 整合（与更大场域/生态系统的对齐）

This sequence mirrors the dynamics of change across all domains: learning, leadership, healing, creativity, organizational development, etc. It is, in essence, a somatic expression of the same principles that underlie genius and generative change. Each of these rhythms can have many forms of expression; dance and "somatic syntax" being the most obvious. They also, however, have corresponding visual and auditory expressions (as in art and music). They are also apparent at the process level of various techniques.

这一序列，映照出所有领域中改变的动力：学习、领导力、疗愈、创造力、组织发展等。它本质上，是支撑天才与生成式改变的同一原则之体感表达。这些节奏每一种，都可以有诸多形式的表达；舞蹈与"体感句法"是最显而易见的。然而，它们也有相应的视觉与听觉表达（如艺术与音乐中）。它们在各类技术的 process 层次上同样显而易见。

For example, in NLP, the Swish Pattern — where a mental image of an existing behavior is replaced by an image of a desired behavior in rapid succession — is an application of a staccato rhythm in order to produce a clear demarcation in behavior. The Dancing SCORE is more like a wave that starts in staccato (each part is first experienced separately and discretely) then adds flow (all of the elements are linked together into a unified sequence of movements) and then lyrical (resourceful/ transformational energies are brought in through the sequence of movements).

例如，在 NLP 中，闪变模式（Swish Pattern）——将既有行为的心理意象，迅速替换为所欲行为的心理意象——是应用断奏节奏以在行为中产生清晰 demarcation 的一例。舞蹈版 SCORE（Dancing SCORE）更像一道波浪：始于断奏（每一部分先被分别、离散地经验），然后加入流动（所有元素被联结为一个统一的运动序列），再是抒情（资源性/转化性能量透过运动序列被带入）。

The 5Rhythms® can also be loosely related to the phases of the Hero's Journey. Flowing (discovering oneself) may be linked to finding one's calling. Staccato (defining oneself) provides the strength and determination to commit to the calling and crossing the threshold. Chaos (dissolving oneself) allows one to enter into the inner state necessary to meet and transform demons and shadows. Lyrical (expressing oneself) can be related to the full connection with one's unique resources and the completion of the task. Stillness (connecting beyond oneself) is a powerful resource for "returning home" and preparing for the next journey.

五律波®也可松散地关联于英雄之旅（Hero's Journey）的各阶段。流动（发现自我）可联系于寻得自己的召唤（calling）。断奏（定义自我）提供承诺于召唤并跨越门槛（crossing the threshold）所需的力量与决心。混沌（消融自我）让人进入必要的内在状态，以遇见并转化恶魔与阴影。抒情（表达自我）可关联于与自己独特资源的完全联结、以及任务的完成。静止（超越自我而联结）&#x662F;"归家"（returning home）并准备下一段旅程的有力资源。

  1. Flowing: Earth – Body - Discovering yourself; finding your calling.
  2. Staccato: Fire – Heart - Defining yourself; crossing the threshold.
  3. Chaos: Water – Mind - Letting go and dissolving boundaries; transforming the "demon".
  4. Lyrical: Air – Soul - Expressing your uniqueness; expanding your resources.
  5. Stillness: Ether – Spirit - Connecting beyond yourself from deep within; returning home.
  1. 流动： 土 – 身体 – 发现自我；寻得你的召唤。
  2. 断奏： 火 – 心 – 定义自我；跨越门槛。
  3. 混沌： 水 – 心智 – 放下并消融边界；转化"恶魔"。
  4. 抒情： 风 – 灵魂 – 表达你的独特性；拓展你的资源。
  5. 静止： 以太 – 精神 – 从最深处与超越自我之物联结；归家。

There are many, many other examples. For instance, the rhythm of flowing is the basis for pacing, creating rapport, joining another person's model of the world and eliciting information about some current state. Flowing reflects the foundational NLP principle that effective change begins by meeting experience where it is. Before leading ourselves or others toward something new or different, we first connect and attune to what is going on in the present.

还有许许多多其他的例子。例如，流动的节奏，是跟（pacing）、建立融洽、融入另一个人的世界观、以及引出关于某一当前状态之信息的基础。流动反映了 NLP 的基础原则：有效的改变，始于在经验所在之处与之相遇。在引领我们自己或他人朝向崭新或不同之物前，我们首先要联结、并调频进入当下正发生什么。

In addition to techniques like the Swish Pattern the rhythm of staccato is the basis for creating well-formed outcomes, the installation of new responses and anchoring states. In the same way that flowing is the rhythm of pacing staccato is the rhythm of leading. Staccato gives form to intention. It involves commitment, differentiation and decisive movement in which the body and the nervous system are oriented in a specific direction.

除了闪变模式这类技术，断奏的节奏，也是创造良构结果（well-formed outcomes）、安装新回应与锚定状态的基础。正如流动是跟的节奏，断奏是带的节奏。断奏赋予意图以形式。 它涉及承诺、区分，以及身体与神经系统定向于一个特定方向的果断运动。

The rhythm of chaos is the foundation for processes like pattern interruption, break states and states of "not knowing." It promotes dissolution, release and letting go of fixed structures. This serves to destabilize automatic loops and free up energy that has been contained in old patterns.

混沌的节奏，是诸如模式中断（pattern interruption）、打破状态（break states）与"不知"（not knowing）状态等过程的基础。它促进消融、释放，并放下固定的结构。这有助于瓦解自动循环，释放被锁在旧模式中的能量。

Lyrical is the rhythm at the basis of creative processes like the 'Dreamer' phase of the Disney Imagineering strategy, identifying resources and Future Pacing. Lyrical is the embodiment of the spirit of exploration and the emergence of new possibilities after old structures have dissolved.

抒情是创造性过程——如迪士尼创想工程策略（Disney Imagineering strategy）的"梦想家"（Dreamer）阶段、辨识资源与未来预演（Future Pacing）——的基础节奏。抒情是探索精神之具身，以及旧结构消融之后、新可能性之涌现。

Stillness naturally leads to the shifts in our awareness associated with Meta Position and the connection with a larger purpose and the "field mind." Stillness promotes a larger systemic awareness through relaxed presence, minimal unnecessary movement and a sense of spaciousness.

静止自然导向与我们的觉察相关的那些转换——元位置（Meta Position），以及与更大目标、"场域心智"的联结。静止通过放松的临在、最少的不必要运动，以及一种空间感，促进一种更大的系统性觉察。

〔图 p187〕

Staccato: giving pace and energy to create your place. Chaos: letting go of structures and limitations. Lyrical: letting new options emerge. Flowing: entering the space and exploring. Stillness: reflecting on what happened and integrating insights.

结论：成为你自己Conclusion: Becoming Yourself

Ultimately, the 5Rhythms® and Gabrielle Roth's other movement practices are not simply something we do — they are something we live. They invite us to experience transformation not as a linear effort of self-improvement, but as an ongoing participatory process:

最终，五律波®与加布里埃尔·罗斯的其他运动练习，不只是我们做的事——它们是我们的生活。它们邀请我们将转化经验为：不是一项线性的自我改进努力，而是一个持续的参与性过程：

  • Grounding in connection and entering experience somatically
  • Giving form to intention and expressing it with clarity
  • Releasing outdated structures and letting go into transformation
  • 扎根于联结，并体感地进入经验
  • 赋予意图以形式，并清晰地表达它
  • 释放过时的结构，并放下进入转化
  • Creating from authenticity and allowing new possibilities to emerge
  • Reconnecting to something larger than ourselves and integrating with a larger field
  • 从真实性中创造，并让新的可能性涌现
  • 重新联结大于我们自身之物，并与更大的场域整合

This is the essence of generative intelligence. It is not about controlling change. It is about participating consciously in the unfolding of something larger than ourselves that we are already a part of. And when we remember to move, we discover that transformation is not something we have to force. It is something that naturally emerges. As Roth said, "Moving is the mystery. We never figure it out."

这便是生生不息的智慧的精髓。它不是关于控制改变。它是关于有意识地参与某种大于我们自身、且我们已然是其一部分之事物的展开。而当我们记起去运动，我们便发现转化并不是我们非得强求的东西。它是自然涌现的。正如罗斯所言："运动就是奥秘。我们永远无法将它想透。"

In many ways, Gabrielle Roth's work represents a return to one of the oldest forms of human knowing. Long before human beings analyzed experience cognitively, they moved with it, enacted it, danced it, ritualized it and embodied it. Roth reminds us that transformation is not merely something we think about — it is something we live through the body. In reconnecting movement, emotion, relationship and awareness, she restores a dimension of intelligence that modern culture has largely forgotten or ignored.

在许多方面，加布里埃尔·罗斯的工作，代表了对人类最古老认知形式之一的回归。远在人类以认知分析经验之前，他们便与之共舞、演之、蹈之、仪式化之、具身化之。罗斯提醒我们：转化不只是我们思及之物——它是我们透过身体活出的东西。在重新联结运动、情感、关系与觉察之中，她修复了现代文化大体上已遗忘或忽视的智能的一个维度。

Gabrielle understood that life itself unfolds rhythmically. Breathing, heartbeat, learning, creativity, grief, relationship, leadership and transformation all move in waves. Symptoms often comes when we resist the movement of these waves — when we cling to fixed identities, suppress feelings, or disconnect from the larger flow of life. Her work teaches us how to move with these rhythms consciously rather than ignore or reject them.

加布里埃尔理解，生命本身以节奏的方式展开。呼吸、心跳、学习、创造、悲伤、关系、领导力与转化，全都以波的形式运动。症状常常在我们抗拒这些波的流动时出现——当我们 cling to 固定身份、压抑感受，或与生命的更大之流失联。她的工作教导我们，如何有意识地与这些节奏共舞，而非忽视或拒斥它们。

One of Gabrielle's deepest teachings was the invitation to "move into the open space." This may be one of the most practical descriptions of generativity ever offered. Whether in dance, leadership, relationships or personal growth, the future rarely emerges from repeating familiar patterns. It emerges when we are willing to step into spaces that are not yet fully defined — to move toward possibility before certainty exists.

加布里埃尔最深的教导之一，是进入"开阔空间"的邀请。这或许是对生成性最实用的描述之一。无论是在舞蹈、领导力、关系还是个人成长中，未来极少从重复熟悉的模式中涌现。它涌现于我们愿意踏入尚未被完全定义的那些空间之时——在确定性存在之前，便朝可能性迈进。

Generativity requires the courage to get out of our comfort zones and go into the empty spaces. Roth would say, "We think we are afraid of death, but I see a room of people who are afraid to breathe. People are afraid of life. We give up our life to be safe. To be boring." She would then go on to ask, "What does it mean to be a human being?" Roth's answer was, "It is something imaginative and changing all the time." "Find out what is going on," she urged, by constantly exploring, "Who am I? Who am I?"

生成性要求我们有勇气走出舒适区、进入空的空间。罗斯会说："我们以为自己害怕死亡，但我看到一屋子害怕呼吸的人。人们害怕生命。我们为了安全而放弃了生命。为了无聊。"她接着会问："做一个人类意味着什么？"罗斯的答案是："它是某种充满想象、且时刻变化的东西。""弄清正在发生什么，"她敦促道，"通过不断探究，'我是谁？我是谁？'"

Like all of the geniuses explored in this volume, Gabrielle Roth was a completely unique human being, and she continually encouraged others to discover and embody their own uniqueness. When students told her they wanted to be more like her, she would smile and reply, "If you want to be more like me, be more like you."

如同本卷中探究的所有天才，加布里埃尔·罗斯是一个完全独特的人，而她持续鼓励他人去发现并具身化他们自身的独特性。当学生告诉她，他们想变得更像她，她会微笑着回答："如果你想变得更像我，那就变得更像你自己。"

For me, this captures the essence of her legacy. Gabrielle showed us that movement is more than motion. It is awareness becoming visible. It is spirit becoming embodied. It is the intelligence of life expressing itself through us moment by moment. And perhaps that is why movement can heal. Because when we move with awareness, we are no longer separate from the "dance" of life itself.

对我而言，这捕捉到了她遗产的精髓。加布里埃尔向我们表明：运动不止是动作。它是变得可见的觉察。它是变得具身的灵性。它是生命之智能一刻一刻地透过我们表达自身。而或许那正是运动能够疗愈的原因。因为当我们带着觉察运动，我们便不再与生命本身之"舞蹈"分离。
